# Head-to-head rounds on the switchboard (UNNUMBERED; this file is mine, new, so no other chat is writing in it)

# Round H1 -- the repo's 963-weight networks against the PD autopilot and against their own untrained start, on the tank's compass dropout (REGISTERED 2026-11-04, before any code and before any of its seeds was flown)

## 1. Why
The switchboard (`brain/switchboard.mjs`) can fly any 963-weight network against the PD autopilot on the tank's compass dropout. Its first smoke run (3 ids) said one network beat the autopilot by +0.125 and another tied it; nothing says which of the eight networks in the tree does what, or whether the training is what pays. The fish and fly notes found, again and again, that the structure and the optimizer pay and the connectome does not (`brain/RUNG_4A_CLOSEOUT.md`, `brain/PLAIN_NET_CONTROL_WIP.md` on `claude/sweet-lovelace-8leivh`). This round asks the one question those notes leave for the networks this tree HAS:

> On the tank's compass dropout, does each trained 963-weight network beat the PD autopilot, does it beat the hand-wired weights it was trained from, and is its gain specific to the dark?

It says nothing about the fish pilot (803 weights, not flown by this tank), the stateful fly (not in this tree) or the plain-net control (not in this tree); those arms are not on the board yet and a result here is not about them.

## 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (ten):** `autopilot` (the PD autopilot); `hand` (the untrained network: `brain/pilotSubPolicy.mjs` handWeights, the weights the training started from); and the eight trained networks in `brain/pilotSubWeights*Wip.json` with 963 weights, by file: **Wip, Actuators, Robust, RobustLong, RobustTail, RobustTail2, RobustWide, RobustNoisy**. The fish file (803 weights) is not flown. **Controls:** every trained network is read against `autopilot` and against `hand`; `hand` against `autopilot`.
**Task:** the switchboard's `dropout`: one-sub tanks, 160 s, the same spawn for every arm (ids sb-<n>), the compass dropout event on; score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi (settled = 12 s after a new target). **Lit control:** the same ids with no event; score from all ticks. **Seeds (fresh; the gate and the smoke run used ids 0-3):** ids **2000-2199 (200 ids)**, read once, dark and lit.
**Readings (the series' conventions):** pooled paired differences, BETTER iff mean > 2 se and positive, WORSE iff mean < -2 se, TIES otherwise. **DARK-SPECIFIC** for a network n: (n minus autopilot in the dark) minus (n minus autopilot lit), paired per id, BETTER iff above 2 se.

## 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no arm has a score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ on at least one id (the event reaches the flight); the networks are pairwise different on at least one id (ten distinct arms).

## 4. The verdicts and bottom lines
1. `<n> OF 8 NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK (<names>)`, with `; <names> ARE BELOW IT` appended when any is WORSE.
2. `<n> OF 8 NETWORKS BEAT THEIR UNTRAINED START IN THE DARK (<names>)`, with `; <names> ARE BELOW IT` appended.
3. `THE UNTRAINED HAND WEIGHTS <BEAT | TIE | ARE BELOW> THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK`.
4. `THE GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC IN <n> OF 8 (<names>)`.
5. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, hand <h>, networks <total over all eight> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; every network's paired difference from the autopilot and from `hand`, dark and lit, with se and the better/worse/same counts; the spread of the eight gains.

## 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any seed is flown
At least 5 of 8 networks beat the autopilot in the dark: about 70 percent; all 8: about 25; none: about 10. At least 5 of 8 beat the hand weights: about 60; the hand weights TIE the autopilot (they are the weights the autopilot's own network form starts from): about 55, BEAT it: 15, BELOW: 30. The gain is dark-specific in at least one network: about 35 (the networks were trained to track targets, not to hold through a dropout). The best network by dark gain is RobustNoisy: about 35 (it was trained under sensor noise, which a dark window resembles). No lost sub in any arm: about 85.

## 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead.mjs` (the arms from a weights map, the paired readings, the dark-specific difference, the checks and the bottom lines; reads nothing from disk), `tools/ship/headToHeadCmd.mjs` (export-free: reads the weight files, flies, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head.json`) and `tools/ship/headToHead-selfcheck.mjs` with a mutation pass; then the run (one process, a few minutes) and the result as section 7 here. **NOT IN THIS ROUND:** the fish pilot, the stateful fly, the plain net, the leaky unit, the survey task, the lesion toggle; more than one boat hull; a task other than the compass dropout; any claim about wiring.

## Round H1, RESULT (2026-11-04): ALL EIGHT TRAINED NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT, IN THE LIT TANK AS WELL AS IN THE DARK; THE HAND WEIGHTS ARE THE AUTOPILOT; THREE NETWORKS' GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC, BY A SMALLER LIT GAIN, NOT A LARGER DARK ONE

200 fresh ids (2000-2199), 160 s, each of ten arms dark and lit, one process (3 min 50 s), read once. The five checks passed. `brain/rig-results/head-to-head.json` holds every run. The result was flown twice with the same numbers: the first print said "the hand weights ARE BELOW the autopilot" for a difference of 1e-10 (two arms that are the same pilot in two number formats, se 0, and a zero-spread list read by its sign); that was a bug in `brain/switchboard.mjs`'s reading, not in the data, and it is fixed (a mean within 1e-9 of 0 is a TIE) with a row and mutants; every other number was identical on the second flight.

**Dark means (autopilot 0.7504):** Wip 0.8365, Actuators 0.8587, Robust 0.8647, RobustLong 0.8513, RobustTail 0.8600, RobustTail2 0.8666, RobustWide 0.8655, RobustNoisy 0.8702. **Dark gain over the autopilot (se about 0.008, better/worse of 200):** Wip +0.0861 (159/41), Actuators +0.1083 (166/34), Robust +0.1142 (171/29), RobustLong +0.1009, RobustTail +0.1096, RobustTail2 +0.1162, RobustWide +0.1150, RobustNoisy **+0.1198 (177/23)**. **Lit gain:** +0.0866 (Wip), +0.1273, +0.1274, +0.1121, +0.1214, +0.0888, +0.0881, +0.0855. **Dark-specific** (dark gain minus lit gain): Wip -0.0005 TIES, Actuators -0.0190 WORSE, Robust -0.0131, RobustLong -0.0112, RobustTail -0.0119 (all TIES), **RobustTail2 +0.0274, RobustWide +0.0270, RobustNoisy +0.0342 BETTER**. Lost subs: none in any arm.
All five registered bottom lines: `8 OF 8 NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK`; `8 OF 8 NETWORKS BEAT THEIR UNTRAINED START IN THE DARK`; `THE UNTRAINED HAND WEIGHTS TIE THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK`; `THE GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC IN 3 OF 8 (RobustTail2, RobustWide, RobustNoisy)`; `LOST SUBS: autopilot 0, hand 0, networks 0 (0 in the worst)`.

**Against the expectations of section 5 (written first).** At least 5 of 8 beat the autopilot, about 70: held (and all 8, about 25, came out). At least 5 beat the hand weights, about 60: held, but see below. The hand weights TIE the autopilot, about 55: held, exactly. Dark-specific in at least one, about 35: held (three). The best network by dark gain is RobustNoisy, about 35: held (+0.1198, 0.004 above RobustTail2). No lost sub, about 85: held.

**WHAT THIS MEANS and does not.**
* **The hand weights ARE the autopilot.** Their dark and lit scores equal the autopilot's to better than 1e-9 on all 200 ids: the PD autopilot is the hand-wired network's form. So "beats its untrained start" and "beats the autopilot" are ONE finding, not two; the registered line 2 carries no extra evidence, and `network-hand` is, in this tree, the autopilot's own twin.
* **The training pays, and pays everywhere.** Every network gains +0.09 to +0.12 in the dark AND +0.09 to +0.13 in the lit tank, with 159 to 177 of 200 ids better. That is a heading-tracking gain over the PD autopilot (the autopilot is the weak baseline on this tank), not a gain in holding a heading through a dropout.
* **"Dark-specific" is a lit shortfall more than a dark gain.** RobustTail2, RobustWide and RobustNoisy (the three trained under sensor noise or over the wide hull set) gain +0.115 to +0.120 in the dark, the same as the others (+0.109 to +0.114), and gain only +0.086 to +0.089 in the lit tank where the others gain +0.112 to +0.127. What the noise training bought is robustness of the dark reading relative to the lit one, mostly by being worse lit. The one honest statement is that they lose less when the compass goes dark than the networks trained clean do; RobustNoisy leads the dark column by 0.004 over RobustTail2, inside noise.
* **Not shown, said plainly.** Nothing here is about the connectome: every network is the Giant Fiber Circuit stand-in topology, and the plain-net control (a net of the same size that is not wired like the circuit) is not in this tree, so "the wiring pays" is not tested; the notes on the other branch found a plain net keeps 100 to 116 percent of the wiring's gain on the fish tasks, and this round cannot say the same here. One hull, one task, one compass event, ids 2000-2199; a survey-task arm for these networks is not on the board. The fish pilot and the stateful fly are not flown here.
* **What it adds to the series:** the switchboard's first real reading is a clean positive on the flying layer (trained networks beat the PD autopilot by about 0.1 on this tank, every time) set against the mostly negative readings on the connectome questions. The next round with a point is the same table with the plain net as one more arm, which needs `brain/pilotSubPlainNet.mjs` from the other branch.

# Round H3 -- the survey: the twelve trained needs circuits against the rule they started from, the tuned rule at the seat's constants and the twin seat (REGISTERED 2026-11-04, before any code and before any of its seeds was flown)

## 1. Why
The survey seat of this tree is a rule plus a search (the tuned rule at low -0.06 and the twin planner over it; Rounds 8-33). Piece 4 of `brain/NEEDS_AND_LIMITS.md` trained twelve STATEFUL circuit pilots for the same task from the weaker `GUARDED-OBS` start (`brain/rig-results/needsCircuitStageA.rerun-amendment3.jsonl`: the fly's heading loop `cx`, `nostate`, `withheld`, and nine shuffled controls `er`, `layer`, `degree`, three each) and never flew them against the tuned rule or the twin: their own audits read about 0.55-0.66 against 0.86 for `GUARDED-FILTER` there. The switchboard (`brain/switchboard.mjs`) can fly all of them on one task. This round does it, on seeds none of them has seen, and asks the two questions the series has left on this layer:

> (a) Does any trained circuit beat the rule it was trained from, the tuned rule at the seat's constants, or the twin? (b) Does the real wiring (`cx`, `nostate`, `withheld`) beat its shuffles?

It says nothing about the fish or the fly as pilots of a body (that is Round H1's layer), nothing about a circuit trained from a better start (they were all trained from `GUARDED-OBS`), and nothing about a larger search.

## 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (sixteen):** `rule-obs` (`T.GUARDED_OBS`, the start of every circuit); `rule-r8` (the tuned rule at the Round 8 constants); `rule-low06` (the same at low -0.06, the seat's constants); `twin-low06` (the survey seat, standard profile); and the twelve champions of the file above, rebuilt from their recorded weights by `tools/ship/trainNeedsCircuit.mjs`'s own `policyOf`: `circ-cx`, `circ-nostate`, `circ-withheld`, `circ-er0..2`, `circ-layer0..2`, `circ-degree0..2`. **Task:** the survey task of `brain/needsTask.mjs`, noisy gauge sd 0.10 (the condition the circuits were trained in), 600 s dives, ALL FOUR kinds (plain, deep, far, both), seeds **15001-15060 of each kind (240 dives an arm)**, read once, every arm on the same dives. **Orientation step:** the first-order step ("euler", `rigidBody6dofEuler`), the step every needs-and-limits number was measured under; the box3d line made RK4 the engine default on 2026-11-04 and this round says euler out loud so its numbers stay comparable with Rounds 8-33.
**Readings:** pooled paired differences over all 240 dives (BETTER iff mean > 2 se and positive, WORSE iff mean < -2 se, TIES otherwise; `brain/switchboard.mjs` `readingOf`). Per circuit: against `rule-obs`, against `rule-low06`, against `twin-low06`. **Family comparison (b):** the three real-wiring circuits' mean scores against the nine controls' (each circuit's own 240-dive mean), the difference over its Welch standard error; REAL ABOVE iff the difference is positive and above 2 se, else NOT ABOVE.

## 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 240 dives; the dives are the same in every arm in the same order; each circuit's rebuilt pilot reproduces its recorded start check (the identity: a pilot with its start weights flies `rule-obs` dive for dive on 4 baseline dives, the check the Piece 4 file records) -- here, the rebuilt pilot's weight count equals the record's; the four rules and the twin are pairwise different on at least one dive; the survey seat, rule-low06 and rule-r8 reproduce their Round 33 selection arms' scores on three dives each (the reproduction of the rule and seat numbers under euler).

## 4. The bottom lines
1. `<n> OF 12 CIRCUITS BEAT THEIR START (RULE-OBS) (<names>)`, with `; <names> ARE BELOW IT` appended.
2. `<n> OF 12 CIRCUITS BEAT THE TUNED RULE AT THE SEAT'S CONSTANTS (<names>)`, with `; <names> ARE BELOW IT` appended.
3. `THE BEST CIRCUIT (<name>, <mean>) <BEATS | TIES | IS BELOW> THE TWIN SEAT (<difference>)`.
4. `THE REAL WIRINGS AVERAGE <m1> AND THE SHUFFLED CONTROLS <m2>: <REAL ABOVE | NOT ABOVE> (difference <d>, 2 se <e>)`.
5. `HULL: crushed or stranded dives: rule-low06 <a>, twin <b>, circuits <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's mean and its bad count; the per-kind means of the four rules, the twin and the best circuit; every circuit's three paired differences with se and counts.

## 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any seed is flown
The circuits were trained from `GUARDED-OBS` and read 0.55-0.66 on their own audits; the tuned rule scores about 0.80 on the hard kinds. At least 10 of 12 circuits BEAT `rule-obs`: about 55 percent (their own audits read them above it, only some by a margin); all 12: 15; fewer than 6: 15. At least one circuit BEATS `rule-low06`: about 4 percent; at least 6 are BELOW it: about 95. The best circuit is BELOW the twin: about 97. The best circuit by mean is a real wiring (`cx`, `nostate`, `withheld`): about 30 (a quarter of the 12 are real). The real wirings are NOT ABOVE the shuffles: about 85. A circuit crushes more dives than `rule-low06`: about 35 (their earlier readings were not hull-clean). The twin BEATS `rule-low06`: about 25 (a gain of +0.02 on 240 dives of all four kinds is at the edge of what 240 dives can see).

## 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/needsHead.mjs` (the arms from the recorded champions, the readings, the family comparison, the checks and the lines; reads the circuit and the record through handed-in objects), `tools/ship/needsHeadCmd.mjs` (export-free: reads `vendor/hemibrain/hemibrain-cx-heading-loop.json` and the record, flies, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head-needs.json`) and `tools/ship/needsHead-selfcheck.mjs` with a mutation pass; then the run (the twin is the long part: about 11 minutes) and the result as section 7. **NOT IN THIS ROUND:** retraining a circuit; a circuit started from the tuned rule; the fish or fly as body pilots; changing a constant.

## Round H3, RESULT (2026-11-04): NO TRAINED NEEDS CIRCUIT BEATS ITS START, OR COMES WITHIN 0.28 OF THE RULE -- every circuit TIES the rule it was trained from; the twin beats the rule it sits on by +0.0135; the real wirings are not above their shuffles

240 dives an arm (seeds 15001-15060 of plain, deep, far, both), sixteen arms, one process (about 15 minutes), noisy gauge sd 0.10, first-order orientation step, read once. The five checks passed, REPRODUCTION included: the rules and the seat reproduce Round 33's scores on the same dives to 1e-12. `brain/rig-results/head-to-head-needs.json` holds every run.

**Means (bad = crushed or stranded dives of 240):** rule-obs 0.6067 (29), rule-r8 0.8875 (0), rule-low06 0.8978 (0), **twin-low06 0.9112 (0)**; circuits cx 0.6079 (25), nostate 0.6021 (32), withheld 0.5977 (29), er0 0.6058 (33), er1 0.6154 (34), er2 0.6183 (24), layer0 0.6075 (36), layer1 **0.6190** (25), layer2 0.5838 (35), degree0 0.5946 (22), degree1 0.5855 (35), degree2 0.6121 (25). By kind, rule-obs scores plain 0.983, deep 0.877, far 0.345, both 0.222; rule-low06 0.977, 0.972, 0.838, 0.804.
**Against the start (rule-obs):** every one of the 12 TIES, differences -0.023 to +0.012 (se about 0.012). **Against the rule at the seat's constants:** every one BELOW, -0.28 to -0.31 (se 0.02; at most 6 of 240 dives better). **Against the twin:** every one BELOW, -0.29 to -0.33. **Twin minus rule-low06:** +0.0135 (se 0.0046; better 16, worse 2, same 222), BETTER.
All five registered bottom lines: `0 OF 12 CIRCUITS BEAT THEIR START (RULE-OBS) (none)`; `0 OF 12 CIRCUITS BEAT THE TUNED RULE AT THE SEAT'S CONSTANTS (none); [all twelve] ARE BELOW IT`; `THE BEST CIRCUIT (layer:1, 0.6190) IS BELOW THE TWIN SEAT (-0.2923)`; `THE REAL WIRINGS AVERAGE 0.6025 AND THE SHUFFLED CONTROLS 0.6047: NOT ABOVE (difference -0.0021, 2 se 0.0108)`; `HULL: crushed or stranded dives: rule-low06 0, twin 0, circuits 355 (36 in the worst)`.

**Against the expectations of section 5 (written first).** At least 10 of 12 circuits BEAT `rule-obs`, about 55 percent: **no, none did**; all 12, about 15, fewer than 6, about 15: the third came out (zero). At least one BEATS `rule-low06`, about 4: no. At least 6 BELOW it, about 95: held (all 12). The best circuit BELOW the twin, about 97: held. The best circuit by mean a real wiring, about 30: no (layer:1, a shuffled control, 0.6190; the best real wiring is cx at 0.6079). The real wirings NOT ABOVE the shuffles, about 85: held. A circuit crushes more dives than `rule-low06`, about 35: held, and far beyond (355 across the twelve against 0). The twin BEATS `rule-low06`, about 25: it came out.
**WHAT THIS MEANS and does not.**
* **The trained circuits are their starting rule.** All twelve tie `rule-obs` on fresh seeds (and crush a similar 22 to 36 of 240 dives as it does, 29), though each had been picked above it on its own audits. The champion selection on those audits was optimistic; on fresh dives the training moved nothing measurable. On the hard kinds `rule-obs` scores 0.34 (far) and 0.22 (both): it is a poor start, and 240 dives of a 2,452-weight reservoir searched 4 x 300 children did not leave it.
* **The rule and the search are the whole result on this layer.** The tuned rule at the seat's constants scores 0.8978 with no lost dive; the twin 0.9112. The gap to any circuit is 0.28-0.33, a hundred times the circuits' own spread. The series' answer to "a smarter sub" stands: structure (a rule, constants searched, a plan over a model of the boat) paid; a learned residual or a connectome-shaped reservoir on top of a weak rule did not.
* **The wiring question.** The real heading-loop wirings do not score above the shuffles (-0.0021, inside 2 se of 0.011), as the fish rung 3 and the plain-net control found on their layer. Three families of three controls and three real circuits cannot see a small effect; this says there is no large one on this task.
* **The twin's gain on all four kinds, +0.0135 over the rule at the same constants, is smaller than the +0.0198 of Round 22 (hard kinds only)**, as it should be: on plain and deep it has little to add. It is the one gain this round measured, and it is REAL by the series' reading.
* **Not shown.** Circuits trained from a better start (the tuned rule), a bigger search, a circuit given the planner's view of the stations, or the same question at gauge noise 0.20 or 0.30; and nothing about the circuits as body pilots (Round H1's layer). Round H3 flew the champions as recorded: it did not retrain any.

# Round H2 -- the 963-weight networks, the plain nets and the leaky unit on the tank's compass dropout, and what is dark-specific (REGISTERED 2026-11-06, before any code and before any of its seeds was flown)

## 1. Why
H1 asked what the eight 963-weight networks do against the autopilot. The merge brought in the arms H1 said it was waiting for: the leaky unit (`brain/pilotLeakyHeading.mjs`, one leaky integrator behind the autopilot's heading rate) and the rung 4b plain nets (`render/subAquariumPlainNet.mjs`, five of the 48 committed champions in `brain/rig-results/plainNetStudy.jsonl`). The other chat flew them once, unregistered, on 60 ids (`brain/BACKLOG_PICKUPS_2026-11-05.md`): the leaky unit TIES the autopilot, the plain nets move the score (one by 0.22 rad), and the settled dark error "cannot tell a heading memory from none". This round reads the same arms on fresh ids, registered, and asks:

> On the tank's compass dropout, does any 963-weight network beat the plain nets and the leaky unit, and is any arm's gain specific to the dark (the same arm's gain over the autopilot in the lit tank subtracted)?

**What this round can NOT say, and says up front.** (1) The plain nets are NOT weight-matched controls to the 963-weight networks: they are the rung 4b nets (589 to 1,348 weights, four tilt signals, trained on the 4b disturbances, no state), not a dense net on the tank's 14 features at about 963 weights, which is not built and would need a training round of its own. A network beating them says it beats the simple nets that exist, not that its structure pays. (2) The score is the board's settled dark heading error; the other chat's note shows it is dominated by how fast a pilot turns onto a target, so a result here is NOT a test of a heading memory. The dark-specific difference removes what a pilot gains everywhere (the lit tank is the control for that); a memory test proper needs a score on the heading the pilot believes, which no arm reports. (3) The navigator (the tank's own `navigator` event, a different event and not a pilot) is not an arm.

## 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (seventeen), flying layer, the board's `dropout` task:** `autopilot`; `network-hand` and the eight trained networks of H1 (Wip, Actuators, Robust, RobustLong, RobustTail, RobustTail2, RobustWide, RobustNoisy); `leaky-unit` and `leaky-unit-off` (the same pilot with its gain zeroed: it must equal the autopilot to the last id, a sanity arm and not a finding); five plain nets by spec `P/B/mn:3`, `P/whole/mn:0`, `E/whole/mn:1`, `P/B/mg:0`, `E/E/mn:0` (the five the other chat flew).
**Task and ids:** the dropout: one-sub tanks, 160 s, the same spawn for every arm, score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi (settled = 12 s after a new target); **lit control:** the same ids with no event (the same settled score over all ticks). **Ids 3000-3199 (200), read once, dark and lit.** (H1 read 2000-2199; the unregistered looks used 1-12 and 9001-9060.)
**Readings (the series' conventions):** pooled paired differences a - b on the score, BETTER iff the mean is positive and above 2 se, WORSE iff negative and below -2 se, otherwise TIES. **DARK-SPECIFIC** for an arm x against the autopilot: (x minus autopilot in the dark) minus (x minus autopilot lit), paired per id, BETTER iff above 2 se.
**Pairs read:** each of the eight networks against each of the five plain nets in the dark (40 pairs); each plain net and the leaky unit against the autopilot dark, lit and dark-specific; each plain net against the leaky unit in the dark; each network against the autopilot dark and dark-specific (H1's reading on fresh ids).

## 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm and condition; no score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ on at least one id (the event reaches the flight); `leaky-unit-off` equals `autopilot` on every id in both conditions (the lesion is the pilot it sits on); the sixteen arms other than `leaky-unit-off` are pairwise different in the dark.

## 4. The bottom lines
1. `NETWORKS AGAINST PLAIN NETS IN THE DARK: <b> OF 40 PAIRS READ BETTER, <t> TIE, <w> WORSE; <names> BEAT ALL FIVE PLAIN NETS` (`none` when none).
2. `PLAIN NETS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK: <n> OF 5 (<names>); IN THE LIT TANK: <n> OF 5 (<names>); THE GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC IN <n> OF 5 (<names>)`.
3. `THE LEAKY UNIT <BEATS | TIES | IS BELOW> THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK AND ITS GAIN <IS | IS NOT> DARK-SPECIFIC`.
4. `<n> OF 5 PLAIN NETS BEAT THE LEAKY UNIT IN THE DARK (<names>)`.
5. `<n> OF 8 NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK; THE GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC IN <m> (<names>)`.
6. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, leaky unit <l>, plain nets <total> (<max> in the worst), networks <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; each arm's paired differences with se and the better/worse/same counts; the spread of the five plain nets' dark gains and of the eight networks'.

## 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any seed is flown
All eight networks beat the autopilot in the dark (as H1): about 70 percent; at least five: 90. `E/E/mn:0` beats the autopilot in the dark: about 90 percent (0.22 rad on 60 ids); `P/B/mg:0` beats it: about 60; at least three of the five plain nets beat it: about 45. The plain nets' gain is dark-specific in none of the five: about 60 percent (a residual that speeds the turn helps lit and dark alike). The leaky unit TIES the autopilot in the dark: about 80 percent; its gain is dark-specific: about 15. At least one network beats `E/E/mn:0` in the dark: about 55 percent; at least one network beats all five plain nets: about 40; no network is WORSE than any plain net: about 35. The networks' gain is dark-specific in at least one: about 35 (H1 found three of eight on other ids). No lost sub in any arm: about 85.

## 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead2.mjs` (the board for this round from the weights and the plain-net records handed in, the readings, the checks and the bottom lines; reads nothing from disk), `tools/ship/headToHead2Cmd.mjs` (export-free: reads the weight files and `brain/rig-results/plainNetStudy.jsonl`, flies, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head2.json`) and `tools/ship/headToHead2-selfcheck.mjs` with a mutation pass; then the run (one process) and the result as a section here. **NOT IN THIS ROUND:** a memory test proper; the navigator; the fish pilot; a 963-weight plain net on the 14 tank features; the survey task; the lesion toggle; any claim about wiring.

## Round H2, RESULT (2026-11-06): EVERY NETWORK BEATS EVERY PLAIN NET (40 OF 40 PAIRS), BUT THE PLAIN NETS ALSO ALL BEAT THE AUTOPILOT -- IN THE LIT TANK AS MUCH AS IN THE DARK; THE LEAKY UNIT BEATS THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY A LITTLE; THE SAME THREE NETWORKS AS H1 ARE DARK-SPECIFIC

200 fresh ids (3000-3199), 160 s, each of seventeen arms dark and lit, one process, read once. The six checks passed. `brain/rig-results/head-to-head2.json` holds every run. The six registered bottom lines:
* `NETWORKS AGAINST PLAIN NETS IN THE DARK: 40 OF 40 PAIRS READ BETTER, 0 TIE, 0 WORSE; all eight BEAT ALL FIVE PLAIN NETS`
* `PLAIN NETS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK: 5 OF 5; IN THE LIT TANK: 5 OF 5; THE GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC IN 0 OF 5 (none)`
* `THE LEAKY UNIT BEATS THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK AND ITS GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC`
* `1 OF 5 PLAIN NETS BEAT THE LEAKY UNIT IN THE DARK (E/E/mn:0)`
* `8 OF 8 NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK; THE GAIN IS DARK-SPECIFIC IN 3 (RobustTail2, RobustWide, RobustNoisy)`
* `LOST SUBS: autopilot 0, leaky unit 0, plain nets 0, networks 0`

**Dark means (autopilot 0.7646):** networks 0.8416 (Wip) to 0.8703 (Robust); leaky unit 0.7761; plain nets 0.7777 (P/B/mg:0) to 0.8223 (E/E/mn:0). **Dark gain over the autopilot (se in brackets):** networks +0.0771 (0.0069, Wip) to +0.1057 (0.0077, Robust); plain nets +0.0132 (0.0028, P/B/mg:0), +0.0180, +0.0184, +0.0248, **+0.0578 (0.0077, E/E/mn:0)**; leaky unit **+0.0116 (0.0041, 2.8 se; 121 of 200 ids better)**. **Lit gain:** networks +0.0803 to +0.1245; plain nets +0.0118 to +0.0823; the leaky unit exactly 0.0000 (its lit flights equal the autopilot's to the last id: it acts only when the compass is dark). **Networks against the best plain net (E/E/mn:0), dark:** +0.0193 (Wip) to +0.0480 (Robust), all BETTER; against the other four plain nets +0.052 to +0.093.

**Against the expectations of section 5 (written first).** All eight networks beat the autopilot in the dark, about 70: held (as H1). `E/E/mn:0` beats the autopilot, about 90: held. `P/B/mg:0` beats it, about 60: held. At least three of five plain nets beat it, about 45: held (all five). The plain nets' gain dark-specific in none, about 60: held. **The leaky unit TIES the autopilot in the dark, about 80: MISSED** -- it BEATS it, at 2.8 se on 200 ids (the 60-id look, -0.014 rad against a standard error of 0.021 rad, could not see an effect this size: +0.0116 on the score is 0.036 rad). Its gain dark-specific, about 15: it IS, but see below. At least one network beats `E/E/mn:0`, about 55: held (all eight). At least one beats all five plain nets, about 40: held (all eight). No network WORSE than any plain net, about 35: held. The networks' gain dark-specific in at least one, about 35: held, and it is the same three as H1 (RobustTail2 +0.0200, RobustWide +0.0215, RobustNoisy +0.0186, each BETTER at about 3 se). No lost sub, about 85: held.

**WHAT THIS MEANS and does not.**
* **40 of 40 is not "the structure pays".** The plain nets are not matched controls: they are the rung 4b nets (85 to 1,348 weights, four tilt signals, trained on the 4b disturbances and not on anything the tank does, no state), while the 963-weight networks were trained on this tank's tracking tasks. A network beating them says a network trained for the task beats nets trained for something else. The control the question needs -- a dense net on the tank's 14 features at about 963 weights, trained the same way -- is still not built (it is a training round).
* **The surprise is the plain nets.** Every one of the five, trained for other disturbances, beats the autopilot in the dark AND in the lit tank, by +0.013 to +0.058 dark and +0.012 to +0.082 lit. The autopilot is a weak baseline on this score, and a residual that turns the sub onto its target faster helps whatever the compass does: this is the generic gain the rung 4a close-out and the other chat's note (`brain/BACKLOG_PICKUPS_2026-11-05.md`) describe, now registered at 200 ids. Four of the five gain MORE lit than dark (only P/B/mg:0 gains a hair more dark, +0.0132 against +0.0118); the two reading WORSE on the dark-specific line are E/whole/mn:1 (-0.0245) and E/E/mn:0 (-0.0246).
* **The leaky unit's "dark-specific" is by construction.** Its lit flights are the autopilot's exactly, so its dark-specific difference IS its dark gain, +0.0116: the line says it has a gain only the dark can show, not that it holds a heading better than a plain net would. Against the nets: only E/E/mn:0 beats it (+0.0462); the other four plain nets tie it (+0.0016 to +0.0133, se 0.0044 to 0.0078). So the cheapest memory moves the dark score by about a fifth of what the best plain net moves it, and the best plain net moves it by turning faster, not by remembering. The unregistered look's reading (the score "cannot tell a heading memory from none") stands in kind: the leaky unit's effect is small, and a score on the heading the pilot believes is still what a memory test needs.
* **The networks' dark-specific gain is again a smaller lit gain.** RobustTail2, RobustWide and RobustNoisy gain +0.1025, +0.1040 and +0.0989 in the dark (the same as the other five networks, +0.0771 to +0.1057) and only +0.0803 to +0.0826 lit, where the other five gain +0.0892 to +0.1245. Same finding as H1 on different ids: the dark-specific networks are the noise-trained ones, and what noise training bought is a smaller lit gain.
* **Not shown, said plainly.** Nothing here is about wiring or memory as such; the matched dense control is not built; one hull, one task, one compass event, ids 3000-3199; the navigator, the fish pilot, the stateful fly and the survey task are not flown; and the plain nets were never trained for this task, so their rank among themselves says what their disturbance training transfers, not what the architecture can do here.

# Round H4 -- the heading each pilot BELIEVES through the compass dropout: the cheapest memory, the dead-reckoning navigator, the fly heading loop and a random wiring, against holding the last heading (REGISTERED 2026-11-06, before any code and before any of its seeds was flown)

## 1. Why
H2 left the memory question where the other chat's note put it: the settled dark heading error "cannot tell a heading memory from none", because it is mostly how fast a pilot turns onto a target, and the plain nets (which have no memory) gained more than the leaky unit. A memory test has to read the thing a memory is for: the heading the pilot believes while the compass is dark, against the true heading. The tank already reports it (`snapshot().compass.error`, belief less truth, wrapped, on every tick of its four compass events), and the rung 4a estimators sit behind the same events (`render/subDropoutScene.mjs` `pilotDefs`). The question, registered:

> Through the tank's compass dropout, does the heading a pilot believes stay closer to the truth than the held heading does, which memory is best (the cheapest, the dead-reckoning navigator, the fly heading loop, a random wiring of the same size), and does a better belief fly better?

## 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (six), each its own one-sub tank per id, the PD autopilot behind the thing named:** `held` (the tank's `dropout` event: the autopilot is shown the last valid heading, no memory); `leaky-unit-off` (the leaky unit with its gain zeroed, registered as an estimator: it must equal `held` to the last id, a sanity arm and not a finding); `leaky-unit` (`brain/pilotLeakyHeading.mjs` at its registered weights, sigma 0.9999, gain 1, as an estimator: one leaky integrator fed the exact heading rate); `navigator` (the tank's `navigator` event: the oracle dead-reckoning navigator, a ceiling with a perfect gyro); `fly` (the vendored heading loop as a reservoir with the study's fitted linear readout, `pilotDefs` id `cx`, as the `fly-estimator` event); `er` (the best random wiring of the same size, the study's control, `pilotDefs` id `er`, as the `er-estimator` event).
**Measures, from the same flights:** BELIEF = 1 - (rms over the dark ticks of `compass.error`)/pi (rad; higher is better; 1 is a perfect belief); FLIGHT = the board's dropout score, 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error to the wandering target)/pi (settled = 12 s after a new target). **Ids 4000-4199 (200), 160 s, one sub per tank, the same spawn for every arm, read once.** (H1 read 2000-2199, H2 3000-3199.)
**Readings (the series' conventions):** pooled paired differences a - b, BETTER iff the mean is positive and above 2 se, WORSE iff negative and below -2 se, otherwise TIES. **Pairs read, on BELIEF and on FLIGHT:** each of the five arms other than the sanity arm against `held`; `navigator` against `leaky-unit`; `fly` and `er` each against `leaky-unit`; `fly` against `er`.

## 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids; the ids are the same in every arm; every belief and flight score is inside (0, 1); every run had dark ticks (a window fell in it); `leaky-unit-off` equals `held` on every id on BELIEF and on FLIGHT (AMENDED 2026-11-06, before any registered id was flown: "equals" is within 1e-9, because the lesion's belief is read off an attitude recomputed through a rotation by zero and differs from `held` in the sixteenth place on some ids -- 1.1e-16 on id 11, 0 on ids 12 and 13, flight identical, probe ids outside 4000-4199); `held` and `navigator` differ on at least one id (the event reaches the flight); the five arms other than the sanity arm are pairwise different on belief.

## 4. The bottom lines
1. `BELIEF AGAINST THE HELD HEADING: <names> BEAT IT; <names> TIE; <names> ARE BELOW IT` (a clause is dropped when empty; `none` when all three are).
2. `THE NAVIGATOR <BEATS | TIES | IS BELOW> THE LEAKY UNIT IN BELIEF`.
3. `THE FLY HEADING LOOP <BEATS | TIES | IS BELOW> THE LEAKY UNIT IN BELIEF; THE RANDOM WIRING <BEATS | TIES | IS BELOW> IT`.
4. `THE FLY HEADING LOOP <BEATS | TIES | IS BELOW> THE RANDOM WIRING IN BELIEF`.
5. `FLIGHT AGAINST THE HELD HEADING: <names> BEAT IT; <names> TIE; <names> ARE BELOW IT`.
6. `BELIEF BEATS THE HELD HEADING BUT FLIGHT DOES NOT: <names>` (`none` when none).
7. `LOST SUBS: held <h>, the other arms <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's mean belief rms in radians and its mean flight score; each arm's paired differences with se and the better/worse/same counts.

## 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any seed is flown
`leaky-unit-off` equals `held` exactly: about 90 percent (the check refuses the round if not). The leaky unit's belief beats the held heading: about 97 percent (its estimate was 0.03 to 0.04 rad rms against a held heading that drifts by whole turns). The navigator's belief beats the leaky unit: about 95. The fly heading loop's belief beats the held heading: about 65 (the study fitted its readout on 60 s study dives, not on the tank's wandering targets, so the estimator may be out of its distribution here); beats the leaky unit: about 10; the random wiring's belief beats the leaky unit: about 10. The fly loop TIES the random wiring on belief: about 65; beats it: about 20; is below it: about 15. On FLIGHT: the navigator TIES the held heading: about 70 (the other chat's 60-id look); the leaky unit beats it: about 65 (H2 found +0.0116 at 2.8 se); the fly loop beats it: about 40. At least one arm's belief beats the held heading while its flight does not: about 75. No lost sub in any arm: about 90.

## 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead4.mjs` (the task, the arms from the two builders handed in, the readings, the checks and the bottom lines; reads nothing from disk), `tools/ship/headToHead4Cmd.mjs` (export-free: reads the heading-loop circuit, builds the study's estimators with `pilotDefs`, flies, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head4.json`) and `tools/ship/headToHead4-selfcheck.mjs` with a mutation pass; then the run and the result as a section here. **WHAT THIS ROUND CAN NOT SAY, up front:** (1) the leaky unit and the navigator integrate the EXACT heading rate (a perfect gyro in the simulator), so this grades the integrator and not a realistic sensor; (2) the networks and the plain nets report no belief, so they are not arms here and H2's flight result stands as it is; (3) the fly and random-wiring estimators were fitted on the study's dives, and the tank's wandering targets are a different process, so their result is a result for this tank and not a replay of rung 4a; (4) one hull, one compass event pattern (the tank's 16 s windows every 40 s); (5) no claim about wiring beyond the fly-against-random pair, which rung 4a already found to tie on its own dives.

## Round H4, RESULT (2026-11-06): THE LEAKY UNIT HOLDS THE HEADING THROUGH THE DARK (0.065 rad against 0.564) AND THE NAVIGATOR HOLDS IT EXACTLY, BUT A PILOT THAT KNOWS ITS HEADING FLIES ONLY 0.015 BETTER; THE FLY HEADING LOOP BELIEVES THE HELD HEADING; A RANDOM WIRING BEATS IT

200 fresh ids (4000-4199), 160 s, each of six arms in its own one-sub tank, read once. The seven checks passed. `brain/rig-results/head-to-head4.json` holds every run. The seven registered bottom lines:
* `BELIEF AGAINST THE HELD HEADING: leaky-unit, navigator, er BEAT IT; fly TIE`
* `THE NAVIGATOR BEATS THE LEAKY UNIT IN BELIEF`
* `THE FLY HEADING LOOP IS BELOW THE LEAKY UNIT IN BELIEF; THE RANDOM WIRING IS BELOW IT`
* `THE FLY HEADING LOOP IS BELOW THE RANDOM WIRING IN BELIEF`
* `FLIGHT AGAINST THE HELD HEADING: leaky-unit, navigator BEAT IT; fly, er TIE`
* `BELIEF BEATS THE HELD HEADING BUT FLIGHT DOES NOT: er`
* `LOST SUBS: held 0, the other arms 0`

**Belief, the RMS of the believed heading's error over the dark ticks (rad):** held 0.5636; leaky unit 0.0654 (88 percent of the held heading's error gone); navigator 0.0000 (exact by construction: a perfect gyro); fly heading loop 0.5645; random wiring 0.3792. **Belief gain over the held heading on the score (se; ids better of 200):** leaky unit +0.1586 (0.0025; 200), navigator +0.1794 (0.0027; 200), fly -0.0003 (0.0003; 95) TIES, random wiring +0.0587 (0.0031; 196). **Flight (the settled dark heading error score):** held 0.7679, leaky unit 0.7821, navigator 0.7836, fly 0.7686, random wiring 0.7731; gain over the held heading +0.0142 (0.0038) BEATS, +0.0157 (0.0040) BEATS, +0.0006 (0.0006) TIES, +0.0052 (0.0028) TIES.

**Against the expectations of section 5 (written first).** The lesion equals the held heading, about 90: held (within 1e-9, as amended). The leaky unit's belief beats the held heading, about 97: held. The navigator beats the leaky unit on belief, about 95: held (+0.0208). **The fly loop's belief beats the held heading, about 65: MISSED -- it TIES (-0.0003).** The fly loop beats the leaky unit, about 10, and the random wiring beats it, about 10: neither did. **The fly loop TIES the random wiring, about 65: MISSED -- it is BELOW it by 0.0590 (197 of 200 ids).** On flight, **the navigator TIES the held heading, about 70: MISSED -- it BEATS it, +0.0157 at 3.9 se** (the other chat's 60-id look, TIES, could not see an effect this size). The leaky unit beats it on flight, about 65: held (+0.0142 here, +0.0116 in H2 on other ids). The fly loop beats it on flight, about 40: no. At least one arm's belief beats the held heading while its flight does not, about 75: held (the random wiring). No lost sub, about 90: held.

**WHAT THIS MEANS and does not.**
* **The two integrators work, and one of my expectations misremembered rung 4a.** The study's own numbers were in the tree the whole time: the fly heading loop explains -1.2 to -0.5 percent of the drift on its audit sets (`render/subDropoutData.mjs`, `STUDY.cx.skill`) and the best random wiring 25 to 35 percent (`STUDY.er.skill`). In the tank, with the wandering targets of a different process, the fly loop's belief error is 0.5645 against the held heading's 0.5636 (skill -0.002) and the random wiring's is 0.3792 (skill 0.327). So H4 REPLICATES rung 4a's estimator result in the tank, and the distribution shift I named as a caveat did not show: the fly loop does not integrate a heading, a random wiring of the same size does, partly, and the leaky unit does almost completely. This says nothing in favour of the fly circuit's wiring (a random one beats it) and is the same finding the notes reached on the fish and fly rungs.
* **A pilot that knows its heading flies only a little better.** The belief error falls from 0.56 to 0.07 rad (leaky unit) or to nothing (navigator), and the settled dark flight score rises by 0.014 to 0.016: 0.045 to 0.050 rad of heading error. The navigator is no better than the leaky unit on flight (+0.0015, TIES) though its belief is exact. This is the other chat's explanation of the dropout score, now measured: a sub in this tank turns at about 0.06 rad/s, and the error is mostly how fast it turns onto a new target, not what it knows of its heading. A compass loss costs this tank 0.015 on the score at most, so a heading memory is worth at most that, and H2's plain nets (+0.013 to +0.058, lit and dark alike) gain as much or more by turning faster.
* **The random wiring's flight gain is in proportion, and its TIE is a threshold.** It explains 33 percent of the drift (the leaky unit 88 percent) and gains +0.0052 on flight, 37 percent of the leaky unit's +0.0142; it reads TIES only because +0.0052 is 1.9 se, just under the 2 se line. So line 6, `BELIEF BEATS THE HELD HEADING BUT FLIGHT DOES NOT: er`, is where a small effect fell either side of the registered threshold, not a gap between belief and flight.
* **Not shown, said plainly.** Every integrator that works here is fed the EXACT heading rate (a perfect gyro in the simulator), so this grades the integrator and not a sensor: a noisy rate would make the leaky unit's 0.065 rad grow with the length of a window, and no arm here has been given one. The random wiring is the best of ten wirings picked on the study's dives, so its 0.327 is an upper-end draw and not a typical random wiring. One hull, the tank's 16 s windows every 40 s. The stateful fly as a pilot with its own residual (`render/subAquariumFlyCx.mjs`) and the networks and plain nets report no belief and are not arms.

# Round H5 -- the connectome-wired network against ten random wirings and ten dense nets of its size, trained by the same recipe, on the tank's compass dropout (REGISTERED 2026-11-06, before any code, before any control was trained and before any of its seeds was flown)

## 1. Why
H2 could say a 963-weight network beats the rung 4b plain nets, and could not say the wiring matters, because those nets were not controls for it (other inputs, other training, no recurrence). The control the question needs is a network of the SAME size, with the same features, outputs, hand-weight start and training, that differs only in how the recurrent core is wired. `brain/pilotSubPolicy.mjs` `createSubPolicy(topology)` takes any circuit as a parameter and `brain/pilotSubFish.mjs` `controlTopology(topo, seed)` draws a random one of the same size; `tools/ship/trainSubPilot.mjs` holds the recipe. The question, registered:

> Trained by the identical recipe, does the Giant Fiber Circuit's wiring beat random wirings of the same size and dense nets of the same weight count on the tank's compass dropout?

## 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (twenty-two: the autopilot, `network-Wip` and twenty controls; a first draft said twenty-three, corrected 2026-11-06 before any registered search finished), flying layer, the board's `dropout` task:** `autopilot`; `network-Wip` (the GFC-wired 963-weight champion in the tree, `brain/pilotSubWeightsWip.json`: the champion of four (1+1)-ES searches, seeds 6000-6003, 400 iterations, sigma 0.3, picked on `HELD_OUT_SET`, which IS the recipe below); `er-0` .. `er-9` (random circuits with the GFC's 34 neurons and 313 synapses drawn uniformly without self-loop or repeat, `controlTopology(GFC, 6300 + c)`, 963 weights); `dense-0` .. `dense-9` (23 neurons, every ordered pair but self-loops, 506 synapses, 947 weights, 1.7 percent under the GFC's 963).
**Training of every control, one recipe:** from the hand weights (the autopilot's laws written through the network), four independent (1+1)-ES searches (sparse mutation of 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, 400 iterations, `trainSubPilot.RECIPE`) on `TRAIN_SET`, each pruned back toward the hand weights; the champion of a control is the search with the best score on `HELD_OUT_SET` (no other selection, as for `Wip`). Search seeds: `6310 + 10 c + k` for `er-c`, `6510 + 10 c + k` for `dense-c`, k = 0..3. The audit sets of the trainer are not read; the flights below are the evaluation. A control whose four searches all fail to beat the hand weights on the held-out is still flown (it is the autopilot or close to it, and it counts as a control that did not learn).
**Task and ids:** the dropout: one-sub tanks, 160 s, the same spawn for every arm, score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi (settled = 12 s after a new target); **lit control:** the same ids with no event. **Ids 5000-5199 (200), read once, dark and lit.** (H1 read 2000-2199, H2 3000-3199, H4 4000-4199.)
**Readings (the series' conventions):** pooled paired differences a - b on the score, BETTER iff the mean is positive and above 2 se, WORSE iff negative and below -2 se, otherwise TIES. **Pairs read:** each control against the autopilot (dark and lit); `network-Wip` against each of the twenty controls (dark and lit); `network-Wip` against the autopilot (dark and lit).
**The verdict, registered before any control is trained:** the controls LEARNED iff at least 8 of the 10 `er` and at least 8 of the 10 `dense` beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02 on the score (the series' validity rule: a control that does not learn says nothing). If not, the round reads **NOT ASSESSED: THE CONTROLS DID NOT LEARN**. If they did: **THE WIRING IS SUPPORTED** iff `network-Wip` reads BETTER than at least 9 of the 10 `er` AND at least 9 of the 10 `dense` in the dark; **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

## 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm and condition; no score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ on at least one id (the event reaches the flight); every control is a champion of exactly four searches with the weights count of its circuit (963 for `er`, 947 for `dense`); the twenty-one trained arms are pairwise different in the dark; the twenty controls' topologies are pairwise different (no two random wirings are the same circuit).

## 4. The bottom lines
1. `THE CONTROLS <LEARNED | DID NOT LEARN>: <a> OF 10 RANDOM WIRINGS AND <b> OF 10 DENSE NETS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02`.
2. `THE CONNECTOME NETWORK BEATS <n> OF 10 RANDOM WIRINGS AND <m> OF 10 DENSE NETS IN THE DARK; TIES <t1> AND <t2>; IS BELOW <w1> AND <w2>` (random wirings first, dense nets second).
3. `THE CONNECTOME NETWORK RANKS <r> OF 21 BY DARK MEAN`.
4. `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: CONNECTOME <g>; RANDOM WIRINGS <mean> (sd <sd>, range <lo> to <hi>); DENSE NETS <mean> (sd <sd>, range <lo> to <hi>)`.
5. `VERDICT: <THE WIRING IS SUPPORTED | THE WIRING IS NOT SUPPORTED | NOT ASSESSED: THE CONTROLS DID NOT LEARN>`.
6. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, connectome <c>, random wirings <total> (<max> in the worst), dense nets <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; every control's four search scores and held-out score from the training; the lit-tank version of lines 2 to 4.

## 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any control is trained or any seed is flown
The controls learn (at least 8 of 10 in each family beat the autopilot by more than 0.02): about 85 percent. **THE WIRING IS SUPPORTED: about 10 percent** (the fish rung 3 found random wirings above the real one, and the plain-net control kept 100 to 116 percent of the wiring's gain). `network-Wip` is BELOW at least one random wiring at 2 se: about 65; below at least one dense net: about 70. `network-Wip` ranks in the top seven of the 21 trained networks by dark mean: about 35. The random wirings' mean dark gain is within 0.01 of `network-Wip`'s: about 55; the dense nets' mean is at or above it: about 55. No lost sub in any arm: about 70 (the controls were trained to finish dives, but the tank's wandering is not their training task).

## 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead5.mjs` (the controls' topologies and policies, a search and a champion by the recipe, the tank kinds, the board, the readings, the checks, the verdict and the bottom lines; reads nothing from disk), `tools/ship/headToHead5Cmd.mjs` (export-free: `train` runs the 80 searches (40 random-wiring and 40 dense-net: twenty controls of four searches each) one process each with a resumable results file (a counting slip in this paragraph's first draft said "80 + 80"; corrected 2026-11-06 before any registered search finished), `fly` reads that file and the Wip weights, flies, prints and writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head5.json`) and `tools/ship/headToHead5-selfcheck.mjs` with a mutation pass; then the training (about two hours on four cores), the flight and the result as a section here. **NOT IN THIS ROUND:** the seven other 963-weight networks (trained by other recipes on other dive sets: each would need its own control); the survey task; the fish pilot; a control for the stateful fly; any claim about a real connectome beyond the Giant Fiber Circuit's stand-in use here. **WHAT THIS ROUND CAN NOT SAY, up front:** the Giant Fiber Circuit is an escape reflex used here as a stand-in recurrent core (`brain/pilotSubPolicy.mjs` says so first), so a result is about this wiring in this pipeline, not about what a fly's heading circuit can do; the network under test is ONE trained champion (`Wip`) against twenty, so its rank carries the luck of its own four searches; and the training dives are the base recipe's, not the tank's wandering.

## Round H5, RESULT (2026-11-06): THE WIRING IS NOT SUPPORTED -- THE CONNECTOME NETWORK IS MID-PACK AMONG 21 NETWORKS OF ONE RECIPE (RANK 12), AND THE SPREAD BETWEEN IDENTICALLY TRAINED CONTROLS (SD 0.021) IS ABOUT THREE TIMES ITS DISTANCE FROM THEIR MEAN

200 fresh ids (5000-5199), 160 s, each of twenty-two arms dark and lit, read once. The seven checks passed. The 80 training searches (20 controls x 4) were run on the rig with 12 workers (the first four, `er:0`, on the sandbox before it restarted); the recipe is deterministic for (spec, k), so where they ran does not matter. `brain/rig-results/head-to-head5-training.jsonl` holds every search and `brain/rig-results/head-to-head5.json` every flight. The six registered bottom lines:
* `THE CONTROLS LEARNED: 9 OF 10 RANDOM WIRINGS AND 10 OF 10 DENSE NETS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02`
* `THE CONNECTOME NETWORK BEATS 5 OF 10 RANDOM WIRINGS AND 2 OF 10 DENSE NETS IN THE DARK; TIES 4 AND 2; IS BELOW 1 AND 6`
* `THE CONNECTOME NETWORK RANKS 12 OF 21 BY DARK MEAN`
* `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: CONNECTOME +0.0662; RANDOM WIRINGS +0.0572 (sd 0.0212, range +0.0132 to +0.0917); DENSE NETS +0.0690 (sd 0.0227, range +0.0205 to +0.0967)`
* `VERDICT: THE WIRING IS NOT SUPPORTED`
* `LOST SUBS: autopilot 0, connectome 0, random wirings 0, dense nets 0`

**Dark means (autopilot 0.7800):** connectome network 0.8462; random wirings 0.7933 (er-0) to 0.8717 (er-2); dense nets 0.8005 (dense-2) to 0.8768 (dense-6). Eleven controls score above the connectome network (four random wirings, seven dense nets) and nine below. **In the lit tank** the connectome network beats 5 of the 10 random wirings and 3 of the 10 dense nets, ties 3 and 0, and is below 2 and 7.

**Against the expectations of section 5 (written first).** The controls learn, about 85: held (9 of 10 random wirings: `er-0` gains +0.0133, under the 0.02 margin; 10 of 10 dense). The wiring is supported, about 10: not (as expected). `network-Wip` below at least one random wiring at 2 se, about 65: held (one). Below at least one dense net, about 70: held (six). `network-Wip` in the top seven of 21, about 35: MISSED (rank 12). The random wirings' mean gain within 0.01 of the connectome's, about 55: held (0.0572 against 0.0662, 0.0090 apart). The dense nets' mean at or above it, about 55: held (0.0690). No lost sub, about 70: held.

**WHAT THIS MEANS and does not.**
* **Under one recipe, the wiring is worth nothing measurable.** The connectome network beats half the random wirings and two of ten dense nets, and sits at rank 12 of 21, the middle. On the tank's dropout it is neither better nor worse than networks with the same inputs, outputs, start and training whose recurrent core is random or dense. This is the finding the fish rung 3 and the plain-net control reached on their layers, now for the Giant Fiber Circuit stand-in and on the tank's own task.
* **The spread between identically trained networks is the bigger fact.** Twenty controls trained by one recipe differ in dark gain from +0.013 to +0.097 (sd 0.021 to 0.023); the connectome network's +0.0662 is 0.4 sd above the random wirings' mean and 0.1 sd under the dense nets'. A single trained network in this tree says what that training run found, not what its circuit can do: in H2 the eight 963-weight networks gained +0.0771 to +0.1057: three of them (`Wip` +0.0771, RobustLong +0.0872, Actuators +0.0960) lie inside the range these twenty controls span, and five (+0.0989 to +0.1057) lie above the best control (+0.0967); those five were trained by other recipes, so this round cannot say whether they owe the difference to the recipe or to the luck of their searches, and a rank among networks of one recipe would carry the luck of four searches.
* **The dense nets are not shown to be better.** Their mean beats the connectome network's by 0.0028, under the standard error of a ten-control mean (about 0.007); six of ten read BETTER than it pairwise because each pair is a paired comparison of two single networks, which is exactly the luck of the training run again.
* **On the recipe's own held-out dives the connectome network is last.** Its champion's held-out score is -0.3275 (recomputed today; its file says -0.3278; the hand weights score -0.4877), against -0.3251 to -0.2896 for the random wirings' champions and -0.3189 to -0.2818 for the dense nets', so it is below all twenty on the dives the recipe selects on, while mid-pack on the tank's dropout. Reported, not weighed, and one network.
* **Not shown, said plainly.** Only the base-recipe network (`Wip`) has matched controls; the seven other networks were trained by other recipes on other dive sets and have none. The Giant Fiber Circuit is an escape reflex used as a stand-in recurrent core, so a result here is about this wiring in this pipeline and not about what a fly's circuit can do. `Wip` is one champion of four searches against twenty. The flights are the tank's wandering dropout, not the training dives. No claim is made about a real connectome beyond this stand-in.

# Round H6 -- is the learned gain a tuning gap? Five tuned autopilots (eleven gains, the networks' own search budget) against the autopilot, the eight networks and the twenty H5 controls on the compass dropout (REGISTERED 2026-11-06, before any code, before any gain was tuned and before any of its seeds was flown)

## 1. Why
Every trained pilot in this series beats the PD autopilot, in the dark and in the lit tank alike: the eight 963-weight networks by +0.077 to +0.106 (H2), the twenty H5 controls (random wirings and dense nets trained by one recipe) by +0.013 to +0.097, and even the rung 4b plain nets, trained for other disturbances, by +0.013 to +0.058. H2 called this "turning onto the target faster", and H4 showed that knowing the heading is worth only +0.015. If that gain is simply the autopilot's gains being detuned for this tank, a search over the autopilot's own eleven gains, given the same search budget, should find most of it, and "learning" in H1, H2 and H5 meant fixing a tuning gap. The question, registered:

> Does a tuned autopilot (the same PD laws, eleven gains searched by the networks' own recipe) match the gain of the trained networks on the compass dropout?

## 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (thirty-four), flying layer, the board's `dropout` task:** `autopilot` (the default gains); `tuned-0` .. `tuned-4` (the autopilot with its gains replaced by a champion of a search, below); the eight trained 963-weight networks of H1 and H2 (`network-Wip` and the seven others); the twenty H5 controls (`er-0..9`, `dense-0..9`, their champions from `brain/rig-results/head-to-head5-training.jsonl`).
**The tuning, one recipe per replicate:** the eleven gains `kDepth, vzMax, maxPitch, kTheta, kQ, kBalD, kBalV, kHdg, kR, kBeta, kSpd` of `brain/autopilotSub.mjs` (the scheduling constants `vRef, vMin, schedMax, schedMin` stay fixed), searched from their defaults by four independent (1+1)-ES searches per replicate, 400 iterations, mutation of 1 to 4 randomly chosen gains by a log-normal factor `exp(0.3 z)` (z standard normal), each gain kept within [default/8, default x 8], scored on `TRAIN_SET` by the same dive score the networks were trained on (`brain/subTask.mjs` `evaluate`); a child that scores exactly its parent replaces it; the champion of a replicate is the search with the best score on `HELD_OUT_SET` (no pruning: there is nothing to prune toward). Search seeds `6700 + 10 r + k` for replicate r = 0..4, k = 0..3. The tuning is NOT shown the tank, the dropout or any flight below.
**Task and ids:** the dropout: one-sub tanks, 160 s, the same spawn for every arm, score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi (settled = 12 s after a new target); **lit control:** the same ids with no event. **Ids 6000-6199 (200), read once, dark and lit.** (H1 read 2000-2199, H2 3000-3199, H4 4000-4199, H5 5000-5199.)
**Readings (the series' conventions):** pooled paired differences a - b on the score, BETTER iff the mean is positive and above 2 se, WORSE iff negative and below -2 se, otherwise TIES. A pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot. **Pairs read:** each tuned autopilot against the autopilot (dark and lit) and against each of the 28 networks (the eight and the twenty, dark and lit).
**The verdict, registered before any gain is tuned.** Let m_T be the mean dark gain of the five tuned autopilots and N the 28 networks' dark gains. If fewer than 4 of the 5 tuned autopilots beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02 on the score, the round reads **NOT ASSESSED: TUNING FOUND NOTHING** (a search that finds no gain cannot say there is none to find). Otherwise: **THE LEARNED GAIN IS A TUNING GAP** iff m_T is at or above the median of N (the mean of its 14th and 15th smallest); **THE LEARNED GAIN IS NOT A TUNING GAP** iff m_T is below the 25th percentile of N (its 7th smallest); **PARTLY A TUNING GAP** between.

## 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ on at least one id (the event reaches the flight); every tuned champion is the champion of exactly four searches with eleven finite gains inside [default/8, default x 8]; the five tuned autopilots are pairwise different in the dark and each differs from the autopilot on at least one id; the thirty-three trained arms are pairwise different in the dark.

## 4. The bottom lines
1. `TUNING <FOUND | FOUND NOTHING>: <n> OF 5 TUNED AUTOPILOTS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02`.
2. `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: TUNED <mean> (sd <sd>, range <lo> to <hi>); THE 28 NETWORKS median <m> (quartiles <q1> to <q3>, range <lo> to <hi>)`.
3. `THE TUNED AUTOPILOTS BEAT <b> OF 140 PAIRS WITH THE 28 NETWORKS IN THE DARK, TIE <t>, ARE BELOW <w>`.
4. `LIT TANK: TUNED GAIN <mean>; THE 28 NETWORKS median <m>`.
5. `WHAT TUNING MOVED (median factor over the five champions, gains moved by more than 1.5x either way): <names with factors, or none>`.
6. `VERDICT: <THE LEARNED GAIN IS A TUNING GAP | THE LEARNED GAIN IS PARTLY A TUNING GAP | THE LEARNED GAIN IS NOT A TUNING GAP | NOT ASSESSED: TUNING FOUND NOTHING>`.
7. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, tuned <total> (<max> in the worst), networks <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; each tuned champion's eleven gains and its four search scores; each tuned autopilot's paired readings.

## 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any gain is tuned or any seed is flown
Tuning finds a gain (at least 4 of 5 beat the autopilot by more than 0.02 in the dark): about 80 percent. The verdict: **A TUNING GAP about 40 percent, PARTLY about 35, NOT a tuning gap about 15, NOT ASSESSED about 10.** (The networks have 963 degrees of freedom and a nonlinear residual; the tuned autopilot has eleven and is linear; but the dropout score is dominated by turn speed, which the heading gains set.) The five tuned autopilots agree to within 0.01 (sd under 0.01): about 60. The best tuned autopilot beats the best of the 28 networks: about 10. The heading-loop gains are among those tuning moves (`kHdg` or `kR` moved by more than 1.5x in at least 4 of 5 champions): about 70. No lost sub in any arm: about 85.

## 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead6.mjs` (the gains, the ES search and champion by the recipe, the tank kind, the board with the controls from H5's records, the readings, the checks, the verdict and the bottom lines; reads nothing from disk), `tools/ship/headToHead6Cmd.mjs` (export-free: `train` runs the 20 searches one process each with a resumable results file, `fly` reads that file, H5's training file and the eight weight files, flies, prints and writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head6.json`) and `tools/ship/headToHead6-selfcheck.mjs` with a mutation pass; then the training (about 30 minutes on four cores), the flight and the result as a section here. **NOT IN THIS ROUND:** tuning the scheduling constants; tuning on the tank's dropout (the networks were not); more than five replicates; the plain nets; any claim about why a particular gain moved. **WHAT THIS ROUND CAN NOT SAY, up front:** a tuning gap found here is a gap for THIS search recipe and these eleven gains (a different parameterisation might find more or less); the tuned autopilots and the networks were trained on the same 40 dives and flown on the tank's wandering dropout, which neither was trained on; and a verdict of NOT A TUNING GAP says the networks use something an eleven-gain linear autopilot cannot reach, not what.


## Round H6, RESULT (2026-11-06): NOT ASSESSED: TUNING FOUND NOTHING -- THE REGISTERED RULE STOPS HERE, AND THE SEARCH HIT ITS OWN BOUNDS, SO "THE LEARNED GAIN IS NOT A TUNING GAP" IS NOT WHAT THIS SAYS
Ids 6000-6199, 160 s, dark and lit, 34 arms, one flight (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head6.json`, training records `head-to-head6-training.jsonl`, 20 searches of 400 iterations, about 150 s each). All seven checks passed. **Bottom lines as printed:** 0 of 5 tuned autopilots beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02; tuned dark gain +0.0104 (sd 0.0008, range +0.0095 to +0.0116) against the 28 networks' median +0.0904 (quartiles +0.0671 to +0.1073, range +0.0152 to +0.1179); the tuned autopilots beat 0 of 140 pairs with the networks, tie 7, are below 133; lit tank: tuned +0.0183, networks' median +0.1002; no lost sub anywhere.
**What it says:** the verdict is the registered `NOT ASSESSED: TUNING FOUND NOTHING`, and it is fair to say so plainly: tuning did move the score (each tuned autopilot reads BETTER than the autopilot, 2.2 to 2.8 se in the dark, +0.0095 to +0.0116; about 6 se lit, about +0.018), but by a tenth of the networks' median gain and below the registered 0.02 line, and it is the same small gain from all five replicates (their dark gains differ by 0.002). No tuned autopilot beats any of the 28 networks, and the weakest network (+0.0152) is still above the best tuned autopilot (+0.0116) only barely. The five replicates agree, which says the search converges, not that the autopilot's best is found.
**What it does NOT say, and the reason is in the champions' table:** the search was bounded to [default/8, default x 8] and every champion sits ON a bound for several gains: kTheta 120 (15 x 8) in 5 of 5, kBalD 0.240 (0.03 x 8) in 5 of 5, kSpd 4.000 (0.5 x 8) in 5 of 5, kQ 1.500 (12 / 8) in 4 of 5, kHdg 24 (3 x 8) in 3 of 5 (22.4 and 16.6 in the others), and tuned-4 has vzMax at 8.000 (1 x 8). So the gain found is the gain available INSIDE the bounds, a flat plateau (the four searches of each replicate, and all 20, end between -0.2863 and -0.2908 held-out), and a wider box could find more; a gain that pins against every bound is a search that wanted to go further. Also the search maximised the dive score (`TRAIN_SET`), the networks' own objective, not the dropout's dark heading error; the same objective for both, which is the registered fairness, but a tuning aimed at the dark heading was not tried. Neither is a failure of the round; both limit what "tuning found nothing" may be read to mean: **it does not support the claim that a tuned PD controller cannot match the networks, only that this search of this box did not.**
**Expectations (registered before any seed):** tuning finds a gain (at least 4 of 5 beat the autopilot by more than 0.02), 80 percent: **it did not**; the verdict, NOT ASSESSED at 10 percent: **it is the outcome I rated least likely** (A TUNING GAP 40, PARTLY 35, NOT 15); the five tuned agree (sd under 0.01), 60 percent: yes (0.0008); the best tuned beats the best network, 10 percent: no (it beats none); kHdg or kR moved by more than 1.5x in at least 4 of 5, 70 percent: yes (kHdg moved 5.5 to 8x in 5 of 5; kR by 1.0 to 1.55x); no lost sub, 85 percent: yes. One thing worth keeping: the heading gain kHdg went to its bound too (3 to 8x), so the search did push on the heading, together with the attitude, balance and speed gains; that all of them pinned against the box at once says the box, not a missing direction, limited the result.
**What the registered rule leaves open (not run):** a wider box (default/64 to default x 64), the same search on the dropout's own score, and a larger random-restart search. Nothing in this round is a reason to stop H7 and H8; it is a reason to read the networks' gain as not explained by an eleven-gain PD retune of this size.

## Round H7, REGISTERED (2026-11-06, before any replicate is trained and before any id is flown): RECIPE OR LUCK -- TEN REPLICATES OF EACH OF TWO TRAINING RECIPES ON THE SAME NETWORK
### 1. Why
H2 gave the eight 963-weight networks gains from +0.077 to +0.106 over the autopilot; the later ones were trained by different recipes (robust hulls, tails, wide hulls, noisy senses) from one seed each. H5 then found identically trained controls spread by sd 0.021 on the same measure, about three times the distance of the connectome network from their mean. So a gap between two single networks of two recipes cannot be read as a recipe effect until the spread of one recipe over its own seeds is known. H6 closed the other door (an eleven-gain PD retune does not explain the gain). H7 asks, once, with replicates: **is the difference between the base recipe and the robust-hull recipe on the compass dropout larger than the luck of the seed?**
> Do ten champions of the base recipe and ten champions of the robust recipe, all the Giant Fiber Circuit network trained from the hand weights, differ on the dropout by more than the spread inside either recipe?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (twenty-three), flying layer, the board's `dropout` task:** `autopilot`; `base-0` .. `base-9`; `robust-0` .. `robust-9`; and the two shipped networks as references, `network-Wip` (the base recipe, one seed) and `network-Robust` (the robust recipe, one seed). All are the 963-weight GFC pilot (`pilot: network, circuit: gfc`); each references the autopilot as its control.
**The base recipe (replicate r = 0..9):** four (1+1)-ES searches of 400 iterations (sigma 0.3, 1..4 weights per mutation) from the hand weights on the nominal vessel and TRAIN_SET, each pruned back toward the hand weights (`FLY.train` and `FLY.prune`, brain/pilotSubPolicy.mjs), seeds `7100 + 10 r + k`; champion = best HELD_OUT_SET score, ties to the smaller k. This is H5's recipe on the GFC.
**The robust recipe (replicate r):** tools/trainSubRobust.mjs reproduced as a library: four (1+1)-ES searches of 200 iterations from the hand weights, each candidate scored as the mean over FIVE vessels (the nominal EXPLORER, Munk x3, Munk 0, fins x0.6, righting moment x2.5) on every other TRAIN_SET dive, no prune, seeds `7300 + 10 r + k`; champion = best mean HELD_OUT_SET score over the five vessels, ties to the smaller k. (The tool's own run is the cross-check: its seed-8000 result is not re-run, the library's first iterations are pinned against the tool's in the gate.)
**Task and ids:** the dropout: one-sub tanks, 160 s, same spawn for every arm, score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi; lit control = the same ids with no event. **Ids 7000-7199 (200), read once, dark and lit.**
**Readings:** pooled paired differences, BETTER iff above 2 se, WORSE iff below -2 se, else TIES; a pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before any replicate is trained.** Let g_b and g_r be the ten dark gains of the base and the robust champions, d = mean(g_r) - mean(g_b), se = sqrt(var(g_b)/10 + var(g_r)/10) (sample variances), s_w = sqrt((var(g_b) + var(g_r))/2) the spread inside a recipe. (1) If fewer than 8 of 10 champions of EITHER recipe beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02: `NOT ASSESSED: A RECIPE DID NOT LEARN`. (2) Else if |d| <= 2 se: `LUCK: THE RECIPES ARE NOT TOLD APART`. (3) Else if |d| <= s_w: `A SMALL RECIPE EFFECT, INSIDE THE SPREAD OF A RECIPE`, with `ROBUST ABOVE` or `ROBUST BELOW`. (4) Else `THE RECIPE MATTERS: ROBUST IS BETTER` (d > 0) or `ROBUST IS WORSE`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every champion is the champion of exactly four searches with 963 finite weights; the twenty replicate arms are pairwise different in the dark; **the robust library's first scored candidate equals trainSubRobust.mjs's on the same seed (the gate pins it).**

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `LEARNED: <b> OF 10 BASE AND <r> OF 10 ROBUST CHAMPIONS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02`.
2. `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: BASE <mean> (sd <sd>, range <lo> to <hi>); ROBUST <mean> (sd, range); DIFFERENCE <d> (se <se>), SPREAD INSIDE A RECIPE <s_w>`.
3. `WHERE THE SHIPPED NETWORKS FALL: network-Wip +<g> IS THE <n>TH OF 11 BASE GAINS; network-Robust +<g> IS THE <n>TH OF 11 ROBUST GAINS` (rank among the ten plus itself, 1 = highest).
4. `LIT TANK: BASE <mean>, ROBUST <mean>, DIFFERENCE <d>`.
5. `DOES THE TRAINING SCORE PREDICT THE DROPOUT: BASE r = <r>, ROBUST r = <r>` (Pearson over the ten champions' held-out scores and dark gains, within a recipe).
6. `VERDICT: <as above>`.
7. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, base <total> (<max> in the worst), robust <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; each champion's held-out score and its four search scores; every replicate's paired readings.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any replicate is trained or any seed is flown
Both recipes learn (at least 8 of 10 beat the autopilot by more than 0.02 in the dark): about 90 percent. The verdict: **LUCK about 45 percent, A SMALL RECIPE EFFECT about 25, THE RECIPE MATTERS about 20 (ROBUST BETTER about 15, WORSE about 5), NOT ASSESSED about 10.** The spread inside a recipe (sd of the ten gains) is about 0.02, as H5's controls: 70 percent between 0.01 and 0.035. The shipped networks lie inside their recipe's ten-replicate range (rank 1 to 11 trivially, between the 2nd and the 10th): 70 percent for each. The training score predicts the dropout gain inside a recipe (|r| above 0.5): 30 percent; I expect it not to, because the training task is a dive and the dropout a compass loss. No lost sub: 85 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead7.mjs` (the two searches and champions, the board, readings, checks, verdict, lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead7Cmd.mjs` (train with `--workers`, resumable `brain/rig-results/head-to-head7-training.jsonl`, 80 searches; fly; `--one base|robust:r:k`) and `headToHead7-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). The training is long (80 searches: about 20 hours of one core, 2 hours on 12 workers); it is run on the rig.

## Round H7, RESULT (2026-11-06): LUCK: THE RECIPES ARE NOT TOLD APART -- BUT TEN REPLICATES CANNOT SEE A DIFFERENCE SMALLER THAN ABOUT 0.025, AND THE SPREAD INSIDE EACH RECIPE (SD 0.027 AND 0.030) IS AS LARGE AS THAT
Run on the rig (80 searches trained, then one flight, ids 7000-7199, 160 s, dark and lit, 23 arms; `brain/rig-results/head-to-head7-training.jsonl`, `head-to-head7.json`). All six checks passed. **Bottom lines as printed:** 9 of 10 base and 8 of 10 robust champions beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02; dark gain over the autopilot BASE +0.0743 (sd 0.0269, range +0.0183 to +0.0981), ROBUST +0.0537 (sd 0.0296, range +0.0038 to +0.0989), difference -0.0206 (se 0.0127), spread inside a recipe 0.0283; network-Wip +0.0727 is the 7th of 11 base gains, network-Robust +0.0999 is the 1st of 11 robust gains; lit tank BASE +0.0856, ROBUST +0.0620, difference -0.0236; the training score against the dropout gain, BASE r = 0.39, ROBUST r = -0.41; no lost sub.
**What it says:** the verdict is the registered `LUCK`: the robust recipe's mean is 0.0206 BELOW the base recipe's, which is 1.6 se, short of the registered 2 se (0.0254). Read it as what it is: a difference of 0.02 is the size of ONE recipe's own spread (sd 0.027 and 0.030, the same size H5 found for controls, 0.021), so ten replicates per arm can tell recipes apart only when they differ by more than about 0.025, and nothing of that size is here. The lit tank says the same in the same direction (-0.0236). The sign is worth noting without leaning on it: the more elaborate recipe (five vessels, 200 iterations, no prune) did not do better on the compass dropout, and the point estimate is lower, not higher.
**The shipped networks against their recipes:** network-Wip (+0.0727) sits at the base mean (+0.0743), the 7th of 11. network-Robust (+0.0999) is above all ten robust replicates (the best is +0.0989) and 1.6 sd above their mean (+0.0537): the shipped Robust network is the lucky end of its recipe, not what the recipe typically gives, and its lead over Wip in H2 (+0.0999 against +0.0727) is therefore within what a recipe's seeds alone produce (the robust replicates range +0.0038 to +0.0989). That is the main use of this round: **a difference between two single trained networks of 0.03 or less, the size of the gaps in H1 and H2 between the eight networks, is not evidence about their recipes.**
**The training score does not predict the dropout:** within a recipe the held-out score and the dark gain correlate at r = 0.39 (base) and r = -0.41 (robust), opposite in sign and both inside what ten points give by chance (|r| must exceed 0.63 for p below 0.05). So picking a champion by held-out score, as every recipe in this series does, does not pick the best dropout pilot; the shipped Robust network was picked that way and is nevertheless the best of its recipe's draws, which is luck, not selection.
**Expectations (registered before any replicate was trained):** both recipes learn (at least 8 of 10 by more than 0.02), 90 percent: yes, narrowly for robust (exactly 8; robust-3 +0.0134 and robust-5 +0.0038 did not); the verdict LUCK at 45 percent, the single most likely outcome: it happened; the spread inside a recipe between 0.01 and 0.035, 70 percent: yes (0.027 and 0.030); each shipped network inside its recipe's ten-replicate range, 70 percent each: Wip yes, Robust NO (just above the range, by 0.001); the training score predicts the dropout (|r| above 0.5), 30 percent: no; no lost sub, 85 percent: yes.
**Open, not run:** a larger replicate count if a recipe effect of about 0.01 matters (it would need about 70 per arm), the noisy-senses recipe as a third arm, and a pilot selected on the dropout's own score instead of the dive held-out score.

## Round H8, REGISTERED (2026-11-06, before any id is flown): MEMORY ON A REAL GYRO -- DOES THE MEMORY SURVIVE A BIASED, NOISY RATE SENSOR?
### 1. Why
