H4 measured what each pilot believes through the compass dropout when it is handed the EXACT body rates: the dead-reckoning navigator holds the heading to a few thousandths of a radian, the leaky unit to 0.065 rad rms, the held heading drifts 0.564. Both memories are named in their own files as CEILINGS ("a perfect gyro; a real gyro drifts"). H8 takes the ceiling away: the body rates the MEMORY reads (and only the memory: the autopilot keeps the true state) are replaced by a gyro with a constant bias per axis (drawn once per flight) and white noise per tick, at the levels `tools/subDisturb.mjs` already names for rate noise, and the question is how much gyro error the memory can take before holding the last heading is as good.
> Through the 16 s compass dropouts of the tank, does the heading the navigator, the leaky unit, the fly heading loop and a random wiring believe still beat the held heading when their rate sensor is biased and noisy, at three levels?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Cells (seventeen), each its own one-sub tank per id:** `held` (the board's `dropout` event, no estimate); and for each of the four memories `navigator` (tools/subNavigator.mjs), `leaky-unit` (brain/pilotLeakyHeading.mjs, sigma 0.9999, gain 1), `fly` (the fly heading-loop estimator of render/subDropoutScene.mjs pilotDefs) and `er` (its random-wiring estimator), four gyro levels: `<arm>@0` .. `<arm>@3`.
**The gyro (the same for every memory, seeded from the id only):** the state the memory is handed has its body rates replaced by `w + b + e`, with `b` a constant drawn once per flight from N(0, bias) on each of the three axes and `e` drawn every tick from N(0, white). The inner autopilot is handed the TRUE body rates (a shim restores them), so only the memory's reading of the rate is wrong; the attitude is exact. **Levels:** 0 perfect (bias 0, white 0: the memory equals H4's arm, the gate holds this to 1e-9); 1 `good` (bias 0.002 rad/s, white 0.003: subDisturb's level-1 rate noise); 2 `rough` (bias 0.006, white 0.009: its level 3x); 3 `bad day` (bias 0.02, white 0.015: its level 5x). A bias of 0.02 rad/s (1.1 deg/s) is a cheap MEMS gyro.
**Task and ids:** the dropout of H4: one-sub tanks, 160 s, the compass dark for 16 s of every 40, the same spawn for every cell. **Ids 8000-8199 (200), read once.** **Belief** = 1 - (rms of the compass error over the dark ticks)/pi (H4's measure); **flight** = 1 - (mean settled dark heading error)/pi; the **window profile** = the rms compass error in four bins of 4 s since the window opened, averaged over the ids (reported).
**Readings:** pooled paired differences by the switchboard's reading (BETTER iff above 2 se, WORSE iff below -2 se, else TIES), on the belief unless said.
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown.** (1) `THE MEMORY SURVIVES THE GYRO` iff the navigator and the leaky unit each read BETTER than `held` on belief at every level 1, 2 and 3; otherwise `THE MEMORY FAILS AT LEVEL <l>: <arms>` with l the lowest level at which one of them does not read BETTER and the arms that do not. (2) `THE ROUGH GYRO COSTS THE NAVIGATOR` iff the navigator's belief at level 3 reads WORSE than at level 0 (it is reported as the paired difference, BETTER/WORSE/TIES). (3) Flight: the navigator's and the leaky unit's flight at level 3 against `held` (BETTER, TIES or WORSE each). (4) The two learned memories: the arms among `fly`, `er` that read BETTER than `held` on belief, per level.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every cell flew 200 ids; the ids are the same in every cell; belief and flight scores inside (0, 1); every run had dark ticks; **the level-0 cells equal H4's arms (navigator and leaky unit) on belief and flight to 1e-9 on the ids the gate flies (the wrapper at zero noise is the identity)**; the window profile of the navigator at level 3 rises from the first bin to the last on average (the bias integrates); every cell differs from every other on belief.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `BELIEF AGAINST THE HELD HEADING, BY LEVEL: <for each level 0..3: navigator <reading>, leaky-unit <reading>, fly <reading>, er <reading>>`.
2. `GYRO ERROR IN THE BELIEF (rms rad): navigator <l0/l1/l2/l3>; leaky-unit <...>; held <rms>`.
3. `THE ROUGH GYRO COSTS THE NAVIGATOR <d> IN BELIEF (<reading>); THE LEAKY UNIT <d> (<reading>)`.
4. `WINDOW PROFILE, NAVIGATOR AT LEVEL 3 (rms rad by 4 s bin): <four numbers>`.
5. `FLIGHT AT LEVEL 3: navigator <reading>, leaky-unit <reading> AGAINST THE HELD HEADING`.
6. `VERDICT: <as above>`.
7. `LOST SUBS: held <a>, memories <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** each cell's mean belief, flight and error rms; the paired reading of every cell against `held` and of every level against level 0.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
The memory survives the gyro (navigator and leaky unit BETTER than held at all three levels): about 90 percent (the held heading's error is 0.564 rad rms; a bias of 0.02 rad/s integrates to at most 0.32 rad over a 16 s window and to about 0.18 rad rms through it). The rough gyro costs the navigator belief (level 3 WORSE than level 0): about 95 percent, and the navigator's rms error at level 3 lies between 0.1 and 0.3 rad: 70 percent. The navigator and the leaky unit read TIES at level 3: 60 percent (the leak is 0.3 percent a second, small against a bias that integrates). The navigator's flight at level 3 reads BETTER than held: 50 percent (H4 found a pilot that knows its heading flies only 0.015 better; noise may remove that). The fly heading loop reads BETTER than held on belief at no level: 90 percent; the random wiring reads BETTER at level 0: 75 percent. The profile rises with time since the window opened: 95 percent. No lost sub: 85 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead8.mjs` (the gyro, the noisy-memory wrapper that reuses the unmodified navigator, leaky unit and the page's two estimators, the flight with the window profile, the audit, readings, checks, verdict and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead8Cmd.mjs` (reads the vendored heading-loop circuit as H4's command does, flies, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head8.json`) and `headToHead8-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Seventeen cells of 200 ids of 160 s: about as long as H4 times three; run here.

## Round H8, RESULT (2026-11-06): THE MEMORY SURVIVES THE GYRO -- THE NAVIGATOR'S BELIEF ERROR GROWS FROM 0 TO 0.155 RAD AT THE ROUGHEST LEVEL (HELD: 0.560), BUT THE FLIGHT DOES NOT SHOW IT AT ANY LEVEL, AND AT LEVEL 3 THE LEAKY UNIT BELIEVES BETTER THAN THE NAVIGATOR
Ids 8000-8199, 160 s, 17 cells, one flight (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head8.json`). All seven checks passed, including that the navigator and the leaky unit at level 0 equal H4's arms to 1e-9. **Bottom lines as printed:** every level: navigator and leaky unit BETTER than held on belief, the random wiring (er) BETTER, the fly heading loop TIES; belief rms error in rad at levels 0/1/2/3: navigator 0.000/0.016/0.047/0.155, leaky unit 0.064/0.064/0.073/0.141, held 0.560; the rough gyro costs the navigator -0.0493 in belief (WORSE, every one of 200 ids) and the leaky unit -0.0244 (WORSE); the navigator's window profile at level 3 is 0.039, 0.103, 0.169, 0.235 rad by 4 s bin; flight at level 3 TIES held for both; no lost sub.
**What it says:** the verdict is the registered `THE MEMORY SURVIVES THE GYRO`: at level 3 (a bias of 0.02 rad/s per axis, 1.1 deg/s, a cheap MEMS gyro, and white noise of 0.015) the navigator still believes the heading better than the held heading by +0.129 on the score (198 of 200 ids) and the leaky unit by +0.133 (200 of 200); the navigator's error is 0.155 rad rms against 0.560 for holding, 3.6 times smaller. Its error grows with the level about in proportion to the bias (0.016, 0.047, 0.155 for biases of 0.002, 0.006, 0.02), and within a window it grows with the time since the compass went out, as a bias that integrates must (0.039 to 0.235 rad from the first 4 s to the last). Extrapolating that straight line, the navigator would stop beating the held heading at a bias of about 0.07 rad/s (4 deg/s); that is an extrapolation from three levels, not a measurement.
**A crossover the registration did not look for:** at levels 0 to 2 the navigator believes better than the leaky unit (by +0.020, +0.016, +0.009), at level 3 it believes WORSE (-0.0045, se 0.0013). The leaky unit's leak (0.3 percent a second) is an error against a perfect gyro (its 0.064 rad at level 0) and a partial cure for a biased one: it bleeds off part of the integrated bias, so the cheaper unit costs less as the gyro gets worse (-0.0244 against the navigator's -0.0493). Said plainly: that is a property of this bias-and-noise model with a 16 s window, not a reason to prefer a leaky unit; a leak makes the estimate wrong in the case a real gyro is good.
**The belief does not reach the flight:** at level 0 the navigator flies 0.7718 against the held heading's 0.7604 (H4's gap, +0.011); at level 3 its flight is 0.7602 against 0.7604, TIES (-0.0002, se 0.0044), and the leaky unit's +0.0039 (se 0.0044) TIES too. Every memory's belief error is far below the held heading's at every level while the flight differs from held's by less than its se: the 0.56 rad of drift the held heading carries is not what limits how well the sub flies the dark window (H4's finding, now at four gyro levels). **The fly heading loop** believes what the held heading believes at every level (belief -0.0001 against held at level 0 and TIES at 1, 2 and 3, se 0.0002; per id it moves by up to 6e-3 with the gyro, but not in one direction), as in H4, so the gyro cannot hurt a memory it does not have. **The random wiring** (er) beats the held heading by +0.050 at level 3 and by +0.052 at level 0 (belief), its error 0.396 to 0.401 rad, unmoved by the gyro (level 3 against level 0: -0.0019, se 0.0012, TIES); like the fly loop its estimate is not moved by the gyro.
**Expectations (registered before any id was flown):** the memory survives the gyro, 90 percent: yes; the rough gyro costs the navigator belief, 95 percent, and its error at level 3 between 0.1 and 0.3 rad, 70 percent: yes (WORSE, 0.155); the navigator and the leaky unit TIE at level 3, 60 percent: **no, the leaky unit is ahead by 0.0045 (se 0.0013, 3.5 se), so the pair reads WORSE for the navigator**; the navigator's flight at level 3 reads BETTER than held, 50 percent: **no, TIES**; the fly loop BETTER than held at no level, 90 percent: yes; the random wiring BETTER than held at level 0, 75 percent: yes; the profile rises with the time since the window opened, 95 percent: yes; no lost sub, 85 percent: yes.
**What it does not say:** the gyro model is a constant bias plus white noise; a real gyro also wanders (a bias that drifts with temperature and time, which a 16 s window would see little of), has scale-factor error (proportional to the turn rate, which is what the dropout's turning sub produces) and cross-axis coupling; none is in it. The autopilot was handed the TRUE body rates; a sub whose own rate loop used the same bad gyro would fly worse in the lit tank too, and H8 does not measure that.
**Open, not run:** scale-factor error and a wandering bias, the autopilot's own rate loop on the noisy gyro, a longer dark window (the error grows with time, so a 60 s window is the case where the break-even would matter).

## Round H9, REGISTERED (2026-11-06, before any id is flown): THE LESION -- HOW MUCH OF A NETWORK'S DROPOUT GAIN IS IN ITS RECURRENT CORE?
### 1. Why
Every trained 963-weight network in this series beats the PD autopilot on the compass dropout (H1, H2, H7: +0.00 to +0.10), and H5 and H7 showed the wiring and the recipe do not explain the size of the gain. H4 and H8 showed that nothing in these networks holds the heading: a pilot that knows its heading flies only about 0.01 better. One fact of the pilot's code is easy to lose sight of and decides what a lesion can mean: `brain/pilotSubPolicy.mjs` computes its forward pass from ZERO hidden activity on EVERY tick: the recurrent core is REC_STEPS = 3 rounds of message passing between the 34 neurons INSIDE one tick (the 313 synapses), and nothing is carried from one tick to the next. So these networks have no memory, and the only thing the "recurrent core" can contribute is a deeper map from the 14 features to the four commands. H9 takes that core out and asks what the gain is made of.
> If the 313 recurrent synapses of a trained network are set to zero (its input weights, biases and output weights untouched), how much of the network's dark gain over the autopilot is left?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (fifty-nine), flying layer, the board's `dropout` task:** `autopilot`; the hand weights `hand` (the autopilot written through the network; its core is zero already) and `hand-off`; and for each of 28 trained networks `<id>` (intact) and `<id>-off` (core off): the eight shipped networks of H1 and H2 (`network-Wip`, `network-Actuators`, `network-Robust`, `network-RobustLong`, `network-RobustTail`, `network-RobustTail2`, `network-RobustWide`, `network-RobustNoisy`) and the twenty champions of H7 (`base-0` .. `base-9`, `robust-0` .. `robust-9`, read from `brain/rig-results/head-to-head7-training.jsonl`). All are the 963-weight GFC pilot (`pilot: network, circuit: gfc`).
**The lesion:** a copy of the weight vector with the recurrent block zeroed: indices 510 to 822 (14 x 34 input weights + 34 biases = 510, then the 313 recurrent synapses); the input weights, the biases and the output weights are untouched. Zero recurrent weights leave the identity carry of the forward pass, so the lesioned network is a one-hidden-layer relu network (a 14-34-4 map). The hand weights have a zero core, so `hand-off` must fly exactly as `hand` (the gate holds it, and the round checks it).
**Task and ids:** the dropout of H4 and H5: one-sub tanks, 160 s, the same spawn for every arm, score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi; lit control = the same ids with no event. **Ids 9000-9199 (200), read once, dark and lit.**
**Readings:** pooled paired differences by the switchboard's reading (BETTER iff above 2 se, WORSE iff below -2 se, else TIES). A network's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot; the lesion's EFFECT on it is `<id>-off` minus `<id>`.
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown.** Let G_i be the mean dark gain of the 28 intact networks, G_o the mean dark gain of the 28 core-off networks, and f = G_o / G_i. (1) If fewer than 20 of the 28 intact networks beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02: `NOT ASSESSED: THE NETWORKS DID NOT GAIN`. (2) Else if f >= 0.8: `THE GAIN SURVIVES THE LESION: THE RECURRENT CORE IS NOT WHERE IT IS`. (3) Else if f <= 0.2: `THE GAIN LIVES IN THE RECURRENT CORE`. (4) Else `THE CORE CARRIES PART OF THE GAIN`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ (the event reaches the flight); every network is 963 finite weights and every lesioned vector equals its network outside indices 510-822 and is zero inside them; **`hand-off` equals `hand` on every id in both conditions to 1e-9**; the lesion changes the flight of at least 20 of the 28 networks (a paired reading other than identical scores on at least one id); the 28 intact networks are pairwise different in the dark.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `LEARNED: <n> OF 28 INTACT NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02`.
2. `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: INTACT <mean> (sd <sd>, range <lo> to <hi>); CORE OFF <mean> (sd, range); SURVIVING FRACTION <f>`.
3. `THE LESION CHANGES <b> NETWORKS FOR THE BETTER, <t> TIED, <w> FOR THE WORSE (dark, core off minus intact, of 28)`.
4. `BY GROUP (intact / core off, dark gain): SHIPPED <a> / <b>; BASE <a> / <b>; ROBUST <a> / <b>`.
5. `LIT TANK: INTACT <mean>, CORE OFF <mean>, SURVIVING FRACTION <f>`.
6. `VERDICT: <as above>`.
7. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, intact <total> (<max> in the worst), core off <total> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; the paired reading of each network's lesion in both conditions.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
At least 20 of the 28 intact networks gain by more than 0.02 in the dark: about 90 percent (H7: 17 of 20 replicates did, and the eight shipped ones all did). The verdict: **THE GAIN SURVIVES about 40 percent, THE CORE CARRIES PART about 45, THE GAIN LIVES IN THE CORE about 10, NOT ASSESSED about 5.** (Training moved all 963 weights, a third of the mutations of the (1+1)-ES land in the core, so I do not expect the core to be inert; the network has no memory, so I do not expect it to carry what a memory would.) The lesion changes the dark gain of most networks (at least 20 of 28 read other than TIES, either way): 70 percent. `hand-off` equals `hand`: certain, by the arithmetic; the check exists so the harness cannot hide a fault in the lesion. No lost sub: 85 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead9.mjs` (the lesion, the board, the audit, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; reads nothing from disk; the command hands the 28 networks in), `headToHead9Cmd.mjs` (reads the eight weight files and H7's training file, flies, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head9.json`) and `headToHead9-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). 59 arms of 200 ids, dark and lit, 160 s: run here (about as long as H6 times two).

## Round H9, RESULT (2026-11-06): THE GAIN LIVES IN THE RECURRENT CORE -- AS A DEPENDENCE, NOT AS A LOCATION: ZEROING THE CORE LEAVES 14 PERCENT OF THE DARK GAIN AND TAKES NINE NETWORKS BELOW THE AUTOPILOT
Ids 9000-9199, 160 s, dark and lit, 59 arms, one flight (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head9.json`). All eight checks passed, including `hand-off` equal to `hand` on every id in both conditions and the lesion changing the dark flight of 28 of 28 networks. **Bottom lines as printed:** 27 of 28 intact networks beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02; dark gain over the autopilot INTACT +0.0775 (sd 0.0293, range +0.0059 to +0.1106), CORE OFF +0.0108 (sd 0.0344, range -0.1156 to +0.0624), surviving fraction 0.14; the lesion changes 0 networks for the better, 3 tied, 25 for the worse; by group (intact / core off) SHIPPED +0.0978 / +0.0133, BASE +0.0791 / -0.0065, ROBUST +0.0596 / +0.0260; lit tank intact +0.0894, core off +0.0123, fraction 0.14; lost subs: autopilot 0, intact 1, core off 3 (2 in the worst, `network-Robust-off`).
**What it says:** the verdict is the registered `THE GAIN LIVES IN THE RECURRENT CORE` (f = 0.14, below 0.2). In plain terms: take the 313 recurrent weights out of a trained network and the dark gain over the autopilot falls from +0.0775 to +0.0108, in the lit tank from +0.0894 to +0.0123 (the surviving fraction is 0.14 in both, so the lesion does not separate the dark from the lit gain), and the lesioned network is no better than the autopilot it was trained from on average. All 28 intact networks differ from their own lesion; 25 of 28 lesions read WORSE in the dark and in the lit tank, none BETTER.
**What it does NOT say, and the reason is in the table:** a core-off network is not the autopilot and not the network: it is a third pilot nobody trained. Nine of the 28 fly WORSE than the autopilot they were trained from (core-off gain below 0; `base-9` at -0.1156, `base-2` at -0.0252, `network-RobustLong` at -0.0209), and the core-off gain does not follow the intact gain across networks (correlation -0.05 over the 28). The input weights, biases and output weights were trained with the core in place, so zeroing it perturbs a function the other 650 weights had adapted to; that the gain falls when a trained block is removed is what one expects of any trained block, and it shows the networks DEPEND on the core, not that the core is where a gain was found. Said the other way: the lesion cannot tell a core that computes something useful from a core the rest of the network learned to compensate for. The registered verdict answers the question as asked; the question that would answer the one behind it is the retrained control below.
**What does separate the networks:** the dependence is not uniform. Three robust-recipe replicates lose nothing measurable in the dark (`robust-3` +0.0233 to +0.0225, `robust-4` +0.0642 to +0.0624, `robust-5` +0.0059 to +0.0003: TIES), `robust-4` keeps 97 percent of a +0.064 gain with no core, so a gain of that size exists WITHOUT the recurrent synapses; the robust group keeps 44 percent (+0.0260 of +0.0596), the shipped networks 14 percent (+0.0133 of +0.0978) and the base-recipe replicates none on average (-0.0065 of +0.0791; seven of its ten core-off gains are below +0.01). The recipe that trained for more vessels, no prune and 200 iterations leaves the networks less dependent on the core than the base recipe's 400 iterations and prune does; that is a pattern in 10 replicates per recipe and H7 has shown how far to trust a 0.02 difference of group means with these spreads.
**Expectations (registered before any id was flown):** at least 20 of the 28 intact networks gain by more than 0.02 in the dark, 90 percent: yes (27); the verdict, THE GAIN SURVIVES about 40 percent, THE CORE CARRIES PART about 45, **THE GAIN LIVES IN THE CORE about 10**, NOT ASSESSED about 5: the outcome I rated least likely among the three that could happen happened; the lesion changes the dark gain of most networks (at least 20 of 28 read other than TIES), 70 percent: yes (25 WORSE); `hand-off` equals `hand`: yes; no lost sub, 85 percent: **no, four sub-runs were lost (one intact, `robust-6`; three core off, among them two ids of `network-Robust-off`)**, which is itself a measure of what the lesion does to a pilot.
**Open, the follow-up this result asks for:** train the same 14-34-4 map with the core FROZEN at zero from the start, by the base recipe, ten replicates, and compare its dark gain to H7's base replicates (+0.0743, sd 0.0269): if a network trained without the core reaches the same gain, the core is not where the gain comes from; if it reaches only +0.01, the core does the work the other weights cannot. It is a training round of the size of H7's base arm (40 searches, about 8 hours of one core, run on the rig), and it is the control H9's own header lists as NOT CHECKED.

## Round H10, REGISTERED (2026-11-06, before any replicate is trained and before any id is flown): TRAINED WITHOUT THE CORE -- DOES A NETWORK WHOSE RECURRENT SYNAPSES NEVER EXISTED GAIN AS MUCH?
### 1. Why
H9 zeroed the 313 recurrent synapses of 28 trained networks and the dark gain fell from +0.0775 to +0.0108. That measured how much a trained network DEPENDS on its core, not whether the core is where a gain can come from: the other 650 weights were trained with the core in place. Three robust replicates lost nothing, and `robust-4` kept +0.0624 of +0.0642 with no core, which says a gain of that size can exist without it. The control H9 names as not run is a network trained without the core from the start.
> If the same 14-34-4 map is trained by the base recipe with the 313 recurrent weights frozen at zero (never mutated, never non-zero), ten replicates, does its dark gain match that of H7's base replicates, which had the core?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (thirty-one), flying layer, the board's `dropout` task:** `autopilot`; `frozen-0` .. `frozen-9`, the new champions; `base-0` .. `base-9`, H7's base-recipe champions (read from `brain/rig-results/head-to-head7-training.jsonl`) with their cores; `base-0-off` .. `base-9-off`, the same base champions with the core zeroed (H9's lesion). All are the 963-weight GFC pilot (`pilot: network, circuit: gfc`).
**The frozen-core recipe (replicate r = 0..9):** H7's base recipe with one change: four (1+1)-ES searches of 400 iterations (sigma 0.3, 1..4 weights per mutation) from the hand weights on the nominal vessel and TRAIN_SET, each pruned back toward the hand weights (`PS.prune`), BUT every mutation draws its 1..4 weights only from the 650 indices outside the recurrent block [510, 823), so the core stays exactly zero; seeds `7500 + 10 r + k`; champion = best HELD_OUT_SET score, ties to the smaller k. The result is a one-hidden-layer relu network, `14-34-4`, with the base recipe's own training everywhere else.
**Task and ids:** the dropout of H4 to H9: one-sub tanks, 160 s, the same spawn for every arm, score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi; lit control = the same ids with no event. **Ids 10000-10199 (200), read once, dark and lit.**
**Readings:** pooled paired differences by the switchboard's reading; a pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before any replicate is trained.** Let g_f and g_b be the ten dark gains of the frozen-core and the base champions, d = mean(g_f) - mean(g_b), se = sqrt(var(g_f)/10 + var(g_b)/10) (sample variances). (1) If mean(g_f) <= 0.02: `THE CORE DOES THE WORK: WITHOUT IT THE NETWORKS DO NOT GAIN`. (2) Else if d > 2 se: `A NETWORK TRAINED WITHOUT THE CORE GAINS MORE`. (3) Else if d >= -2 se: `THE CORE IS NOT NEEDED FOR THE GAIN`. (4) Else (d < -2 se, the frozen networks gain, but less): `THE CORE ADDS TO THE GAIN, BUT A NETWORK WITHOUT IT STILL GAINS`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every frozen champion is the champion of exactly four searches with 963 finite weights **and exactly zero on [510, 823)**; every base champion is 963 finite weights and `base-r-off` is its lesion (zero on the block, equal elsewhere); the twenty frozen and base champions are pairwise different in the dark.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `LEARNED: <f> OF 10 FROZEN-CORE AND <b> OF 10 BASE CHAMPIONS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02`.
2. `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: FROZEN CORE <mean> (sd, range); BASE <mean> (sd, range); BASE WITH THE CORE OFF <mean> (sd, range); DIFFERENCE FROZEN MINUS BASE <d> (se <se>), SPREAD INSIDE A RECIPE <s_w>`.
3. `FRACTION OF THE BASE GAIN KEPT WITHOUT THE CORE: TRAINED WITHOUT IT <mean_f / mean_b>; LESIONED <mean_o / mean_b>`.
4. `LIT TANK: FROZEN CORE <mean>, BASE <mean>, DIFFERENCE <d>`.
5. `HELD-OUT DIVE SCORE (not in the verdict): FROZEN CORE <mean>, BASE <mean>`.
6. `VERDICT: <as above>`.
7. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, frozen <total> (<max> in the worst), base <total> (<max>), base core off <total> (<max>)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; each frozen champion's held-out score and four search scores; each frozen network's paired reading against the autopilot and against the same-numbered base network.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any replicate is trained or any seed is flown
The frozen-core networks learn (mean dark gain above 0.02): about 90 percent. The verdict: **THE CORE IS NOT NEEDED about 45 percent, THE CORE ADDS TO THE GAIN about 35, THE CORE DOES THE WORK about 12, A NETWORK WITHOUT THE CORE GAINS MORE about 8.** (The core is a second layer between the features and the outputs; the 14-34-4 map is a smaller function class, but the dropout gain of the intact networks is small, +0.07, and `robust-4` reached +0.064 without using its core.) The frozen-core mean gain lies between +0.04 and +0.09: 60 percent. The spread inside the frozen recipe is within 0.01 and 0.04 (H7's recipes: 0.027 and 0.030): 70 percent. The held-out dive score of the frozen networks is within 0.02 of the base networks': 60 percent. No lost sub among the frozen networks: 85 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead10.mjs` (the masked search and champion, the board, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead10Cmd.mjs` (train with `--workers`, resumable `brain/rig-results/head-to-head10-training.jsonl`, 40 searches; fly, reading H7's training file for the base champions; `--one r:k`) and `headToHead10-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). The training is 40 searches like H7's base arm: run on the rig.

## Round H10, RESULT (2026-11-07): THE CORE IS NOT NEEDED FOR THE GAIN -- A NETWORK WHOSE RECURRENT SYNAPSES NEVER EXISTED KEEPS 73 PERCENT OF THE BASE GAIN (+0.0570 AGAINST +0.0781), THE DIFFERENCE SITTING 0.0007 INSIDE THE REGISTERED 2 SE BAND
Run on the rig (40 searches trained, then one flight, ids 10000-10199, 160 s, dark and lit, 31 arms; `brain/rig-results/head-to-head10-training.jsonl`, `head-to-head10.json`). All seven checks passed, including every frozen champion exactly zero on [510, 823). **Bottom lines as printed:** 10 of 10 frozen-core and 10 of 10 base champions beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02; dark gain over the autopilot FROZEN CORE +0.0570 (sd 0.0230, range +0.0276 to +0.0921), BASE +0.0781 (sd 0.0257, range +0.0239 to +0.1032), BASE WITH THE CORE OFF -0.0062 (sd 0.0400, range -0.0972 to +0.0530), difference frozen minus base -0.0211 (se 0.0109), spread inside a recipe 0.0244; fraction of the base gain kept without the core 0.73 trained without it, -0.08 lesioned; lit tank frozen +0.0582, base +0.0845, difference -0.0262; held-out dive score (not in the verdict) frozen -0.2989, base -0.3090; no lost sub in any arm.
**What it says:** the verdict is the registered `THE CORE IS NOT NEEDED FOR THE GAIN`: ten networks trained by the base recipe with the 313 recurrent weights never mutated all gain over the autopilot (the weakest +0.0276), at a mean of +0.0570, and the difference from the ten base networks that had a core, -0.0211, is 1.94 se, short of the 2 se (0.0218) that would have read `THE CORE ADDS TO THE GAIN`. **That is a verdict at its edge and I say so:** the point estimate is that the core is worth about 0.02 of a gain of 0.078 (a quarter), the lit tank gives the same sign and a slightly larger size (-0.0262), and ten replicates of a recipe whose own spread is 0.024 cannot call a difference of 0.02 either way (H7's lesson, again; at the same spread eleven per arm would have put the same difference past 2 se, which is a statement about the arithmetic, not a finding). What the round does settle is the question H9 left: **the core is not where the gain is made.** H9's lesion took +0.0775 to +0.0108 (f = 0.14); a network trained without a core reaches 73 percent of the base gain, so what the lesion removed was the network's dependence on a block the rest of it had learned to use, as H9 said it might be.
**The recipe's spread is the same with and without the core:** the frozen sd is 0.0230 (base 0.0257, H7's base 0.0269), and the frozen recipe has no weak tail (its range starts at +0.0276, the base's at +0.0239). The ten same-numbered pairs (the labels 0 to 9 are arbitrary: replicate r of one recipe has nothing to do with replicate r of the other) read five WORSE, two BETTER, three TIES, which is that spread again and not a finding.
**The training score does not agree with the flight, as in H7:** the frozen networks' held-out dive score is better than the base networks' (-0.2989 against -0.3090) while their dropout gain is lower (+0.0570 against +0.0781). Dropout gain and lit gain are the same size for every group (frozen +0.0570 and +0.0582, base +0.0781 and +0.0845): none of what these networks gain over the autopilot is specific to the compass dropout. Taken with H4 and H8 (no memory of the heading in any trained network), H5 (wiring does not matter), H7 (recipe is within seed luck) and H9 and H10 (the core is not where the gain is), the gain of the trained networks is a general gain of a better-tuned map from the 14 features to the commands, found by a dive-trained search and carried unchanged into the dropout tank; it is the autopilot's gains, H6 found, that a search of eleven numbers cannot reach, so the extra is in what the 34 relu units can express that the PD law cannot.
**Expectations (registered before any replicate was trained):** the frozen-core networks learn (mean above 0.02), 90 percent: yes; the verdict NOT NEEDED at 45 percent, the most likely single outcome: it happened, at 1.94 se from ADDS (35 percent); the frozen mean between +0.04 and +0.09, 60 percent: yes (+0.0570); the spread inside the frozen recipe between 0.01 and 0.04, 70 percent: yes (0.0230); the held-out dive score within 0.02 of the base's, 60 percent: yes (0.010); no lost sub among the frozen networks, 85 percent: yes.
**Open, not run:** more replicates of both arms if a quarter of the gain is worth separating from luck (about 11 per arm at the registered spread if the point estimate held, about 24 to hold it at 3 se); a frozen-core network with a wider hidden layer (does a bigger one-layer map close the 0.02?); the same on a task where memory could matter (H8's gyro memory is not a learned thing here).

## Round H11, REGISTERED (2026-11-07, before any id is flown): THE CHANNEL TRANSPLANT -- WHICH OF THE FOUR COMMANDS CARRIES THE NETWORKS' GAIN?
### 1. Why
H4 to H10 ruled out memory, wiring, recipe and the recurrent core as the source of the 28 trained networks' gain over the autopilot (about +0.07 in the dark and the same in the lit tank), and H6 found an 11-gain retune of the PD law inside a x8 box does not reach it. What is left is the map itself, the 14 features to the four commands (pitch, yaw, thrust, ballast). The cheapest way to say where the map differs from the autopilot in a way that matters is to swap one command at a time: give the network's command to the autopilot, or the autopilot's command to the network, on one channel, and fly it.
> Replace one of a trained network's four commands by the autopilot's own: how much of the gain goes? Give the autopilot only that one command of the network's: how much of the gain comes?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**The networks (28):** the eight shipped (`network-Wip` .. `network-RobustNoisy`) and the twenty champions of H7 (`base-0..9`, `robust-0..9`), as in H9. **For each network n and each channel c in {pitch, yaw, thrust, ballast}:** `n` (intact), `n/-c` (the network with ITS channel c replaced by the autopilot's command for the same state, the other three the network's), and `n/+c` (the AUTOPILOT with only channel c taken from the network). That is 9 arms per network and 252 in all, plus `autopilot`. The autopilot's command is `brain/autopilotSub.mjs` `pilot()` called on the same state, target and current the network is handed (inside the tank's compass dropout the two see the same turned world). All are 963-weight GFC networks seated through a tank kind of the round (`h11-<mode>-<channel>`), and `hand`, which is the autopilot through the network, must make every transplant the autopilot.
**Task and ids:** the compass dropout of H4 to H10 (one-sub tanks, 160 s, the dark windows of the tank) read in the DARK tank only (H4, H5, H7 to H10 found the lit gain the same size as the dark, so the lit tank is not flown here: 252 arms of 200 ids would be three hours). **Ids 11000-11199 (200), read once.** Score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi. A pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The statistics (per channel, over the 28 networks):** with G_n the intact gain, T_{n,c} the gain of `n/-c` and S_{n,c} the gain of `n/+c`: NECESSITY N_c = mean_n(G_n - T_{n,c}) / mean_n(G_n), SUFFICIENCY S_c = mean_n(S_{n,c}) / mean_n(G_n). The readings of each transplant against its intact network (BETTER, TIES, WORSE) are counted over the 28.
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown.** Let c* be the channel with the largest N_c. (1) If mean_n(G_n) <= 0.02: `NOT ASSESSED: THE NETWORKS DID NOT GAIN`. (2) Else if N_c* >= 0.5 and S_c* >= 0.5: `THE GAIN IS IN THE <C*> CHANNEL`. (3) Else if N_c* >= 0.5: `THE GAIN NEEDS THE <C*> CHANNEL BUT IT IS NOT ENOUGH ALONE`. (4) Else: `THE GAIN IS SPREAD ACROSS THE CHANNELS`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); every network is 963 finite weights; **`hand/-c` and `hand/+c` equal the autopilot on every id for every channel to 1e-9**; **the four `-c` transplants of a network, applied together, give the autopilot's command (the gate holds this on real flights: the network with all four channels replaced flies as the autopilot)**; the transplants change the flight of at least 20 of the 28 networks on at least one channel; the 28 intact networks are pairwise different.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `LEARNED: <n> OF 28 INTACT NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT BY MORE THAN 0.02; MEAN GAIN <g>`.
2. `NECESSITY (share of the gain lost when the channel is the autopilot's): pitch <x>, yaw <x>, thrust <x>, ballast <x>`.
3. `SUFFICIENCY (share of the gain the autopilot gains from the network's channel alone): pitch <x>, yaw <x>, thrust <x>, ballast <x>`.
4. `TRANSPLANTS READ, OF 28 (better / ties / worse than the intact network): pitch <a>/<b>/<c>, yaw ..., thrust ..., ballast ...`.
5. `THE LARGEST: <c*> N <x> S <x>`.
6. `VERDICT: <as above>`.
7. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, intact <t>, transplants <t> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark mean; each network's N and S per channel.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
The networks gain (mean above 0.02): about 95 percent. The verdict: **THE GAIN IS IN THE YAW CHANNEL about 30 percent** (the score is a heading error and the yaw command steers the heading), **IN THE THRUST CHANNEL about 20** (a faster sub has more rudder authority; the PD law's yaw gain is scheduled by speed), IN PITCH or IN BALLAST about 5 each, **NEEDS ONE CHANNEL BUT NOT ENOUGH about 15**, **SPREAD about 20**, NOT ASSESSED about 5. The `+c` arms give back less than the `-c` arms take (the sum of the four S_c is below 1): 60 percent. The largest N_c is above 0.3: 70 percent. `hand/-c` and `hand/+c` equal the autopilot: certain, by the arithmetic; the check exists so the harness cannot hide a fault in the transplant. No lost sub among the intact networks: 90 percent (H9 lost one of 28 in 400 flights); among the transplants: 60 percent (a mixed pilot is a pilot nobody trained).

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead11.mjs` (the four channels, the transplant pilots and tank kinds, the board, the audit over a subset of arms so the command can run in parallel, the statistics, checks, verdict and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead11Cmd.mjs` (reads the eight weight files and H7's training file; flies the 253 arms in four parallel parts and merges; prints; writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head11.json`) and `headToHead11-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here.

## Round H12, REGISTERED (2026-11-07, before any replicate is trained and before any id is flown): THE WIDER BOX -- DOES A PD LAW WITH A MUCH WIDER SEARCH OF ITS ELEVEN GAINS REACH THE NETWORKS' GAIN?
### 1. Why
H6 searched the autopilot's eleven gains inside [default/8, default x 8] by the networks' own recipe and found +0.0104 (the networks' median +0.0904), `NOT ASSESSED` by its own rule, with every champion on the bound for several gains: kTheta, kBalD and kSpd at 8x in 5 of 5, kQ at 1/8 in 4 of 5. It also searched only the dive score, not the dropout. Both limits are removed here.
> If the eleven gains are searched in [default/64, default x 64], and separately against the dive score UNDER the compass dropout, does a PD autopilot reach the networks' dark gain?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (forty-four), flying layer, the board's `dropout` task:** `autopilot`; `wideA-0..4`, five autopilots tuned for the dive score in the wide box; `wideB-0..4`, five tuned for the dive score UNDER THE COMPASS DROPOUT (`tools/subDropout.mjs` windows around the same autopilot, default windows: two of 16 s in a 60 s dive) in the wide box; `box8-0..4`, H6's five champions (the narrow box, read from `brain/rig-results/head-to-head6-training.jsonl`) flown on these ids for the comparison; and the 28 trained networks of H9 (as in H11) as the reference. Tuned arms are `pilot: tuned` kinds of the H6 shape (circuit `h12-tuned`, 11 weights).
**The search (replicate r = 0..4, group A or B):** H6's recipe unchanged except the box and, for B, the objective: four (1+1)-ES searches of 400 iterations (log-normal factor exp(0.3 z) on 1..4 gains per mutation) from the default gains, each gain bounded in [default/64, default x 64], on TRAIN_SET (A) or on TRAIN_SET with every dive flown through `subDropout.dropout` (B), champion = best HELD_OUT_SET score of the SAME objective, ties to the smaller k; seeds `12000 + 100 g + 10 r + k` (g = 0 for A, 1 for B). 40 searches in all, run here.
**Task and ids:** the dropout of H4 to H10, dark and lit, one-sub tanks, 160 s. **Ids 12000-12199 (200), read once.** A pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before any replicate is trained.** Let m_A and m_B be the mean dark gains of the five `wideA` and the five `wideB` champions and m the larger, M the median of the 28 networks' dark gains (the mean of the 14th and 15th smallest) and q1 the 7th smallest. (1) If fewer than 4 of the 5 champions of the group with the larger mean beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02: `THE WIDE BOX STILL FINDS NOTHING`. (2) Else if m >= M: `THE GAIN IS A TUNING GAP AFTER ALL`. (3) Else if m < q1: `A WIDER PD TUNE DOES NOT REACH IT`. (4) Else `A WIDER PD TUNE REACHES PART OF IT`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every tuned champion is the champion of exactly four searches with eleven finite gains inside its box; the fifteen wide champions are pairwise different in the dark and each differs from the autopilot; the 28 networks are 963 finite weights.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `WIDE BOX: <a> OF 5 OBJECTIVE-A AND <b> OF 5 OBJECTIVE-B CHAMPIONS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02`.
2. `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: WIDE A <mean> (sd, range); WIDE B <mean> (sd, range); NARROW BOX (H6) <mean>; THE 28 NETWORKS median <m> (quartiles <q1> to <q3>, range <lo> to <hi>)`.
3. `WIDENING THE BOX: A minus the narrow box <d>; B minus the narrow box <d>; B minus A <d>`.
4. `GAINS ON A BOUND (champions x gains of 55, at default x 64 or default / 64): A <n>, B <n>`.
5. `LIT TANK: WIDE A <mean>, WIDE B <mean>; THE 28 NETWORKS median <m>`.
6. `VERDICT: <as above>`.
7. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, tuned <t> (<max> in the worst), networks <t> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean; each champion's gains as factors of the defaults, its held-out score and four search scores.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any replicate is trained or any seed is flown
The larger of the two groups has at least 4 of 5 champions above 0.02 in the dark: about 55 percent (the box is 64 times wider than H6's, but H6's champions pinned the bound and gained +0.010). The verdict: **A WIDER PD TUNE DOES NOT REACH IT about 40 percent, THE WIDE BOX STILL FINDS NOTHING about 25, REACHES PART OF IT about 25, THE GAIN IS A TUNING GAP AFTER ALL about 10.** The dropout-objective group (B) has the larger mean: 55 percent. Widening the box moves the mean gain by more than 0.01 over the narrow box in the larger group: 50 percent. At least 20 of the 55 champion-gains of group A sit on a bound: 60 percent. No lost sub among the tuned autopilots: 70 percent (a gain of 64x can unstabilise a loop).

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead12.mjs` (the wide search and the dropout-objective search reusing H6's mutation and champion code, the board with the H6 champions and the 28 networks, readings, checks, verdict and lines), `headToHead12Cmd.mjs` (train with `--workers`, resumable `brain/rig-results/head-to-head12-training.jsonl`, 40 searches; fly; `--one g:r:k`) and `headToHead12-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here.

## Round H11, RESULT (2026-11-08): THE GAIN IS IN THE THRUST CHANNEL -- REPLACE ONLY THE NETWORK'S THRUST COMMAND BY THE AUTOPILOT'S AND THE WHOLE GAIN GOES (102 PERCENT); GIVE THE AUTOPILOT ONLY THE NETWORK'S THRUST COMMAND AND 99 PERCENT OF IT COMES
Ids 11000-11199, 160 s, the dark tank, 262 arms, one flight in three parallel parts (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head11.json`). **A DEVIATION FROM THE REGISTRATION, said first:** the check `HAND TRANSPLANTS ARE THE AUTOPILOT` was registered at a tolerance of 1e-9 and called "certain, by the arithmetic". It FAILED on the real flight (so by the registered rule the first report said NOT WEIGHED): `hand/-c` and `hand/+c` differ from the autopilot by up to 2.19e-9 in a score (7 of 1600 arm-ids above 1e-9, none above 2.2e-9), and `hand` itself, the autopilot written through the network, differs from the autopilot by up to 1.95e-9: the registration itself says the hand weights are the autopilot "to float32", and float32 rounding over 160 s of flight is of that size. The tolerance was restated as 1e-6 (`HAND_TOL` in the library; the gate now holds 2.5e-9 inside and 1e-4 outside) AFTER seeing only the hand arms' differences and before reading any verdict line; a fault in a transplant would show at 1e-3 or more. No id was re-flown: the saved runs were re-reported by `node tools/ship/headToHead11Cmd.mjs --from-json brain/rig-results/head-to-head11.json`, and the report below is that one. All seven checks then pass.
**Bottom lines as printed:** 26 of 28 intact networks beat the autopilot by more than 0.02 (`base-0` +0.0196 and `robust-5` +0.0070 do not), mean gain +0.0817; NECESSITY (share of the gain lost when the channel is the autopilot's) pitch 0.02, yaw -0.02, thrust 1.02, ballast 0.01; SUFFICIENCY (share of the gain the autopilot gains from the network's channel alone) pitch 0.00, yaw -0.01, thrust 0.99, ballast -0.00; the `-c` transplants read against their intact network (better / ties / worse of 28) pitch 2/19/7, yaw 6/22/0, thrust 0/0/28, ballast 3/23/2; the largest is thrust, N 1.02, S 0.99; verdict `THE GAIN IS IN THE THRUST CHANNEL`; lost subs: autopilot 0, intact 4 (all `robust-6`), transplants 262.
**What it says:** all of what the 28 networks gain over the autopilot on the compass dropout is in ONE of their four commands, thrust. Mean gains over the autopilot: intact +0.0817; with the thrust command the autopilot's (and pitch, yaw and ballast still the network's) -0.0020; the autopilot with ONLY the network's thrust command +0.0808; with only the network's pitch, yaw or ballast +0.0003, -0.0011, -0.0003. It holds network by network: the thrust necessity runs from 0.89 to 1.14, no `-thrust` arm gains more than +0.0049 (their range is -0.0150 to +0.0049), all 28 read WORSE than the intact network, and the autopilot given only a network's thrust gains from +0.0115 to +0.1173. The other three channels are noise at this resolution (the largest `+c` gain among them is 0.0108 for any network).
**What that points to, not what was measured:** the registration guessed yaw (the score is a heading error) at 30 percent and thrust at 20. The mechanism that fits is speed: the autopilot's yaw law is scheduled by (2.5/V)^2 and a sub that flies faster has more rudder authority, so a network that commands more thrust than the autopilot's speed law turns to its heading sooner; the score is a heading error and nothing in it prices the speed. That is a hypothesis; this round did not measure the networks' speeds. If it is right, the networks' gain over the autopilot on this task is the gain of going faster than the speed the task asked for, which a constant thrust offset on the PD law would also give (the next round, below, tests exactly that).
**The mixed pilots that were lost:** 262 sub-runs were lost among the transplants, all in two of the sixteen cells: `-thrust` (148 runs in 7 networks: the network's pitch, yaw and ballast commands at the autopilot's speed) and `+ballast` (110 runs in 8 networks: the autopilot's pitch, yaw and thrust under the network's ballast command); the other six cells lost 4 runs in all (`-yaw`, one network). A network's depth command and its speed command are a pair that was trained together, and half of each is not safe alone. This is the registered expectation of 60 percent for no lost transplant failing, and it is the same fact as the thrust finding seen from the other side.
**Expectations (registered before any id was flown):** the networks gain (mean above 0.02), 95 percent: yes (+0.0817); the verdict: IN THE THRUST CHANNEL at 20 percent, the second most likely single outcome after IN THE YAW CHANNEL at 30: it happened; the sum of the four sufficiencies below 1, 60 percent: yes (0.98); the largest necessity above 0.3, 70 percent: yes (1.02); `hand/-c` and `hand/+c` equal the autopilot "certain by the arithmetic": **not at the registered 1e-9, see the deviation above**; no lost sub among the intact networks, 90 percent: **no, 4 runs of `robust-6`** (H9 lost one network too); among the transplants, 60 percent: no (262).
**Not checked, as the gate says:** the lit tank (not flown) and that a transplanted pilot is one the network was trained to be (it is not: the lost subs say so).

## Round H12, RESULT (2026-11-08): THE WIDE BOX STILL FINDS NOTHING -- 0 OF 10 WIDE CHAMPIONS GAIN, ALTHOUGH THEIR DIVE SCORE BEATS EVERY TRAINED NETWORK'S (HELD-OUT -0.244 AGAINST -0.33 TO -0.40)
Ids 12000-12199, 160 s, dark and lit, 44 arms, 40 searches trained here and one flight (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head12-training.jsonl`, `head-to-head12.json`). All seven checks passed. **Bottom lines as printed:** 0 of 5 objective-A and 0 of 5 objective-B champions beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02; dark gain over the autopilot WIDE A +0.0014 (sd 0.0010, range +0.0002 to +0.0025), WIDE B +0.0067 (sd 0.0020, range +0.0041 to +0.0090), narrow box (H6) +0.0027, the 28 networks median +0.0856 (quartiles +0.0508 to +0.0953, range +0.0050 to +0.1063); widening the box: A minus the narrow box -0.0014, B minus it +0.0039, B minus A +0.0053; gains on a bound (of 55 each) A 10, B 13; lit tank WIDE A +0.0107, WIDE B -0.0115, the networks' median +0.0775; verdict `THE WIDE BOX STILL FINDS NOTHING`; lost subs: autopilot 0, tuned 0, networks 2 (2 in the worst).
**What it says:** a PD autopilot whose eleven gains were searched over a box 64 times wide, for the dive score and, separately, for the dive score under the compass dropout, gains nothing on the compass-dropout tank: every one of the ten champions reads TIES with the autopilot in the dark (the best, `wideB-3`, +0.0090 at se 0.0049), against the networks' +0.0856 median. Widening the box from 8x to 64x changed the dark gain by -0.0014 (A) and +0.0039 (B), inside the noise of one champion.
**The tuned autopilots are good at what they were searched for and it does not transfer:** their held-out DIVE score is -0.2441 to -0.2446 (objective A, a plateau all twenty searches reach), against the autopilot's -0.4877 and every trained network's, -0.3275 (`network-Wip`), -0.3589 (`network-Robust`), -0.3958 (`network-RobustNoisy`); H6's narrow-box champions were -0.2863. A PD law with the right gains is a better DIVE controller than any network in this tree, and on the tank it flies like the autopilot. The searches pushed `kBalD` (the depth-to-ballast gain) and `kSpd` (the speed-error-to-thrust gain) to the upper bound, 64x, in all ten champions (that is A's 10 gains on a bound, and 10 of B's 13; B's other three are `kBeta` on the lower bound, which every B champion prints as x0.02 and three sit on exactly), which is what a dive score that rewards holding depth and speed would ask for, and neither is what the compass-dropout score, a heading error, is made of. Together with H11 (the networks' whole gain is their thrust command) this closes the retune question as H6 left it: **the networks' gain on the tank is not a dive skill that a better PD tuning reproduces; the dive objective and the tank dropout reward different things and the networks' gain is on the tank's.**
**Objective B's lit tank is worse than the autopilot (-0.0115)** while its dark tank is slightly better (+0.0067): tuned for the dive under stale heading windows, it traded lit performance for a small dark gain.
**Expectations (registered before any replicate was trained):** the larger group has at least 4 of 5 champions above 0.02, 55 percent: **no, 0 of 5**; the verdict: DOES NOT REACH IT 40, **FINDS NOTHING 25 (it happened)**, PART 25, GAP 10; the dropout-objective group (B) has the larger mean, 55 percent: yes (+0.0067 against +0.0014); widening the box moves the larger group's mean by more than 0.01 over the narrow box, 50 percent: **no (+0.0039)**; at least 20 of A's 55 champion-gains on a bound, 60 percent: **no (10)**; no lost sub among the tuned autopilots, 70 percent: yes.
**Open:** the search is a (1+1)-ES of the networks' own recipe on 40 training dives; the dive plateau at -0.2444 is its own, and a different objective (the tank's own, 160 s with the tank's retargets) was not tried because it is the one the hypothesis of H11 says matters: see the next round.

## Round H13, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any id is flown): THE THRUST HYPOTHESIS -- DO THE NETWORKS FLY FASTER, AND DOES A ONE-NUMBER THRUST CHANGE ON THE AUTOPILOT GIVE THEIR GAIN?
### 1. Why
H11 found that all of the 28 networks' gain over the autopilot on the compass dropout is in their thrust command: replace only that command by the autopilot's and the gain is gone (necessity 1.02), give the autopilot only that command and 99 percent comes (sufficiency 0.99). H12 found that a PD law with its gains searched 64 times wider does not reach the gain although it beats every network on the dive score. The mechanism that fits is speed: the autopilot's yaw law is scheduled by (2.5/V)^2, so a sub that flies faster than the autopilot's speed law has more rudder authority and turns to its heading sooner, and the score (a heading error) does not price the speed. This round tests that mechanism directly, with two measurements the earlier rounds did not make: how fast each pilot flies, and what a single number added to the autopilot's thrust does.
> Do the networks fly faster than the autopilot (the speed through the water against the target speed, every tick)? And does the autopilot with a constant thrust offset, or with a higher speed setpoint, reach their gain?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (thirty-nine), flying layer:** `autopilot`; `thr+0` (the autopilot with a thrust offset of 0: the identity) and `thr+0.1`, `thr+0.2`, `thr+0.3`, `thr+0.4`, `thr+0.6` (the autopilot's thrust command plus the offset, clamped to [-1, 1], pitch, yaw and ballast untouched); `spd*1` (the identity) and `spd*1.25`, `spd*1.5`, `spd*2` (the autopilot flown to the target heading and depth at the target SPEED multiplied by the factor); and the 28 trained networks of H9 (as in H11). `thr+0` and `spd*1` must fly exactly as the autopilot (the round checks it). The simple arms are tank kinds of one weight (`h13-thr`, `h13-spd`, the weight the offset or the factor).
**Task and ids:** the compass dropout of H4 to H12, dark and lit, one-sub tanks, 160 s. **Ids 13000-13199 (200), read once.** Score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi; a pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot. **Per flight the round also records** the mean speed through the water (m/s, every tick), the mean speed EXCESS (speed minus the target speed, every tick), the mean thrust command, and whether the sub was lost.
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown.** Let G_net be the mean dark gain of the 28 networks and g* the largest mean dark gain among the eight simple arms (`thr+0.1` .. `thr+0.6`, `spd*1.25` .. `spd*2`). **Part 1 (the reproduction):** (1) if G_net <= 0.02: `NOT ASSESSED: THE NETWORKS DID NOT GAIN`; (2) else if g* >= 0.8 G_net: `A ONE-NUMBER THRUST CHANGE REPRODUCES THE GAIN`; (3) else if g* >= 0.4 G_net: `A ONE-NUMBER THRUST CHANGE REPRODUCES PART OF THE GAIN`; (4) else `A ONE-NUMBER THRUST CHANGE DOES NOT REPRODUCE THE GAIN`. **Part 2 (the speed):** the per-id difference between the mean over the 28 networks of the mean speed excess and the autopilot's, read by the switchboard's reading (BETTER iff above 2 se): `THE NETWORKS FLY FASTER THAN THE AUTOPILOT` (BETTER), `THE NETWORKS FLY SLOWER THAN THE AUTOPILOT` (WORSE), `THE NETWORKS FLY AT THE AUTOPILOT'S SPEED` (TIES). The verdict line is the two parts joined by `; `.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); every speed and thrust a finite number; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **`thr+0` and `spd*1` equal the autopilot on every id in both conditions to 1e-6** (the tolerance H11 had to restate: the identity arms here are the autopilot through a kind, not through float32 weights, and should be exact, and 1e-6 is stated in advance); **the board's dropout score and this round's own flight agree** (the gate flies one arm on one id both ways); the offset arms fly faster than the autopilot (a larger offset has a larger mean speed excess, `thr+0.1` < `thr+0.6`); the 28 networks are pairwise different.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `LEARNED: <n> OF 28 NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02; MEAN GAIN <g>`.
2. `DARK GAIN OVER THE AUTOPILOT: THRUST OFFSETS <+0.1, +0.2, +0.3, +0.4, +0.6 in order>; SPEED SETPOINTS <x1.25, x1.5, x2>; THE NETWORKS <mean> (range)`.
3. `SPEED EXCESS (m/s, mean over every tick and id): AUTOPILOT <x>; THRUST OFFSETS <five>; SPEED SETPOINTS <three>; THE NETWORKS <mean> (range)`.
4. `THE NETWORKS' SPEED AGAINST THEIR GAIN, ACROSS THE 28: r = <r> (speed excess against dark gain)`.
5. `THE SIMPLE ARM NEAREST THE NETWORKS IN SPEED EXCESS: <arm> (excess <x>, dark gain <g>)`.
6. `LIT TANK: THRUST OFFSETS <five>; SPEED SETPOINTS <three>; THE NETWORKS <mean>`.
7. `VERDICT: <part 1>; <part 2>`.
8. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, simple arms <t> (<max> in the worst), networks <t> (<max> in the worst)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit mean score and mean thrust command; each network's speed excess and dark gain.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
The networks fly faster than the autopilot (part 2 reads FASTER): about 85 percent (the hypothesis; a network's thrust command was above the autopilot's by 0.2 to 0.4 on the near-trim states I looked at). Part 1: **REPRODUCES THE GAIN about 40 percent, REPRODUCES PART OF IT about 35, DOES NOT REPRODUCE IT about 20, NOT ASSESSED about 5.** (If speed is the whole mechanism one number should do it, but a network's thrust varies with its state and a constant offset may overshoot or undershoot; and the sub can be lost at high offsets.) Across the 28 networks the speed excess correlates with the dark gain at r above 0.5: 65 percent. The dark gain rises with the thrust offset from 0.1 to 0.4: 70 percent. The simple arm nearest the networks in speed excess has a dark gain within 0.02 of the networks' mean: 40 percent. The identity arms equal the autopilot: certain, as arithmetic (no float32 weights in them). No lost sub among the simple arms: 70 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead13.mjs` (the two tank kinds, the flight that records the score, the speed excess, the thrust and the loss, the board-free audit over a subset of arms, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead13Cmd.mjs` (reads the eight weight files and H7's training file, flies the 39 arms in parallel parts and merges, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head13.json`) and `headToHead13-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here.

## Round H13, RESULT (2026-11-08): A ONE-NUMBER THRUST CHANGE REPRODUCES THE GAIN; THE NETWORKS FLY FASTER THAN THE AUTOPILOT -- BUT THEY GET ABOUT TWICE THE GAIN PER METRE PER SECOND OF EXTRA SPEED THAT ANY CONSTANT OFFSET DOES
Ids 13000-13199, 160 s, dark and lit, 39 arms, one flight in four parallel parts (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head13.json`). All nine checks passed (the identity arms `thr+0` and `spd*1` fly exactly as the autopilot to the last digit; the gate also holds this round's own flight to `switchboard.dropout`'s score to the last digit). **Bottom lines as printed:** 27 of 28 networks beat the autopilot in the dark by more than 0.02 (`robust-5` +0.0091 does not), mean gain +0.0806 (range +0.0091 to +0.1092); dark gain of the thrust offsets +0.1 +0.0172, +0.2 +0.0321, +0.3 +0.0411, +0.4 +0.0511, +0.6 +0.0705 and of the speed setpoints x1.25 +0.0451, x1.5 +0.0775, x2 +0.1073; speed excess (m/s, every tick) autopilot -0.17, offsets -0.04, +0.08, +0.20, +0.33, +0.56, setpoints +0.31, +0.76, +1.42, the networks +0.24 (-0.14 to +0.47); across the 28 networks the speed excess against the dark gain r = 0.98; the simple arm nearest the networks in speed excess is `thr+0.3` (excess +0.20, dark gain +0.0411); lit tank offsets +0.0216 to +0.0927, setpoints +0.0585, +0.0977, +0.1618, networks +0.0829; verdict `A ONE-NUMBER THRUST CHANGE REPRODUCES THE GAIN; THE NETWORKS FLY FASTER THAN THE AUTOPILOT`; lost subs: autopilot 0, simple arms 0, networks 2 (`robust-6`).
**What it says, part 2 first:** the networks fly faster than the autopilot, on all 200 ids: their mean speed excess minus the autopilot's is +0.4092 m/s (se 0.0072; 200 better, 0 worse). The autopilot flies BELOW the speed the task asks for (mean excess -0.17 m/s; thrust command 0.273) and the networks above it on average (+0.24; thrust 0.414, range 0.277 to 0.504 across the networks; speed 2.474 against the autopilot's 2.063). Across the 28 networks the gain follows the speed almost exactly: r = 0.98, a fit of gain = 0.038 + 0.179 x excess, which at the autopilot's own excess (-0.17) predicts +0.007: **a network that flew at the autopilot's speed would gain nothing.** The one network that does not clear 0.02, `robust-5` (+0.0091), is the one that flies slowest (excess -0.14, below the autopilot's).
**Part 1:** the registered verdict reads REPRODUCES, because the best simple arm, `spd*2`, gains +0.1073, 133 percent of the networks' +0.0806, and `spd*1.5` (+0.0775) and `thr+0.6` (+0.0705) are over the registered 80 percent as well (0.0645). Said straight: **a one-number change reproduces the gain's SIZE, at the price of flying much faster than the networks do.** `spd*1.5` gains the networks' amount at an excess of +0.76 and `spd*2` more at +1.42 m/s, against the networks' +0.24; the arm nearest the networks in speed, `thr+0.3` at +0.20, gains +0.0411, a half. Per metre per second of extra speed over the autopilot (a mean speed 2.063): the networks gain 0.197, the thrust offsets 0.133 falling to 0.097 as the offset grows, the speed setpoints 0.092 falling to 0.067. So the networks are roughly twice as efficient as any constant. The lit tank says the same (offsets +0.0216 to +0.0927; setpoints up to +0.1618 with the networks at +0.0829).
**What the efficiency gap points to, not what was measured:** the networks' thrust command is state-dependent (H11: it is their whole gain, and it cannot be a constant), while the offset adds the same amount at every tick. The score is the heading error over the SETTLED DARK ticks only; the mean speed above is over every tick. If a network commands more thrust exactly while it is turning to a new heading (when rudder authority is what the score is waiting for) and less when it is not, its speed in the scored ticks is higher than its overall mean shows, and a constant offset spends its speed everywhere. That is a hypothesis; this round recorded no speed split by phase.
**Expectations (registered before any id was flown):** the networks fly faster (FASTER), 85 percent: yes (200 of 200 ids); the verdict REPRODUCES THE GAIN 40 percent, PART 35, DOES NOT 20, NOT ASSESSED 5: **the most likely single outcome happened**, with the caveat above; across the 28 networks speed excess against dark gain at r above 0.5, 65 percent: yes (0.98); the dark gain rises with the thrust offset from 0.1 to 0.4, 70 percent: yes (0.0172, 0.0321, 0.0411, 0.0511); **the simple arm nearest the networks in speed excess has a dark gain within 0.02 of the networks' mean, 40 percent: no (+0.0411 against +0.0806)**; the identity arms equal the autopilot: yes; no lost sub among the simple arms, 70 percent: yes.
**Open, the round this asks for:** the speed of each pilot in the SCORED ticks (the settled dark bucket) and in the 12 s after each retarget, against its overall speed; the networks' thrust command as a function of the heading error and of the speed error (a regression on the features the pilot is given); and a scheduled thrust law (the autopilot's thrust plus a term in the heading error) at the networks' mean speed, to see whether it closes the factor of two.

## Round H14, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any id is flown): WHEN DO THE NETWORKS GO FASTER, AND WHAT DRIVES THEIR THRUST?
### 1. Why
H13 found the networks fly 0.41 m/s faster than the autopilot and that a network's gain follows its speed (r = 0.98), but that a constant thrust offset needs three to six times the extra speed to give the same gain: per metre per second of extra speed the networks gain 0.197, a constant offset 0.097 to 0.133, a speed setpoint 0.067 to 0.092. So the networks' thrust is not a constant. The score is the heading error over the SETTLED DARK ticks only, and H13's speeds were averaged over every tick. Three things are open: whether the networks fly faster in the scored ticks than overall; what the networks' thrust, taken as an excess over the autopilot's on the very same state, is a function of; and whether a thrust law that depends on the heading error (the one state variable the score is about) is as efficient as the networks.
> (A) In which part of the flight do the networks go faster? (B) Is a network's thrust excess over the autopilot a linear function of the 14 features it is given, and which feature leads? (C) Does a thrust law scheduled on the heading error give the networks' gain per m/s of extra speed?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (forty-seven), flying layer:** `autopilot`; four identity arms `thr+0`, `spd*1`, `sched*0`, `turn+0` (each must fly exactly as the autopilot); the eight H13 constants `thr+0.1`, `thr+0.2`, `thr+0.3`, `thr+0.4`, `thr+0.6` (the autopilot's thrust plus the offset, clamped to [-1, 1]) and `spd*1.25`, `spd*1.5`, `spd*2` (the autopilot at the target speed times the factor), re-flown on this round's ids; six SCHEDULED arms: `sched*0.5`, `sched*1`, `sched*2`, `sched*4` (the autopilot's thrust plus c times |heading error|/pi, clamped; the heading error is the autopilot's own guidance heading error, in the world the pilot is shown) and `turn+0.3`, `turn+0.6` (plus the offset only while |heading error| is above 0.2 rad); and the 28 trained networks of H9, flown through a recording tank kind (`h14-rec`) that returns the network's own command exactly and, on every tick, adds to a regression the 14 features the network was given and the thrust command less the autopilot's thrust command on the same state (the gate holds that a recorded network flies exactly as the same network unrecorded).
**Phases of a flight** (every tick belongs to one): TURN = the first 12 s after the tank gives the sub a new target (age below 12 s); SCORED = from 12 s after a target, in the dark window (the ticks the score is made of; in the lit tank, every tick from 12 s); OTHER = from 12 s after a target, with the compass valid. Per flight and phase the round records the number of ticks, the mean speed excess over the target speed, the mean thrust command and the mean |heading error|.
**Task and ids:** the compass dropout of H4 to H13, dark and lit, one-sub tanks, 160 s. **Ids 14000-14199 (200), read once.** Score = 1 - (mean SETTLED dark heading error)/pi; a pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot; EXTRA SPEED = a pilot's mean speed excess (every tick, dark) minus the autopilot's; EFFICIENCY = gain / extra speed, defined only for a pilot with an extra speed of at least 0.1 m/s.
**The regression:** for each network and condition, ordinary least squares (ridge of 1e-8 of the mean diagonal, for the collinear features) of the thrust excess on the 14 features of brain/pilotSubPolicy.mjs (feature 0 is the constant), over every tick of every id; R^2 and the coefficients; a feature's weight is |coefficient| x the feature's standard deviation within that network's ticks.
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown; three parts joined by `; `.** **Part A (the phase speed):** per id, the mean over the 28 networks of (speed excess in the SCORED ticks minus speed excess over every tick), minus the autopilot's own such difference, pooled and paired and read by the switchboard's reading: BETTER `THE NETWORKS SPEED UP IN THE SCORED TICKS`, WORSE `THE NETWORKS SLOW DOWN IN THE SCORED TICKS`, TIES `THE NETWORKS FLY THE SAME IN THE SCORED TICKS AS OVERALL`. **Part B (the regression):** R2 = the mean over the networks of R^2 in the dark and F the feature with the largest mean weight: if R2 >= 0.5, `THE NETWORKS' THRUST EXCESS IS A LINEAR FUNCTION OF THE FEATURES (R2 <x>), LED BY <F>`, else `... IS NOT A LINEAR FUNCTION OF THE FEATURES (R2 <x>), LED BY <F>` (R2 to two places, F by its name in FEATURE_NAMES). **Part C (the scheduled law):** let G be the mean dark gain of the 28 networks and E_net = G / (the networks' mean extra speed); the best constant efficiency is the largest efficiency among the eight constants, the best scheduled efficiency the largest among the six scheduled arms with a gain above 0.02: (1) if G <= 0.02: `NOT ASSESSED: THE NETWORKS DID NOT GAIN`; (2) else if the best scheduled efficiency >= 0.8 E_net: `A SCHEDULED THRUST LAW MATCHES THE NETWORKS' EFFICIENCY`; (3) else if it is >= 1.25 x the best constant efficiency: `A SCHEDULED THRUST LAW IS MORE EFFICIENT THAN A CONSTANT, NOT AS EFFICIENT AS THE NETWORKS`; (4) else (or no scheduled arm gains more than 0.02): `A SCHEDULED THRUST LAW IS NO MORE EFFICIENT THAN A CONSTANT`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); every speed, thrust and phase mean a finite number or an empty phase; **in every flight the three phases' ticks add up to the flight's ticks**; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **the four identity arms equal the autopilot on every id in both conditions to 1e-6**; **every network's regression has the same number of samples as its ticks (200 ids x 4800), R^2 in [0, 1] and finite coefficients**; the offsets fly faster (`thr+0.6` above `thr+0.1` above... as H13's check: the largest above the smallest and the autopilot); the 28 networks are pairwise different. The gate also holds that a recorded network flies as the unrecorded one and that the round's flight scores as switchboard.dropout does.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `LEARNED: <n> OF 28 NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02; MEAN GAIN <g>; EXTRA SPEED <x> m/s; EFFICIENCY <e>`.
2. `SPEED EXCESS BY PHASE (m/s, dark; turn / scored / other): AUTOPILOT <a / b / c>; THE NETWORKS <a / b / c>; thr+0.3 <a / b / c>; spd*1.5 <a / b / c>`.
3. `THRUST BY PHASE (command, dark; turn / scored / other): AUTOPILOT <...>; THE NETWORKS <...>`.
4. `THE NETWORKS' PHASE SPEED-UP (scored less overall, networks less autopilot): <d> m/s (<reading>)`.
5. `REGRESSION OF THE NETWORKS' THRUST EXCESS ON THE FEATURES (dark): R2 <mean> (range <lo> to <hi>); THE LEADING FEATURES <F1> <w1>, <F2> <w2>, <F3> <w3>`.
6. `EFFICIENCY (gain per m/s of extra speed): THE NETWORKS <e>; BEST CONSTANT <name> <e>; SCHEDULED <name> <e>, ...`.
7. `DARK GAIN OF THE SCHEDULED ARMS: sched*0.5 .. turn+0.6 <six numbers>; EXTRA SPEED <six numbers>`.
8. `VERDICT: <part A>; <part B>; <part C>`.
9. `LOST SUBS: autopilot <a>, constants <t>, scheduled <t> (<max>), networks <t> (<max>)`.
**REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit score and phase means; each network's R^2 and its three leading features.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
Part A: the networks SPEED UP in the scored ticks relative to their overall mean (BETTER) about 55 percent, SAME about 30, SLOW DOWN about 15. Part B: R2 at or above 0.5, 70 percent; the leading feature `speedErr` (feature 7) 40 percent, `headingErr` (4) 25, `speed` (12) 15, `thrTrim` (1) 10, another 10. Part C: **MATCHES THE NETWORKS' EFFICIENCY about 25 percent, MORE EFFICIENT THAN A CONSTANT BUT NOT AS EFFICIENT AS THE NETWORKS about 35, NO MORE EFFICIENT THAN A CONSTANT about 35, NOT ASSESSED about 5.** The dark gain of `sched*c` rises with c from 0.5 to 2: 60 percent. The identity arms and the recorded networks equal the autopilot and the unrecorded networks: certain by construction (the checks and the gate hold it). No lost sub among the scheduled arms: 70 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead14.mjs` (the scheduled and recording tank kinds, the flight with its three phases, the regression accumulator and solver, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; imports H13's constants and H9's network ids; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead14Cmd.mjs` (as H13's: reads the eight weight files and H7's training file, flies the 47 arms in parallel parts, merges the runs and the regressions, prints, writes `brain/rig-results/head-to-head14.json`) and `headToHead14-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here.

## Round H14, RESULT (2026-11-08): A THRUST LAW THAT DEPENDS ON THE HEADING ERROR MATCHES THE NETWORKS -- ONE OF ITS NUMBERS REACHES THE BEST NETWORK'S GAIN AT THE SAME EXTRA SPEED
Flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead14Cmd.mjs --parts 4` on ids 14000-14199, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; all eleven checks PASS (the four identity arms equal the autopilot on every id; every network has 960000 samples in both conditions). Data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head14.json` (runs, phases and the regression sums, so `--from-json` reports it again). **Verdict as printed: `THE NETWORKS SLOW DOWN IN THE SCORED TICKS; THE NETWORKS' THRUST EXCESS IS A LINEAR FUNCTION OF THE FEATURES (R2 0.73), LED BY speedErr; A SCHEDULED THRUST LAW MATCHES THE NETWORKS' EFFICIENCY`.**
**(A) When do the networks go faster?** Overall the autopilot flies -0.17 m/s below the target speed and the 28 networks +0.41 m/s above the autopilot (26 of 28 beat it by more than 0.02; mean gain +0.0777; efficiency 0.192 gain per m/s). By phase (speed excess, turn / scored / other): autopilot -0.17 / -0.16 / -0.17; networks +0.33 / +0.19 / +0.13, so over the autopilot +0.50 / +0.35 / +0.30. Their thrust command is +0.48 / +0.40 / +0.30 against the autopilot's +0.26 / +0.27 / +0.27. The networks' scored-less-overall speed, less the autopilot's, is -0.053 m/s (se 0.0066; better 46 ids, worse 154) WORSE: **the extra speed is largest in the first 12 s after a new target and falls away through the settled ticks the score is made of, but it is still +0.35 m/s there.**
**(B) What drives the thrust?** A linear fit of each network's thrust excess over the autopilot's on the 14 features has R2 0.73 on average (range 0.31 to 0.83; 19 of 28 at 0.7 or more; only base-0 below 0.5). The leading features by weight are speedErr 0.201, speed 0.113 and beta 0.059; speedErr leads in 24 of the 28 fits. The mean coefficients (dark) are bias -0.341, thrTrim -0.425, speedErr -0.493, speed +0.704, headingErr +0.046, no other above 0.08 in size. The autopilot's own thrust weighs speedErr at +0.5, so a mean excess of -0.49 on it means **the fitted network law puts about no weight on the speed error and a positive one on the measured speed.** That describes the states the flights visited; it is not a statement of what any hidden unit computes.
**(C) Does a scheduled law match?** Efficiency (gain per m/s of extra speed): networks 0.192; best constant thr+0.1 0.122 (the constants run 0.070 to 0.122); scheduled sched*0.5 0.248, sched*1 0.237, sched*2 0.190, sched*4 0.138, turn+0.3 0.178, turn+0.6 0.144. Dark gain of sched*0.5 / 1 / 2 / 4: +0.0477 / +0.0816 / +0.1125 / +0.1110 at extra speed +0.19 / +0.34 / +0.59 / +0.80. **sched*1 gains more than the networks' mean (+0.0816 against +0.0777) at less extra speed (+0.34 against +0.41); sched*2 (+0.1125, +0.59) sits at the best network's point (network-Robust +0.1102, +0.57).** In the lit tank the scheduled gains are larger still (+0.0475, +0.0838, +0.1239, +0.1440). No scheduled arm lost a sub; the networks lost 4 (all robust-6, dark).
**Against the registered expectations.** Part A: SLOW DOWN was the 15 percent outcome, not the 55 (BETTER). Part B: R2 at or above 0.5 (70 percent) held and speedErr (feature 7) led (my 40 percent plurality). Part C: MATCHES was the 25 percent branch, not the 35 (MORE EFFICIENT) or 35 (NO MORE). The dark gain of sched*c rose from c 0.5 to 2 (60 percent held; it flattens at c 4). No lost sub among the scheduled arms (70 percent held). The identity and recorded-network equalities held, as certain.
**What this does NOT say.** It does not say a network is a scheduled law: the two are matched on gain per extra speed, and the fit above is linear while the network is not. It does not say sched*c is the best thrust law (four values of one family were flown). The efficiency ratio is only defined from 0.1 m/s of extra speed and 0.8 of the networks' ratio is a generous bar (four of the six scheduled arms clear 0.8 x 0.192 = 0.154: sched*0.5, 1, 2 and turn+0.3; sched*4 at 0.138 and turn+0.6 at 0.144 do not); the verdict names no margin beyond that. The heading error used is the autopilot's own, in the world the pilot is shown (the held heading in the dark). The task prices speed at nothing: a sub that is 0.4 m/s faster spends more energy and the round does not say what that costs. One id set, one tank.

## Round H15, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any id is flown): DOES THE NETWORKS' ADVANTAGE SURVIVE A PRICE ON SPEED?
### 1. Why
Every round so far scores heading error only. H13 and H14 found the networks gain by flying 0.41 m/s faster, and that a thrust law scheduled on the heading error gains as much per m/s. Speed is not free: the tank's own needs model (physics/mechanics/subNeeds.mjs) charges electrical power = hotel load 200 W + |thrust (N) x forward speed|/0.5 efficiency, which grows about with the cube of the speed. So the speed comparison of H14 may flatter the pilots that fly fastest. The question is the gain per unit of POWER, not per unit of speed. Nothing here changes the score; the price is a second measure.
> (A) Is a network's gain per extra kW higher than a constant thrust change's? (B) Is a scheduled thrust law's gain per extra kW as high as the networks'? (C) What do the networks' extra speed cost, in kW?

### 2. The arms and the measure
**Arms (forty-four), flying layer:** `autopilot`; the identity `thr+0`; H13's eight constants (`thr+0.1`, `0.2`, `0.3`, `0.4`, `0.6`; `spd*1.25`, `1.5`, `2`); H14's six scheduled arms (`sched*0.5`, `sched*1`, `sched*2`, `sched*4`, `turn+0.3`, `turn+0.6`); the 28 networks of H9, flown as built-in gfc networks (no recorder). **Task and ids:** the compass dropout, dark and lit, one-sub tanks, 160 s. **Ids 15000-15199 (200), read once.**
