**Power:** per flight, the mean over every one of the 4800 ticks of `power(thrustN, speed)` from subNeeds.mjs, with thrustN = the model's thrustMax (3000 N) x the commanded thrust (x 0.6 when reversing), the submerged fraction taken as 1 and the speed the snapshot's speed through the water in place of the forward component (the angle between them is small; said plainly). The gate holds this to the needs model's own watts on a state. EXTRA POWER = a pilot's mean power minus the autopilot's, paired by id. **POWER EFFICIENCY** = the pilot's mean dark gain over the autopilot / its extra power in kW, defined only for an extra power of at least 0.1 kW (it is also the break-even price: the score per kW at which the priced gain is zero).
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown; three parts joined by `; `.** Let G = the mean dark gain of the 28 networks and E_net = G / their mean extra power; best constant = the largest power efficiency among the eight constants; best scheduled = the largest among the six scheduled arms with a gain above 0.02. **Part A:** (1) G <= 0.02: `NOT ASSESSED: THE NETWORKS DID NOT GAIN`; (2) E_net not defined (extra power under 0.1 kW): `NOT ASSESSED: THE NETWORKS' EXTRA POWER IS BELOW 0.1 kW`; else by E_net / best constant (no defined constant counts as 0): at least 1.25 `THE NETWORKS ARE MORE POWER-EFFICIENT THAN EVERY CONSTANT`; at most 0.8 `THE NETWORKS ARE LESS POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE BEST CONSTANT`; between `THE NETWORKS ARE ABOUT AS POWER-EFFICIENT AS THE BEST CONSTANT`. **Part B:** by best scheduled / E_net (no scheduled arm gaining more than 0.02 counts as 0): at least 1.25 `A SCHEDULED LAW IS MORE POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE NETWORKS`; at most 0.8 `A SCHEDULED LAW IS LESS POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE NETWORKS`; between `A SCHEDULED LAW IS ABOUT AS POWER-EFFICIENT AS THE NETWORKS`. **Part C:** `THE NETWORKS DRAW <x> kW MORE THAN THE AUTOPILOT (<y> PERCENT)` with x the mean extra power to two places and y the percentage of the autopilot's mean power, to the nearest whole number. (Parts B and C are written even when part A is NOT ASSESSED.)

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdicts are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; the ids are the same in every arm; no score outside (0, 1); every power a finite number of at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **`thr+0` equals the autopilot in score and in power on every id in both conditions to 1e-6**; the largest thrust offset draws more power than the autopilot; the 28 networks are pairwise different. The gate also holds that the flight scores as switchboard.dropout does and that the power formula is the needs model's.

### 4. The bottom lines
1. `LEARNED: <n> OF 28 NETWORKS BEAT THE AUTOPILOT IN THE DARK BY MORE THAN 0.02; MEAN GAIN <g>`. 2. `MEAN POWER (kW, dark): AUTOPILOT <p>; CONSTANTS <eight>; SCHEDULED <six>; THE NETWORKS <mean> (<lo> to <hi>)`. 3. `EXTRA POWER (kW over the autopilot): CONSTANTS <eight>; SCHEDULED <six>; THE NETWORKS <mean>`. 4. `POWER EFFICIENCY (dark gain per extra kW): THE NETWORKS <e>; BEST CONSTANT <name> <e>; SCHEDULED <six, n/a under 0.1 kW>`. 5. `PRICED GAIN (dark gain less the price x extra kW) AT 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20 PER kW: THE NETWORKS <three>; BEST NETWORK <name> <three>; thr+0.1 <three>; sched*1 <three>; sched*2 <three>` (the three prices are reported, arbitrary, not in the verdict). 6. `VERDICT: <A>; <B>; <C>`. 7. `LOST SUBS: ...`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit score, power, extra power and efficiency; the lit-tank power efficiency; per-network rows.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
Part A: MORE POWER-EFFICIENT THAN EVERY CONSTANT 45 percent, ABOUT AS 40, LESS 10, NOT ASSESSED 5. Part B: a scheduled law MORE 30 percent, ABOUT AS 45, LESS 20, NOT ASSESSED 5. Part C: the networks draw between 0.5 and 2 kW more than the autopilot: 65 percent (the autopilot's own mean power is about 4 kW: 60 percent between 3 and 5). Power rises faster than speed, so the speed setpoints (which fly fastest) have the lowest power efficiency of the constants: 70 percent. `thr+0` equals the autopilot: certain.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead15.mjs` (the flight with power, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; imports H13's and H14's arms and H9's network ids; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead15Cmd.mjs` (parallel parts, `--from-json`) and `headToHead15-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here.

## Round H16, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any search is run and before any id is flown): HOW GOOD IS A TUNED FOUR-NUMBER THRUST LAW?
### 1. Why
H14 flew four values of one hand-made law (`sched*c`) and found it as efficient as the networks; `sched*2` reached the best network's gain at the same extra speed. The fit of the networks' thrust (H14, part B) pointed at a law with about no weight on the speed error and a positive weight on the measured speed. Before saying anything about what the networks are, find how far a TUNED law in that family goes. 
> (A) Does a tuned four-number thrust law beat the networks' mean, `sched*2` and the best network? (B) Where do its numbers go? (C) Is it as power-efficient (H15's measure) as the networks?

### 2. The law, the search and the audit
**The family:** thrust = clamp(autopilot's thrust + c x |heading error|/pi + d + k x speedErr + s x V/3) with the autopilot's guidance heading error and speed error and V the speed through the water; the other three commands are the autopilot's. Weights (c, d, k, s); (0, 0, 0, 0) is the autopilot exactly. Search box: c in [0, 8], d in [-0.2, 0.6], k in [-0.6, 1], s in [-1, 1].
**The search:** four (1+1)-ES searches, seeds 16001 to 16004, 150 iterations each, started at the autopilot (0, 0, 0, 0); each iteration changes one to four weights by a Gaussian step of 0.1 x the width of the weight's box (clamped to the box) and keeps the candidate when its mean dark dropout score on the 24 TRAIN ids (16000-16023, 160 s) is at least the parent's. **Champion** = the search whose final law has the best mean dark score on the 40 HELD-OUT ids 16100-16139. No audit id is touched by the search.
**The audit:** ids **16200-16399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s. Arms: `autopilot`, `champion`, `sched*2` (H14's law, for reference) and the 28 networks of H9. H15's flight (the score as the board scores it, plus power). Gain = mean paired dark difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; four parts joined by `; `.** **Part A:** the champion minus the networks' per-id mean score, paired, read by the switchboard (BETTER / WORSE / TIES at 2 se): `THE TUNED LAW BEATS THE NETWORKS' MEAN`, `... IS BEHIND THE NETWORKS' MEAN`, `... TIES THE NETWORKS' MEAN`. **Part B:** the champion minus `sched*2`, paired: `THE TUNED LAW BEATS sched*2` / `IS BEHIND sched*2` / `TIES sched*2`. **Part C:** the champion minus the BEST of the 28 networks on the audit ids (the best is chosen on the same ids, which favours the network: said plainly): `THE TUNED LAW BEATS THE BEST NETWORK` / `IS BEHIND THE BEST NETWORK` / `TIES THE BEST NETWORK`. **Part D (power):** E_law = the champion's dark gain over its extra power, E_net as in H15; if the champion's gain <= 0.02 `THE TUNED LAW DID NOT GAIN`; if its extra power is under 0.1 kW or the networks' is, `POWER EFFICIENCY NOT ASSESSED`; else E_law / E_net at least 1.25 `THE TUNED LAW IS MORE POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE NETWORKS`, at most 0.8 `... LESS ...`, else `... ABOUT AS ...`.

### 3. The checks and the lines
Checks (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); powers finite and at least 200 W; the zero law equals the autopilot (a fifth identity flight, `law0`, in score and power to 1e-6 on every id, both conditions); the four searches' champions' laws lie inside the box; the held-out and train ids are disjoint from the audit's; the champion's held-out score is the largest of the four. Lines: `SEARCH: the four final laws (c d k s) with their train and held-out scores; CHAMPION <law>`; `AUDIT: autopilot / champion / sched*2 / the networks' mean / the best network, each dark and lit mean score, dark gain, mean power`; `PAIRED: champion minus networks' mean, minus sched*2, minus best network (mean, se, reading)`; `POWER: extra power and power efficiency of the champion, of sched*2 and of the networks`; `VERDICT`; `LOST SUBS`.

### 4. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: BEATS the networks' mean 75 percent, TIES 20, BEHIND 5. Part B: BEATS sched*2 45, TIES 45, BEHIND 10. Part C: BEATS the best network 35, TIES 40, BEHIND 25. Part D: MORE POWER-EFFICIENT 25, ABOUT AS 50, LESS 15, DID NOT GAIN/NOT ASSESSED 10. The champion's k ends below 0 (the speed-error weight of the autopilot being removed, as the networks' fit says): 50 percent; its s above 0: 55 percent; its c above 1: 70 percent. At least one of the four searches ends within 0.005 of the best of the four on the held-out ids (the search converges): 60 percent.

### 5. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead16.mjs` (the law kind, the ES search with its box, the champion choice, the audit using H15's flight, the readings, checks, verdict and lines), `headToHead16Cmd.mjs` (`search` mode: four children writing a jsonl, resumable; `fly` mode: parallel parts with `--from-json`), and `headToHead16-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here after H15.

## Round H15, RESULT (2026-11-08): PER KILOWATT THE NETWORKS ARE NO BETTER THAN THE BEST CONSTANT, AND A SCHEDULED LAW IS 1.5 TIMES AS EFFICIENT
Flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead15Cmd.mjs --parts 4` on ids 15000-15199, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; all nine checks PASS (`thr+0` equals the autopilot in score and in power on every id). Data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head15.json`. **Verdict as printed: `THE NETWORKS ARE ABOUT AS POWER-EFFICIENT AS THE BEST CONSTANT; A SCHEDULED LAW IS MORE POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE NETWORKS; THE NETWORKS DRAW 2.99 kW MORE THAN THE AUTOPILOT (79 PERCENT)`.**
**The cost.** The autopilot draws 3.79 kW on average; the 28 networks 6.78 kW (3.96 to 9.03), +2.99 kW, 79 percent more, for a mean dark gain of +0.0714 (26 of 28 above 0.02). The constants draw +0.68 / +1.43 / +2.28 / +3.19 / +5.21 kW (thr+0.1 to +0.6) and +3.38 / +7.88 / +15.97 kW (spd*1.25, 1.5, 2); the scheduled arms +1.11 / +2.44 / +4.93 / +7.44 (sched*0.5 to *4) and +1.55 / +3.49 (turn+0.3, +0.6).
**Gain per extra kW.** Networks 0.024; constants 0.022 (thr+0.2, the best), 0.021, 0.018, 0.016, 0.014 for the other offsets and 0.015, 0.009, 0.007 for the setpoints; scheduled sched*0.5 0.036, sched*1 0.030, sched*2 0.021, sched*4 0.014, turn+0.3 0.025, turn+0.6 0.019. Part A: 0.024 / 0.022 = 1.09, between 0.8 and 1.25: ABOUT AS. Part B: best scheduled 0.036 / 0.024 = 1.5, above 1.25: MORE. **In H14 the networks and the scheduled law matched per m/s of speed; per kW the low-c scheduled law gains half as much again per kW as the networks. My reading, not tested here: it buys its gain with less extra speed in the steady ticks, and the power curve (about the cube of the speed) punishes the constants and the setpoints.** At the networks' own point: `sched*2` draws +4.93 kW for +0.1055; the best network by gain (robust-2) +4.31 kW for +0.1027; so the match H14 saw in speed does not hold in power, `sched*2` costs about 0.6 kW more for +0.003 gain. `sched*1` gains +0.0723 for +2.44 kW, the networks' mean +0.0714 for +2.99 kW. In the lit tank the scheduled arms draw less than in the dark (sched*4 8.92 against 11.23 kW).
**Priced gain** (reported, arbitrary, not in the verdict): at 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20 per kW every arm shown is below zero (the networks -0.0781 / -0.2275 / -0.5265; thr+0.1 -0.0198 / -0.0536 / -0.1211; sched*1 -0.0497 / -0.1718 / -0.4160). **I chose those prices before seeing any power number and they all lie above every arm's break-even price (the largest efficiency is 0.036 per kW): the grid says only that the gain is worth less than 0.05 score per kW, not what it is worth.** The efficiencies above are the break-even prices.
**Against the registered expectations.** Part A: ABOUT AS (my 40 percent branch). Part B: MORE (30 percent). **Part C missed: I put the networks' extra draw between 0.5 and 2 kW at 65 percent and it is 2.99 kW.** The autopilot's own draw (3 to 5 kW, 60 percent) held at 3.79. **"The speed setpoints have the lowest power efficiency of the constants" (70 percent) did not hold as stated:** spd*2 (0.007) and spd*1.5 (0.009) are the two lowest, but spd*1.25 (0.015) sits above thr+0.6 (0.014). `thr+0` equals the autopilot: held.
**What this does NOT say.** It does not say the networks are inefficient: the verdict compares ratios of means, and the individual networks run from 0.019 (network-Actuators, base-3) to 0.058 (robust-5, which barely moves: about +0.17 kW for about +0.010; four networks are above 0.030). It does not say how the price should be set: a score that charges 0.024 per kW would make every pilot here break even with the autopilot. The power is the model's formula applied to the tank's commanded thrust and speed, with the speed through the water in place of the forward speed (said in the registration; the gate holds the formula to the needs model on a state). Two flights of one network (robust-6, dark) lost a sub; no other arm lost one. One id set, one tank, a score that still ignores energy.

## Round H16, RESULT (2026-11-08): A FOUR-NUMBER THRUST LAW, TUNED ON 24 IDS, TIES THE BEST NETWORK AT 2 KW LESS POWER AND BEATS THE NETWORKS' MEAN BY +0.038
Searched by `node tools/ship/headToHead16Cmd.mjs search` (four (1+1)-ES searches, 150 iterations each, train ids 16000-16023, held-out 16100-16139) and flown by `... fly --parts 4` on the audit ids 16200-16399, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; all eleven checks PASS (`law0` equals the autopilot in score and power on every id). Data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head16-search.jsonl`, `brain/rig-results/head-to-head16.json`. **Verdict as printed: `THE TUNED LAW BEATS THE NETWORKS' MEAN; THE TUNED LAW TIES sched*2; THE TUNED LAW TIES THE BEST NETWORK; THE TUNED LAW IS MORE POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE NETWORKS`.**
**The searches (law = c, d, k, s; train / held-out dark score; accepted of 150).** 16001: [1.232, -0.061, -0.600, -0.159], 0.9094 / 0.8868, 12. 16002: [1.822, -0.200, +0.074, +0.554], 0.9033 / 0.8700, 18. 16003: [2.819, +0.297, +0.808, +0.363], 0.9028 / 0.8702, 11. 16004: [1.528, +0.269, -0.600, -0.596], 0.9003 / 0.8868, 25. **The champion is 16001 by 0.00004 of held-out score over 16004, whose law differs in every weight but c: the search converges on a score, not on a law.** All four c lie between 1.2 and 2.8; k sits at the lower edge of its box (-0.6) in two of the four, so the box limits that weight (the autopilot's own speed-error gain is +0.5, so the champion's net weight on the speed error is about -0.1).
**The audit (dark score; gain over the autopilot; mean power).** Autopilot 0.7495, 3.79 kW. **Champion 0.8723, +0.1228, 6.86 kW.** `sched*2` 0.8704, +0.1209, 8.90 kW. The 28 networks' mean 0.8344, +0.0849, 6.87 kW. Best network (by dark gain, chosen on these ids) network-Robust 0.8690, +0.1195, 8.78 kW. Paired in the dark: champion minus the networks' mean +0.0379 (se 0.0042; better 162 ids, worse 38) BETTER; minus `sched*2` +0.0019 (se 0.0041; 110 / 90) TIES; minus the best network +0.0033 (se 0.0042; 114 / 86) TIES.
**Power.** The champion draws +3.08 kW over the autopilot, the networks' mean +3.09 kW, so it takes the same extra power as the average network for +0.038 more score; efficiency (gain per extra kW) champion 0.040, networks 0.028 (ratio 1.43, above 1.25: MORE), `sched*2` 0.024. It matches the best network's score on 1.9 kW less draw (6.86 against 8.78 kW).
**The lit tank (reported, not in the verdict; the search saw the dark task only).** The champion's lit gain is +0.1004 at +1.41 kW (efficiency 0.071), but paired in the lit tank it is +0.0193 over the networks' mean (BETTER), **-0.0233 behind `sched*2` and -0.0233 behind the best network (both WORSE, 154 and 152 ids of 200)**: a law tuned on the dark task gives up more of the lit tank than the hand-made law and the best network do. 3 flights of networks lost a sub; no other arm lost one.
**Against the registered expectations.** Part A: BEATS the networks' mean (my 75 percent). Part B: TIES `sched*2` (my 45 percent branch; BEATS was equally likely at 45). Part C: TIES the best network (40 percent). Part D: MORE POWER-EFFICIENT (my 25 percent branch). c above 1 (70 percent): held, in all four searches. k below 0 (50 percent): held in the champion (and one other search). **s above 0 (55 percent): did not hold; the champion's s is -0.159 and the four searches split two and two.** At least one other search within 0.005 of the best on held-out (60 percent): held (16004, 0.00004); the other two are 0.017 behind.
**What this does NOT say.** It does not say the networks are such a law, nor that this law is the best of its family: the search is four runs of 150 iterations, the box clips k, and its two best runs found different laws of the same score. It does not show the champion beats the networks in general: it was tuned on a dark task like the one it is audited on, it is behind two arms in the lit tank, and the "best network" is picked on the audit ids themselves, which favours the network (it still ties). It does not price speed in the score: the power result is the H15 measure (power per gain) and the champion draws +3.08 kW for +0.1228. The four searches agree on c above 1; only two of them (the two best) push k to the box edge, a small net speed-error weight (the other two end at net weights of about 0.6 and 1.3). That is a pattern across four searches in one tank, not a mechanism. The networks' mean gain on these ids (+0.0849) against H14's (+0.0777) and H15's (+0.0714) shows what a change of id set does to a mean gain: about 0.01.

## Round H17, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any search is run and before any id is flown): DOES A WIDER BOX AND A LIT-TANK OBJECTIVE FIX WHAT H16'S LAW GAVE UP?
### 1. Why
H16's champion tied the best network in the dark on 1.9 kW less power, but (a) its `k` weight sat on the lower edge of the search box in the two best searches, so the box may have limited it, and (b) it was tuned on the dark task only and ended behind `sched*2` and the best network in the lit tank (-0.0233, 154 and 152 ids of 200). Two changes are open and are run apart so they can be told apart: a WIDER BOX (a), and an objective that also scores the LIT tank (b).
> (A) Does the wider box, with the dark objective, beat H16's law in the dark? (B) What does adding the lit tank to the objective do to the dark score and to the lit score? (C) Does the lit-tuned law match the best network in both tanks? (D) Do its weights still sit on the box edge? (E) Is it still more power-efficient than the networks?

### 2. The searches, the arms and the audit
**The family** is H16's (thrust = clamp(autopilot's thrust + c |heading error|/pi + d + k speedErr + s V/3); the same tank kind `h16-law`). **The wider box:** c in [0, 16], d in [-0.4, 1.0], k in [-1.5, 2], s in [-2, 2] (H16's was [0, 8], [-0.2, 0.6], [-0.6, 1], [-1, 1]).
**Two groups of searches, six seeds each (seeds 17001-17006 group A, 17011-17016 group B), 300 iterations each**, (1+1)-ES from the autopilot's law (0, 0, 0, 0) as in H16: one to four weights changed by a Gaussian step of 0.1 x the width of the (new) box, clamped to it, the candidate kept when its objective is at least the parent's. **Group A's objective** is the mean dark dropout score on the 24 TRAIN ids 17000-17023 (160 s); **group B's objective** is the mean of the dark mean score and the lit mean score on the same ids (one flight each, so twice the cost). **Champions:** each group's champion is its search with the best held-out value of that group's own objective on the 40 HELD-OUT ids 17100-17139 (A: the dark mean; B: the mean of the dark and lit means); the first of a tie. No audit id is touched by a search.
**The audit:** ids **17200-17399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, H15's flight (the score as the board scores it, plus power). Arms (33): `autopilot`; `law0` (the zero law, must equal the autopilot); `champA`; `champB`; `h16` (H16's champion, fixed here as the law [1.2322693581401742, -0.061415416013719054, -0.6, -0.15850584538981266]); `sched*2`; the 28 networks of H9. Gain = mean paired difference over the autopilot. The BEST NETWORK is the network with the largest mean of its dark and lit gains (so that neither tank is favoured; chosen on the audit ids, which still favours the network).
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; five parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER / WORSE / TIES), paired by id.**
**Part A (the box):** champA minus `h16`, dark: BETTER `THE WIDER BOX BEATS H16'S LAW IN THE DARK`; WORSE `... IS BEHIND H16'S LAW IN THE DARK`; TIES `... TIES H16'S LAW IN THE DARK`.
**Part B (the objective):** champB minus champA in the dark and in the lit tank: `THE LIT OBJECTIVE <dark phrase> AND <lit phrase>` with the dark phrase BETTER `RAISES THE DARK SCORE`, WORSE `COSTS DARK SCORE`, TIES `LEAVES THE DARK SCORE UNCHANGED` and the lit phrase BETTER `RAISES THE LIT SCORE`, WORSE `COSTS LIT SCORE`, TIES `LEAVES THE LIT SCORE UNCHANGED`.
**Part C (the networks):** champB minus the best network in the dark and in the lit tank: `THE LIT-TUNED LAW <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE BEST NETWORK IN THE DARK AND <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> IT IN THE LIT TANK`.
**Part D (the edge):** the number n of champB's four weights within 1 percent of the box width of an edge of the wider box: `THE LIT-TUNED LAW HAS <n> OF 4 WEIGHTS AT THE BOX EDGE`.
**Part E (power):** as H16's part D for champB (dark gain and extra power against the networks'): gain <= 0.02 `THE LIT-TUNED LAW DID NOT GAIN`; an extra power under 0.1 kW for it or for the networks `POWER EFFICIENCY NOT ASSESSED`; else its efficiency over the networks' at least 1.25 `... IS MORE POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE NETWORKS`, at most 0.8 `... LESS ...`, else `... ABOUT AS ...`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power finite and at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **`law0` equals the autopilot in score and power to 1e-6, both conditions**; all twelve searches' laws and both champions lie inside the wider box; the train, held-out and audit ids are disjoint; **each champion has the largest held-out value in its own group**; **`h16` flown here is the law written above (checked against the constant)**; the 28 networks are pairwise different.

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH A`, `SEARCH B`: the six laws of the group with train and held-out values, the accepted counts and the champion; `SPREAD`: the largest minus the smallest held-out value within each group; `AUDIT`: dark and lit mean score, dark gain and mean power of the autopilot, `h16`, `champA`, `champB`, `sched*2`, the networks' mean and the best network (named); `PAIRED`: the five pairings of the verdict with mean, se and reading (dark and lit where the verdict reads both); `EDGE`: each champion's weights and which sit at an edge; `POWER`: extra power and efficiency of both champions, `h16`, `sched*2` and the networks; `VERDICT`; `LOST SUBS`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the lit-tank power; every arm's dark and lit gain; champB minus `sched*2` in the lit tank.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: BEATS 25 percent, TIES 60, BEHIND 15. Part B: dark phrase COSTS 35, UNCHANGED 55, RAISES 10; lit phrase RAISES 70, UNCHANGED 25, COSTS 5. Part C: dark: TIES 45, BEHIND 35, BEATS 20; lit: TIES 40, BEHIND 35, BEATS 25. Part D: at least one of champB's weights at the edge of the wider box: 40 percent (champA's likewise: 40). Part E: MORE POWER-EFFICIENT 35, ABOUT AS 45, LESS 15, DID NOT GAIN / NOT ASSESSED 5. Champions' `k` negative: 60 percent for each. At least four of the six searches of a group end within 0.005 of the group's best held-out value: 30 percent for each group (the landscape is flat: H16's best two differed in every weight but c). The wide box lowers a searched law's lit-tank gap to `sched*2` only through the objective, not the box: I expect champA's lit-tank gap to `sched*2` within 0.01 of H16's (-0.0233): 60 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead17.mjs` (the wider box, the search with its two objectives, the champion per group, the audit using H15's flight and H16's law kind, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; imports H15's flight and H16's `KIND` and `lawArm`; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead17Cmd.mjs` (`search` mode: twelve children writing a jsonl, resumable; `fly` mode: parallel parts with `--from-json`) and `headToHead17-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here after the build.

## Round H17, RESULT (2026-11-08): THE WIDER BOX FINDS FULL THROTTLE -- BEST LIT SCORE OF ANY ARM, THE SAME DARK SCORE, SIX TIMES THE POWER; THE LIT OBJECTIVE CHANGES NOTHING
Searched by `node tools/ship/headToHead17Cmd.mjs search --workers 4` (twelve (1+1)-ES searches of 300 iterations: group A on the dark score, group B on the mean of the dark and lit scores, in the wider box; train ids 17000-17023, held-out 17100-17139) and flown by `... fly --parts 4` on the audit ids 17200-17399, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; all twelve checks PASS. Data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head17-search.jsonl`, `brain/rig-results/head-to-head17.json`. **Verdict as printed: `THE WIDER BOX TIES H16'S LAW IN THE DARK; THE LIT OBJECTIVE LEAVES THE DARK SCORE UNCHANGED AND LEAVES THE LIT SCORE UNCHANGED; THE LIT-TUNED LAW TIES THE BEST NETWORK IN THE DARK AND BEATS IT IN THE LIT TANK; THE LIT-TUNED LAW HAS 1 OF 4 WEIGHTS AT THE BOX EDGE; THE LIT-TUNED LAW IS LESS POWER-EFFICIENT THAN THE NETWORKS`.**
**What the wider box found.** Both champions (champA seed 17005 [c 4.803, d -0.031, k -0.721, s +0.159]; champB seed 17015 [1.798, -0.400, -0.518, +0.798]) fly at a mean thrust command of 0.97 and 0.98 and a speed of 3.84 and 3.85 m/s, against the autopilot's 0.27 and 2.07 (H16's law 0.41 and 2.34; `sched*2` 0.50 and 2.67; the best network, base-3, 0.51 and 2.73). **The laws sit on a positive feedback: with the autopilot's own speed-error weight of +0.5 and a champion's k of about -0.5 to -0.7 the net weight on the speed error is near zero or below, and s x V/3 adds thrust as the speed rises, so the speed climbs to the thrust clamp.** The wider box did not make a better dark law; it made room for full throttle.
**The audit (dark / lit mean score; dark / lit gain; dark mean power).** Autopilot 0.7635 / 0.7763, 3.79 kW. h16 0.8790 / 0.8734, +0.1155 / +0.0971, 6.97 kW. **champA 0.8734 / 0.9481, +0.1099 / +0.1717, 22.60 kW; champB 0.8734 / 0.9472, +0.1099 / +0.1709, 22.87 kW.** `sched*2` 0.8755 / 0.8958, +0.1120 / +0.1194, 8.94 kW. Networks' mean 0.8389 / 0.8559, +0.0753 / +0.0796, 6.93 kW; best network (by the mean of its two gains) base-3 0.8701 / 0.8999, +0.1066 / +0.1235, 9.30 kW. **No arm gains more than about +0.116 in the dark, whatever it does (the networks' best, `sched*2`, h16's law and the full-throttle laws all lie between +0.107 and +0.116); in the lit tank full throttle gains +0.171, well above any other arm.**
**Paired.** champA minus h16, dark: -0.0055 (se 0.0055; 79 ids better, 121 worse) TIES. champB minus champA: dark -0.0001 (se 0.0022) TIES, lit -0.0009 (se 0.0010) TIES. champB minus the best network (base-3): dark +0.0032 (se 0.0049) TIES, lit +0.0473 (se 0.0042; 166 better, 34 worse) BEATS. champB minus `sched*2`, lit: +0.0514 (se 0.0048) BEATS. **Both groups reached the same place: group A's dark-only objective already finds full throttle, which is also best in the lit tank, so adding the lit tank to the objective changed neither score.**
**Power.** champB draws +19.09 kW over the autopilot, champA +18.81 kW, against h16 +3.19, `sched*2` +5.15 and the networks' mean +3.14. Power efficiency (dark gain per extra kW): champB 0.006, champA 0.006, h16 0.036, `sched*2` 0.022, the networks 0.024: LESS than the networks (ratio 0.25).
**The searches.** Group A held-out values 0.8602 to 0.8773 (spread 0.0171), group B 0.8921 to 0.9087 (spread 0.0166). In group B five of six searches end within 0.0020 of the best (0.9067 to 0.9087); in group A only seed 17004 is within 0.005 of the best (0.8752 against 0.8773). A's seed 17002 ended at [0, 0.152, 0.435, 2.0]: s on the box edge (2.0), c at 0. **The champion edge count: champB has d at its lower edge (-0.400): 1 of 4. champA has none.** (c = 0 is also an edge of its box, as the gate found: a law with c at 0 counts it.) 6 flights of one network lost a sub; no other arm lost one.
**Against the registered expectations.** Part A: TIES (60 percent). Part B: dark UNCHANGED (55 percent); **lit UNCHANGED, which was my 25 percent branch, not RAISES (70 percent): the lit objective did nothing**. Part C: dark TIES (45 percent); lit BEATS (25 percent branch). Part D: champB at least one weight at the edge (40 percent): held; champA's (40 percent): did not. Part E: LESS POWER-EFFICIENT (my 15 percent branch). The champions' k negative (60 percent each): held. **At least four of six searches within 0.005 of the group's best: held for group B (five), did not for group A (two).** **The expectation that champA's lit gap to `sched*2` stays within 0.01 of H16's -0.0233: did not hold, champA is +0.05 ahead of it in the lit tank.**
**What this does NOT say.** It does not say full throttle is a good pilot: the round scores heading error only, and the champions draw six times the power of the autopilot for a dark gain that others reach on a quarter of it. It does not say the lit objective is useless: group A's search already reached a law that is best in the lit tank, so the objective was not tested where it would have to choose. It does not say the wider box is the cause of the full-throttle law: that law needs both a negative k and a positive s, which the box [-0.6, 1] x [-1, 1] of H16 excludes only in part (H16's champion had k at its limit of -0.6 and s negative); a run in H16's box with 300 iterations was not made. The dark plateau (about +0.11 to +0.116 for every arm class) is an observation across arm classes in one tank, not a bound. The best network is picked on these ids, which favours the network (it ties in the dark). One id set, one tank, a score that ignores energy.

## Round H18, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any search is run and before any id is flown): WHAT DOES A TUNED THRUST LAW DO WHEN POWER HAS A PRICE?
### 1. Why
H17's searches ended at full throttle (mean thrust 0.97, 22.6 to 22.9 kW against the autopilot's 3.79) because nothing in their objective cost power; they reached the dark plateau that every arm class reaches (+0.107 to +0.116) and the best lit gain of any arm (+0.171) at six times the power. H15's break-even prices (the gain per extra kW: the networks 0.024, h16's law 0.036, `sched*0.5` 0.036, the constants 0.007 to 0.022) say what a kW is worth to the pilots we have. A price in that band, put INTO the objective, asks whether the same family of laws finds something the networks and the hand-made laws do not.
> (A) At each of three prices, does the priced-law champion beat the best other pilot (or the autopilot) at that price? (B) Does it still fly at full throttle? (C) Does its power fall as the price rises?

### 2. The searches, the arms and the audit
**The family and the box** are H17's (thrust = clamp(autopilot's thrust + c |heading error|/pi + d + k speedErr + s V/3), the tank kind `h16-law`; the wider box c in [0, 16], d in [-0.4, 1.0], k in [-1.5, 2], s in [-2, 2]).
**The priced objective** of a law on a set of ids: PRICED = (the mean dark score + the mean lit score)/2 - LAMBDA x (the mean dark power + the mean lit power)/2 in kW (the power is H15's, the mean over every tick of a flight, the absolute power, not the extra: the autopilot's share is a constant). **Three prices, four seeds each, 300 iterations**, (1+1)-ES from the autopilot's law (0, 0, 0, 0) as in H17 (one to four weights changed by a Gaussian step of 0.1 x the box width, clamped, kept when the objective is at least the parent's): LAMBDA 0.01 (seeds 18001-18004), 0.02 (18011-18014), 0.04 (18021-18024) score per kW. Train ids 18000-18023 (24), held-out 18100-18139 (40), 160 s. **Champions:** for each price the search with the best held-out PRICED value at that price (the first of a tie). No audit id is touched by a search.
**The audit:** ids **18200-18399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, H15's flight. Arms (35): `autopilot`; `law0` (the zero law, must equal the autopilot); `c01`, `c02`, `c04` (the champions of the three prices); `h16` (H16's champion, the fixed law [1.2322693581401742, -0.061415416013719054, -0.6, -0.15850584538981266]); `sched*2`; the 28 networks of H9. For an arm and a price L the per-id PRICED SCORE is (dark score + lit score)/2 - L x (dark kW + lit kW)/2 and the PRICED GAIN its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; five parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER / WORSE / TIES), paired by id on the per-id priced score at the price of the champion compared.**
**Parts A1, A2, A3 (one per price 0.01, 0.02, 0.04):** the champion of that price against the BEST OTHER PILOT AT THAT PRICE, the arm with the largest priced gain at that price among `autopilot`, `h16`, `sched*2`, the 28 networks and the other two champions (law0 excluded; chosen on the audit ids, which favours it): `AT <L> PER kW THE PRICED LAW <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE BEST OTHER PILOT (<name>)`.
**Part B (full throttle):** the prices at which the champion's mean dark thrust command is 0.9 or more: `FULL THROTTLE AT: <the prices separated by "/", or NONE>`.
**Part C (monotone power):** the three champions' mean dark power in kW p01, p02, p04: if p01 > p02 > p04 `POWER FALLS WITH THE PRICE (<p01> / <p02> / <p04> kW)` else `POWER DOES NOT FALL WITH THE PRICE (<p01> / <p02> / <p04> kW)` (two places).

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power finite and at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **`law0` equals the autopilot in score and power to 1e-6, both conditions**; all twelve searches' laws and the three champions lie inside the wider box; the train, held-out and audit ids are disjoint; **each champion has the largest held-out priced value among the four searches of its price**; **`h16` flies the registered law**; the 28 networks are pairwise different.

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH <L>` for each price: the four laws with train and held-out priced values and accepted counts and the champion; `SPREAD`: largest minus smallest held-out priced value per price; `AUDIT`: dark and lit mean score, mean power, mean thrust (dark) of the autopilot, `h16`, the three champions, `sched*2`, the networks' mean and the best network at 0.02 (named); `PRICED GAIN AT 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.04`: for the same arms; `BEST OTHER PILOT`: the name at each price; `PAIRED`: the three comparisons with mean, se and reading; `EDGE`: each champion's weights and the ones at an edge (within 1 percent of the box width); `POWER`: mean power and extra power of the three champions; `VERDICT`; `LOST SUBS`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** each champion's dark and lit gains; the networks' priced gains (mean and range); the power efficiency (gain per extra kW) of each champion.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
At 0.01 per kW: BEATS the best other pilot 35 percent, TIES 45, BEHIND 20. At 0.02: BEATS 25, TIES 50, BEHIND 25. At 0.04: BEATS 10, TIES 60, BEHIND 30 (at this price I expect the autopilot, or a pilot close to it, to be the best other pilot, since every pilot of H15 had a gain per kW below 0.04, and the champion to sit near it). Full throttle: at 0.01 30 percent, at 0.02 10, at 0.04 3. Power falls with the price: 75 percent. The 0.04 champion draws within 1 kW of the autopilot's 3.79: 45 percent. The 0.02 champion has c above 1: 60 percent. At least three of the four searches of a price end within 0.005 of that price's best held-out priced value: 40 percent for each price.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead18.mjs` (the priced objective, the search, the champion per price, the audit using H15's flight and H16's law kind, the priced readings, checks, verdict and lines; imports H17's box, edge count and law constant; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead18Cmd.mjs` (`search` mode: twelve children writing a jsonl, resumable; `fly` mode: parallel parts with `--from-json`) and `headToHead18-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). Run here after the build.

## Round H18, RESULT (2026-11-08): WITH A PRICE ON POWER THE SEARCH FINDS A LAW THAT GAINS +0.106 AT THE AUTOPILOT'S OWN SPEED ON 1.2 KW EXTRA -- ABOUT 3.4 TIMES THE NETWORKS' EFFICIENCY
Searched by `node tools/ship/headToHead18Cmd.mjs search --workers 4` (twelve (1+1)-ES searches of 300 iterations in H17's wider box, three prices, four seeds each; train ids 18000-18023, held-out 18100-18139) and flown by `... fly --parts 4` on the audit ids 18200-18399, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; all twelve checks PASS. Data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head18-search.jsonl`, `brain/rig-results/head-to-head18.json`. **Verdict as printed: `AT 0.01 PER kW THE PRICED LAW IS BEHIND THE BEST OTHER PILOT (c02); AT 0.02 PER kW THE PRICED LAW BEATS THE BEST OTHER PILOT (h16); AT 0.04 PER kW THE PRICED LAW TIES THE BEST OTHER PILOT (c02); FULL THROTTLE AT: NONE; POWER FALLS WITH THE PRICE (8.47 / 4.89 / 2.90 kW)`.**
**The champions (c d k s).** c01 (0.01 per kW, seed 18001) [1.558, 0.286, -0.384, -0.426]; c02 (0.02, seed 18011) [2.204, 0.876, -0.643, -2.000]; c04 (0.04, seed 18023) [2.642, 0.150, -0.027, -2.000]. c02's and c04's `s` lie on the lower edge of the box (-2). Held-out priced values and spreads: 0.01: 0.7935 to 0.8100 (spread 0.0166); 0.02: 0.7577 to 0.7819 (0.0242); 0.04: 0.6821 to 0.6986 (0.0164); at each price three of the four searches end within 0.005 of the best.
**The audit (dark / lit mean score; dark power; dark mean thrust).** Autopilot 0.7607 / 0.7745, 3.73 kW, 0.27. h16 0.8792 / 0.8733, 6.46 kW, 0.38. c01 0.8780 / 0.8956, 8.47 kW, 0.49. **c02 0.8664 / 0.8597, 4.89 kW, 0.30. c04 0.8051 / 0.7976, 2.90 kW, 0.19.** `sched*2` 0.8790 / 0.8914, 8.55 kW. Networks' mean 0.8413 / 0.8498, 6.70 kW; the best network at 0.02 is robust-8 (0.8593 / 0.8672, 6.93 kW).
**Gains over the autopilot (dark / lit), extra power, efficiency (dark gain per extra kW).** c01 +0.1174 / +0.1211, +4.74 kW, 0.025. **c02 +0.1058 / +0.0852, +1.16 kW, 0.091 (lit extra power +0.02 kW).** c04 +0.0445 / +0.0231, -0.83 kW (below the autopilot's), efficiency n/a. h16 +0.1186 / +0.0987, +2.73 kW, 0.044. `sched*2` +0.1184 / +0.1169, +4.82 kW, 0.025. **c02 flies at the autopilot's own mean speed (2.06 against 2.05 m/s; 1.90 against 2.07 in the lit tank) with a mean thrust of 0.30 against 0.27**, c04 at 1.71 m/s with thrust 0.19, c01 at 2.63.
**Priced gain at 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.04 per kW** (the mean of the two tanks' gains less the price x the mean of the two tanks' extra power). c01 +0.0787 / +0.0383 / -0.0427. **c02 +0.0896 / +0.0837 / +0.0720. c04 +0.0450 / +0.0562 / +0.0787.** h16 +0.0892 / +0.0697 / +0.0308. `sched*2` +0.0765 / +0.0354 / -0.0468. Networks' mean +0.0520 / +0.0260 / -0.0260 (range +0.0042 to +0.0711, +0.0027 to +0.0395, -0.0635 to +0.0052). Best other pilot at each price: c02 (+0.0896), h16 (+0.0697), c02 (+0.0720).
**Paired (champion minus the best other pilot, per-id priced score).** At 0.01: -0.0108 (se 0.0051; 82 ids better, 118 worse) WORSE. At 0.02: +0.0140 (se 0.0032; 137 / 63) BETTER. At 0.04: +0.0067 (se 0.0048; 110 / 90) TIES.
**What it says.** (1) A price of 0.01 per kW is too low to stop a search from buying gain with power: c01 draws 8.47 kW and its priced gain is below h16's and c02's; the 0.02 champion is better than the 0.01 champion at 0.01 on these ids. (2) At 0.02 and 0.04 the search gives up power and keeps most of the gain: **c02 keeps +0.106 of the dark gain (h16's +0.119, the networks' best about +0.11) on 1.16 kW, about 40 percent of the extra power of h16's law (+2.73 kW) and of the networks' mean (+2.96 kW)** (the networks' mean here: dark gain +0.0807 on +2.96 kW, efficiency 0.027; the best network's dark gain +0.1115). (3) **c02 does it at the autopilot's mean speed.** H13 and H14 found a network's gain follows its extra speed (r = 0.98) and that a constant extra speed buys gain; c02 shows the same dark gain arrives without extra mean speed (and in the lit tank with no extra power at all: +0.0852 for +0.02 kW): so the extra speed was one way to the gain, not the only one. The law's form (a large c, a positive offset d of +0.876 and a strong negative weight s of -2 on the speed, about -0.67 per m/s at 2 m/s, with a net weight near -0.14 on the speed error) is thrust that is high when the heading error is large and low in straight flight; I read that as thrust scheduled on the heading error at equal mean speed, which H14's `sched*c` pointed at; this reading was not tested here (no per-phase speeds or thrusts were recorded in H18).
**Against the registered expectations.** At 0.01: BEHIND was my 20 percent branch (BEATS 35, TIES 45). At 0.02: BEATS was my 25 percent branch. At 0.04: TIES (60 percent) held, **but not for the reason I gave: I expected the autopilot or a pilot near it to be the best other pilot, because every pilot of H15 had a gain per kW below 0.04; the best other pilot is c02, whose efficiency is 0.091**. Full throttle: NONE (at 0.01 a 30 percent chance of some: did not happen). Power falls with the price: held (75 percent). The 0.04 champion draws within 1 kW of the autopilot's power: held (-0.83 kW). The 0.02 champion has c above 1: held (2.20). At least three of four searches within 0.005 of the best at each price: held at all three (40 percent each).
**What this does NOT say.** It does not say the laws are better pilots: the score is heading error, the price is mine (0.01, 0.02, 0.04 per kW, chosen from the H15 efficiencies), and the "best other pilot" is chosen on the audit ids, which favours it (c02 is chosen as the best other at two prices, so at 0.04 c04 competes with a law found for 0.02). It does not say what c02 does state by state: the thrust and speed by phase were not recorded. `s` sits on the box edge for c02 and c04 (three of the eight searches at the two higher prices ended at s = -2, both champions among them), so the box limits that weight, as `k` was limited in H16. The dark gain here is +0.1058 for c02 against a dark plateau of +0.107 to +0.119 for the arm classes of H17; c02 sits at its lower end on about 40 percent of the power, which is the result, not a break of the plateau. 1 network flight lost a sub; no other arm lost one. One id set, one tank.

## Round H19, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any id is flown): WHERE DOES THE PRICED LAW SPEND ITS THRUST, AND IS IT WHAT THE NETWORKS DO?
### 1. Why
H18's 0.02-per-kW champion `c02` gains +0.106 (dark) on +1.16 kW at the autopilot's own mean speed (2.06 against 2.05 m/s). H13 and H14 found a network's gain follows its extra speed; `c02` does not need it. My reading of `c02`'s law (c +2.20, d +0.876, k -0.643, s -2.0: a large heading-error term and a strong negative weight on the speed) is thrust that is high when the heading error is large and low in straight flight; H18 recorded no per-phase or per-error thrust, so that reading is untested, and so is whether the networks do the same. This round records it.
> (A) Does `c02` fly at the autopilot's speed in the flights, tick for tick on average? (B) Does it raise thrust in large heading errors more than the autopilot does? (C) Where does its extra power go? (D) What do the networks do in the same measure? (E) Is `c02` like the networks?

### 2. The arms, the flight and the measures
**Arms (35), the laws fixed as constants:** `autopilot`; `law0` (the zero law, must equal the autopilot); `c01` = [1.5583196668982158, 0.28628511493415953, -0.383655969709773, -0.4262203519156169]; `c02` = [2.2035859388880334, 0.8758638340144, -0.6433501233386668, -2] and `c04` = [2.6422458154661608, 0.14972036308189385, -0.02662452197229641, -2] (H18's champions, read from `brain/rig-results/head-to-head18-search.jsonl`); `h16` (H16's law, [1.2322693581401742, -0.061415416013719054, -0.6, -0.15850584538981266]); `sched*2`; the 28 networks of H9 (built-in gfc networks). **Task and ids:** the compass dropout, dark and lit, one-sub tanks, 160 s. **Ids 19000-19199 (200), read once.** The flight scores exactly as switchboard.dropout does (the gate holds it) and records, per flight and from the snapshot every tick (4800 ticks): the speed through the water, the thrust command, the power (H15's formula) and the TRUE heading error |e| (target heading less the heading, wrapped: the quantity the score is made of, also in the dark).
**Bands:** every tick belongs to BIG (|e| above 0.2 rad, H14's band) or SMALL (0.2 rad or less). **Phases:** H14's (TURN: the first 12 s after a target; SCORED: from 12 s after a target, in the dark window, in the lit tank every tick from 12 s; OTHER: the rest). Per flight and band: the number of ticks, the mean thrust, mean speed excess over the target speed and mean power; per flight and phase the same four numbers (an empty band or phase is reported as empty).
**Measures.** SPEED EXCESS = the mean over every tick of (speed less the target speed). SHAPE = the mean thrust over the BIG ticks less the mean thrust over the SMALL ticks, per flight; an id enters a shape comparison only if both bands hold at least 20 ticks in the flights of both arms compared (ids left out are counted). BIG SHARE of the extra power = (the BIG band's contribution to an arm's mean power less the autopilot's, where a band's contribution is its fraction of ticks x its mean power) / the extra mean power, over the 200 ids in the dark.
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown; five parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER / WORSE / TIES), paired by id, in the dark.**
**Part A (speed):** `c02` minus the autopilot on the speed excess: BETTER `C02 FLIES FASTER THAN THE AUTOPILOT`, WORSE `C02 FLIES SLOWER THAN THE AUTOPILOT`, TIES `C02 FLIES AT THE AUTOPILOT'S SPEED`.
**Part B (shape):** `c02` minus the autopilot on SHAPE: BETTER `C02 RAISES THRUST MORE IN LARGE HEADING ERRORS THAN THE AUTOPILOT`, WORSE `... LESS ...`, TIES `C02 RAISES THRUST AS MUCH IN LARGE HEADING ERRORS AS THE AUTOPILOT`.
**Part C (where the power goes):** if `c02`'s extra mean power is under 0.1 kW in magnitude `C02'S EXTRA POWER IS BELOW 0.1 kW`; else `<x> PERCENT OF C02'S EXTRA POWER IS DRAWN IN LARGE HEADING ERRORS` with x = 100 x the BIG SHARE rounded to a whole number (it may exceed 100 or be negative).
**Part D (the networks):** the networks' per-id mean SHAPE (the mean over the 28 networks) minus the autopilot's: `THE NETWORKS RAISE THRUST MORE IN LARGE HEADING ERRORS THAN THE AUTOPILOT` / `... LESS ...` / `THE NETWORKS RAISE THRUST AS MUCH IN LARGE HEADING ERRORS AS THE AUTOPILOT`.
**Part E (the resemblance):** `c02` minus the networks' per-id mean SHAPE: `C02 RAISES THRUST MORE IN LARGE HEADING ERRORS THAN THE NETWORKS` / `... LESS ...` / `C02 RAISES THRUST AS MUCH IN LARGE HEADING ERRORS AS THE NETWORKS`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power finite and at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **`law0` equals the autopilot in score, power, speed and thrust to 1e-6, both conditions**; **in every flight the two bands' ticks add up to the 4800 and the three phases' ticks add up to the 4800**; **`c01`, `c02`, `c04` and `h16` fly the laws written above (checked against the constants)**; the 28 networks are pairwise different.

### 4. The lines
`SPEED EXCESS BY PHASE (turn / scored / other, dark)` and `THRUST BY PHASE`: for the autopilot, `c02`, `c04`, `h16`, `sched*2` and the networks' mean; `BANDS (dark)`: for the same arms the fraction of ticks in BIG, the mean thrust in BIG and in SMALL, the mean power in BIG and in SMALL; `SHAPE (dark / lit)` for the same arms; `PAIRED`: the four pairings of the verdict (mean, se, reading, ids left out); `POWER`: the extra mean power of `c02` and the BIG SHARE (and `c04`, `h16`, `sched*2`, the networks' mean); `VERDICT`; `LOST SUBS`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit score, power and gain; each network's SHAPE and BIG fraction (range).

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
Part A: TIES 55 percent, FASTER 25, SLOWER 20. Part B: MORE 80, AS MUCH 12, LESS 8. Part C: x between 50 and 100: 55 percent, above 100: 20, below 50: 25. Part D: MORE 70, AS MUCH 20, LESS 10. Flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead19Cmd.mjs --parts 4` on ids 19000-19199, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; all ten checks PASS (`law0` equals the autopilot in score, power, speed and thrust on every id; every flight's bands and phases add up to 4800 ticks; the four laws are the registered ones). **The bands are not "turning" and "straight": most ticks are BIG.** The autopilot has 0.767 of its ticks above 0.2 rad of heading error (c02 0.698, c04 0.756, h16 0.690, `sched*2` 0.672, the networks 0.708 on average, 0.669 to 0.765 across the 28), because a new target arrives every 20 to 45 s and the sub takes a long time to bring the error under 0.2 rad. SMALL is the settled quarter to third of the flight.
**Thrust by band (dark; BIG / SMALL command; BIG / SMALL power in kW).** Autopilot 0.27 / 0.30; 3.72 / 4.36. **c02 0.42 / 0.09; 6.65 / 1.64.** c04 0.25 / 0.06; 3.72 / 1.13. h16 0.53 / 0.15; 9.05 / 2.50. `sched*2` 0.61 / 0.31; 11.00 / 5.07. Networks (mean) 0.48 / 0.30; 8.03 / 4.85. **SHAPE (BIG thrust less SMALL thrust), dark / lit:** autopilot -0.025 / -0.028; **c02 +0.327 / +0.312**; c04 +0.197 / +0.174; h16 +0.381 / +0.407; `sched*2` +0.301 / +0.296; **networks +0.174 / +0.178 (range -0.016 to +0.317 over the 28)**. The autopilot has no shape (it does not change its thrust with the heading error); the tuned laws and the hand-made one do; the networks do on average and a few do not.
**Where the extra power goes (extra mean kW; BIG share).** c02 +1.26 kW, 146 percent (its BIG band draws 146 percent of the whole extra: the SETTLED band draws LESS than the autopilot's, 1.64 against 4.36 kW); c04 -0.79 kW; h16 +3.15 kW, 111 percent; `sched*2` +5.14 kW, 89 percent; c01 +5.04 kW, 91 percent; the networks +3.15 kW, 88 percent. **So the networks and `sched*2` buy their gain by adding thrust in the large errors AND keeping the settled thrust at or above the autopilot's (0.30, 0.31); c02 and c04 buy theirs by adding it in the large errors and TAKING IT AWAY in the settled ones (0.09, 0.06).**
**Speed and thrust by phase (dark; turn / scored / other).** Speed excess: autopilot -0.18 / -0.17 / -0.18; **c02 +0.06 / -0.26 / -0.35**; c04 -0.32 / -0.53 / -0.70; h16 +0.23 / -0.07 / -0.05; `sched*2` +0.57 / +0.36 / +0.29; networks +0.34 / +0.19 / +0.14. Thrust: autopilot +0.27 / +0.29 / +0.28; **c02 +0.46 / +0.30 / +0.12**; c04 +0.30 / +0.22 / +0.06; h16 +0.56 / +0.34 / +0.23; `sched*2` +0.63 / +0.48 / +0.33; networks +0.49 / +0.41 / +0.32. **c02's mean speed is level with the autopilot's (+0.0331 m/s, se 0.0194; 112 ids faster, 88 slower: TIES) but its profile is not: +0.24 m/s faster than the autopilot in the first 12 s after a target, 0.09 slower in the scored ticks and 0.17 slower after.** H18's "flies at the autopilot's own speed" holds as a mean, not phase by phase.
**Paired (dark).** c02 minus the autopilot on the shape +0.3525 (se 0.0099; 200 ids better, 0 worse) BETTER. The networks minus the autopilot +0.1984 (se 0.0051; 197 / 2; 1 id left out for a thin band) BETTER. c02 minus the networks +0.1522 (se 0.0077; 190 / 9; 1 id left out) BETTER. Gains here: c02 +0.0952 dark / +0.0920 lit on +1.26 kW (the id set differs from H18's, where it was +0.1058 / +0.0852 on +1.16 kW); h16 +0.1139 / +0.1098 on +3.15 kW; `sched*2` +0.1112 / +0.1332 on +5.14 kW; c04 +0.0330 / +0.0262 on -0.79 kW. 4 flights of one network lost a sub; no other arm lost one.
**Against the registered expectations.** Part A: TIES (55 percent) held. Part B: MORE (80 percent) held. **Part C: x above 100 was my 20 percent branch (50 to 100: 55 percent): 146.** Part D: MORE (70 percent) held. Part E: MORE (55 percent) held. c02's BIG fraction below the autopilot's (70 percent): held (0.698 against 0.767). c02's SMALL thrust below the autopilot's (75 percent): held (0.09 against 0.30). c02's lit SHAPE above the autopilot's (75 percent): held (+0.312 against -0.028). c04's SHAPE above the autopilot's (70 percent): held (+0.197).
**What this does NOT say.** The band means mix the law with where the pilot is: c02 spends fewer ticks in BIG than the autopilot (0.698 against 0.767), so its band thrusts are taken over different states; the ticks in a band are not the same ticks. It does not say the shape is what produces the gain: this round measures the shape, not its effect (no transplant of the c02 thrust into the autopilot or a network was made). It does not say the networks compute a heading-error schedule: their mean shape is half c02's, with a range from about zero to c02's level, and the mean hides which networks do what. The 146 percent is a share of a difference of two powers (the SETTLED band's draw falls), not a fraction of anything drawn. The heading error used is the true one, also in the dark (the pilot sees the held heading); the `c` term of the law reads the pilot's own error, which differs in the dark. One id set, one tank; `c02` is H18's champion, found on other ids.

## Round H20, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any id is flown): IS THE THRUST SHAPE THE CAUSE OF THE GAIN? A TRANSPLANT ROUND
### 1. Why
H19 measured what `c02` does (thrust high in large heading errors, almost none in settled ones: shape +0.327 against the autopilot's -0.025; the networks +0.174) but not whether that is what produces the gain. H11 showed the networks' gain is entirely in their thrust channel by moving channels between a network and the autopilot; `c02` is the autopilot with only its thrust command changed (c + d + k + s terms), so the same tool can now split `c02` itself. Three things are open: which of `c02`'s four terms the gain needs; whether the half of the shape in the large errors (adding thrust) or the half in the settled errors (removing it) carries the gain; and whether the networks' other channels do anything once the thrust is `c02`'s.
> (A) Which term of `c02` does its gain need? (B) Does any single term gain by itself? (C) Does the large-error half or the settled half carry the gain? (D) What does `c02`'s thrust do in a network's body? (E) Do the networks' other channels add anything over the autopilot's? (F) Is H11's result (the autopilot with the networks' thrust gains what they do) still true on fresh ids?

### 2. The arms and the measures
**Task and ids:** the compass dropout, dark and lit, one-sub tanks, 160 s. **Ids 20000-20199 (200), read once.** The flight is H15's (it scores as switchboard.dropout does; it also records power and thrust). A pilot's GAIN is its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The laws** are H19's constants: `c02` = [2.2035859388880334, 0.8758638340144, -0.6433501233386668, -2] (c, d, k, s), `c04` = [2.6422458154661608, 0.14972036308189385, -0.02662452197229641, -2], `h16` = [1.2322693581401742, -0.061415416013719054, -0.6, -0.15850584538981266]; the law is thrust = clamp(autopilot's thrust + c |heading error|/pi + d + k speedErr + s V/3) with the autopilot's guidance heading error and speed error, the other three commands the autopilot's.
**Arms (46), a mixed pilot being a body (the source of pitch, yaw and ballast) and a thrust source:**
- `autopilot`; `law0` (the zero law); `c02`, `c04`, `h16` flown as laws; `ap+c02thr` (the autopilot's body with `c02`'s thrust through the mixer, checked against `c02`); `ap+apthr` (autopilot body, autopilot thrust through the mixer: must equal the autopilot) and `net+netthr` (network-Wip body and thrust through the mixer: must equal network-Wip).
- **Term ablations of `c02` (4):** `c02-c`, `c02-d`, `c02-k`, `c02-s` = `c02` with that one weight set to 0. **Single terms (4):** `only-c`, `only-d`, `only-k`, `only-s` = the law with only that weight of `c02`'s kept (the others 0).
- **Halves (6):** `c02-big`, `c02-small`, `c04-big`, `c04-small`, `h16-big`, `h16-small` = the autopilot with the law's thrust used only on the ticks where the pilot's own heading error is above 0.2 rad (`-big`) or at most 0.2 rad (`-small`), the autopilot's thrust on the others.
- **Networks (8 shipped: network-Wip, -Actuators, -Robust, -RobustLong, -RobustTail, -RobustTail2, -RobustWide, -RobustNoisy), three arms each (24 arms):** the network itself (`network-...`); `net+c02thr` (the network's body with `c02`'s thrust computed on the same state); `ap+netthr` (the autopilot's body with the network's thrust).
**The verdict, registered before any id is flown; six parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER / WORSE / TIES), paired by id on the dark score.**
**Part A (what the gain needs):** for each of c, d, k, s the arm `c02-<term>` minus `c02`; the terms whose removal reads WORSE are listed in order: `C02'S GAIN NEEDS: <terms joined by ", " or NONE>`.
**Part B (single terms):** the single-term arms whose gain over the autopilot is above 0.02 AND reads BETTER: `A SINGLE TERM GAINS: <the terms, in the order c, d, k, s, joined by ", ", or NONE>`.
**Part C (halves):** `C02'S LARGE-ERROR HALF ALONE <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE AUTOPILOT AND ITS SETTLED HALF ALONE <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> IT` (`c02-big` and `c02-small` against the autopilot, BETTER / WORSE / TIES).
**Part D (c02's thrust in a network's body):** the mean over the 8 networks, per id, of (`net+c02thr` score) minus the mean of the networks' own scores, paired: BETTER `C02'S THRUST RAISES THE NETWORKS' SCORE`, WORSE `C02'S THRUST LOWERS THE NETWORKS' SCORE`, TIES `C02'S THRUST LEAVES THE NETWORKS' SCORE UNCHANGED`.
**Part E (the networks' other channels):** the mean over the 8, per id, of (`net+c02thr`) minus `c02`: BETTER `THE NETWORKS' OTHER CHANNELS ADD TO C02'S THRUST`, WORSE `... COST ...`, TIES `THE NETWORKS' OTHER CHANNELS CHANGE NOTHING`.
**Part F (H11 again):** the mean over the 8 of `ap+netthr` minus the mean of the networks: BETTER `THE AUTOPILOT WITH THE NETWORKS' THRUST BEATS THE NETWORKS`, WORSE `... IS BEHIND THE NETWORKS`, TIES `THE AUTOPILOT WITH THE NETWORKS' THRUST TIES THE NETWORKS`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **`law0`, `ap+apthr` equal the autopilot and `net+netthr` equals network-Wip in score, power and thrust to 1e-6, both conditions**; **`c02` flown as a law equals `net+c02thr` of a body that is the autopilot** (checked on `ap+c02thr`, an arm (the 46th) that is the autopilot's body with `c02`'s thrust through the mixer, equal to `c02` to 1e-6); the laws are the registered constants; the 8 networks are pairwise different.

### 4. The lines
`GAINS (dark / lit)`: every arm's gain over the autopilot; `ABLATION`: `c02-<term>` minus `c02` with mean, se and reading for the four terms; `SINGLE`: the four single-term gains with readings; `HALVES`: the six gates' gain, extra power, and reading against the autopilot; `NETWORKS`: the 8 networks', `net+c02thr`'s and `ap+netthr`'s mean gain and extra power; `PAIRED`: the three pairings of parts D, E, F; `VERDICT`; `LOST SUBS`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** every arm's dark and lit score, power and thrust; each network's three gains.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE any id is flown
Part A: removing c is WORSE 85 percent, d 60, s 60, k 30 (k is small in `c02`). Part B: a single term gains: only c 40 percent, none 35, c and another 15, only d or s 10. Part C: the large-error half BEATS the autopilot 90 percent; the settled half alone: BEHIND 50, TIES 35, BEATS 15. Part D: RAISES 45, UNCHANGED 40, LOWERS 15. Part E: CHANGE NOTHING 65, ADD 20, COST 15. Part F: TIES 70, IS BEHIND 20, BEATS 10. **`c02-big` keeps more than half of `c02`'s dark gain: 70 percent.** The settled half (`c02-small`) draws less power than the autopilot: 90 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead20.mjs` (the mixer kinds, the gated laws, the arms, the audit using H15's flight, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; imports H15's flight and H16's law kind; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead20Cmd.mjs` (parallel parts, `--from-json`) and `headToHead20-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass). ## Round H20, RESULT (2026-11-08): THE GAIN IS THE HEADING-ERROR TERM; THE REST OF c02 IS A POWER-SAVING SHELL; c02'S THRUST GIVES THE NETWORKS THE SAME SCORE ON A QUARTER OF THEIR POWER
Flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead20Cmd.mjs --parts 4` on ids 20000-20199, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; all ten checks PASS (`law0` and `ap+apthr` equal the autopilot, `net+netthr` equals network-Wip, `ap+c02thr` equals `c02` flown as a law, all to 1e-6). Data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head20.json` (2.6 MB). **Verdict as printed: `C02'S GAIN NEEDS: c, d, k; A SINGLE TERM GAINS: c, d; C02'S LARGE-ERROR HALF ALONE BEATS THE AUTOPILOT AND ITS SETTLED HALF ALONE TIES IT; C02'S THRUST LEAVES THE NETWORKS' SCORE UNCHANGED; THE NETWORKS' OTHER CHANNELS COST C02'S THRUST; THE AUTOPILOT WITH THE NETWORKS' THRUST TIES THE NETWORKS`.**
**Gains (dark / lit) and extra power (dark kW).** c02 +0.1104 / +0.0912, +1.02 kW; c04 +0.0337 / +0.0206, -0.82; h16 +0.1179 / +0.1107, +2.75.
**A. Terms removed from c02 (arm minus c02, dark):** without c -0.2097 (se 0.0096; 192 of 200 ids worse), the arm's gain over the autopilot -0.0993; without d -0.1880 (196 worse), gain -0.0776; without k -0.0379 (167 worse), gain +0.0725; **without s +0.0058 (se 0.0063) TIES: gain +0.1161 dark and +0.1795 lit, but on +19.84 kW (20.53 lit): without s the law goes to full throttle, as H17's did.** So c and d (and k, less) carry the gain, and s is what holds the power down at the same gain.
**B. One term kept (gain over the autopilot, dark / lit; extra kW dark):** only-c +0.1163 / +0.1362, +5.32 kW (BETTER); only-d +0.0965 / +0.1336, +8.32 kW (BETTER: a constant thrust offset of 0.876 is most of the way to full throttle); only-k -0.0671 / -0.0747 (WORSE); only-s -0.1335 / -0.1511 (WORSE). **The heading-error term c alone gains slightly more than the whole of c02 (+0.1163 against +0.1104) and draws five times its power (+5.32 against +1.02 kW).**
**C. Halves.** c02-big (c02's thrust only above 0.2 rad of the pilot's own heading error, the autopilot's below) +0.1068 / +0.0973, +2.11 kW BETTER: **97 percent of c02's dark gain on twice its extra power.** c02-small (the settled half alone) +0.0067 / +0.0043, **-0.76 kW**, TIES the autopilot. c04: -big +0.0375 / +0.0121, +0.08 kW BETTER (c04 whole +0.0337); -small +0.0040 / -0.0018, -0.65 kW, TIES. h16: -big +0.1210 / +0.1339, +4.07 kW BETTER (h16 whole +0.1179 on +2.75 kW); -small -0.0009 / -0.0061, -0.73 kW, TIES. **In all three laws the large-error half carries the gain and the settled half is a power saving of 0.65 to 0.76 kW at no cost in the score.**
**D to F. The eight shipped networks.** Mean dark / lit gain and extra kW: the networks +0.1007 / +0.1068, +4.29 kW; **with c02's thrust +0.1047 / +0.0837, +1.12 kW**; the autopilot with their thrust +0.1019 / +0.1219, +4.47 kW. Paired (dark): net+c02thr minus the networks +0.0040 (se 0.0048; 107 ids better, 93 worse) TIES (their score unchanged, **their power down from +4.29 to +1.12 kW**); net+c02thr minus c02 -0.0057 (se 0.0015; 67 / 133) WORSE (the networks' pitch, yaw and ballast under c02's thrust do slightly worse than the autopilot's); ap+netthr minus the networks +0.0012 (se 0.0017; 101 / 99) TIES (H11 again: moving the networks' thrust into the autopilot's body loses nothing). In the lit tank the networks with c02's thrust gain less than the networks (+0.0837 against +0.1068) and less than the autopilot with their thrust (+0.1219).
**Against the registered expectations.** A: removing c WORSE (85 percent) and d WORSE (60) held; **k needed was a 30 percent outcome; s not needed (TIES) was the 40 percent against my 60 percent for s needed.** B: c and d gaining together was my 15 percent branch (only c, 40). C: the large-error half BEATS (90 percent) held; the settled half TIES (35 percent branch). D: UNCHANGED (40 percent branch; RAISES 45). **E: COST was my 15 percent branch (CHANGE NOTHING 65).** F: TIES (70 percent) held. `c02-big` keeps more than half of c02's dark gain (70 percent): held (97 percent). `c02-small` draws less power than the autopilot (90 percent): held (-0.76 kW). No lost sub anywhere.
**What this does NOT say.** `c02` is a law, found for a price on power; the round shows its thrust, moved into other pilots, does what it does here (a network body with c02's thrust keeps the networks' score at a quarter of the power) on one id set and one tank. The terms interact (d and s act as a speed regulator around a higher setpoint, d alone is nearly full throttle), so the ablations say what each term does in c02's company, not what it does alone, except in the single-term arms. "The large-error half carries the gain" is for the three laws and a gate on the pilot's own heading error (the held heading in the dark). The networks' other channels costing 0.006 is small beside the 0.1 gain, one id set, 8 networks. The lit tank is not in the verdict: there the networks' own thrust beats c02's in the networks' body.

## Round H21, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any search is run and before any id is flown): IS THE c, d, s CORE ENOUGH? A PRICED SEARCH OVER THE REDUCED LAW
### 1. Why
H20 found that `c02`'s gain is its heading-error term `c` (alone +0.1163 on +5.32 kW), that its constant `d` is needed beside it (without `d` the gain is -0.0776), that `s` holds the power down (without it the same gain on +19.84 kW) and that `k` matters less (without it +0.0725). That says what each term does inside `c02`, found by one search at one price; it does not say whether a search that is NOT allowed to move `k` reaches the same priced result, nor whether a fresh full search reproduces `c02`. This round searches the reduced family and the full one on fresh ids at the price of H18's best champion (0.02 per kW), with `s` allowed a wider range than before (three of H18's eight searches at the higher prices ended on its lower edge).
> (A) Does the reduced law (c, d, s with `k` held) match a full four-weight search? (B) Does either match `c02`? (C) Does the wider `s` range keep the champions off its edge? (D) Does anything go back to full throttle?

### 2. The searches, the arms and the audit
**The law and the tank kind** are H16's (`h16-law`: thrust = clamp(autopilot's thrust + c |heading error|/pi + d + k speedErr + s V/3), weights c, d, k, s). **The box (new):** c in [0, 16], d in [-0.4, 1.0], k in [-1.5, 2], **s in [-4, 2]** (H17's box with s widened below).
**The priced objective** is H18's at LAMBDA = 0.02: PRICED = (the mean dark score + the mean lit score)/2 - 0.02 x (the mean dark power + the mean lit power)/2 in kW, on the TRAIN ids.
**Three groups of four (1+1)-ES searches, 300 iterations each**, from the autopilot's law as in H17/H18 (one to all free weights changed by a Gaussian step of 0.1 x the width of the weight's box, clamped, kept when the objective is at least the parent's): group **F4** (all four weights free; seeds 21001-21004); group **K0** (c, d, s free, `k` held at 0; seeds 21011-21014); group **KC** (c, d, s free, `k` held at -0.6433501233386668, `c02`'s; seeds 21021-21024). Train ids 21000-21023 (24), held-out 21100-21139 (40), 160 s. **Champions:** in each group the search with the best held-out priced value (the first of a tie). No audit id is touched by a search.
**The audit:** ids **21200-21399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, H15's flight. Arms (16): `autopilot`; `law0` (the zero law: must equal the autopilot); `c02` = [2.2035859388880334, 0.8758638340144, -0.6433501233386668, -2] and `h16` = [1.2322693581401742, -0.061415416013719054, -0.6, -0.15850584538981266] as fixed laws; `sched*2`; `f4`, `k0`, `kc` (the three champions); the 8 shipped networks (H9's). The per-id PRICED SCORE of an arm is (dark score + lit score)/2 - 0.02 x (dark kW + lit kW)/2; PRICED GAIN its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; seven parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER / WORSE / TIES), paired by id on the per-id priced score.**
**Parts A1, A2:** `k0` against `f4`, and `kc` against `f4`: `THE C,D,S LAW WITH k = 0 <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE FOUR-WEIGHT LAW` and `THE C,D,S LAW WITH k AT C02'S <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE FOUR-WEIGHT LAW`.
**Parts B1, B2, B3:** `k0`, `kc` and `f4` each against `c02`: `THE C,D,S LAW WITH k = 0 <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02`, `THE C,D,S LAW WITH k AT C02'S <...> C02`, `A FRESH FOUR-WEIGHT SEARCH <...> C02`.
**Part C (the edge):** the champions whose `s` lies within 1 percent of the s-range's width (0.06) of an edge of [-4, 2]: `S AT THE BOX EDGE: <the champions among f4, k0, kc in that order joined by ", ", or NONE>`.
**Part D (full throttle):** the champions whose mean dark thrust command is 0.9 or more: `FULL THROTTLE AT: <the champions in the order f4, k0, kc joined by ", ", or NONE>`.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; **`law0` equals the autopilot in score, power and thrust to 1e-6, both conditions**; all twelve searches' laws and the three champions lie inside the box; **in the K0 searches and champion `k` is exactly 0 and in the KC ones exactly -0.6433501233386668**; the train, held-out and audit ids are disjoint; **each champion has the largest held-out priced value in its group**; `c02` and `h16` are the registered laws; the 8 networks are pairwise different.

### 4. The lines

**What it says.** (1) Holding `k` costs nothing against a free search: the c, d, s laws (with k at 0 or at c02's value) tie the four-weight champion on the priced score, and the two reduced champions have a HIGHER dark gain (+0.1150) than f4 (+0.1020) on more power. (2) **None of the three fresh champions reaches c02: each is 0.013 to 0.022 behind it on the priced score, on 200 ids that no search saw and that c02 was not selected on.** c02 was the best of four searches in H18 on other ids; its priced gain on H18's audit ids (+0.0837) and here (+0.0910) is above h16's both times (+0.0697, +0.0765). (3) So a batch of four searches of 300 iterations, at this price in this family, usually ends below the law H18 found; the best held-out values here (F4 0.7993, K0 0.7811, KC 0.7850) say only that the searches converged on their own ids. (4) What separates c02 on the audit is the lit tank: c02's lit gain is +0.0908 at -0.04 kW, the fresh champions +0.066 to +0.079; their dark gains are at or above c02's.

B1: IS BEHIND (50 percent) held. **B2: IS BEHIND was my 30 percent branch (TIES 45, BEATS 25).** **B3: IS BEHIND was my 25 percent branch (TIES 50): a fresh full search did not reproduce c02.** C: NONE (55 percent) held. D: NONE (90 percent) held. At least one of K0 and KC with `s` below -2 (50 percent): held (K0's -2.82). **`kc`'s priced gain within 0.01 of c02's (55 percent): did not hold (0.0134 behind).** At least three of four searches within 0.005 of the group's best held-out value (40 percent): held for K0 (3), not for F4 (1) or KC (2).
**What this does NOT say.** It does not say c02 is the optimum or that the family cannot do better than c02: four searches of 300 iterations is a small sample of the (1+1)-ES, the search finds three different laws of similar held-out value here, and the best law found in this round is not the best law in the family. It does not say the reduced family is as good as c02: it ties the typical full search, which is below c02. The champions were chosen on 40 held-out ids and flown on 200 others; c02 was chosen on H18's ids and flown here, so c02's advantage is a measured one on a third id set, but it is one law against three, and c02 as one of four H18 searches is itself a best-of-four. The lit tank is part of the priced score (the mean of the two tanks); the audit's dark-only reading is in the gains above. One id set, one tank.

## Round H22, REGISTERED (2026-11-08, before any search is run and before any id is flown): WHERE DOES c02 STAND AMONG TWENTY SEARCHES?
### 1. Why
H21 found that three fresh champions, each the best of four searches, were 0.013 to 0.022 behind `c02` on the priced score (200 audit ids). That is three laws from twelve searches against one law that was itself the best of four in H18. It does not say how often a search of this recipe reaches `c02`'s level, or whether `c02` was a lucky draw. This round runs twenty independent searches of the full four-weight family at the same price (0.02 per kW) on fresh ids, scores `c02` on the same held-out ids the searches are ranked on, and reads where `c02` falls in that distribution, then flies the best, the median and the worst search against `c02` on 200 audit ids.
> (A) How many of twenty searches reach `c02`'s held-out priced value? (B) Does the best of twenty beat `c02` on the audit? (C) Does the median search? (D) Does even the worst search beat the autopilot? (E) How many searches land in `c02`'s basin, and how many end on the edge of `s`?

### 2. The searches, the arms and the audit
**The law, the tank kind, the box and the objective are H21's** (H16's law c, d, k, s; box c [0, 16], d [-0.4, 1.0], k [-1.5, 2], s [-4, 2]; PRICED = (dark score + lit score)/2 - 0.02 x (dark kW + lit kW)/2; (1+1)-ES, 300 iterations, from the zero law, step 0.1 x the box width, clamped, kept when at least the parent's). Only the ids and the seeds are new. **Twenty searches, all four weights free: seeds 22001-22020.** Train ids 22000-22023 (24), held-out 22100-22139 (40), 160 s. `c02` = [2.2035859388880334, 0.8758638340144, -0.6433501233386668, -2] is scored on the held-out ids with the same objective (nothing is searched from it).
**Ranking.** The twenty searches are ranked by held-out priced value, best first (ties by lower seed). **best** = rank 1, **median** = rank 10, **worst** = rank 20.
**The audit:** ids **22200-22399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, H15's flight. Seven arms: `autopilot`; `law0` (must equal the autopilot); `c02` and `h16` = [1.2322693581401742, -0.061415416013719054, -0.6, -0.15850584538981266] as fixed laws; `best`, `median`, `worst`. The per-id PRICED SCORE is (dark score + lit score)/2 - 0.02 x (dark kW + lit kW)/2, PRICED GAIN its mean paired difference over the autopilot. (The networks are not flown this round: H21 already has them at +0.0334.)
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; six parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER / WORSE / TIES), paired by id on the per-id priced score.**
**Part A:** `C02'S HELD-OUT VALUE IS REACHED BY <n> OF 20 SEARCHES`, n = the searches whose held-out priced value is at least `c02`'s on the same held-out ids.
**Parts B, C:** `THE BEST SEARCH <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02` and `THE MEDIAN SEARCH <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02` (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND).
**Part D:** `THE WORST SEARCH <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE AUTOPILOT`.
**Part E:** `SEARCHES IN C02'S BASIN: <n> OF 20; S AT THE BOX EDGE: <m> OF 20`. A law is in the basin when its c is in [1.2, 4.0] and its d is at least 0.3 (c02 has c 2.20, d 0.876); at the edge when `s` is within 1 percent of the s-width (0.06) of -4 or 2.

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; **`law0` equals the autopilot in score, power and thrust to 1e-6, both conditions**; exactly twenty searches, seeds 22001-22020 each once, every law inside the box; the train, held-out and audit ids are disjoint; **best, median and worst are the ranks 1, 10 and 20 of the held-out ranking**; `c02` and `h16` are the registered laws; **`c02`'s held-out value is recomputed from the stored law and equals the stored one**.

### 4. The lines
`SEARCHES`: the twenty laws sorted by held-out value with train and held-out priced values and accepted counts; `C02 ON THE HELD-OUT IDS`: its value and its rank among the twenty; `SPREAD`: largest minus smallest held-out value, and the quartiles; `AUDIT`: dark and lit mean score, dark mean power and mean dark thrust of the autopilot, `c02`, `h16`, `best`, `median`, `worst`; `PRICED GAIN`; `PAIRED` (best, median against `c02`; worst against the autopilot); `BASIN`: each search's c, d and whether it is in the basin; `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** each of the three searched arms' dark and lit gain and extra power; the distribution of c, d, k, s over the twenty (min, median, max).

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: n = 0, 35 percent; n in 1-2, 40 percent; n of 3 or more, 25 percent. Part B (best against `c02`): IS BEHIND 40, TIES 40, BEATS 20. Part C (median against `c02`): IS BEHIND 80, TIES 15, BEATS 5. Part D (worst against the autopilot): BEATS 85, TIES 15. Part E: basin count 0 of 20, 55 percent (1-3 of 20, 35; more, 10); `s` at the edge 0-2 of 20, 75 percent. `c02`'s held-out value is above the median of the twenty: 85 percent. The spread (best minus worst held-out value) is above 0.02: 60 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead22.mjs` (imports H21's `searchOne` with the new id sets, H21's audit/flight and H16's `lawArm`; the ranking, the basin and edge counts, the readings, checks, verdict and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead22Cmd.mjs` (`search` mode: twenty children writing a jsonl, resumable; `fly` mode with `--from-json`) and `headToHead22-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass).
