Missed: **Part B at 0.04 IS BEHIND (my 35, not the 40 TIES I put first)**; **Part C at 0.04 IS BEHIND (my 40, against the 50 on TIES)**; **Part E NONE (my 65): a02 and a04 each lost runs (1 and 3)**.

**What this does NOT say.** It does not say a network cannot match a law: the search is a (1+1)-ES of 600 iterations on 24 train ids, and all six champions trail the law of their price. It does not say the start never matters: ACT is a near neighbour of Wip (175 of 963 weights differ) so its tie is weak evidence, and the hand start HND is behind Wip at 0.04 only, in one search group, with one champion of four. It does not say m04's lead is false: the 1.5 se on these ids and the 2.2 se on H27's are two readings of the same champion on different ids, neither alone a verdict; the afterwards-computed readings above were not registered. It does not say the repair to the flight is neutral for a search that met it: only seed 28032 did, and it is not a champion. It does not test other penalties, longer searches or a finer price grid.

## Round H29, REGISTERED (2026-11-09, before anything is built, searched or flown): LONGER SEARCHES -- IS 600 ITERATIONS THE LIMIT?
### 1. Why
Every network result since H26 is a (1+1)-ES of 600 iterations, and in all of them a large share of the candidates were still being accepted at the end (H28: 53 to 191 accepted of 600; no search had stopped climbing by any visible sign). All six H28 champions and all three H27 champions trail the law of their price at two of three prices, and H28 could not tell "networks are worse" from "networks needed more search". This round asks the one question that separates them: **what does the same search do with four times the iterations?** It does not change the objective, the prices, the ids or the seeds.
> (A) Does the held-out value of the best of four searches keep rising from 600 to 1200, 1800 and 2400 iterations? (B) Does the audit read the 2400-iteration champion better than the 600-iteration one? (C) Does the longer network reach the law of its price? (D) Does it reach `c02`? (E) Do the longer champions lose subs?

### 2. The searches, the checkpoints and the arms
**The searches (8):** H27's penalised network search from `network-Wip` (963 weights, (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, penalised priced objective on the TRAIN ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s) **run for 2400 iterations instead of 600, with H27's own seeds**: groups **L02** (price 0.02; seeds 26011, 26012, 26013, 26014) and **L04** (0.04; seeds 26021, 26022, 26023, 26024). Train ids 26000-26023, held-out 26100-26139 (H27's, unchanged). Because the generator and the loop are the same, **the first 600 iterations of each search ARE H27's search of that seed** (a check below). At iterations **600, 1200, 1800 and 2400** the search records the weights, the train value and the held-out penalised value at the group's price. The searches are not stopped early. Prices 0.005 is not run (H27's `m005` already ties `c02` there and its search climbs least; a limit of this round).
**Champions:** at each checkpoint of a group, the search with the best held-out penalised value among the four (the first of a tie in seed order): `x02_600` .. `x02_2400`, `x04_600` .. `x04_2400`. `x02_600` and `x04_600` must be H27's `m02` and `m04` (a check).
**The audit:** ids **29200-29399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s. Arms (13): `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; the laws of the price `q02`, `q04` (H27's, from `brain/rig-results/head-to-head27.json`); `m02`, `m04` (H27's network champions, = the 600-iteration ones); and the longer champions `x02_1200`, `x02_1800`, `x02_2400`, `x04_1200`, `x04_1800`, `x04_2400`. Every arm is read at the group's own price (and, for the table, at all three) on the PENALISED priced score: per id (dark score' + lit score')/2 - lambda x (dark kW + lit kW)/2, a lost run scoring 0; PRICED GAIN its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; ten parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id on the penalised priced score at the stated price.**
**Parts A1-A2:** `AT <0.02/0.04> THE 2400 CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE 600 CHAMPION`: `x02_2400` against `m02`, `x04_2400` against `m04`. **Parts B1-B2:** `AT <price> THE 2400 CHAMPION <...> THE 1200 CHAMPION`. **Parts C1-C2:** `AT <price> THE 2400 CHAMPION <...> THE LAW OF THE PRICE`: against `q02`, `q04`. **Parts D1-D2:** `AT <price> THE 2400 CHAMPION <...> C02`. **Part E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE LONGER CHAMPIONS: <those of the six x arms, in the order x02_1200, x02_1800, x02_2400, x04_1200, x04_1800, x04_2400, that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part F:** `HELD-OUT VALUE RISES AT EVERY CHECKPOINT AT <the prices, in the order 0.02, 0.04, at which the group champion's held-out value is strictly larger at 1200 than at 600, at 1800 than at 1200 and at 2400 than at 1800, or NONE>`.
(That is A1-A2, B1-B2, C1-C2, D1-D2, E, F: ten.)

### 3. The checks
(a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; `law0` equals the autopilot in score, power and thrust to 1e-6; exactly 8 searches, each seed once in its group, each with 4 checkpoints at 600, 1200, 1800 and 2400; **the 600-iteration checkpoint of every search equals H27's stored search of the same seed (the weights, the train and held-out values) to the last digit, except where H27's search met a flight that `flyPen` changes (none is expected; any exception is named)**; the first scored candidate of every search equals `wip`'s penalised train value at the group's price; `x02_600`, `x04_600` equal H27's `m02`, `m04`; each champion at each checkpoint has the largest held-out penalised value of its group at that checkpoint; the train, held-out and audit ids are disjoint from each other and past every earlier audit (28399); `c02`, `wip` and H27's champions are the registered ones.

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH L02 / L04`: for each search the train and held-out values at 600 / 1200 / 1800 / 2400 and the accepted counts at each; the champion seeds per checkpoint; `LOSSES IN THE SEARCHES`; `AUDIT`: dark and lit mean score, dark power and thrust; `LOST`; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED` (the eight pairings plus each longer champion against `c02` at its own price); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the champion's weights' L1 distance from `wip` and from the 600-iteration champion; the unpenalised priced gain; the extra power (does the longer network spend or save power?).

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part F: both prices rise at every checkpoint 25 percent, exactly one price 35 percent, neither 40 percent (the best of four is a noisy max; a flat step is likely somewhere). The group champion's held-out value is larger at 2400 than at 600 at both prices: 85 percent. Parts A: at 0.02 TIES 50, BEATS 40, IS BEHIND 10; at 0.04 TIES 50, BEATS 40, IS BEHIND 10. Parts B (2400 against 1200): TIES 65, BEATS 30, IS BEHIND 5 at each price. Parts C: at 0.02 IS BEHIND 55, TIES 40, BEATS 5; at 0.04 TIES 45, IS BEHIND 40, BEATS 15. Parts D: at 0.02 IS BEHIND 45, TIES 45, BEATS 10; at 0.04 TIES 50, BEATS 40, IS BEHIND 10. Part E: NONE 50 percent (a longer search has more chances to find the lost-sub corner, and the penalty scores that corner 0, so most such candidates are rejected, but the audit ids are new). The 600-iteration checkpoints reproduce H27's rows exactly: 90 percent (the 10 is a flight the repair changes).

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead29.mjs` (the checkpointed network search, reproducing H27's loop and `PS.mutate` stream exactly so that iteration 600 is H27's; champions per checkpoint; the 13 arms; the audit; readings; the ten-part verdict; checks and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead29Cmd.mjs` (`search` mode: 8 children writing a jsonl, resumable; `fly` mode: parallel parts; `--from-json`) and `headToHead29-selfcheck.mjs` (with a mutation pass, including an identity test of the checkpointed search against `X27.netSearch` at 600 iterations on scripted flights). The first mutation pass is serial in ONE worktree (a parallel pass raced in H28). Cost: 2400 iterations is four times H27's 600, about 2 hours per search with 4 on the cores; the 8 searches are two waves, about 4 hours; the flight about 10 minutes.

### 7. What this round does NOT say
It does not say that more iterations is the right lever (a different mutation size, a population or a different start are not tried here), that 2400 is long enough, or that a network that matched a law would be a better pilot (the question is only whether searching longer closes a gap). Four seeds per price is a champion of four, as before. 0.005 is not covered.

## Round H29, RESULT (2026-11-09): FOUR TIMES THE ITERATIONS DOES NOT HELP THE AUDIT -- THE 2400 CHAMPION TIES THE 600 ONE AT BOTH PRICES, STAYS BEHIND c02 AT 0.02 AND TIES IT AT 0.04

**What this does NOT say.** It does not say a network cannot catch the law at 0.02: the champion is the best of four held-out values of searches that overfit the 24 train ids in some seeds, and a larger or rotating train set, a different step size or a population are not tried. It does not say 2400 is long enough at 0.04, where three of four seeds were still creeping up at the end (the 2400 champion's lead over the 600 one is -0.0005, so the creep is worth nothing on the audit so far). The +0.0112 at 1200 is a reading made after the verdict and not registered; with the 8 searches and 6 champions it is one of several possible post-hoc pairings. It does not cover 0.005 or other penalties.

## Round H30, REGISTERED (2026-11-09, before anything is built, searched or flown): THE LARGER TRAIN SET -- DOES TRAINING ON 72 IDS INSTEAD OF 24 STOP THE OVERFITTING?
### 1. Why
H29 found that train value rose at every checkpoint of every search while held-out value did not: at 0.02 seed 26011's held-out peaked at 1200 iterations (0.7519) and fell to 0.7113 by 2400 while its train value kept climbing, two more seeds peaked at 1200 and 1800, and the 1200-iteration champion beat the 600-iteration one by +0.0112 on the audit (a reading made after the verdict) while the 2400 one only tied it. Every search since H26 scores its candidates on the same 24 train ids (26000-26023) and keeps one when it is at least as good as its parent: a (1+1)-ES with an accept-if-not-worse rule on 24 ids is a selection on those ids. This round changes ONLY the train set, to **72 ids (three times larger)**, and asks whether the search then generalises better.
> (A) At the same iteration count, does the larger train set beat H27's 600-iteration champion? (B) Does a short search on the larger set beat H29's longest search (2400 iterations) on the small one? (C) Does it reach the law of the price? (D) Does it reach `c02`? (E) Do its champions lose subs? (F) Does the champion's held-out value now rise with every checkpoint?

### 2. The searches, the checkpoints and the arms
**The searches (8):** H29's search exactly (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, the penalised priced objective, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, H27's seeds) with **one change: the TRAIN ids are 26000-26023 (H27's) plus 30000-30047 (48 new), 72 in all**, and the search runs for **900 iterations** with checkpoints at **300, 600 and 900**. Groups **P02** (price 0.02; seeds 26011-26014) and **P04** (0.04; seeds 26021-26024). The HELD-OUT ids are H27's, H28's and H29's 26100-26139, unchanged, so held-out values are comparable across the four rounds. Same seeds means the same stream of proposed mutations; the paths part at the first candidate whose acceptance differs. The champion of a group at a checkpoint is the search with the best held-out penalised value among the four at that checkpoint (the first of a tie in seed order): `y02_300`, `y02_600`, `y02_900`, `y04_300`, `y04_600`, `y04_900`.
**The audit:** ids **30200-30399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s (the 48 new train ids are 30000-30047, disjoint). Arms (15): `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; the laws of the price `q02`, `q04` (H27's); H27's 600-iteration champions `m02`, `m04`; H29's 2400-iteration champions `x02_2400`, `x04_2400` (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head29.json`, `fresh.nets`: the third entry is the L02 champion at 2400 and the sixth the L04 champion at 2400); the six new champions. Every arm is read at its group's own price (and, for the table, at all three) on the PENALISED priced score: per id (dark score' + lit score')/2 - lambda x (dark kW + lit kW)/2, a lost run scoring 0; PRICED GAIN its mean paired difference over the autopilot.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; ten parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id on the penalised priced score at the stated price.**
**Parts A1-A2:** `AT <0.02/0.04> THE 900 CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> H27'S 600 CHAMPION`: `y02_900` against `m02`, `y04_900` against `m04`. **Parts B1-B2:** `AT <price> THE 900 CHAMPION <...> H29'S 2400 CHAMPION`: against `x02_2400`, `x04_2400`. **Parts C1-C2:** `AT <price> THE 900 CHAMPION <...> THE LAW OF THE PRICE`: against `q02`, `q04`. **Parts D1-D2:** `AT <price> THE 900 CHAMPION <...> C02`. **Part E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE LARGER-SET CHAMPIONS: <those of y02_300, y02_600, y02_900, y04_300, y04_600, y04_900 that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part F:** `HELD-OUT VALUE RISES AT EVERY CHECKPOINT AT <the prices, in the order 0.02, 0.04, at which the group champion's held-out value is strictly larger at 600 than at 300 and at 900 than at 600, or NONE>`.
(That is A1-A2, B1-B2, C1-C2, D1-D2, E, F: ten.)

### 3. The checks
(thirteen rows; a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; every network is 963 finite weights; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; **exactly 8 searches, each seed once in its group, each with 3 checkpoints at 300, 600 and 900**; **the train set is 72 ids and contains H27's 24 (26000-26023) and 48 new ones, disjoint from the held-out ids 26100-26139, from the audit ids and from every earlier audit (to 29399)**; the first scored candidate of every search equals `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 train ids at the group's price (the value is computed in the round, not read); each champion at each checkpoint has the largest held-out penalised value of its group at that checkpoint; `c02`, `wip`, H27's `q` and `m` champions and H29's 2400 champions are the registered ones (read from the stored files and compared with the champions the stored searches name by their rules); the penalised objective is H27's, unchanged (a fixture: it equals H18's without a lost run and a lost run scores 0).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH P02 / P04`: for each search its start value on the 72 ids, then the train and held-out values and accepted counts at 300 / 600 / 900; the champion seeds per checkpoint; `LOSSES IN THE SEARCHES`; `GENERALISATION GAP`: for the champion of each group at each checkpoint the train value minus the held-out value (the same quantity for H29's 2400 champions, from its stored searches); `AUDIT`; `LOST`; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED` (the eight pairings plus each new champion against `c02` at its own price); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the L1 distances of each new champion from `wip` and from H27's 600-iteration champion; the unpenalised priced gain.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part F: both prices rise at every checkpoint 30 percent, exactly one price 40 percent, neither 30 percent. The group champion's held-out value at 900 exceeds H29's 2400 champion's (0.7650 at 0.02, 0.7037 at 0.04) at both prices: 40 percent; at exactly one price: 40 percent. Parts A: at 0.02 BEATS 40, TIES 55, IS BEHIND 5; at 0.04 TIES 60, BEATS 30, IS BEHIND 10. Parts B: at each price TIES 60, BEATS 25, IS BEHIND 15. Parts C: at 0.02 IS BEHIND 50, TIES 45, BEATS 5; at 0.04 TIES 55, IS BEHIND 35, BEATS 10. Parts D: at 0.02 IS BEHIND 40, TIES 50, BEATS 10; at 0.04 TIES 55, BEATS 35, IS BEHIND 10. Part E: NONE 40 percent. The champion's generalisation gap (train minus held-out, at 900) is smaller than H29's 2400 champion's gap at both prices: 70 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead30.mjs` (H29's checkpointed search with a train set handed in as a list of ranges, H27's loop and stream, the champions, the 15 arms, the audit, readings, the ten-part verdict, checks and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead30Cmd.mjs` (output and input paths named for H30, checked by a row in a smoke run this time) and `headToHead30-selfcheck.mjs` (with an identity test: with a one-range train set equal to H29's, the search equals H29's `longSearch` at every checkpoint; and a mutation pass, serial in ONE worktree). The train objective flies the 72 ids as two ranges, 26000-26023 and 30000-30047. Cost: 72 train ids is three times the flights of H29 per iteration, about 9 s an iteration on 4 workers, 900 iterations about 2.3 hours per search, the 8 searches in two waves about 4.6 hours; the flight about 10 minutes.

### 7. What this round does NOT say
It does not say that more train ids is the only cure (a rotating train set, a held-out early stop, a population or a smaller step are not tried), that 72 is enough, or that a network that generalised would match a law (that is C and D). Four seeds per price is a champion of four, as before. It does not cover 0.005. The comparison with H29's 2400 champions mixes two things (the train set and the iteration count) and says so; A isolates the train set at 600 iterations only if the 900-iteration champion is replaced by the 600 one, which the lines print.

## Round H30, RESULT (2026-11-09): THREE TIMES THE TRAIN IDS HALVES THE GENERALISATION GAP BUT THE 900-ITERATION CHAMPION DOES NOT BEAT THE SMALL-SET ONES -- IT TIES THE LAW OF THE PRICE AND c02 AT 0.04 AND IS BEHIND BOTH AT 0.02
Searched by `node tools/ship/headToHead30Cmd.mjs search --workers 4` (8 (1+1)-ES searches of 900 iterations from `network-Wip` on **72 train ids** (26000-26023 and 30000-30047), H27's penalised objective and seeds, checkpoints at 300 / 600 / 900; 9,628 to 10,169 s each on 4 workers, about 5.6 hours of wall clock in two waves) and flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead30Cmd.mjs fly --parts 4` on ids 30200-30399, 160 s, dark and lit, read once (data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head30.json`, `head-to-head30-search.jsonl`). **All 13 checks PASS.** *(One slip, before anything was read: the first flight crashed at its start because the command's progress line, copied from H29, asked for a 2400-iteration champion that H30 does not have; the line now names the 900 one and the flight was started again. The library, the gate and the search were not involved.)* The searches took longer than registered: 2.8 hours a wave against the 2.3 I had estimated.

**The verdict, as printed (10 parts):** AT 0.02 THE 900 CHAMPION TIES H27'S 600 CHAMPION; AT 0.04 THE 900 CHAMPION IS BEHIND H27'S 600 CHAMPION; AT 0.02 THE 900 CHAMPION IS BEHIND H29'S 2400 CHAMPION; AT 0.04 THE 900 CHAMPION TIES H29'S 2400 CHAMPION; AT 0.02 THE 900 CHAMPION IS BEHIND THE LAW OF THE PRICE; AT 0.04 THE 900 CHAMPION TIES THE LAW OF THE PRICE; AT 0.02 THE 900 CHAMPION IS BEHIND C02; AT 0.04 THE 900 CHAMPION TIES C02; LOST SUBS IN THE LARGER-SET CHAMPIONS: y04_600 5; HELD-OUT VALUE RISES AT EVERY CHECKPOINT AT 0.04.

**The champions** (best held-out value of the four at each checkpoint): P02 seed 26014 at all three (0.7513, 0.7627, 0.7540); P04 seed 26021 at 300 and 600 (0.6697, 0.6795) and seed 26023 at 900 (0.6838). Their weights sit L1 41.10 (`y02_900`) and 69.45 (`y04_900`) from `wip`.

**The pairings** (penalised priced score, paired by id). 900 against H27's 600-iteration champion: `y02_900` minus `m02` at 0.02 -0.0041 (se 0.0028) TIES; `y04_900` minus `m04` at 0.04 -0.0063 (0.0031; better 70, worse 130) IS BEHIND. Against H29's 2400-iteration champion: -0.0122 (0.0035) IS BEHIND at 0.02; -0.0059 (0.0047) TIES at 0.04. Against the law of the price: `y02_900` minus `q02` -0.0184 (0.0032) IS BEHIND; `y04_900` minus `q04` -0.0058 (0.0050) TIES. Against `c02`: `y02_900` -0.0183 (0.0033) IS BEHIND; `y04_900` -0.0022 (0.0038) TIES. Every earlier checkpoint of the 0.02 group is behind `c02` as well (`y02_300` -0.0229, `y02_600` -0.0197), and so are `y04_300` (-0.0186) and `y04_600` (-0.0237). Penalised priced gain at the group's price: `c02` +0.0850 (0.02) / +0.0697 (0.04); `q02` +0.0852, `q04` +0.0732; `m02` +0.0709, `m04` +0.0737; `x02_2400` +0.0790, `x04_2400` +0.0734; `y02_300` / `y02_600` / `y02_900` +0.0621 / +0.0653 / +0.0668; `y04_300` / `y04_600` / `y04_900` +0.0510 / +0.0459 / +0.0674. The larger-set 0.02 networks spend more power for their score (extra power `y02_900` +1.06 kW against `c02` +0.77 and `x02_2400` +0.58). Lost runs in the audit: `y04_600` 5, `x04_2400` 4, `x02_2400` 1, every other arm 0 of 400.

**The generalisation gap** (train value minus held-out value of the group champion): at 0.02 0.0344 / 0.0259 / 0.0357 at 300 / 600 / 900, against 0.0665 for H29's 2400-iteration champion; at 0.04 0.0257 / 0.0292 / 0.0278, against 0.0684. **The larger train set roughly halves the gap at both prices** (the two are train values on different train sets, so this is how much each train score flatters its search, not a common scale). Held-out values within a group are steadier across seeds too: the four P02 seeds' held-out values at 900 lie within 0.012 of each other.

**Not in the verdict, computed afterwards from the stored flight and said as such:** the larger-set searches are still climbing at 0.04: `y04_900` minus `y04_600` +0.0215 (se 0.0059) BETTER and minus `y04_300` +0.0164 (0.0034) BETTER; at 0.02 `y02_900` minus `y02_300` +0.0046 (0.0013) BETTER and minus `y02_600` +0.0015 (0.0010) TIES. On these fresh ids H29's small-set champions read: `x02_2400` minus `m02` at 0.02 +0.0081 (0.0028) BETTER (H29 read +0.0052 TIES) and `x02_2400` minus `c02` -0.0060 (0.0037) TIES (H29 read -0.0110 IS BEHIND); `x04_2400` minus `m04` -0.0003 (0.0045) TIES and minus `c02` +0.0037 (0.0056) TIES. So the same networks read a little differently on different audit ids, by about one standard error.

**Expectations, hit and missed (registered in section 5).** Held: Parts A at 0.02 TIES (my 55); Parts B at 0.04 TIES (60); Parts C IS BEHIND at 0.02 (50) and TIES at 0.04 (55); Parts D TIES at 0.04 (55) and IS BEHIND at 0.02 (my 40, not the 50 I put on TIES); Part F exactly one price (40; it is 0.04); the generalisation gap smaller than H29's at both prices (70). Missed: **Part A at 0.04 IS BEHIND (my 10)**; **Part B at 0.02 IS BEHIND (my 15)**; **Part E NONE (my 40): `y04_600` lost 5 runs**; **the champion's held-out value at 900 above H29's 2400 champion at both prices (my 40) or at one (my 40): it is at neither (0.7540 against 0.7650 and 0.6838 against 0.7037), a 20 percent branch**.

**What this does NOT say.** It does not say a larger train set does not help: it halves the gap and the 0.04 searches are still climbing at 900 (+0.0215 over the 600-iteration champion), so 900 iterations at 72 ids may simply be short; H29's small-set champions had 2400 iterations. Nor does it say the train set caused the overfitting alone: the ids, the iteration count and the number of seeds all differ between the two rounds in ways the experiment did not separate, and A isolates the train set only at the 600 checkpoint, which the lines print (`y02_600`, `y04_600`). It does not say the champion of four is stable: at 0.04 the champion changes seed between checkpoints (26021 to 26023). The afterwards pairings were not registered. It does not cover 0.005, other penalties, a rotating train set or an early-stopping rule on the held-out ids.

## Round H31, REGISTERED (2026-11-09, before anything is built, searched or flown): THE LONGER SEARCH ON 72 IDS -- AT 0.04, WHERE H30'S SEARCHES WERE STILL CLIMBING
### 1. Why
H30 gave the networks a train set three times larger and halved their generalisation gap (train minus held-out of the group champion: 0.026 to 0.036 against H29's 0.067 and 0.068), but its 900-iteration champions did not beat H29's small-set champions on the audit. Read afterwards from the stored flight, the 0.04 searches were still climbing at 900 (`y04_900` minus its 600-iteration champion +0.0215, se 0.0059) while the 0.02 searches had levelled (`y02_900` minus `y02_600` +0.0015, se 0.0010). H29 asked whether 600 iterations on 24 ids was the limit and found the searches overfit; H30 removed most of the overfitting and found the search short. **This round runs the 0.04 searches longer on the 72 ids.** It is the one remaining untried combination of what H29 and H30 each fixed.
> (A) Does a longer search on 72 ids beat H30's 900-iteration champion? (B) Beat H27's 600-iteration champion? (C) Beat H29's 2400-iteration champion on 24 ids? (D) Reach the law of the price? (E) Reach `c02`? (F) Do its champions lose subs? (G) Does the held-out value keep rising with every checkpoint?

### 2. The searches, the checkpoints and the arms
**The searches (4):** H30's search exactly (`network-Wip`, (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, H27's penalised priced objective on the **72 TRAIN ids** 26000-26023 and 30000-30047, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s) **run for 1800 iterations instead of 900, price 0.04 only, H27's seeds 26021, 26022, 26023, 26024**, group **R04**. Held-out ids are 26100-26139, unchanged. Checkpoints at **900, 1200, 1500 and 1800** iterations. Because the generator and the loop are H30's, **the first 900 iterations of each search ARE H30's P04 search of that seed** (a check below). Champion of the group at a checkpoint: the search with the best held-out penalised value among the four (the first of a tie in seed order): `r04_900`, `r04_1200`, `r04_1500`, `r04_1800`. **0.02 is not run**: its H30 searches had levelled by 600 (a limit of this round, said here and not in the verdict).
**The audit:** ids **31200-31399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s. Arms (11): `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; the law of the price `q04` and H27's 600-iteration champion `m04` (from `brain/rig-results/head-to-head27.json`); H29's 2400-iteration champion `x04_2400` (from `head-to-head29.json`); H30's 900-iteration champion `y04_900` (from `head-to-head30.json`: the sixth entry of its `fresh.nets`); the four new champions. Every arm is read at 0.04 on the PENALISED priced score (and at all three prices for the table): per id (dark score' + lit score')/2 - 0.04 x (dark kW + lit kW)/2, a lost run scoring 0.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; seven parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id on the penalised priced score at 0.04.**
**Part A:** `THE 1800 CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> H30'S 900 CHAMPION` (`r04_1800` against `y04_900`). **Part B:** `... H27'S 600 CHAMPION` (against `m04`). **Part C:** `... H29'S 2400 CHAMPION` (against `x04_2400`). **Part D:** `... THE LAW OF THE PRICE` (against `q04`). **Part E:** `... C02`. **Part F:** `LOST SUBS IN THE R04 CHAMPIONS: <those of r04_900, r04_1200, r04_1500, r04_1800 that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part G:** `HELD-OUT VALUE RISES AT EVERY CHECKPOINT: <YES or NO>`: the group champion's held-out value strictly larger at 1200 than at 900, at 1500 than at 1200 and at 1800 than at 1500.
(That is A to G: seven.)

### 3. The checks
(thirteen rows; a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each seed once, each with 4 checkpoints at 900, 1200, 1500 and 1800 of 963 finite weights; **the 900-iteration checkpoint of every search equals H30's stored P04 search of the same seed to the last digit (weights, train and held-out values, accepted count)**; the train set is 72 ids, H27's 24 and 48 new, disjoint from the held-out ids, from the audit ids and from every earlier audit (to 30399); the first scored candidate of every search equals `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 ids at 0.04; each champion at each checkpoint has the largest held-out penalised value of its group at that checkpoint; `c02`, `wip`, H27's `q04` and `m04`, H29's `x04_2400` and H30's `y04_900` are the registered ones (read from the stored files and compared with the champions the stored searches name by their rules).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH R04`: start value on the 72 ids, then train and held-out value and accepted count at each checkpoint; the champion seeds per checkpoint; `LOSSES IN THE SEARCHES`; `GENERALISATION GAP` of the champion at each checkpoint (H29's and H30's printed beside it); `AUDIT`; `LOST`; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED` (the five pairings and each new champion against `c02`); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the L1 distances of each new champion from `wip` and from `y04_900`; the unpenalised priced gain.

`tools/ship/headToHead31.mjs` (H30's checkpointed search handed `iters` 1800 and `checkpoints` [900, 1200, 1500, 1800] under a group mapped to the P04 price; champions per checkpoint; the 11 arms; the audit; readings; the seven-part verdict; checks and lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead31Cmd.mjs` (every input and output path named for H31 and checked by a smoke run before anything is searched) and `headToHead31-selfcheck.mjs` (with an identity test against H30's `longSearch` at each checkpoint, and a serial mutation pass in ONE worktree). ### 7. What this round does NOT say
It does not say that more iterations is the right lever beyond 1800, that 72 ids is enough, or that the 0.02 searches would not also gain from more iterations (they levelled in H30 at one start and four seeds; they were not run). It does not cover 0.005 or other penalties. Four seeds are a champion of four, as before.

Every checkpoint against `c02`: `r04_900` +0.0026 TIES, `r04_1200` **-0.0139 (0.0065) IS BEHIND**, `r04_1500` +0.0034 TIES, `r04_1800` +0.0060 TIES. Penalised priced gain at 0.04: `c02` +0.0812; `q04` +0.0897; `x04_2400` +0.0878; `r04_1800` +0.0872; `m04` +0.0849; `y04_900` +0.0837; `r04_1500` +0.0845; `r04_1200` +0.0672. **Not in the verdict, computed afterwards from the stored flight and said as such:** `r04_1800` minus `r04_1500` +0.0026 (se 0.0012) BETTER (2.2 se); minus `r04_1200` +0.0199 (0.0061) BETTER; `r04_1500` minus `r04_900` +0.0008 (0.0018) TIES; `r04_1200` minus `r04_900` -0.0165 (0.0059) IS BEHIND. ## Round H32, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown): RESUMABLE SEARCHES, USED ON THE 0.02 PRICE -- THE LONGER SEARCH ON 72 IDS THAT H31 DID NOT RUN
### 1. Why
H31's first run was lost at 4.5 hours when the container restarted: a search writes its row only when it ends, so nothing survived and the 5.6-hour run was started again from the beginning. Every long round from here on is exposed to the same loss. This round does two things. **(1) It builds a search that can be resumed**, and proves, on real flights in the round itself, that a resumed search equals an uninterrupted one to the last digit. **(2) It uses that search for the one experiment H31 left open**: H30 showed the 0.02 searches on 72 ids levelled by 600 iterations and behind `c02`, H31 showed at 0.04 that 1800 iterations changed nothing the audit could see, and the 0.02 price has not been run longer. Same question as H31, at the other price.
> (A) Does a longer search on 72 ids beat H30's 900-iteration champion at 0.02? (B) Beat H27's 600-iteration champion? (C) H29's 2400-iteration champion on 24 ids? (D) Reach the law of the price? (E) Reach `c02`? (F) Do its champions lose subs? (G) Does the held-out value keep rising with every checkpoint? And: **(H) is a resumed search identical to an uninterrupted one?**

### 2. The resumable search, the searches and the arms
**The resumable search.** The loop is H30's and H31's (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, H27's penalised priced objective on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s), written out in the round's library with two additions. **State:** every **50 iterations**, and at every checkpoint, it writes a state file for the seed, replacing the last (a write to a temporary file and a rename, so a kill during the write leaves the old one): the iteration, the current weights, the best train value, the accepted count, the count of lost flights so far, the checkpoints recorded so far, and **the number of draws the generator has made**. **Resume:** the generator `PS.mulberry` keeps one 32-bit state that moves by the constant 0x6D2B79F5 at every draw, so after n draws a generator seeded with `(seed + n x 0x6D2B79F5) mod 2^32` continues exactly where the first stopped; the draws are counted by wrapping the generator handed to `PS.mutate`. A search that finds a state file for its seed starts from it; a search whose final row is already in the results file is not run again.
**The searches (4):** H30's P02 search **run for 1800 iterations instead of 900, price 0.02, H27's seeds 26011, 26012, 26013, 26014**, group **S02**, train ids the 72 (26000-26023, 30000-30047), held-out 26100-26139, checkpoints at **900, 1200, 1500 and 1800**. **The first 900 iterations ARE H30's P02 search of that seed** (a check below). Champion of the group at a checkpoint: the best held-out penalised value of the four (the first of a tie in seed order): `s02_900`, `s02_1200`, `s02_1500`, `s02_1800`.
**The resume proof (real flights, in the round):** before the searches, the command runs a short real search twice from `network-Wip` on 2 + 2 train ids at 40 s, 12 iterations with state every 4: once straight through, and once **in a child process killed at iteration 6** and resumed from its state file by a second child. The two results (final weights, train and held-out values, accepted count, lost flights, draws) must be identical; the pair is recorded in the result file and read by the check. The searches themselves are run through the same resumable code; if the container restarts, the run is started again and continues.
**The audit:** ids **32200-32399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s. Arms (11): `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; the law of the price `q02` and H27's 600-iteration champion `m02` (`brain/rig-results/head-to-head27.json`); H29's 2400-iteration champion `x02_2400` (`head-to-head29.json`, the third entry of `fresh.nets`); H30's 900-iteration champion `y02_900` (`head-to-head30.json`, the third entry of `fresh.nets`); the four new champions. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score (all three prices in the table): per id (dark score' + lit score')/2 - 0.02 x (dark kW + lit kW)/2, a lost run scoring 0.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; seven parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id on the penalised priced score at 0.02.**
**Part A:** `THE 1800 CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> H30'S 900 CHAMPION` (`s02_1800` against `y02_900`). **Part B:** `... H27'S 600 CHAMPION` (`m02`). **Part C:** `... H29'S 2400 CHAMPION` (`x02_2400`). **Part D:** `... THE LAW OF THE PRICE` (`q02`). **Part E:** `... C02`. **Part F:** `LOST SUBS IN THE S02 CHAMPIONS: <those of s02_900, s02_1200, s02_1500, s02_1800 that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part G:** `HELD-OUT VALUE RISES AT EVERY CHECKPOINT: <YES or NO>`: the group champion's held-out value strictly larger at 1200 than at 900, at 1500 than at 1200 and at 1800 than at 1500. (A to G: seven.) The resume proof is a CHECK (H), not a verdict part: if it fails the verdict is not weighed.

### 3. The checks
(fourteen rows; a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each seed once, each with 4 checkpoints of 963 finite weights; **the 900-iteration checkpoint of every search equals H30's stored P02 search of the same seed to the last digit (weights, train and held-out values, accepted count)**; the train set is the 72 ids, disjoint from the held-out ids, from the audit ids and from every earlier audit (to 31399); the first scored candidate of every search equals `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 ids at 0.02; each champion at each checkpoint is the best held-out penalised value of its group at that checkpoint; `c02`, `wip`, `q02`, `m02`, `x02_2400` and `y02_900` are the registered ones (from the stored files, compared with the champions the stored searches name by their rules); **a resumed search equals an uninterrupted one in every recorded field, on the real pair of the proof above**.

### 4. The lines
`RESUME PROOF`: the two results side by side and whether they are equal, the iteration the child was killed at and the draws counted; `SEARCH S02`: start value, then train and held-out value and accepted count at each checkpoint; the champion seeds per checkpoint; `RESUMES`: for each search how many times it was resumed (0 unless a restart happened) and from which iterations; `GENERALISATION GAP`; `AUDIT`; `LOST`; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED`; `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the L1 distances of each new champion from `wip` and from `y02_900`; the unpenalised priced gain.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: TIES 65, BEATS 25, IS BEHIND 10. Part B: TIES 55, BEATS 25, IS BEHIND 20. Part C: IS BEHIND 50, TIES 45, BEATS 5 (H30's 900 champion was 0.0122 behind it). Part D: IS BEHIND 65, TIES 33, BEATS 2. Part E: IS BEHIND 65, TIES 33, BEATS 2 (H30's was 0.0183 behind `c02`). Part F: NONE 50 percent. Part G: YES 25 percent (the 0.02 searches were flat at 900). Check H, a resumed search equals an uninterrupted one on the real pair: 95 percent (what could break it is a draw the counting wrapper misses). The first 900 iterations reproduce H30's P02 rows exactly: 90 percent. The champion's gap at 1800 stays below H29's 0.0665 (0.02): 80 percent. A container restart interrupts these searches at least once: 20 percent (the last two long runs: one in two).

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead32.mjs` (the resumable search with the counting generator, the state record, `resume`, and the atomic write handed in as a function so a gate can kill it between the temporary write and the rename; champions per checkpoint; the 11 arms; the audit; readings; the seven-part verdict; the fourteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead32Cmd.mjs` (the search that resumes from state files and skips finished seeds, the real kill-and-resume proof, `fly`; every input and output path named for H32 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead32-selfcheck.mjs`, **with rows that matter more than usual:** the search is identical to H30's `longSearch` (and so to H31's) at every checkpoint on scripted flights; a search stopped at an iteration k and resumed equals an uninterrupted one in every field for every k in a scripted run, including k at a checkpoint, k at a state boundary and k with a crash in the score; the draws are counted exactly (a generator drawing a variable number per iteration); a corrupt, truncated, wrong-seed, wrong-group, wrong-version or future state file is refused and named; a kill between the temporary write and the rename leaves the previous state readable; and a serial mutation pass in ONE worktree. State files are `brain/rig-results/head-to-head32-state-<seed>.json`, committed as "resumable; not a result" whenever the Stop hook asks. Cost: the searches are as long as H31's (1800 iterations at 0.02 on 72 ids, about 5.2 to 5.6 hours, four in parallel), the proof about 2 minutes, the flight about 10 minutes.

### 7. What this round does NOT say
It does not say that a resumed search is the same as an uninterrupted one in general: it says so for the sizes and the kill point the round tests, on this code. It does not say 1800 iterations is the limit at 0.02, or that more iterations is the right lever (H31 found it was not at 0.04). It does not cover 0.005, other penalties or a rotating train set. Four seeds are a champion of four, and H31 showed that champion can cost 0.0165.

## Round H32, RESULT (2026-11-10): THE RESUMABLE SEARCH IS PROVEN ON REAL FLIGHTS; AT 0.02 THE 1800-ITERATION CHAMPION TIES c02 AND THE LAW OF THE PRICE FOR THE FIRST TIME -- BUT ITS NEIGHBOURING CHECKPOINTS DO NOT, AND IT LOST 2 RUNS
Searched by `node tools/ship/headToHead32Cmd.mjs search --workers 4` (4 resumable (1+1)-ES searches of 1800 iterations from `network-Wip` on the 72 train ids at price 0.02, H27's penalised objective and seeds, checkpoints at 900 / 1200 / 1500 / 1800; 19,365 to 19,399 s each on 4 workers, 5.4 hours of wall clock; state saved every 50 iterations and at each checkpoint) and flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead32Cmd.mjs fly --parts 4` on ids 32200-32399, 160 s, dark and lit, read once (data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head32.json`, `head-to-head32-search.jsonl`, `head-to-head32-proof.json`, state files `head-to-head32-state-<seed>.json`). **All 14 checks PASS, among them that the 900-iteration checkpoint of every search equals H30's stored P02 search of the same seed to the last digit** (`s02_900` is H30's `y02_900` bit for bit: L1 0.00) **and that a resumed search equals an uninterrupted one on the real pair**: a child killed by SIGKILL after iteration 6 and resumed from the state at 4 gave 93 draws against 93, identical final weights, start value, lost flights and both checkpoints. **No container restart happened in this run (RESUMES: every seed never resumed), so the resume code was proven by the kill-and-resume pair and was not used in anger by the searches.**

**The verdict, as printed (7 parts):** THE 1800 CHAMPION BEATS H30'S 900 CHAMPION; THE 1800 CHAMPION BEATS H27'S 600 CHAMPION; THE 1800 CHAMPION TIES H29'S 2400 CHAMPION; THE 1800 CHAMPION TIES THE LAW OF THE PRICE; THE 1800 CHAMPION TIES C02; LOST SUBS IN THE S02 CHAMPIONS: s02_1800 2; HELD-OUT VALUE RISES AT EVERY CHECKPOINT: NO.

**The searches.** Held-out penalised value at 900 / 1200 / 1500 / 1800: seed 26011 0.7494 / 0.7629 / 0.7473 / 0.7592; 26012 0.7490 / 0.7586 / 0.7491 / 0.7387; 26013 0.7421 / 0.7381 / 0.7403 / 0.7401; 26014 0.7540 / 0.7587 / 0.7653 / 0.7558 (start value 0.7316 on the train ids). Train rises to 0.8086 in seed 26011 (0.7868 at 900) and to 0.7910 to 0.7941 in the other three. Accepted counts at 1800 run 166 to 263; lost flights among a search's evaluations 195 to 661. **The group champion is seed 26014 at 900 (0.7540), 26011 at 1200 (0.7629), 26014 at 1500 (0.7653) and 26011 at 1800 (0.7592): the held-out value does not rise at every checkpoint, and its best is at 1500.** Generalisation gap of the champion: 0.0357 / 0.0384 / 0.0252 / 0.0494 (H30's 900 champion 0.0357, H29's 2400 champion 0.0665).

**The pairings** (penalised priced score at 0.02, paired by id). `s02_1800` minus `y02_900` (H30's 900) +0.0123 (se 0.0038; better 152, worse 48) BETTER; minus `m02` (H27's 600) +0.0133 (0.0039; 149, 51) BETTER; minus `x02_2400` (H29's 2400) +0.0063 (0.0038; 132, 68) TIES; minus `q02` (the law of the price) +0.0001 (0.0042; 122, 78) TIES; minus `c02` -0.0012 (0.0038; 111, 89) TIES. Every checkpoint against `c02`: `s02_900` -0.0134 (0.0031; 66, 134) IS BEHIND, `s02_1200` -0.0031 (0.0030; 90, 110) TIES, `s02_1500` -0.0133 (0.0031; 64, 136) IS BEHIND, `s02_1800` -0.0012 TIES. Penalised priced gain at 0.02: `c02` +0.0871; `q02` +0.0857; `s02_1800` +0.0859; `s02_1200` +0.0840; `x02_2400` +0.0796; `y02_900` and `s02_900` +0.0736; `s02_1500` +0.0737; `m02` +0.0726 (at 0.04: `c02` +0.0740, `s02_1800` +0.0728, `q02` +0.0682). Extra power (mean of dark and lit): `c02` +0.65 kW, `s02_1800` +0.65, `q02` +0.88. **Lost runs of 400 in the audit: `s02_1800` 2 (both dark: ids 32263 and 32392), every other arm 0.**

**Not in the verdict, computed afterwards from the stored flight and said as such:** `s02_1800` minus `s02_1500` +0.0122 (se 0.0038) BETTER; minus `s02_1200` +0.0019 (0.0031) TIES; minus `s02_900` +0.0123 (0.0038) BETTER; `s02_1200` minus `s02_900` +0.0104 (0.0025) BETTER; `s02_1500` minus `s02_900` +0.0001 (0.0003) TIES; `s02_1800` minus the autopilot +0.0859 (0.0061; better 187, worse 13). The earlier 0.02 champions against `c02` on these ids: `q02` -0.0013 (0.0020) TIES, `x02_2400` -0.0075 (0.0027) IS BEHIND, `m02` -0.0145 (0.0025) IS BEHIND, `y02_900` -0.0134 (0.0031) IS BEHIND. **So at 0.02 the 900-iteration network is behind `c02` (H30's finding, reproduced), the 1200 and 1800 champions tie it, and the 1500 champion is behind it again by the same amount as the 900 one: the audit value of the champion goes -0.0134, -0.0031, -0.0133, -0.0012 over the four checkpoints, which is not a trend.**

I expected the 0.02 networks to stay behind `c02` and the law, as H30's did; the 1800 champion does not, on this audit.

**What this does NOT say.** It does not say 1800 iterations at 0.02 reaches `c02`: ONE champion of four seeds, picked by a noisy held-out value (0.7592 here, below the 0.7653 and 0.7629 of two earlier checkpoints), ties it on 200 ids, where TIES means a difference no larger than about 0.0076 (two standard errors), and its neighbours at 1500 and 900 are behind by 0.0133. A second audit of the same networks, or other seeds, might read differently; none was run. It does not say the new champion is safe: it lost 2 of 400 runs and every other arm lost none, and its generalisation gap (0.0494) is the largest of the four checkpoints. It does not say a resumed search is the same as an uninterrupted one in general: the proof kills once, at iteration 6 of a 12-iteration search, and the 5.4-hour searches ran without a restart, so the resume code has not been used on a long run. The afterwards pairings were not registered. It does not cover 0.005 or other penalties.

## Round H33, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built or flown): A SECOND AUDIT OF H32'S NETWORKS ON FRESH IDS -- DOES THE 1800 CHAMPION'S TIE WITH c02 HOLD, AND DOES IT LOSE SUBS AGAIN
### 1. Why
H32's one audit read the 1800-iteration champion TIES `c02` (-0.0012, se 0.0038) and TIES the law of the price, but the same audit read the 900 and 1500 champions BEHIND `c02` by 0.013, the champion lost 2 of 400 runs where every other arm lost none, and the champion at each checkpoint came from a best-of-four held-out pick that is noisy at this resolution (H31's 1200 champion was a worse network than its held-out value said). One audit of 200 ids cannot say whether the 1800 tie is the network or the ids. This round **flies the same networks again on 200 fresh ids** and also **pools the two audits**; no search is run, nothing is trained, and the networks are the ones H32 stored.
> (A) Does `s02_1800` still TIE `c02` on fresh ids? (B) Still TIE the law of the price? (C) Is it still better than the 1500-iteration champion, the neighbour H32 read 0.0122 behind it? (D) Still tie the 1200 one? (E) Does it lose runs again? (F) On both audits pooled (400 ids), where does it stand against `c02`? (G) Does H32's pattern around `c02` repeat: the 1500 champion behind, the 1800 one not behind?

### 2. The audit and the arms

## Round H35, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H33's result is read, H34's search was still running and nothing of it has been read): A ROTATING TRAIN SET AT 0.02, WITH THE SAME NUMBER OF FLIGHTS AS H32'S SEARCH
### 1. Why
Every search of this series has trained on the same 72 ids: the held-out value moves little after 900 iterations while the train value creeps up (H31, H32), and the generalisation gap of the H32 champion at 1800 (0.0494) is the largest of its four checkpoints. The champion at each checkpoint zigzags against `c02` (behind at 900 and 1500, tied at 1200 and 1800: H32, replicated in H33). A fixed train set can be overfitted; a **rotating** one cannot be memorised in the same way. This round **rotates the train ids** and keeps everything else of H32's search, spending **the same number of flights per seed**, and pairs each rotating network with the fixed-set network of the **same seed** (H32's).
> (A) Does the rotating group's champion tie `c02`? (B) The law of the price? (C) Is it better than H32's champion? (D) Seed by seed, do the rotating networks beat their fixed-set twins? (E) Do they lose subs? (F) Is the champion's generalisation gap smaller than H32's? (G) How many of the four rotating networks are not behind `c02`?

### 2. The rotating search, the searches and the arms
**The train pool.** Twelve ranges of 24 ids: H27's `26000-26023`, H30's two halves `30000-30023` and `30024-30047` (so the old 72 ids are three of them), and nine new ranges `36000-36023, 36024-36047, ..., 36192-36215` (216 new ids; 288 ids in the pool). The held-out ids stay 26100-26139.
**The search** is H32's loop and generator stream (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, state saved every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, resumable) with ONE change: **the train ids are one range of the pool for a block of 150 iterations**, block k using range k mod 12 of the order above (26000-23, 30000-23, 30024-47, 36000-23, ..., 36192-215, then round again). At the start of a block the parent is scored on the block's ids (its value on them replaces the best value), and within the block a candidate is kept when its value on the block's ids is at least that of the parent. **5400 iterations (36 blocks, three passes through the pool): 24 ids x 5400 iterations = 72 ids x 1800 iterations, H32's flights per seed.** Checkpoints at **2700, 3600, 4500 and 5400** (the flights of H32's 900 / 1200 / 1500 / 1800). At a checkpoint the record holds the weights, the accepted count, the held-out penalised value (the 40 held-out ids), and, as the **train value, the penalised value on the old 72 ids** (so the gap is comparable with H32's: train 72 ids minus held-out). Seeds **26011, 26012, 26013, 26014** (H27 to H32's, so each rotating search has a fixed-set twin), group **U02**. Champion of the group at a checkpoint: the best held-out value of the four (the first of a tie in seed order).
