### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead35.mjs` (the rotating, resumable search written as H32's loop with the block structure: the pool, the block's ids, the parent re-scored at each block start, the counted generator and state with the block position; the champion rule; the twelve arms; the audit; the readings; the seven-part verdict; the fifteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead35Cmd.mjs` (`search` with state files named for H35, resuming and skipping finished seeds; `fly`; a smoke mode; every path named for H35 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead35-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that **a stopped and resumed search equals an uninterrupted one at every saved state including block boundaries and with a crash in the score**, that with one block (the pool reduced to the old range and a block as long as the run) the rotating search equals H32's `longSearch` on the same range at every checkpoint (the identity row), that the block's ids are as registered, that the parent is re-scored at each block start and a candidate is compared with the parent on the same ids, the champion rule and the twin counts in every branch, and a serial mutation pass whose survivors become rows or are declared equivalent. The search takes about 5.4 hours on 4 workers (H32's flights per seed; the parent re-scores add 36 batches of 24 ids a seed).

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say rotation is the cause of any difference: the rotating search also differs from H32's in its number of iterations (5400 against 1800), its step count per scored batch and its candidate stream, and H32's searches are four draws, as these are. A twin pairing across the same seed is not an independent pairing, since the streams diverge from the first accepted candidate. It does not say 5400 iterations is the right length, it does not cover 0.005 or other penalties, and it says nothing about H34's result. The gap compares a train value on the old 72 ids, which the rotating search flew in three of twelve blocks, with a held-out value; both searches have seen those ids.

## Round H36, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H34's search and H35's search were not finished and nothing of either has been read): A POPULATION SEARCH AT 0.02, WITH THE CANDIDATE EVALUATIONS OF H32'S SEARCH
### 1. Why
Every search in this series is a (1+1)-ES: one parent, one candidate at a time, kept when at least as good. Its held-out value flattens after about 900 iterations while the train value creeps, and the champion zigzags against `c02` from checkpoint to checkpoint (H32, H33). A search with a **population** keeps several parents at once, mixes them, and chooses survivors among many candidates, so it can hold on to more than one direction and it does not depend on one parent's luck. This round runs a **(4+16) evolution strategy with crossover** from `network-Wip` at 0.02 on the same 72 train ids, spending **the same number of candidate evaluations per seed as H32's search (about 1800)**, and pairs each population network with the (1+1) network of the **same seed** (H32's).
> (A) Does the population champion tie `c02`? (B) The law of the price? (C) Is it better than H32's champion? (D) Seed by seed, do the population networks beat their (1+1) twins? (E) Do they lose subs? (F) Is the champion's generalisation gap smaller than H32's? (G) How many of the four are not behind `c02`?

### 2. The population search, the searches and the arms
**The search.** Parents: 4 networks of 963 weights, all `network-Wip` at the start (rounded to float32), scored once on the 72 train ids (H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s). A **generation**: 16 offspring, each made from the parents by one of two rules decided by one draw of the seeded generator: **half the time** (draw below 0.5) a copy of one parent chosen by a draw; **otherwise** a uniform crossover of two different parents chosen by draws (each weight taken from the first parent with probability 0.5), and then in both cases `PS.mutate` (1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, the series' mutation) on the child; every offspring is scored on the 72 train ids. **Survivors:** the 4 best of the 4 parents and the 16 offspring by train value, **parents first among equals** (so a tie keeps the older network), in order of value. **112 generations = 1792 candidate evaluations** (plus the first parent's: 1793 against H32's 1801), checkpoints after generations **56, 75, 94 and 112** (896, 1200, 1504 and 1792 evaluations: H32's 900 / 1200 / 1500 / 1800). The network at a checkpoint is the **best parent** (first of a tie); the record holds its weights, its train value, its held-out value (the 40 held-out ids 26100-26139), the accepted count (offspring that entered the parents), the number of those that came from crossover, and the mean pairwise L1 distance among the four parents. State (all four parents with their values, the generation, the draws of the counted generator, the checkpoints so far) is saved **every 5 generations and at every checkpoint**, atomically, and the search **resumes** from it as H32's does. Seeds **26011, 26012, 26013, 26014** (H27 to H32's, so each population search has a (1+1) twin), group **V02**. Champion of the group at a checkpoint: the best held-out value of the four (the first of a tie in seed order).
**The audit:** ids **37200-37399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit and the rotating pool of H35 (36000-36215). **Arms (12):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; **H32's four 1800-iteration networks each by itself**, `s02_26011` (H32's champion), `s02_26012`, `s02_26013`, `s02_26014`; and the four final population networks each by itself, `v02_26011` to `v02_26014`. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score (all three prices in the table). `v02_champ` is whichever of the four the held-out values name.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; seven parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id at 0.02.**
**Part A:** `THE POPULATION CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02`. **Part B:** `... THE LAW OF THE PRICE`. **Part C:** `... H32'S CHAMPION` (against `s02_26011`). **Part D:** `TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `v02_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>`). **Part E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE V02 NETWORKS: <those that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part F:** `THE CHAMPION'S GAP IS SMALLER THAN H32'S: <YES or NO>` (the champion's train value at the last checkpoint minus its held-out value, against H32's champion's 0.0494 as stored; both train values are on the same 72 ids). **Part G:** `POPULATION NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4`. (A to G: seven.)

### 3. The checks
(sixteen rows; a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each seed once, each with four checkpoints of 963 finite weights, and the flown networks are the seeds' last-checkpoint weights; the evaluation counts are as registered (a scripted search flown by the gate's counting flight makes 1793 train evaluations, 16 a generation, and the checkpoints at generations 56, 75, 94, 112); the first parent's value equals `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 ids, computed in this round and equal to H32's stored start value to the last digit; the group champion is the best held-out value of the four at the last checkpoint; every checkpoint's accepted count is at least its crossover count and at most 16 times its generation; the four `s02` arms are the networks of H32's stored searches at 1800 and `s02_26011` is H32's champion; `c02`, `wip` and `q02` are the registered ones; the seeds are H32's four; **the search is resumable: H32's stored resume proof is identical, and the gate shows the H36 search stopped at every saved state and resumed equal to an uninterrupted one on scripted flights, including a stop at a checkpoint and one between two states.**

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH V02` (start value, train and held-out value, accepted and crossover counts and parent diversity at each checkpoint of each seed, lost flights); `RESUMES`; `CHAMPIONS PER CHECKPOINT`; `GENERALISATION GAP` (the champion at each checkpoint, each seed at the end, H32's champion); `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED` (champion against c02, q02, `s02_26011`; each `v02` against c02 and against its twin); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the L1 distances of each population network from `wip` and from its twin; the unpenalised priced gain; the audit means in rank order of all eight networks.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 30, BEATS 20. Part B: TIES 55, IS BEHIND 30, BEATS 15. Part C: TIES 55, IS BEHIND 25, BEATS 20. Part D: ahead count modal 2 (0 ahead: 15, 1: 25, 2: 30, 3: 20, 4: 10 percent). Part E: at least one network loses a run 60 percent. Part F: NO 60 percent (a population that chooses the best 4 of 20 each generation presses harder on the train ids). Part G: modal 2 (0: 15, 1: 25, 2: 30, 3: 20, 4: 10 percent). The champion's held-out value at the last checkpoint is above 0.7592, H32's best: 50 percent. Crossover offspring make up at least a quarter of the accepted ones: 70 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead36.mjs` (the resumable population search with the counted generator and a state that holds the whole population, the champion rule, the twelve arms, the audit, the readings, the seven-part verdict, the sixteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead36Cmd.mjs` (`search` with state files named for H36, resuming and skipping finished seeds; `fly`; a smoke mode; every path named for H36 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead36-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that **a stopped and resumed search equals an uninterrupted one at every saved state and with a crash in the score**, that with one parent and no crossover and one offspring a generation the search is H32's (1+1) loop exactly (the identity row, on scripted flights), the survivor rule (parents first among equals, best four of twenty, order by value), the crossover rule (a child's weights each from one of its two parents, the two distinct), the evaluation counts and the champion rule, and a serial mutation pass whose survivors become rows or are declared equivalent. The search takes about 5.4 hours on 4 workers (H32's evaluations per seed).

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say population or crossover is the cause of any difference: there is no (4+16) run without crossover and no (1+1) run with the same candidate stream, so the effect of population size and of recombination are not separated, and four seeds are four draws of a noisy search. A twin pairing across the same seed is not independent. It does not say 1792 evaluations is the right length, it does not cover 0.005 or other penalties, and it says nothing about H34's or H35's results. Parent diversity is reported, not tested.

## Round H37, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H34, H35 and H36 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read): A LARGER NETWORK -- TWICE THE HIDDEN NEURONS, STARTING EXACTLY AS `network-Wip`, SEARCHED AS H32'S WAS
### 1. Why
Every network in this series has the fly's Giant Fiber Circuit shape: 34 hidden neurons with 313 recurrent synapses, 963 weights (14 x 34 + 34 + 313 + 34 x 4 + 4). Its searches level off by about 900 iterations and the champion zigzags against `c02`; one candidate explanation no round has touched is that the network is simply **too small** to hold more than the law of the price plus a little. This round gives the search **a bigger network that starts as the same pilot**: the 34 fly neurons plus 34 new ones, wired in at the same density, with the new neurons' outputs at zero so that the larger net computes exactly what `network-Wip` computes until the search opens them. Then it runs H32's search on it, and pairs each larger network with the 963-weight network of the **same seed** (H32's).
> (A) Does the larger champion tie `c02`? (B) The law of the price? (C) Is it better than H32's champion? (D) Seed by seed, do the larger networks beat their 963-weight twins? (E) Do they lose subs? (F) Is the champion's generalisation gap smaller than H32's? (G) How many of the four are not behind `c02`?

### 2. The larger network, the searches and the arms
**The topology (WIDE).** 68 hidden neurons: the 34 of the fly circuit in their order (the real body ids of `brain/gfcTopology.mjs`), then 34 new neurons (synthetic ids 1 to 34, not cells of any connectome). Edges: the fly's 313 in their order, then every ordered pair (target, source) with target not equal to source and **at least one end a new neuron** is an edge **with probability 313/1122** (the fly core's density, 313 of its 34 x 33 pairs), each decided by one draw of `PS.mulberry(4243)` taken in order of target then source (the fish control used 4242). The edge count is read at build time and printed; it is about 1270 in all (about 958 new), and the weight count is about 2570 (14 x 68 + 68 + edges + 68 x 4 + 4). The pilot is `createSubPolicy` of this topology (the same code that makes the fly's and the fish's), flown through the tank as `pilotSubFish.mjs`'s circuits are (registered with `registerPilotKind`; if the board cannot take a kind the round stops and says so).
**The start (WIDE-Wip).** Every weight of `network-Wip` goes to its own place in the larger vector (feature to neuron, neuron bias, fly edge, neuron to output, output bias). **Everything that reads a new neuron is zero**: its outgoing recurrent edges and its weights to the four outputs. **Everything that feeds a new neuron is small and random**: its feature weights and its incoming recurrent edges are N(0, 0.3), drawn in index order by `PS.mulberry(4243)` after the edge draws; its bias is 0. Because the new neurons' activity reaches nothing, **the larger pilot computes exactly what `network-Wip` computes** (a check: on 50 random feature vectors the forward passes agree to 1e-9, and on real flights the larger start scores exactly as `network-Wip`, dark and lit, on 5 ids at 160 s).
**The searches (4).** H32's search, byte for byte the same loop on the larger weight vector (the (1+1)-ES, `mutate` of the larger policy: 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, resumable with state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint), **1800 iterations, checkpoints at 900, 1200, 1500 and 1800**, seeds **26011, 26012, 26013, 26014** (H32's, so each larger search has a 963-weight twin), group **W02**; the first parent's value equals `network-Wip`'s. Champion of the group at a checkpoint: the best held-out value of the four (the first of a tie in seed order); the networks flown are the 1800-iteration ones.
**The audit:** ids **38200-38399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (to 37399). **Arms (12):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; H32's four 1800-iteration networks each by itself, `s02_26011` (H32's champion) to `s02_26014`; and the four larger networks each by itself, `w02_26011` to `w02_26014`. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score (all three prices in the table).
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; seven parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id at 0.02.**
**Part A:** `THE LARGER CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02`. **Part B:** `... THE LAW OF THE PRICE`. **Part C:** `... H32'S CHAMPION` (against `s02_26011`). **Part D:** `TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `w02_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>`). **Part E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE W02 NETWORKS: <those that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part F:** `THE CHAMPION'S GAP IS SMALLER THAN H32'S: <YES or NO>` (train value on the 72 ids minus held-out value at 1800, against H32's champion's 0.0494 as stored). **Part G:** `LARGER NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4`. (A to G: seven.)

### 3. The checks
(sixteen rows, named in the library; a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each seed once, each with four checkpoints of finite weights of the larger length, and the flown networks are the seeds' 1800-iteration weights; **the topology is as registered** (68 neurons, the first 34 and 313 edges the fly's, no self-loop or repeated edge, every added edge touching a new neuron, the edge count equal to the one the draw gives and printed); **the start is function-preserving** (the forward passes of the larger start and `network-Wip` agree to 1e-9 on 50 random feature vectors; every weight that reads a new neuron is zero; the new inputs are the registered draw) **and flies as `network-Wip` does** (exactly, 5 ids, dark and lit, 160 s); the first parent's value equals `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 ids, computed in this round and equal to H32's stored start value to the last digit; the group champion is the best held-out value of the four at 1800; the four `s02` arms are the networks of H32's stored searches at 1800 and `s02_26011` is H32's champion; `c02`, `wip` and `q02` are the registered ones; the seeds are H32's four; the audit ids are disjoint from the train and held-out ids and past every earlier audit; **the search is resumable: H32's stored resume proof is identical, and the gate shows the H37 search stopped at every saved state and resumed equal to an uninterrupted one on scripted flights.**

### 4. The lines
`TOPOLOGY` (neurons, edges, new edges, weights, the draw's seeds); `SEARCH W02` (start value, train and held-out value and accepted count at each checkpoint of each seed, lost flights); `RESUMES`; `CHAMPIONS PER CHECKPOINT`; `GENERALISATION GAP`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED` (champion against c02, q02, `s02_26011`; each `w02` against c02 and its twin); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** how much of each larger network's L1 distance from its start lies in the new neurons' weights (the part of the search that used the new neurons) and in the old ones; the unpenalised priced gain; the audit means in rank order.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: TIES 45, IS BEHIND 35, BEATS 20. Part B: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 35, BEATS 15. Part C: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 35, BEATS 15. Part D: ahead count modal 2 (0 ahead: 20, 1: 25, 2: 30, 3: 15, 4: 10 percent). Part E: at least one network loses a run 60 percent. Part F: NO 65 percent (more weights to fit the same ids). Part G: modal 1 or 2 (0: 20, 1: 30, 2: 25, 3: 15, 4: 10 percent). The champion's held-out value at 1800 is above 0.7592, H32's best: 40 percent. The larger start is exactly `network-Wip` (both checks): 95 percent. Some weight of a new neuron ends nonzero in every larger network (the search uses the new neurons): 85 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead37.mjs` (the WIDE topology and its start, built from `brain/gfcTopology.mjs` and `createSubPolicy` with no change to any file under `brain/`; the search, which is H32's loop on the larger policy; the champion rule, the twelve arms, the audit, the readings, the seven-part verdict, the sixteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead37Cmd.mjs` (`search` with state files named for H37, resuming and skipping finished seeds; `fly`, which registers the larger circuit with the tank before flying and runs the start-flies-as-Wip check; a smoke mode; every path named for H37 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead37-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that **the topology is as registered** (counts, density, the first block, no loops or repeats, the draw), that the start is function-preserving on random inputs (and that a one-weight change in a new neuron's output makes it differ), that the search equals H32's loop at every checkpoint when the larger network is replaced by the fly's own (the identity row), that a stopped and resumed search equals an uninterrupted one at every saved state, the champion rule and the twin counts in every branch, and a serial mutation pass whose survivors become rows or are declared equivalent. The search takes about 6 hours on 4 workers (a larger forward pass costs more per flight than H32's: measured at build and printed).

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say width is the cause of any difference: the new neurons are wired at random, so size and random extra wiring are not separated (there is no run with the fly's 34 neurons and the same number of extra random weights, and no run with a wider plain net), and the new wiring is ONE draw (seed 4243). The new neurons are synthetic, not cells of any connectome. It does not say 68 is the right size, that the search can use a larger vector as well as it uses a small one (per-weight mutation effect is diluted, 1 to 4 weights of about 2570), or that 1800 iterations is enough for it. Four seeds are four draws; a twin pairing across the same seed is not independent; it does not cover 0.005 or other penalties and says nothing about H34's, H35's or H36's results.

## Round H38, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H34, H35, H36 and H37 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read): A SMALLER NETWORK -- HALF THE HIDDEN NEURONS, PRUNED BY A RULE OR DRAWN AT RANDOM, SEARCHED AS H32'S WAS
### 1. Why
H37 asks whether the network is too small; this round asks the other way: **is it too big?** Its 963 weights are searched one to four at a time, the searches level off by about 900 iterations, and the champion's generalisation gap is large (0.0494 at 1800 in H32). A network with half the hidden neurons has about a third of the weights, so each mutation is a larger share of it and there is less to over-fit; the price is that it cannot start as the same pilot. This round cuts the fly circuit to **17 of its 34 hidden neurons in two ways**: **by a rule** (the 17 neurons that carry the most weight out of the circuit in `network-Wip`) and **at random** (17 neurons drawn by a seeded generator). The random subset is the control that separates the effect of SIZE from the effect of choosing WELL. Both start from `network-Wip`'s own weights on the neurons and edges they keep, so the start is a damaged `network-Wip` whose value is read and printed; both are then searched exactly as H32's was, and each network is paired with the 963-weight network of the **same seed** (H32's).
> (A) Does the pruned champion tie `c02`? (B) The law of the price? (C) Is it better than H32's champion? (D) Seed by seed, do the pruned networks beat their 963-weight twins? (E) Do they lose subs? (F) Is the champion's generalisation gap smaller than H32's? (G) How many of the four are not behind `c02`? (H) Does the pruned group beat the random-subset group?

### 2. The smaller networks, the searches and the arms
**The pruned topology (X).** The importance of a hidden neuron of the fly circuit in `network-Wip` is the sum of the absolute values of its weights **out of** the circuit: its four output weights and its outgoing recurrent edges (the edges of the fly circuit with it as the source). The 17 neurons of greatest importance are kept (a tie goes to the lower index), in their original order. **The random topology (Y).** 17 of the 34 neurons chosen by a partial Fisher-Yates shuffle drawn from `PS.mulberry(4244)` (the wide circuit used 4243, the fish control 4242), kept in their original order. In both, the edges kept are those of the fly circuit with **both ends kept**, renumbered; the weights of the start are `network-Wip`'s on what is kept (its feature weights and bias of each kept neuron, its kept edges, its output weights from each kept neuron, the four output biases). The pilot is `createSubPolicy` of the topology (the code that makes the fly's, the fish's and the wide circuit), registered with the tank by `registerPilotKind` as H37's is. The kept edge counts and weight counts are read at build time and printed (about 17 x 14 + 17 + edges kept + 17 x 4 + 4, so roughly 340 plus the edges: about a third of 963).
**The start.** Not `network-Wip`'s function: removing half of the neurons changes the pilot. **The start values of X and of Y on the 72 train ids at 0.02 are read, compared with `network-Wip`'s (0.7316 on those ids, H32), and printed**; a start whose flights lose a run is reported as such.
**The searches (8).** H32's search, byte for byte the same loop on the smaller weight vector (the (1+1)-ES, `mutate`: 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, resumable with state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint), **1800 iterations, checkpoints at 900, 1200, 1500 and 1800**, seeds **26011 to 26014** (H32's, so each smaller search has a 963-weight twin), groups **X02** (pruned) and **Y02** (random). The first parent's value is the start value above (a check). Champion of a group at a checkpoint: the best held-out value of its four (the first of a tie in seed order); the networks flown are the 1800-iteration ones.
**The audit:** ids **39200-39399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (to 38399). **Arms (16):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; H32's four 1800-iteration networks `s02_26011` (H32's champion) to `s02_26014`; the four pruned `x02_26011` to `x02_26014`; the four random-subset `y02_26011` to `y02_26014`. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score (all three prices in the table).
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; eight parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id at 0.02.**
**Part A:** `THE PRUNED CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02`. **Part B:** `... THE LAW OF THE PRICE`. **Part C:** `... H32'S CHAMPION` (against `s02_26011`). **Part D:** `TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `x02_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>`). **Part E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE SMALLER NETWORKS: <those of the eight that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part F:** `THE PRUNED CHAMPION'S GAP IS SMALLER THAN H32'S: <YES or NO>` (train value on the 72 ids minus held-out value at 1800, against H32's champion's 0.0494). **Part G:** `PRUNED NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4`. **Part H:** `THE PRUNED CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> THE RANDOM CHAMPION` (`x02_champ` against `y02_champ`, each the best of its group by held-out value). (A to H: eight.)

### 3. The checks
(eighteen rows; a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 8 searches (each group's four seeds once) with four checkpoints of finite weights of the group's length, and the flown networks are the seeds' 1800-iteration weights; **the topologies are as registered** (17 neurons each; the kept neurons of X are the 17 of greatest importance in `network-Wip` by the rule, computed again independently; those of Y are the registered draw, read again independently; the edges are exactly the fly's with both ends kept, renumbered; no self-loop or repeat); **the starts are `network-Wip`'s weights on what is kept** (each read back by position) and fly through the tank; the first parent of every search has the start value computed in this round; each group's champion is the best held-out value of its four at 1800; the four `s02` arms are the networks of H32's stored searches at 1800 and `s02_26011` is H32's champion; `c02`, `wip` and `q02` are the registered ones; the seeds are H32's four; the audit ids are past every earlier audit and disjoint from the train and held-out ids; **the search is resumable: H32's stored resume proof is identical, and the gate shows the H38 search stopped at every saved state and resumed equal to an uninterrupted one on scripted flights.**

### 4. The lines
`TOPOLOGY` (neurons kept in each, the edges and weights, the importances of the kept and dropped neurons for X, the draw's seed for Y); `STARTS` (the start value of X and of Y against `network-Wip`'s, lost runs among them); `SEARCH X02` and `SEARCH Y02` (start value, train and held-out value and accepted count at each checkpoint of each seed, lost flights); `RESUMES`; `CHAMPIONS PER CHECKPOINT` of each group; `GENERALISATION GAP`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED` (the pruned champion against c02, q02, `s02_26011` and the random champion; each `x02` against c02 and its twin; each `y02` against its twin); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the recovery of each search (start value to the 1800 train value) and the seconds a search took against H32's; the unpenalised priced gain; the audit means in rank order of all twelve networks.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: IS BEHIND 55, TIES 35, BEATS 10. Part B: IS BEHIND 65, TIES 30, BEATS 5. Part C: IS BEHIND 55, TIES 40, BEATS 5. Part D: ahead count modal 0 or 1 (0 ahead: 35, 1: 30, 2: 20, 3: 10, 4: 5 percent). Part E: at least one network of the eight loses a run 50 percent. Part F: YES 60 percent (a third of the weights). Part G: modal 0 or 1 (0: 35, 1: 30, 2: 20, 3: 10, 4: 5 percent). Part H: TIES 50, BEATS 35, IS BEHIND 15 (the rule should help, but the circuit is dense in useful neurons). The pruned start's value on the 72 ids is more than 0.02 below `network-Wip`'s 0.7316: 80 percent; the random start's is lower than the pruned start's: 65 percent. The pruned champion's held-out value at 1800 is above 0.7592, H32's best: 15 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead38.mjs` (the two topologies and their starts, built from `brain/gfcTopology.mjs`, `network-Wip` and `createSubPolicy` with no change to any file under `brain/`; H32's loop on the smaller policies; the champion rule for each group, the sixteen arms, the audit, the readings, the eight-part verdict, the eighteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead38Cmd.mjs` (`search` with state files named for H38, resuming and skipping finished seeds; `fly`, which registers the smaller circuits with the tank before flying; a smoke mode; every path named for H38 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead38-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that the topologies are as registered (the importance ranking on a hand-made weight vector with ties, the shuffle draw, the edge filtering and renumbering on a small example), that the starts carry `network-Wip`'s weights by position, that the loop equals a reference loop written out in the gate, that a stopped and resumed search equals an uninterrupted one at every saved state, the champion rule, the twin counts and the pairing of the two champions in every branch, and a serial mutation pass whose survivors become rows or are declared equivalent. Eight searches of a smaller net take about as long as four of H32's (each flight is cheaper; measured at build and printed).

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say size is the cause of any difference between X and `network-Wip`'s twins: pruning also damages the start, and the search then repairs it, so a difference could be the repair; the random control is ONE draw of 17 (seed 4244), so a difference between X and Y is a hint about choosing well and not a measurement of it. The importance rule is one rule among many, it does not say 17 is the right size, that the weights of the dropped neurons were useless (they were useful: the start value says what dropping them cost), or that a network trained at this size from scratch would do the same. Four seeds are four draws; a twin pairing across the same seed is not independent; it does not cover 0.005 or other penalties and says nothing about H34, H35, H36 or H37.

## Round H39, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H34 to H38 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read): A DIFFERENT MUTATION STEP SIZE -- SIGMA 0.1 AND SIGMA 0.9 AGAINST THE SERIES' 0.3
### 1. Why
Every search in this series moves one to four weights by a Gaussian step of **sigma 0.3** and keeps the candidate when it is at least as good. That number was chosen in H25's era and has never been varied since: the accepted counts at 1800 iterations are 166 to 263 of 1800 (H32), and the searches level off by about 900 iterations. If 0.3 is too large, most candidates overshoot and the search stalls near its start; if too small, it crawls and over-fits the train ids slowly. This round runs H32's search **with the step size changed and nothing else**: **sigma 0.1** (a third) and **sigma 0.9** (three times), each with H32's four seeds, so each network has a sigma-0.3 twin (H32's) of the same seed.
> (A) Does the small-step champion tie `c02`? (B) The large-step champion? (C) Is the small-step champion better than H32's? (D) The large-step champion? (E) Seed by seed, do the small-step networks beat their sigma-0.3 twins? (F) The large-step networks? (G) Do any lose subs? (H) Which step size wins?

### 2. The searches and the arms
**The searches (8).** H32's search, byte for byte: the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` of 1 to 4 weights, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, 1800 iterations, checkpoints at 900, 1200, 1500 and 1800, resumable with state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, held-out ids 26100-26139, seeds **26011, 26012, 26013, 26014** (H32's) -- **with only the step size changed**: group **A02 at sigma 0.1** and group **B02 at sigma 0.9**. The first parent's value is `network-Wip`'s on the 72 ids (a check, equal to H32's stored start value). The accepted counts and the number of lost flights among a search's evaluations are reported (the acceptance rate is the quickest reading of what a step size does). Champion of a group at a checkpoint: the best held-out value of its four (the first of a tie in seed order); the networks flown are the 1800-iteration ones.
**The audit:** ids **40200-40399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (to 39399). **Arms (16):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; H32's four sigma-0.3 networks `s02_26011` (H32's champion) to `s02_26014`; the four small-step `a02_26011` to `a02_26014`; the four large-step `b02_26011` to `b02_26014`. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score (all three prices in the table).
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; eight parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id at 0.02.**
**Part A:** `THE SMALL-STEP CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02`. **Part B:** `THE LARGE-STEP CHAMPION <...> C02`. **Part C:** `THE SMALL-STEP CHAMPION <...> H32'S CHAMPION` (against `s02_26011`). **Part D:** `THE LARGE-STEP CHAMPION <...> H32'S CHAMPION`. **Part E:** `SMALL-STEP TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `a02_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>`). **Part F:** `LARGE-STEP TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED`. **Part G:** `LOST SUBS IN THE STEP-SIZE NETWORKS: <those of the eight that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part H:** `THE SMALL-STEP CHAMPION <...> THE LARGE-STEP CHAMPION`. (A to H: eight.)

### 3. The checks
(eighteen rows; a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 8 searches (each group's four seeds once) with four checkpoints of 963 finite weights, and the flown networks are the seeds' 1800-iteration weights; **the step sizes are as registered** (every state file and record carries its group's sigma, 0.1 and 0.9, and the gate shows the H39 search equal to H32's `longSearch` at every checkpoint when sigma is 0.3 and different when it is not); the first parent of every search has `network-Wip`'s value on the 72 ids, computed in this round and equal to H32's stored start value to the last digit; each group's champion is the best held-out value of its four at 1800; the four `s02` arms are the networks of H32's stored searches at 1800 and `s02_26011` is H32's champion; `c02`, `wip` and `q02` are the registered ones; the seeds are H32's four; the audit ids are past every earlier audit and disjoint from the train and held-out ids; **the search is resumable: H32's stored resume proof is identical, a state file whose sigma is not its group's is refused, and the gate shows the H39 search stopped at every saved state and resumed equal to an uninterrupted one on scripted flights.**

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH A02` and `SEARCH B02` (start value, train and held-out value and accepted count at each checkpoint of each seed, lost flights); `ACCEPTANCE` (accepted counts at 1800 of the eight searches against H32's four, as rates of 1800); `RESUMES`; `CHAMPIONS PER CHECKPOINT` of each group; `GENERALISATION GAP`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `PAIRED` (each champion against c02, q02, `s02_26011` and the other champion; each `a02` and `b02` against its twin); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the L1 distance of each network from `wip` and from its twin; the unpenalised priced gain; the audit means in rank order of all twelve networks.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 35, BEATS 15. Part B: TIES 40, IS BEHIND 45, BEATS 15. Part C: TIES 55, IS BEHIND 30, BEATS 15. Part D: IS BEHIND 50, TIES 40, BEATS 10. Part E: ahead count modal 1 or 2 (0 ahead: 15, 1: 30, 2: 30, 3: 15, 4: 10 percent). Part F: ahead count modal 0 or 1 (0: 35, 1: 30, 2: 20, 3: 10, 4: 5 percent). Part G: at least one of the eight loses a run 60 percent (a large step is likelier to throw a candidate out of the flying envelope, and the search keeps only candidates at least as good). Part H: TIES 45, THE SMALL-STEP CHAMPION BEATS 30, IS BEHIND 25. The acceptance order at 1800 is small step above sigma 0.3 above large step in the mean of each group: 85 percent. The small-step champion's held-out value at 1800 is above 0.7592, H32's best: 35 percent; the large-step's: 25 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 20 percent (eight searches take about eleven hours).

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead39.mjs` (H32's `longSearch` called with the step size handed in and the group relabelled, its state saved with the group and the sigma and refused on load when either is not the group's; the champion rule for each group, the sixteen arms, the audit, the readings, the eight-part verdict, the eighteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead39Cmd.mjs` (`search` with state files named for H39, group and seed, resuming and skipping finished searches; `fly`; a smoke mode; every path named for H39 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead39-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that the search equals H32's at every checkpoint when sigma is 0.3 and equals a reference loop written out in the gate at 0.1 and 0.9 (the step actually used is the one handed in), that a stopped and resumed search equals an uninterrupted one at every saved state in both groups, that a state of the other group or sigma is refused, the champion rule for each group and the twin counts in every branch, and a serial mutation pass whose survivors become rows or are declared equivalent. Eight searches on four workers take about eleven hours (two of H32's).

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say sigma is the only thing that matters or that 0.1 and 0.9 bracket the best value: two other values of one setting, four seeds each, so a difference from sigma 0.3 is a hint about the direction and not a map. The step size interacts with the number of weights changed (1 to 4), the acceptance rule (at least as good) and the 1800-iteration budget, none of which is varied. A twin pairing across the same seed is not independent (the streams diverge from the first accepted candidate); it does not cover 0.005 or other penalties and says nothing about H34 to H38.

## Round H40, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H34 to H39 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read): A DIFFERENT PRICE -- THE SEARCH AT 0.005
### 1. Why
Every network search since H27 has been run at price **0.02** (H27, H28, H29, H32 and their kin) or **0.04** (H30, H31): the objective is the penalised priced score, the mean of the dark and lit scores (a lost run scoring 0) minus the price times the mean power in kilowatts. The price is the lever that says how much power a pilot may spend for a better score. At 0.02 and 0.04 the networks never beat the law of their price convincingly; the lowest price of the series, **0.005**, is where power is nearly free and the pilot is least constrained, and it is the one price where the networks of H27 (600 iterations on 24 ids) and the law of the price (`Q005`) are both stored as comparators. This round runs **H32's search at 0.005**: the same loop, the same 72 train ids, 1800 iterations, the same seeds, with only the price changed, and reads everything at 0.005, with H32's 0.02 networks as twins read at the new price.
> (A) Does the champion tie `c02`? (B) The law of its price? (C) Is it better than H27's 600-iteration network at 0.005? (D) Than H32's champion (trained at 0.02), read at 0.005? (E) Seed by seed, do these networks beat their 0.02 twins at 0.005? (F) Do they lose subs? (G) How many are not behind `c02`? (H) Does a cheaper price make the champion draw more power than H32's?

### 2. The searches and the arms
**The searches (4).** H32's search, byte for byte, except that **the price is 0.005**: the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, no prune, the penalised priced objective on the 72 train ids at **0.005**, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, 1800 iterations, checkpoints at 900, 1200, 1500 and 1800, resumable with state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, held-out ids 26100-26139, seeds **26011, 26012, 26013, 26014** (H32's), group **P005**. The first parent's value is `wip`'s penalised value at 0.005 on the 72 ids (a check, computed in this round; it differs from H32's 0.02 start value). Champion at a checkpoint: the best held-out value (at 0.005) of the four (the first of a tie in seed order); the networks flown are the 1800-iteration ones.
**The audit:** ids **41200-41399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (to 40399). **Arms (13):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; **`q005`** (the law of the price 0.005: the champion of H27's stored `Q005` law search by H27's rule); **`m005`** (H27's 600-iteration network at 0.005: the champion of its stored `M005` search); H32's four 0.02 networks `s02_26011` (H32's champion) to `s02_26014`; the four new `p005_26011` to `p005_26014`. **Every arm is read at 0.005 on the PENALISED priced score**: per id (dark score' + lit score')/2 - 0.005 x (dark kW + lit kW)/2, a lost run scoring 0; the table shows 0.005, 0.02 and 0.04, and the pairings at 0.02 are reported.
**The verdict, registered before anything is run; eight parts joined by `; `. A reading is the switchboard's at 2 se (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES), paired by id at 0.005.**
**Part A:** `THE CHAMPION <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02`. **Part B:** `... THE LAW OF THE PRICE` (`q005`). **Part C:** `... H27'S 600-ITERATION NETWORK` (`m005`). **Part D:** `... H32'S CHAMPION` (`s02_26011`, read at 0.005). **Part E:** `TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `p005_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>`). **Part F:** `LOST SUBS IN THE P005 NETWORKS: <those that lost at least one of their 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **Part G:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4`. **Part H:** `THE CHAMPION'S MEAN POWER IS ABOVE H32'S CHAMPION'S: <YES or NO>` (the mean of the dark and lit mean powers over the audit ids, `p005` champion against `s02_26011`). Part G: modal 3 or 4 (0: 5, 1: 10, 2: 20, 3: 30, 4: 35 percent). Part H: YES 85 percent. The champion's held-out value at 0.005 at 1800 is above `wip`'s start value: 90 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead40.mjs` (H32's loop written out with the price handed in, because H32's `longSearch` has the price fixed: the same generator stream, state and checkpoints, state saved with the price and refused on load when it is not 0.005; the champion rule, the thirteen arms, the audit read at the price, the readings, the eight-part verdict, the sixteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead40Cmd.mjs` (`search` with state files named for H40, resuming and skipping finished seeds; `fly`; a smoke mode; every path named for H40 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead40-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that the loop equals H32's at price 0.02 and a reference loop written out in the gate at 0.005, that a stopped and resumed search equals an uninterrupted one at every saved state, that a state of another price is refused, the champion rule, the twin counts and the power part in every branch, and a serial mutation pass whose survivors become rows or are declared equivalent. The search takes about 5.4 hours on 4 workers (H32's).

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say 0.005 is the price that matters, that the networks respond to price the way a pilot should (the power part reads one champion against one), or that a price-0.005 network is better than a 0.02 one at any other price (the table and the 0.02 pairings say what they say on these ids only). The comparators are as stored: `m005` is 600 iterations on 24 ids, `q005` is a four-weight law, so a network that beats them is not a larger effect of the price. Four seeds are four draws; a twin pairing across the same seed is not independent; it does not cover a price above 0.04 and says nothing about H34 to H39.

## Round H41, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H34 to H40 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read): A DIFFERENT TRAIN SET SIZE -- 24 IDS AND 144 IDS AGAINST THE SERIES' 72
### 1. Why
Every network search since H32 has trained on the same **72** ids (26000-26023 and 30000-30047) and generalised with a gap (H32: train value well above held-out value; the checkpoints zigzag). Fewer ids make a search cheaper and noisier and should widen the gap; more ids make it dearer and should narrow it, and may or may not lift the audit. This round changes only the size of the fixed train set, at H32's price 0.02, and reads the champion of each size on 200 fresh ids.

## Round H42, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built or flown; H35 to H41 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H34's result is read): A DIFFERENT HELD-OUT SET -- DOES THE CHAMPION PICK SURVIVE IT, AND DOES A BIGGER ONE PICK BETTER?
### 1. Why
Every champion of this series is the best of four seeds by its **held-out value on 40 ids** (26100-26139). H34 found that this pick was not the best of its four on the audit (the pick `t02_26031` was second on 200 fresh ids, 0.005 behind the best), and the selection is the one thing separating H32's tie with `c02` from H34's four defeats. **A search never reads the held-out value**: it only writes it at the checkpoints, so the searches of H32 and H34 are the same searches whatever held-out set is used, and this round **runs no search**. It re-reads the 32 stored checkpoint networks at 1800 iterations (H32's four seeds and H34's four seeds, at 900, 1200, 1500 and 1800: 32 networks) on two NEW held-out sets and asks what the pick would have been.
> (A) Do the new held-out sets pick the same champion as the original one? (B) Is the held-out value of the 1800 networks on a bigger set closer to their audit value than on 40 ids? (C) Does the re-fly on the original held-out ids reproduce the stored values? (D) Is the champion's held-out value on a new set lower than on the original (the winner's curse of picking the best of four on 40 ids)?

### 2. What is flown and what is read
**Held-out sets (new, disjoint from every train, audit and earlier held-out id):** **HA = ids 52000-52039 (40 ids)**, the same size as the original, other ids; **HB = ids 52100-52299 (200 ids)**, five times the size. Original: HO = 26100-26139.
**Flights:** each of the 32 stored networks (H32's `S02` seeds 26011-26014 and H34's `T02` seeds 26031-26034, each at checkpoints 900, 1200, 1500, 1800; the weights are read from `head-to-head32-search.jsonl` and `head-to-head34-search.jsonl`) is flown dark and lit at 160 s on HA and HB by H28's `flyPen`, scored by H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 (the objective of the searches), and re-flown on HO as a check. **Audit values** (read, not flown): the penalised priced mean at 0.02 of `t02_26031` to `t02_26034` from H34's stored audit (ids 34200-34399); of `s02_26011` to `s02_26014` from **H35's stored audit (ids 35200-35399) if it exists when this round is read**, otherwise those parts read NOT AVAILABLE.
**The verdict, registered before anything is built; seven parts joined by `; `.**
**Part A:** `S02 PICK AT 1800: HO <seed>, HA <seed>, HB <seed>` (the seed with the best held-out value among the four at 1800, first of a tie in seed order, on each set). **Part B:** `T02 PICK AT 1800: HO <seed>, HA <seed>, HB <seed>`. **Part C:** `PICKS AT ALL FOUR CHECKPOINTS THAT AGREE WITH HO: HA a OF 8, HB b OF 8` (8 = 4 checkpoints x 2 groups). **Part D:** `RE-FLY ON HO EQUALS THE STORED VALUES: <YES or NO>` (all 32 values to the last digit; a NO makes the rest NOT WEIGHED). **Part E:** `CHAMPION'S VALUE: S02 HO x, HA y, HB z; T02 HO x, HA y, HB z` (the champion's held-out value at 1800 on each set, four decimals). **Part F:** `T02 RANK ON HB VS H34'S AUDIT: rho <r>` (Spearman rank correlation over the four final T02 networks between the HB value and the audit value; with four items rho is one of 21 values and is NOT a test). **Part G:** `S02 RANK ON HB VS H35'S AUDIT: rho <r>` or `NOT AVAILABLE`. (A to G: seven.)

### 3. The checks
Twelve rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every flight returned the ids asked for in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the 32 networks are exactly the stored checkpoint weights (963 finite weights each, read again from the stored files, seeds 26011-26014 and 26031-26034 each once, each with the four checkpoints); the original held-out ids, HA and HB are pairwise disjoint and disjoint from the train ids (26000-26023, 30000-30047), every audit id (up to 42399) and 50000-50071; HA has 40 ids and HB 200; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); **the re-fly on HO equals the stored value of every network to the last digit** (Part D, also a check); the champion rule is H32's (first of a tie in seed order); Spearman's rho is computed on ranks with ties averaged (a gate row against hand-worked cases); H34's audit means are re-computed from its stored runs; no search was run (the command has no search mode).

### 4. The lines
`HELD-OUT VALUES` (each of the 32 networks on HO, HA and HB); `PICKS PER CHECKPOINT` (the seed picked on each set per group per checkpoint); `CHAMPION'S VALUE`; `RANK TABLES` (the four final networks of each group by HO, HA, HB and audit); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the mean difference (HA minus HO) and (HB minus HO) over the 32 networks, and the standard deviation of the HA-minus-HO differences (the noise of a 40-id held-out value).

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: HA picks the same seed as HO 45 percent, HB 40 percent. Part B: HA picks the same seed as HO 40 percent, HB 35 percent. Part C: HA agrees at 4 or more of 8 checkpoint picks 55 percent, HB 45 percent. Part D: YES 92 percent. Part E: the champion's value on HA is below its value on HO for both groups 75 percent, and on HB below 80 percent. Part F: rho at least 0.4 55 percent. Part G (if available): rho at least 0.4 55 percent. HB picks the best of the four on the audit in T02 (26032) 40 percent. ### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead42.mjs` (reads nothing from disk: the command hands in the stored search rows, H34's stored audit and H35's if present; the held-out sets, the champion rule, the flights through H28's `flyPen`, the penalised objective, Spearman's rho, the seven-part verdict, the twelve checks and the lines), `headToHead42Cmd.mjs` (`fly` only, no `search`; a smoke mode on tiny sets; every path named for H42) and `headToHead42-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against hand-worked ranks and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say a held-out set can rescue a search that does not reach `c02`: the networks are the stored ones and the best pick of four stays the best of four. A pick on a bigger held-out set still rests on one draw of ids; the audit values are 200-id readings with their own error (about 0.004 to 0.007 in these pairings), and a rank correlation over four networks has 21 possible values and cannot reject anything. The audit ids are a different draw from HB, so a disagreement between the HB ranking and the audit is both sets' noise. It does not change a verdict of any earlier round: H32's champion stays H32's by H32's rule. It says nothing about H35 to H41.

## Round H43, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built, searched or flown; H35 to H42 were not finished and nothing of H35 to H41 has been read; H42's flight was in progress and nothing of it has been read): A DIFFERENT PRICE PAIR -- ONE SEARCH THAT MUST BEAT THE LAW OF 0.005 AND THE LAW OF 0.04 AT THE SAME TIME
### 1. Why
Each search of the series has one price (0.02 in H32 and H34, 0.04 in H30 and H31, 0.005 in H40). The pilot that results is read at that price only, and the laws of the other prices are bounds nobody has asked it to meet. A pilot that pays for power at ONE price is no use to a ship whose price changes. This round asks one search for a pilot that is **at least as good as the law of each of two prices at once**, 0.005 and 0.04, the two ends of the prices of H27.
**The pair is not a minimum of the two prices' values**: the penalised priced value is `S - p x P` (score minus price times mean kW, P at least 0), so the smaller of the values at two prices is the value at the higher price, and the larger is the value at the lower: both are a single-price search in disguise. The pair is made non-degenerate by measuring each price against the law OF that price: the objective is the **smaller of the two margins**, `min(V(0.005) - L005, V(0.04) - L04)`, where `V(p)` is the penalised priced value of the network at price `p` on the ids flown, and `L005`, `L04` are the values of H27's two law champions (`q005`, `q04`) at their price on the SAME ids (a constant of the id set, computed once in the round). A positive objective means the network beats both laws.
> (A) Does the champion beat or tie `q005` at 0.005? (B) `q04` at 0.04? (C) `c02` at 0.02, a price it was not asked about? (D) H32's 0.02 champion at 0.02? (E) Is its worse margin above zero on the audit? (F) Do the networks lose subs? (G) How many of the four are not behind either law? (H) Does it draw power between the two laws' power?

### 2. The searches and the arms
**The searches (4).** H32's search, byte for byte, except **the objective**: the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, the 72 train ids, 1800 iterations, checkpoints 900, 1200, 1500, 1800, resumable (state every 50 and at every checkpoint), held-out ids 26100-26139, seeds **26011-26014**, group **R54**. A candidate's two values are computed from ONE set of flights (the mean penalised score and the mean power of its dark and lit flights on the ids, as H27's objective does), `V(p) = 0.5 x (mean dark score' + mean lit score') - p x 0.5 x (mean dark kW + mean lit kW)`. `L005` and `L04` are the laws' own `V` on the 72 train ids and on the 40 held-out ids (four constants, flown in the round before the search and stored in every record). The champion of a checkpoint is the best held-out objective of the four seeds (the first of a tie). The first parent's objective is `wip`'s on the 72 ids (a check).
**The audit:** ids **43200-43399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (42399) and every held-out set. **Arms (14):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q005` and `q04` (the champion laws of H27's stored `Q005` and `Q04` searches by H27's rule); H32's four 1800-iteration 0.02 networks `s02_26011` (its champion) to `s02_26014`; the four new `r54_26011` to `r54_26014`. Every arm is read on the penalised priced score at 0.005, 0.02 and 0.04 (the table shows all three).
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `, readings the switchboard's at 2 se (BEATS / IS BEHIND / TIES), paired by id.** **A:** `THE CHAMPION <..> THE LAW OF 0.005 AT 0.005` (against `q005`). **B:** `THE CHAMPION <..> THE LAW OF 0.04 AT 0.04` (against `q04`). **C:** `THE CHAMPION <..> C02 AT 0.02`. **D:** `THE CHAMPION <..> H32'S CHAMPION AT 0.02`. **E:** `THE CHAMPION'S WORSE MARGIN IS ABOVE ZERO: <YES or NO>` (the smaller of the two mean paired differences of A and B). **F:** `LOST SUBS IN THE R54 NETWORKS: <those that lost at least one of 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **G:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND EITHER LAW: n OF 4` (readings against `q005` at 0.005 AND against `q04` at 0.04 both not WORSE). **H:** `THE CHAMPION'S MEAN POWER IS BETWEEN THE TWO LAWS': <YES or NO>` (between `q04`'s and `q005`'s mean powers, the mean of dark and lit, inclusive).

### 3. The checks
Sixteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each seed once, each with four checkpoints of 963 finite weights, and the flown networks are the 1800 weights; **the objective is as registered** (every record carries the prices 0.005 and 0.04 and the four constants; the objective of a scripted candidate equals `min(V(0.005) - L005, V(0.04) - L04)` to the last digit; and with the two constants set to give equal margins the loop equals H32's at the price they imply); the constants are the laws' values on the stated ids, recomputed; the first parent is `wip`'s objective; the champion is the best held-out; the s02 arms are H32's stored 1800 networks; `c02`, `wip`, `q005`, `q04` as registered; the seeds are H32's; id sets disjoint, the audit past 42399; the resume proof (H32's).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH R54` (the constants, the start objective, then train and held-out objective and accepted count at each checkpoint, lost flights); `RESUMES`; `CHAMPIONS PER CHECKPOINT`; `GENERALISATION GAP`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `MARGINS` (each r54 network's mean margin over `q005` at 0.005 and over `q04` at 0.04); `PAIRED` (the registered pairings); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** pairings at 0.02, the distances from `wip` and from the twin, the audit means in rank order.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: IS BEHIND 45, TIES 40, BEATS 15 (the law of 0.005 draws much power and a network's search is held back by the harder price). Part B: TIES 45, BEATS 30, IS BEHIND 25 (at 0.04 H31's champion tied the law). Part C: IS BEHIND 45, TIES 45, BEATS 10. Part D: IS BEHIND 40, TIES 50, BEATS 10. Part E: YES 25 percent. Part F: at least one network loses a run 65 percent. Part G: modal 0 of 4 (0: 40, 1: 30, 2: 15, 3: 10, 4: 5 percent). Part H: YES 70 percent. The champion's objective on the 40 held-out ids at 1800 is above `wip`'s start objective: 85 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead43.mjs` (H32's loop written out with the pair objective and the constants handed in; state saved with the prices and the constants and refused when they differ; the champion rule, the fourteen arms, the audit read at three prices, the readings, the eight-part verdict, the sixteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead43Cmd.mjs` (`laws` to fly the constants, `search` with state files named for H43, resuming and skipping finished seeds, `fly`, a smoke mode, every path named for H43 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead43-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that the objective is the registered minimum of two margins on scripted values, that equal margins reproduce H32's loop, a resume proof of its own and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say a pilot for a PAIR of prices is a pilot for every price between them (the margin is measured at the two ends and the price pair is one pair), or that the smaller margin is the right trade (it rewards the worse end and ignores the better one). The laws are four-weight laws chosen by H27's own search; a network that beats them is beating those two laws. The constants make the objective a difference to a fixed bar, not a price; the search's choices at 0.02 are not asked for. Four seeds are four draws, and H34 showed that four draws of this search can differ by more than a tie from H32's. It says nothing about H35 to H42.

## Round H42, RESULT (2026-11-10): ON A BIGGER HELD-OUT SET H32'S CHAMPION IS STILL THE PICK, AND H34'S PICK CHANGES TO THE NETWORK THAT WAS BEST ON ITS AUDIT -- THE 40-ID HELD-OUT VALUE IS TOO NOISY TO PICK AMONG FOUR
Flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead42Cmd.mjs fly --parts 4` (no search mode; the 32 stored checkpoint networks of H32's S02 and H34's T02 at 900, 1200, 1500 and 1800 on HO 26100-26139, HA 52000-52039 and HB 52100-52299, 160 s, dark and lit, read once; data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head42.json`). **All 12 checks PASS, among them that the re-fly on HO equals the value the search wrote for every one of the 32 networks to the last digit.** The first flight's printed report was made by a library version whose Part E read the best value ON each set; the registered Part E (the HO pick's value on each set) was fixed in the library after the flight started and the report re-made from the same stored runs with `fly --from-json` (no flight repeated); the flown numbers are the same.

**The verdict, as printed (seven parts; Part E holds a "; " inside, as registered):** S02 PICK AT 1800: HO 26011, HA 26011, HB 26011; T02 PICK AT 1800: HO 26031, HA 26032, HB 26032; PICKS AT ALL FOUR CHECKPOINTS THAT AGREE WITH HO: HA 3 OF 8, HB 3 OF 8; RE-FLY ON HO EQUALS THE STORED VALUES: YES; CHAMPION'S VALUE: S02 HO 0.7592, HA 0.7536, HB 0.7663; T02 HO 0.7591, HA 0.7287, HB 0.7589; T02 RANK ON HB VS H34'S AUDIT: rho 1.00; S02 RANK ON HB VS H35'S AUDIT: NOT AVAILABLE (H35's audit is not flown yet).

**What the numbers say.** *H32's group:* all three sets pick `s02_26011` at 1200 and 1800 and agree at 900 (26014); at 1500 HO picks 26014 and HA and HB pick 26011. **H32's champion is not an artefact of its 40 held-out ids.** *H34's group:* HO picked `t02_26031` at every checkpoint; **HA and HB pick `t02_26032` at every checkpoint, which is the network that was best on H34's 200-id audit** (H34 recorded "the held-out pick is not the best of the four on the audit"). The HB ranking of the four 1800 networks (26032 > 26031 > 26034 > 26033) is exactly H34's audit ranking (rho 1.00; with four items that is one of 24 orders, 1 in 24 by chance, and it is a reading, not a test). The HA ranking (26032 > 26034 > 26031 > 26033) agrees at the top and the bottom. **The 40-id held-out value of `t02_26031` was 0.7591; on HA it is 0.7287, 0.030 lower, on HB 0.7589.** The noise of a 40-id value is large: the standard deviation of HA minus HO over the 32 networks is 0.0166 (HB minus HO 0.0087), against gaps between the four seeds of 0.0005 to 0.005 at 1800 on HO. The mean of HA minus HO is -0.03 over the 32 networks (the 40 ids of HA are harder than HO's), HB minus HO +0.00 (the 200 ids of HB are about as hard as HO).

**What this changes.** For H32: nothing; its champion is the pick on all three sets and its 1800-iteration network is the one H33 audited twice. For H34: its verdict was read for `t02_26031` (BEHIND `c02` by 0.0131); the HB pick `t02_26032` was BEHIND `c02` by 0.0082 (se 0.0036; 67 better, 133 worse, in H34's own audit), so **the verdict that none of the four new seeds reaches `c02` is the same for either pick**; only the pick's size of defeat changes. The held-out value of H32's champion on HB (0.7663) is above its value on HO (0.7592); the HB value of `t02_26032` is 0.7669 at 1800, within 0.0006 of it: **on a 200-id held-out set the best of H34's four is no worse than H32's champion**, while H34's audit read it 0.0111 behind (H34: `s02_1800` +0.0855 against 0.0744 gain at 0.02). The held-out reading and the audit reading do not tell the same story about H32's champion against the best of the new four; the two readings use different ids and different noise.

**Expectations, hit and missed (registered in section 5).** Held: Part D YES (my 92); Part E the champion's value on HA below its value on HO for both groups (75: S02 -0.0056, T02 -0.0304); Part F rho at least 0.4 (55: 1.00); HB picks the best of the four on the audit in T02 (40: it does); the standard deviation of the HA-minus-HO differences above 0.01 (65: 0.0166). Missed: **Part C HA agrees at 4 or more of 8 checkpoint picks (my 55; 3 of 8) and HB at 4 or more (45; 3 of 8); Part E the champion's value on HB below its value on HO (80; S02 +0.0071 above, T02 -0.0002 below, one of two).** Part A (HA picks the same S02 champion as HO 45; HB 40) and Part B (HA picks T02's 40; HB 35) were registered as less than even; they came out S02 same, T02 different on both sets. Part G is NOT AVAILABLE.

**What this does NOT say.** It does not say a bigger held-out set rescues the search: the networks are the stored ones and the best pick of four on any set is the best of four; the pick moved within H34's group and not within H32's. It does not say `t02_26032` is better than `t02_26031` in general: the audit ranking agrees with HB and the audit's own pairings carry se 0.004 to 0.006, and a rank correlation over four networks cannot reject chance. HA and HB are two draws of ids; HA (40 ids) disagrees with HB on the order of the middle two (26031 and 26034). It does not change any earlier verdict: H32's champion stays H32's by H32's rule, and the picks of H30 and H31 were made on HO and have not been re-read here. HO's 40 ids were drawn once for the whole series, so every champion since H27 shares its noise; the champion rule at 40 ids is a noisy pick, and the rounds before H34 that compared one champion against another carry that noise. S02's agreement at 3 of 8 includes the three checkpoints where all three sets agree and the 1500 one where they do not; T02's is 0 of 4. It says nothing about H35 to H41 and nothing about H43.

## Round H44, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built or flown; H35 to H41 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H42's and H34's results are read): A DIFFERENT SEED COUNT -- WHAT DOES THE BEST OF 1, 2, 4 AND 8 SEEDS BUY?
### 1. Why
Every champion of the series is the best of four seeds by held-out value. H34 drew four other seeds and none reached `c02`; H42 found that the 40-id held-out pick was noisy. Together the eight 1800-iteration networks that exist (H32's `S02` seeds 26011-26014 and H34's `T02` seeds 26031-26034, the same search, start, price, train ids and iteration count) are **eight draws of one search**. This round runs **no new search**. It flies the eight on ONE fresh audit and asks, by resampling the eight, **how much the best of N seeds gains over one seed for N = 1, 2, 4 and 8**, with the pick made on the 40 held-out ids of the series (HO) and on H42's 200 ids (HB).
> (A) Does the best of all 8 by HB beat or tie `c02` on a fresh audit? (B) The best of 8 by HO? (C) The best of the eight on the audit itself (the oracle)? (D) Which seed does HB pick? (E) What is the mean audit value of the pick of N seeds, N = 1, 2, 4, 8, by HB and by HO? (F) Does it rise with N? (G) Is the HB pick better than the HO pick on the mean over the 70 subsets of four? (H) Do the eight lose subs?

### 2. What is flown and what is read
**Arms (12):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02` (the champion law of H27's stored `Q02` search by H27's rule); the eight networks `s02_26011` to `s02_26014` (H32's stored 1800-iteration weights) and `t02_26031` to `t02_26034` (H34's). **Audit: ids 44200-44399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (43399) and disjoint from the train ids, HO and HB (52100-52299). Every arm is read on the penalised priced score at 0.02 (the table shows 0.005, 0.02 and 0.04).
**Read, not flown:** each network's value on HO (the value the search wrote, from the stored rows) and on HB (H42's stored flight, `head-to-head42.json`; if that file is missing this round is NOT WEIGHED).
**The pick of a subset** is the network with the largest value on the set (HO or HB) among the subset's members, the first in the order `s02_26011 ... s02_26014, t02_26031 ... t02_26034` on a tie. **The mean audit value of the pick of N** is the mean, over all C(8, N) subsets (8, 28, 70, 1), of the audit mean (penalised priced score at 0.02 over the 200 ids) of the subset's pick; the oracle's mean is the mean over the same subsets of the best audit mean in the subset.
**The verdict, registered before anything is built; eight parts joined by `; `, readings the switchboard's at 2 se paired by id at 0.02.** **A:** `THE BEST OF 8 BY HB <BEATS/IS BEHIND/TIES> C02` (the network HB names among all eight, against `c02`). **B:** `THE BEST OF 8 BY HO <..> C02`. **C:** `THE BEST OF 8 ON THE AUDIT <..> C02` (the network with the largest audit mean, which is chosen on the audit itself and is NOT an unbiased reading; it is the ceiling). **D:** `HB PICKS <name>, HO PICKS <name>`. **E:** `MEAN AUDIT VALUE OF THE PICK OF N BY HB: 1 x, 2 x, 4 x, 8 x; BY HO: 1 x, 2 x, 4 x, 8 x` (four decimals; N = 1 is the same for both). **F:** `THE PICK BY HB RISES WITH N: <YES or NO>` (strictly at 1 < 2 < 4 < 8, to four decimals). **G:** `THE HB PICK BEATS THE HO PICK ON THE 70 SUBSETS OF FOUR: <YES or NO>` (the HB mean at N = 4 above the HO mean at N = 4 by more than 0.0005). **H:** `LOST SUBS IN THE EIGHT: <those that lost at least one of 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. (A to H: eight.)

### 3. The checks
Fourteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); the eight networks are the stored 1800-iteration weights (963 finite weights each, read again from H32's and H34's stored searches); H42's stored values are present for all eight, equal their re-read stored values on HO and are finite on HB (H42's HO re-fly equals the stored values); the stored HO values equal the search rows' held-out values at 1800; `c02`, `wip` and `q02` are the registered ones; the audit ids are disjoint from the train ids, HO, HB and every earlier audit (past 43399); the subset resampling is checked against a loop written out in the gate and against hand-worked cases; the subset counts are 8, 28, 70 and 1; the audit means are recomputed from the runs.

### 4. The lines
`AUDIT` (scores, power, thrust of all arms); `LOST` with ids; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at three prices; `VALUES` (each network's HO, HB and audit mean); `PICKS` (HB's and HO's pick and the oracle among all eight); `PICK BY N` (the mean audit value of the pick by HB, by HO and the oracle's, N = 1 to 8); `PAIRED` (the three picks against `c02` and `q02`; each of the eight against `c02`); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the sd of the eight audit means; the pairings at 0.04 and 0.005; the rank correlation (Spearman) of HO, of HB against the audit over all eight.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: IS BEHIND 55, TIES 40, BEATS 5. Part B: IS BEHIND 60, TIES 38, BEATS 2. Part C: IS BEHIND 20, TIES 60, BEATS 20 (the best of eight on its own audit is flattered). Part D: HB and HO name the same network 25 percent. Part E: the HB pick's mean at N = 8 is above its mean at N = 1: 90 percent; by more than 0.005: 55 percent. Part F: YES 55 percent. Part G: YES 60 percent. Part H: at least one network loses a run 70 percent. The eight networks' audit means have a standard deviation above 0.008: 60 percent. The Spearman rank correlation of HB against the audit over the eight is above that of HO: 65 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead44.mjs` (reads nothing from disk: the command hands in the stored rows, H42's stored flight and H27's search; the eight arms, the audit, the subset enumeration and the resampling (C(8, N) subsets, the pick rule, the oracle), Spearman, the readings, the eight-part verdict, the fourteen checks and the lines), `headToHead44Cmd.mjs` (`fly` only, no search; a smoke mode; every path named for H44) and `headToHead44-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against hand-worked subsets and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say eight seeds are enough or that the best of eight is the best a search can do: eight draws of one search and a resampling WITHOUT replacement of them, so the subsets of four overlap heavily (the 70 means are not 70 independent trials) and N = 8 is one subset. It does not say the audit value of a pick is an unbiased reading of any pick it was used to choose (the oracle) and the HB pick shares nothing with the audit's ids. A mean of the pick over subsets is a property of these eight networks, not of the search at large. HB is one draw of 200 ids. It says nothing about H35 to H43.

By the HO pick: N = 2 0.7658, N = 4 0.7727, N = 8 0.7793; by HB: N = 2 0.7671, N = 4 0.7711, and then 0.7714 at 5, 0.7711 at 6, 0.7702 at 7, 0.7689 at 8.
