## Round H46, REGISTERED (2026-11-10, before anything is built or flown; H35 to H41, H43 and H45 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H42's and H44's results are read): A DIFFERENT AUDIT SET SIZE -- HOW MANY IDS DOES A READING NEED?
### 1. Why
Every verdict of the series is read on an audit of 200 ids (H27 to H31 on 200 or fewer: the first rounds on 24 to 40). The switchboard's reading is "BETTER / WORSE at 2 standard errors, paired by id". H42 found a 40-id value with a standard deviation of 0.0166 between sets; H44 found two networks 0.0006 apart in one ranking and 0.0104 apart in another. **What the 200-id reading can and cannot tell has never been measured against a bigger audit.** This round flies the eight stored 1800-iteration networks (H32's `S02` four and H34's `T02` four) with `c02`, `q02`, the autopilot and `law0` on **800 fresh ids once**, and reads the same flights on nested and disjoint blocks of 50, 100, 200, 400 and 800 ids. It runs **no search**.
> (A) What do the eight networks read against `c02` on 800 ids? (B) How often does a reading on a block of n ids agree with the 800-id reading? (C) At what size does every block agree for all eight? (D) How does the standard error of a pairing fall with n? (E) How often is the order of two networks the same on a block as on 800 ids? (F) Which network is best on 800, and how many blocks of 200 name it? (G) Does the standard error fall as one over the square root of n? (H) Do the networks lose subs?

### 2. What is flown and what is read
**Arms (12):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02` (the champion law of H27's stored `Q02` search by H27's rule); the eight stored 1800-iteration networks `s02_26011` to `s02_26014` (H32) and `t02_26031` to `t02_26034` (H34). **Audit: ids 46200-46999 (800), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (45399), disjoint from every train, held-out and earlier audit id. Every arm is read on the penalised priced score at 0.02.
**Blocks:** for each n in 50, 100, 200, 400, 800 the 800 ids are cut into consecutive blocks of n ids (16, 8, 4, 2, 1 blocks, in the order of the ids). A **reading of a network on a block** is the switchboard's reading (BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES, at 2 se, paired by id) of the network against `c02` on the block's ids, at price 0.02; the **800-id reading** is the reading on all 800. A reading **agrees** when it equals the 800-id reading. The **order of two networks on a block** is the order of their mean penalised priced scores on the block's ids; it agrees with 800 when it is the same order (a tie on a block counts as a disagreement).
**The verdict, registered before anything is built; eight parts joined by `; `.** **A:** `AT 800 IDS AGAINST C02: b BEATS, w IS BEHIND, t TIES` (of the eight networks). **B:** `READINGS THAT AGREE WITH THE 800-ID READING: 50 x OF 128, 100 x OF 64, 200 x OF 32, 400 x OF 16` (eight networks times the number of blocks). **C:** `EVERY BLOCK AGREES FOR ALL EIGHT AT: <the smallest n of 50, 100, 200, 400 whose blocks all agree, or NONE>`. **D:** `MEAN SE OF A PAIRING AGAINST C02: 50 x, 100 x, 200 x, 400 x, 800 x` (the mean over the eight networks and the blocks of the pairing's standard error, four decimals). **E:** `PAIR ORDERS THAT AGREE WITH 800: 50 x OF 448, 100 x OF 224, 200 x OF 112, 400 x OF 56` (28 pairs of the eight times the number of blocks). **F:** `BEST OF THE EIGHT ON 800: <name>; BLOCKS OF 200 THAT NAME IT: k OF 4`. **G:** `SE FALLS AS ONE OVER THE SQUARE ROOT OF N WITHIN 20 PERCENT: <YES or NO>` (the ratio of the mean se at 50 to the mean se at 800 lies within 20 percent of 4). **H:** `LOST SUBS IN THE EIGHT: <those that lost at least one of 1600 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. (A to H: eight.)

### 3. The checks
Fourteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 800 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); the eight networks are the stored 1800-iteration weights (read again from H32's and H34's stored searches); `c02`, `wip` and `q02` are the registered ones; the audit ids are disjoint from the train ids, the held-out sets and every earlier audit (past 45399); the blocks of every size partition the 800 ids exactly (counts 16, 8, 4, 2, 1; every id in exactly one block of each size); the 800-id readings equal the switchboard's own on the full flights; the readings on blocks are checked against a loop written out in the gate; the audit means are recomputed from the runs.

### 4. The lines
`AUDIT` (scores, power, thrust of all arms on 800 ids); `LOST` with ids; `READINGS AT 800` (each network's pairing against `c02` and `q02`); `AGREEMENT` (per size: per network, how many of its blocks agree); `SE BY SIZE`; `ORDERS`; `BEST` (the name by block size 200 and by 400); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the mean pairing differences per network at 800; the smallest and largest block means of the best network at each size; the share of block readings that are WORSE where the 800-id reading is TIES (and the reverse).

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run

Part E: the share of orders that agree is above 75 percent at 200: 60 percent. Part F: the best of the eight on 800 is `s02_26011` 55 percent, and 3 or 4 of 4 blocks of 200 name it 40 percent. Part G: YES 70 percent. Part H: at least one network loses a run 85 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead46.mjs` (reads nothing from disk: the command hands in the stored rows and H27's search; the twelve arms, the audit, the block cutting, the readings on a block through the switchboard's own paired reading on sliced flights, the agreement and order counts, the standard errors, the eight-part verdict, the fourteen checks and the lines), `headToHead46Cmd.mjs` (`fly` only, no search; a smoke mode; every path named for H46) and `headToHead46-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against hand-worked blocks and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say 800 ids are the truth: the 800-id reading has its own standard error (about half the 200-id one), and "agreement with the 800-id reading" is agreement with a better but still finite reading. The blocks are consecutive in id order and the eight networks are eight networks: counts of agreement are of a small family of readings that share ids, so the blocks of one network are not independent trials of the same network, and eight networks that are close to `c02` produce more TIES-to-WORSE changes than eight that are far from it. The ids are one task; a different task would have another noise. It says nothing about H35 to H45.

## Round H47, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H35 to H41, H43, H45 and H46 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H42's and H44's results are read): A DIFFERENT PRICE PAIR -- THE SMALLER MARGIN OVER THE LAWS OF 0.01 AND 0.02
### 1. Why
H43 asked one search to beat the law of 0.005 and the law of 0.04 at the same time (the ends of H27's prices; its result is not read). **This round takes the NEAR pair, 0.01 and 0.02**, the two middle prices of H27's four laws. The objective is the same as H43's and is not degenerate for the same reason: the smaller of the values at two prices is the value at the higher price, so the pair is measured against **the law of each price**: `min(V(0.01) - L01, V(0.02) - L02)`, where `V(p)` is the penalised priced value of the network at price `p` on the ids flown and `L01`, `L02` are the values of H27's champion laws `q01` and `q02` at their price on the SAME ids (constants of the id set, computed once in the round). A positive objective means the network beats both laws. Two neighbouring prices ask for one pilot that is good at both, with less room for a trade between them than H43's far pair: **if a pair objective helps at all, a near pair is where the two margins are closest to each other and the search is least torn.**
> (A) Does the champion beat or tie `q01` at 0.01? (B) `q02` at 0.02? (C) `c02` at 0.02? (D) H32's 0.02 champion at 0.02? (E) Is its worse margin above zero on the audit? (F) Do the networks lose subs? (G) How many of the four are not behind either law? (H) Does it draw power between the two laws' power?

### 2. The searches and the arms
**The searches (4).** H32's search, byte for byte, except **the objective**: the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, the 72 train ids, 1800 iterations, checkpoints 900, 1200, 1500, 1800, resumable (state every 50 and at every checkpoint), held-out ids 26100-26139, seeds **26011-26014**, group **R12**. A candidate's two values come from ONE set of flights (the mean penalised score and the mean power of its dark and lit flights on the ids), `V(p) = 0.5 x (mean dark score' + mean lit score') - p x 0.5 x (mean dark kW + mean lit kW)`, computed with X30.netObj's operations in its order. `L01` and `L02` are the laws' own `V` on the 72 train ids and on the 40 held-out ids (four constants, flown before the search and stored in every record). The champion of a checkpoint is the best held-out objective of the four seeds (the first of a tie). The first parent's objective is `wip`'s on the 72 ids (a check).
**The audit:** ids **47200-47399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (46999). **Arms (13):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q01` and `q02` (the champion laws of H27's stored `Q01` and `Q02` searches by H27's rule); H32's four 1800-iteration 0.02 networks `s02_26011` (its champion) to `s02_26014`; the four new `r12_26011` to `r12_26014` (thirteen: five laws and bounds, four and four networks). Every arm is read on the penalised priced score at 0.005, 0.02 and 0.04 (the table shows all three).
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `, readings the switchboard's at 2 se paired by id.** **A:** `THE CHAMPION <..> THE LAW OF 0.01 AT 0.01` (against `q01`). **B:** `THE CHAMPION <..> THE LAW OF 0.02 AT 0.02` (against `q02`). **C:** `THE CHAMPION <..> C02 AT 0.02`. **D:** `THE CHAMPION <..> H32'S CHAMPION AT 0.02`. **E:** `THE CHAMPION'S WORSE MARGIN IS ABOVE ZERO: <YES or NO>` (the smaller of the two mean paired differences of A and B). **F:** `LOST SUBS IN THE R12 NETWORKS: <those that lost at least one of 400 audit runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **G:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND EITHER LAW: n OF 4` (readings against `q01` at 0.01 AND against `q02` at 0.02 both not WORSE). **H:** `THE CHAMPION'S MEAN POWER IS BETWEEN THE TWO LAWS': <YES or NO>` (between `q02`'s and `q01`'s mean powers, the mean of dark and lit, inclusive).

### 3. The checks
Seventeen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each seed once, each with four checkpoints of 963 finite weights, and the flown networks are the 1800 weights; **the objective is as registered** (every record carries the prices 0.01 and 0.02 and the four constants, and the objective of a scripted candidate equals `min(V(0.01) - L01, V(0.02) - L02)` to the last digit); the constants are the laws' values on the stated ids, recomputed; the first parent is `wip`'s objective; the champion is the best held-out; the s02 arms are H32's stored 1800 networks; `c02`, `wip`, `q01`, `q02` as registered; the seeds are H32's; id sets disjoint, the audit past 46999; the resume proof (H32's). **The gate also holds:** with a margin that cannot bind the loop equals H40's at the price of the other, and a reference loop at the pair.

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH R12` (the constants, the start objective, then train and held-out objective and accepted count at each checkpoint, lost flights); `RESUMES`; `CHAMPIONS PER CHECKPOINT`; `GENERALISATION GAP`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN` at the three prices; `MARGINS` (each r12 network's mean margin over `q01` at 0.01 and over `q02` at 0.02); `PAIRED` (the registered pairings); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** pairings at 0.04 and 0.005, the distances from `wip` and from the twin, the audit means in rank order.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: TIES 40, IS BEHIND 40, BEATS 20. Part B: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 35, BEATS 15. Part C: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 40, BEATS 10. Part D: TIES 55, IS BEHIND 30, BEATS 15 (H32's champion was trained at 0.02 on the same ids). Part E: YES 30 percent. Part F: at least one network loses a run 60 percent. Part G: modal 1 of 4 (0: 25, 1: 30, 2: 25, 3: 15, 4: 5 percent). Part H: YES 60 percent. The champion's held-out objective at 1800 is above `wip`'s start objective: 85 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead47.mjs` (H43's library with the price pair 0.01 and 0.02 and the laws q01 and q02: the objective as the smaller of two margins of one set of flights, constants handed in and stored in the state and every record, the champion rule, the thirteen arms, the audit, the readings, the eight-part verdict, the seventeen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead47Cmd.mjs` (`laws` to fly the constants, `search` with state files named for H47 resuming and skipping finished seeds, `fly`, a smoke mode, every path named for H47 and checked by a smoke run) and `headToHead47-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that the objective is the registered minimum on scripted values, that a margin that cannot bind reproduces H40's loop, a reference loop, a resume proof of its own and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say a pair objective helps: H43 asks that of the far pair and its result is not read here. The laws of 0.01 and 0.02 are close (their margins over the same network are highly correlated), so a positive worse margin here would say less about a trade between prices than one over H43's far pair, and a null result says nothing about the far pair. The laws are four-weight laws chosen by H27's own search; a network that beats them is beating those two laws. The constants make the objective a difference to a fixed bar, not a price. Four seeds are four draws, and H34 and H44 showed that four draws of this search can differ by more than a tie. It says nothing about H35 to H46.

## Round H46, RESULT (2026-11-11): THE 200-ID AUDIT READS A NETWORK AGAINST c02 CORRECTLY WHEN IT IS MORE THAN ABOUT 0.01 AWAY AND FLIPS NEAR-TIES; 50 IDS AGREE WITH 800 ONLY 72 PERCENT OF THE TIME, 200 IDS 91 PERCENT, 400 IDS ALWAYS
Flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead46Cmd.mjs fly --parts 4` (no search mode; the eight stored 1800-iteration networks of H32 and H34 with `c02`, `q02`, the autopilot and `law0`, once on ids 46200-46999 (800), 160 s, dark and lit, read once; data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head46.json`). **All 13 library checks PASS; the registration's fourteenth row is the block-reading row of the gate (readings on blocks against a loop written out there), which is green.**

**The verdict, as printed (eight parts; Part F holds a "; " inside, as registered):** AT 800 IDS AGAINST C02: 0 BEATS, 7 IS BEHIND, 1 TIES; READINGS THAT AGREE WITH THE 800-ID READING: 50 92 OF 128, 100 54 OF 64, 200 29 OF 32, 400 16 OF 16; EVERY BLOCK AGREES FOR ALL EIGHT AT: 400; MEAN SE OF A PAIRING AGAINST C02: 50 0.0094, 100 0.0068, 200 0.0049, 400 0.0035, 800 0.0025; PAIR ORDERS THAT AGREE WITH 800: 50 402 OF 448, 100 206 OF 224, 200 105 OF 112, 400 55 OF 56; BEST OF THE EIGHT ON 800: s02_26011; BLOCKS OF 200 THAT NAME IT: 4 OF 4; SE FALLS AS ONE OVER THE SQUARE ROOT OF N WITHIN 20 PERCENT: YES; LOST SUBS IN THE EIGHT: s02_26011 8, s02_26012 28, s02_26013 42, t02_26031 7, t02_26033 37, t02_26034 1.

**The eight against `c02` on 800 ids** (penalised priced score at 0.02, paired by id): `s02_26011` -0.0000 (se 0.0021; better 472, worse 328) TIES; `s02_26014` -0.0155 (se 0.0016), `t02_26031` -0.0110 (0.0020), `t02_26032` -0.0116 (0.0015), `t02_26034` -0.0187 (0.0018), `t02_26033` -0.0400 (0.0036), `s02_26012` -0.0247 (0.0030), `s02_26013` -0.0455 (0.0039): all WORSE. So **on 800 ids one of the eight ties `c02` (to within 0.004) and seven are behind it, none ahead: H44's reading on 200 ids (one tie, seven behind, none ahead) is the 800-id reading.** H32's champion is the best of the eight on 800 ids and the best in every one of the four blocks of 200.

**What the audit size does.** The standard error of a pairing against `c02` is 0.0094 on 50 ids, 0.0068 on 100, **0.0049 on 200**, 0.0035 on 400 and 0.0025 on 800: the ratio 50 to 800 is 3.8, so it falls as one over the square root of n within 20 percent of the ideal 4. **The two-standard-error line is therefore about 0.019 on 50 ids, 0.0098 on 200 ids and 0.005 on 800 ids**: a network is read BEHIND `c02` on 200 ids only when it is at least about 0.01 behind, and a network 0.0110 behind (`t02_26031`) is read WORSE in 3 of 4 blocks of 200 (2.2 se at the mean 200-id se of 0.0049, 5.5 se at its own 800-id se of 0.0020). The readings agree with the 800-id reading in 92 of 128 cases on 50-id blocks (72 percent), 54 of 64 on 100 (84 percent), 29 of 32 on 200 (91 percent) and in all 16 on 400. The three disagreeing blocks of 200 are `s02_26011` (2 of 4 agree, the network that is level with `c02`) and `t02_26031` (3 of 4). Of the 210 block readings of a network whose 800-id reading is WORSE, 45 (21 percent) read TIES; of the 30 block readings of the one network that TIES at 800 ids, 2 read WORSE. **The order of two networks agrees with the 800-id order in 89.7 percent of the 448 pairs on 50-id blocks, 92.0 percent on 100, 93.8 percent on 200 and 98.2 percent on 400.**

**What this says about the series.** Every verdict of H27 to H45 read on 200 ids is trustworthy for a gap of 0.01 or more and is a coin-flip-sized reading for a gap smaller than 0.005: "TIES" on 200 ids means "within about 0.01", not "equal". The first rounds read on 24 to 40 ids (two standard errors of about 0.02 or more) cannot separate a network from `c02` unless it is 0.04 or more away. A TIES between a network and `c02` at 200 ids and a TIES at 800 are different statements: at 800 ids H32's champion is within 0.004 of `c02`.

**Expectations, hit and missed (registered in section 5).** Held: Part A 5 or more IS BEHIND (my 80: 7), none BEATS (90), 1 or 2 TIE (65: 1); Part B the agreeing shares above 60 percent at 50, 75 at 100, 85 at 200 and 92 at 400 (each 60: 72, 84, 91 and 100 percent); Part E the share of orders agreeing above 75 percent at 200 (60: 93.8); Part F the best of the eight on 800 is `s02_26011` (55) and 3 or 4 of 4 blocks of 200 name it (40: 4); Part G YES (70); Part H some network loses a run (85: six do). Missed: **Part C NONE (my 55; 400 was 25): every block of 400 agrees for all eight; Part D the mean se at 800 below 0.002 (70): it is 0.0025.** Twelve of fourteen statements held.

**What this does NOT say.** It does not say 800 ids are the truth: the 800-id reading has a standard error of 0.0025 and "agreement with 800" is agreement with a better but finite reading (a network within 0.004 of `c02` would flip on a different 800). The blocks are consecutive and the eight networks are eight networks that share ids: the 128 readings on 50-id blocks are 16 blocks times 8 networks, not 128 independent trials, and the networks that sit near `c02` (`s02_26011`, `t02_26031`) account for most of the disagreements while the six far from it agree in every block of 200. The agreement shares depend on how far the networks are from `c02`: a set of networks far from it would read nearly 100 percent at every size and a set clustered at it would read much less. The ids are one task; another task has other noise. It says nothing about H35 to H45 or H47.

## Round H48, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built or flown; H35 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H42's, H44's and H46's results are read): A DIFFERENT HELD-OUT SET -- TWENTY OF THEM: HOW OFTEN DOES A 40-ID HELD-OUT SET PICK THE NETWORK THAT IS BEST ON 800?
### 1. Why
Every champion of this series is the best of four seeds by its value on **one** 40-id held-out set (26100-26139). H42 re-picked on two other sets (one of 40 ids, one of 200) and found the picks moved: S02 stayed on `s02_26011` on all three sets, T02 changed from `t02_26031` to `t02_26032` on both new sets. That is two draws of a 40-id set, and one of them disagreed. **A search never reads its held-out set** (H42), so a different held-out set is a different PICK, not a different search, and this round **runs no search**. It draws **twenty** disjoint 40-id held-out sets (800 ids in all, 53000-53799) and asks how often a 40-id set picks what 800 ids pick, for each of the two groups of four stored 1800-iteration networks (H32's `s02_26011` to `s02_26014`, H34's `t02_26031` to `t02_26034`). H42 had one 40-id draw per group; twenty turns "it moved once" into a rate.
> (A) How often does a 40-id held-out set pick the same network as the 800 ids, S02 and T02? (B) Is the 800-id pick the same network as the pick of H42's 200-id set HB (S02 `s02_26011`, T02 `t02_26032`)? (C) Does the rate of agreement depend on the gap between the best and the second best (the 800-id gap)? (D) Does the picked network's value on its own 40-id set exceed its value on the 800 ids on average (the winner's curse)? (E) Does the pick by a 40-id set ever choose the network that is WORST of its four on 800?

### 2. What is flown and what is read
**Sets (new, disjoint from every train, audit, held-out and earlier id):** `HC_k` = ids 53000+40k to 53039+40k, k = 0..19. **Flights:** the eight stored final networks (weights read from `head-to-head32-search.jsonl` and `head-to-head34-search.jsonl`, 1800 iterations) flown dark and lit, 160 s, on the 800 ids by H28's `flyPen`, scored by H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 (the objective of the searches), per id; plus the autopilot (a check that the ids are alike). Read once. **Per set and group:** the mean over its 40 ids of the objective of each of the four; the pick is the best of the four (the first of a tie in seed order, H32's rule). **Reference:** the pick on all 800 ids (the 20 sets pooled).
**The verdict, registered before anything is built; seven parts joined by `; `.**
**Part A:** `S02 20 SETS AGREE WITH 800: a OF 20; T02 20 SETS AGREE WITH 800: b OF 20`. **Part B:** `800-ID PICKS: S02 <seed>, T02 <seed>; SAME AS H42'S HB: <YES or NO>` (YES when both equal H42's HB picks, 26011 and 26032). **Part C:** `800-ID GAP (BEST MINUS SECOND): S02 x, T02 y; AGREEMENT: S02 a, T02 b` (four decimals; the agreement repeated so the two read side by side). **Part D:** `WINNER'S CURSE: S02 mean (own-set value minus 800-id value of the picked network) x, T02 y` (four decimals with sign; the mean of the 20 differences). **Part E:** `SETS THAT PICK THE WORST OF THEIR FOUR ON 800: S02 a, T02 b`. **Part F:** `SETS WITH A TIE AT THE BEST: n` (should be 0). **Part G:** `SE OF A 40-ID SET'S MEAN DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE BEST TWO ON 800: S02 x, T02 y` (the standard error of the per-set mean of the paired per-id difference between the 800-id best and second best, over the 20 sets, from the spread of the 20 set means; four decimals). (A to G: seven.)

### 3. The checks
Eleven rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 800 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the eight networks are exactly the stored 1800 weights (963 finite weights, seeds once); the twenty sets are pairwise disjoint, 40 ids each, disjoint from the train ids (26000-26023, 30000-30047), every audit and held-out id of H27 to H47 (up to 47399), H42's 52000-52299 and 50000-50071; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); the pick rule (first of a tie in seed order) and the agreement counts are computed in the gate against loops written out there; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; no search was run (the command has no search mode); the 800-id pick is the pick of the pooled 800 values (a gate row).

### 4. The lines
`SETS` (the ids); `MEANS ON 800` (each of the eight, dark/lit and penalised); `PICKS PER SET` (twenty rows per group: the pick and the margin of the best over the second on that set); `AGREEMENT`; `GAPS`; `WINNER'S CURSE`; `WORST PICKS`; `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the agreement of a 40-id set with the 800-id ranking over all four (rank correlation per set), and the agreement with c02-relative readings of H46.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: S02 agrees in 8 to 14 of 20 (modal 11), T02 in 3 to 8 of 20 (modal 5) -- S02's best is probably clearer (`s02_26011` was far ahead in H42 and H46) and T02's four are close. Part B: YES 35 percent. Part C: S02 gap at least 0.01 with 70 percent; T02 gap below 0.005 with 60 percent; agreement larger for the group with the larger gap: 85 percent. Part D: both means positive, S02 about +0.003 and T02 about +0.006: both above zero 90 percent. Part E: S02 zero sets 70 percent, T02 at least one set 55 percent. Part F: 0 sets with a tie: 99 percent. Part G: both below 0.01: 70 percent. A container restart interrupts the flight: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead48.mjs` (the library: the twenty sets, the eight networks from stored rows, the arms, the per-set means and picks, the agreement, the gap, the winner's curse, the standard errors, the seven-part verdict in every branch, the eleven checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk, built from H46's library), `headToHead48Cmd.mjs` (`fly --parts 4` with `--from-json` to report again; no search mode; a smoke mode on 2 sets x 2 networks writing to a temporary directory) and `headToHead48-selfcheck.mjs`, with a serial mutation pass and a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say the 800-id pick is the truth: 800 ids carry a standard error (about 0.0025 on a paired reading, H46) and the best two of a group may be within it, so "agreement with 800" is agreement with a better but finite reading. The twenty sets are twenty slices of ONE 800-id draw, so each set's pick shares ids with the reference and the agreement rate is pushed up by that overlap (a set is one twentieth of the 800); the rate is therefore an upper bound on the agreement with an independent reference. Eight networks in two groups of four are two small examples; another search has other gaps. It says nothing about the champion rule of the later rounds (H35 to H41, H43, H45, H47) or about whether a larger held-out set would have changed any claim of the series: H42 and H44 read that, and this round only puts a rate on the 40-id case.

## Round H49, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H35 to H41, H43, H45, H47 and H48 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H42's, H44's and H46's results are read): A DIFFERENT SEED COUNT -- EIGHT MORE SEEDS OF THE SAME SEARCH, SIXTEEN IN ALL
### 1. Why
H44 asked what the best of 1, 2, 4 and 8 seeds buys, but only from the eight searches that already existed (H32's S02, seeds 26011-26014, and H34's T02, seeds 26031-26034): it found the best of eight by held-out value ties `c02` and the other seven are all behind it, a single seed 0.024 behind. That curve stops at eight because there are no more networks. **This round runs EIGHT NEW SEEDS of the same search** (**U02**, seeds **26051-26058**, H32's search byte for byte at 0.02: 1800 iterations, checkpoints 900, 1200, 1500, 1800) and flies the sixteen 1800-iteration networks, **all on one new audit**, each by itself. Sixteen same-recipe networks make three things readable that eight could not: whether the held-out pick over sixteen still lands on a `c02`-tie, whether the eight new seeds behave like the eight old (same recipe, different seeds: a replication of the seed-to-seed spread), and how well the held-out rank orders sixteen networks on the audit.
> (A) Does the champion of the eight new (best held-out value at 1800) tie `c02`? (B) Does the best of the sixteen by held-out value tie `c02`? (C) Does the best of the eight new on the audit tie `c02`? (D) How many of the sixteen are not behind `c02`? (E) Do any lose subs? (F) How well does the held-out rank order the sixteen on the audit? (G) Do the new eight differ from the old eight in mean audit value? (H) Does the best of sixteen by held-out value beat the best of the old eight by held-out value on the audit?

### 2. The searches, the audit and the arms
**The searches (8):** H32's search, byte for byte (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, state file every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, held-out ids 26100-26139), **seeds 26051-26058** (not used by any earlier round), group **U02**. The champion of a group at 1800 is the best held-out value of its members (the first of a tie in seed order). Resumable as in H32. Eight searches run four at a time.
**The audit:** ids **49200-49399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (47399) and disjoint from H48's 53000-53799. **Arms (20):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; the old eight `s02_26011` to `s02_26014`, `t02_26031` to `t02_26034` (stored weights at 1800, read again from the stored searches); the new eight `u02_26051` to `u02_26058`. Every arm is read on the penalised priced score at 0.02 (the objective of the searches), paired by id, at 2 se (the switchboard's reading).
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `.** **A:** `THE NEW CHAMPION <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02` (by held-out value among the eight new). **B:** `THE BEST OF 16 BY HO <..> C02` (the best held-out value at 1800 over all sixteen stored values; the first on a tie in the order s02, t02, u02). **C:** `THE BEST OF THE NEW 8 ON THE AUDIT <..> C02` (the new network with the best audit mean). **D:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: NEW n OF 8, OLD m OF 8, ALL k OF 16` (reading not WORSE). **E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE SIXTEEN: <each network that lost at least one of 400 audit runs as name n, or NONE>`. **F:** `HO RANK VS AUDIT RANK OVER 16: rho <r>` (Spearman rho with ties averaged between the stored held-out value at 1800 and the audit mean, two decimals). **G:** `NEW MINUS OLD MEAN AUDIT VALUE: <x> (se <s>); WITHIN 2 SE OF ZERO: <YES|NO>` (four decimals; `se` = the square root of the two groups' variances of the audit means over their eight networks, each divided by eight; a network is one observation). **H:** `THE BEST OF 16 BY HO BEATS THE BEST OF THE OLD 8 BY HO ON THE AUDIT: <YES|NO>` (strictly higher audit mean).

### 3. The checks
Fourteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 8 searches, each seed once, each with four checkpoints of 963 finite weights, and the new networks are their 1800 weights; the old eight are the stored 1800 weights of H32 and H34; `c02`, `wip`, `q02` as registered; the seeds are 26051-26058 and not any earlier seed; id sets disjoint and the audit past 47399 and clear of 53000-53799; Spearman is computed on ranks with ties averaged (a gate row against hand cases); the resume proof (H32's).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH U02` (start, then train and held-out value and accepted count at each checkpoint, lost flights, per seed); `RESUMES`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN`; `PAIRED` (each of the sixteen against `c02`, in order s02, t02, u02, with mean, se, better, worse, same and reading); `HO VS AUDIT` (each network's held-out value and audit mean and their ranks); `GROUPS` (mean audit value, sd and best of the old eight and of the new eight); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the best of N by held-out value on the audit for N = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 over the sixteen in a fixed order (the mean over all subsets of that size, as in H44), the distances of the new networks from `wip`.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 45, BEATS 5. Part B: TIES 50, IS BEHIND 40, BEATS 10. Part C: TIES 60, IS BEHIND 30, BEATS 10. Part D: ALL 16 modal 3 (0 to 6: 85 percent); NEW modal 1 of 8. Part E: at least one network loses a run 90 percent. Part F: rho above 0.3: 60 percent; above 0.6: 25 percent. Part G: WITHIN 2 SE YES 80 percent (the new eight are the old eight's distribution). Part H: YES 40 percent. The champion of the eight new has a held-out value at 1800 above `wip`'s start value 90 percent. At least one of the eight new reads WORSE than every old network's best held-out value on HO (that is, the new group's best HO value is below the old group's best HO value, 0.7592): 45 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 25 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead49.mjs` (H32's loop through H34's library pattern with group U02 and seeds 26051-26058, the sixteen-arm audit, the readings, the eight-part verdict in every branch, the fourteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead49Cmd.mjs` (`search --workers 4` resumable with state files named for H49 and skipping finished seeds, `fly --parts 4`, `fly --from-json`, a smoke mode that writes only to a temporary directory) and `headToHead49-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against loops written out in the gate and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say the search reaches `c02` or that a seed count of sixteen is enough: sixteen is still sixteen draws of one search on one task, and the readings are on one 200-id audit with a standard error near 0.005 on a paired reading (H46). The old eight and the new eight were audited on one audit here but their searches were run at different times with the same code, so any drift in the box between runs would not show. The held-out rank is the 40-id value and is noisy by H42 and H48's design; a high rho would not say 40 ids are enough. It says nothing about H35 to H41, H43, H45, H47 or H48, or about other prices.

## Round H35, RESULT (2026-11-11): A ROTATING TRAIN SET WITH THE SAME FLIGHTS IS BEHIND `c02` ON ALL FOUR SEEDS AND BEHIND ITS FIXED-SET TWIN ON ONE -- IT LEARNS LESS OF THE TRAIN IDS (GAP -0.0019 AGAINST H32'S +0.0494) AND THE HELD-OUT PICK IS NOT THE BEST OF THE FOUR ON THE AUDIT
Flown by `node tools/ship/headToHead35Cmd.mjs search --workers 4` then `fly --parts 4` (four searches of 5400 iterations in 36 blocks of 150 over twelve 24-id ranges, seeds 26011-26014, group U02, checkpoints 2700 / 3600 / 4500 / 5400; the 12 arms flown dark and lit on ids 35200-35399, 160 s, read once; data: `brain/rig-results/head-to-head35.json`, the searches `head-to-head35-search.jsonl`). **All 15 checks PASS.** No search was ever resumed (RESUMES: all four "never resumed"). *Naming, disclosed:* this round's group label `U02` and network names `u02_26011` to `u02_26014` are the ROTATING networks of seeds 26011-26014; H49 reuses the label `U02` for eight other networks (seeds 26051-26058, fixed train set) and names them `u02_26051` and up. The two are different networks in different result files.

**The verdict, as printed (seven parts):** THE ROTATING CHAMPION IS BEHIND C02; THE ROTATING CHAMPION IS BEHIND THE LAW OF THE PRICE; THE ROTATING CHAMPION IS BEHIND H32'S CHAMPION; TWINS: 1 AHEAD, 1 BEHIND, 2 TIED; LOST SUBS IN THE U02 NETWORKS: u02_26011 7, u02_26012 16, u02_26013 5, u02_26014 6; THE CHAMPION'S GAP IS SMALLER THAN H32'S: YES; ROTATING NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: 0 OF 4.

**The champion and the four.** The group champion at 5400 by the 40-id held-out value is `u02_26012` (held-out 0.7652, above H32's best 0.7592 and H34's 0.7591 (the other rounds' values are not read here)), and it is the **second worst** of the eight networks on the audit: audit means (penalised priced score at 0.02) in rank order `s02_26011` 0.7657, `s02_26014` 0.7450, `u02_26014` 0.7439, `s02_26012` 0.7424, `u02_26013` 0.7411, `u02_26011` 0.7369, `u02_26012` 0.7242, `s02_26013` 0.7110. `u02_26012` minus `c02` is -0.0372 (se 0.0087; better 65, worse 135) WORSE, minus `q02` -0.0360 WORSE, minus H32's champion -0.0415 (se 0.0100) WORSE. **All four rotating networks are WORSE than `c02`:** `u02_26011` -0.0244 (se 0.0063), `u02_26012` -0.0372, `u02_26013` -0.0203 (0.0077), `u02_26014` -0.0174 (0.0065). The best of the four ON THE AUDIT is `u02_26014`, not the held-out champion: the 40-id held-out pick failed again (H34, H42, H44 and H46 read the same noise).

**The twins** (each rotating network against the fixed-set network of its seed): `u02_26013` minus `s02_26013` **+0.0301** (se 0.0097; better 124, worse 76) BETTER; `u02_26011` minus `s02_26011` -0.0288 (se 0.0075) WORSE; `u02_26012` minus `s02_26012` -0.0182 (se 0.0108) TIES; `u02_26014` minus `s02_26014` -0.0010 (se 0.0073) TIES. The one BETTER is against `s02_26013`, the worst of the eight and the network that loses 12 of 400 runs; the one WORSE is against H32's champion.

**Why it did not help: the rotating networks do not fit the old 72 ids.** The registered gap (value on the old 72 ids minus held-out value) of the champion at 5400 is -0.0019 (train 0.7633, held-out 0.7652) against H32's +0.0494; the four seeds' gaps at 5400 are 0.0243, -0.0019, 0.0102 and 0.0172, and the train values on the old 72 ids sit at 0.757 to 0.789 at every checkpoint, below the 0.81 the fixed-set champion reached. So the gap is small because **the network never learned the old ids as the fixed-set search did, not because it generalised better**: the audit means of the rotating networks (0.7242 to 0.7439) are below H32's champion (0.7657) and the mean of the four (0.7365) is below the mean of the four fixed-set twins (0.7410) by 0.0045. The held-out value of the rotating champion is above both earlier groups' best and its audit value is the second lowest of the eight: the same winner's curse as H34's pick, larger.

**Lost subs and power.** Every rotating network loses runs (7, 16, 5 and 6 of 400; the fixed-set twins lose 2, 5, 12 and 0), and the searches themselves lost 1651, 615, 1699 and 676 flights over 5400 iterations (each seed's `lost-flights` in the SEARCH line). The rotating networks draw less extra power than their twins at seeds 26011 (+0.24 kW against +0.74) and 26012 (+0.07 against +1.50) and more at 26013 and 26014 (+0.94 and +1.49 against +0.76 and +1.08); the penalised priced gain at 0.02 is +0.0604, +0.0477, +0.0646 and +0.0674 for the four, against `c02`'s +0.0848.

**Expectations, hit and missed (registered in section 5).** Held: Part E at least one network loses a run (my 65: all four lose); Part F YES (80); the champion's held-out value at 5400 above 0.7592 (55: 0.7652); a restart interrupts the searches at least once (my 15 percent said no: none did). Missed: **Part A TIES (my 55; BEHIND was 35); Part B TIES (55; BEHIND 35); Part C TIES (50; BEHIND 30); Part D ahead count modal 2 (1 ahead was 25 percent); Part G modal 1 or 2 (0 of 4 was 20).** Four of nine statements held; every miss is on the optimistic side: I expected a rotating set to at least tie where it did not.

**What this does NOT say.** It does not say a rotating train set cannot help. The registration fixed the flights per seed, so a rotating network saw each id 150 iterations per pass and three passes: one rotation schedule, one block length, four seeds, 200 audit ids (a pairing's se is 0.0063 to 0.0108, so the twin pairings that TIE could be a gap of 0.02 either way). The held-out pick failing on the audit is the 40-id noise of H42/H48's design and not a property of rotation. The small gap is not generalisation: the train value on the old 72 ids is below the fixed set's, so the networks may be simply under-trained on the task the ids represent, and 5400 iterations of 24 ids may reach less than 1800 iterations of 72. The lost-flight count of the searches is a count of flights (4 per candidate), not of candidates. It says nothing about H36 to H49.

## Round H50, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45, H47, H48 and H49 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's result is read, H40's is not): A DIFFERENT PRICE -- THE SEARCH AT 0.01
### 1. Why
The prices of this series' network searches are 0.02 (H27, H28, H29, H32, H34, H35 and their kin), 0.04 (H30, H31) and, registered in H40 and not yet read, 0.005; H47 registers a pair of 0.01 and 0.02 as one objective. **No single-price search has been registered at 0.01**, the second of H27's four prices, where its law of the price `q01` is the champion of a stored six-seed law search. The price says how much power a pilot may spend for a better score: at 0.01 a kilowatt costs half what it does at 0.02, and `c02` (the law of H21's reduced family tuned at 0.02) was not tuned for it. This round runs **H32's search at 0.01**: the same loop, the same 72 train ids, 1800 iterations, the same seeds, with only the price changed (H40's library with the price handed in), and reads everything at 0.01, with H32's four 0.02 networks as twins read at the new price.
> (A) Does the champion tie `c02` at 0.01? (B) The law of its price, `q01`? (C) Is it better than H32's champion (trained at 0.02), both read at 0.01? (D) Seed by seed, do these networks beat their 0.02 twins at 0.01? (E) Do they lose subs? (F) How many are not behind `c02`? (G) Does the champion draw more power than H32's? (H) Read at 0.02, does the champion tie `c02` (the price cross)?

### 2. The searches and the arms
**The searches (4).** H32's search, byte for byte, except **the price is 0.01** (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, the penalised priced objective on the 72 train ids at 0.01, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, 1800 iterations, checkpoints 900, 1200, 1500, 1800, resumable with state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, held-out ids 26100-26139), seeds **26011-26014** (H32's), group **P01**. The first parent's value is `wip`'s penalised value at 0.01 on the 72 ids (a check, computed in this round). Champion at a checkpoint: the best held-out value (at 0.01) of the four (the first of a tie in seed order); the networks flown are the 1800-iteration ones.
**The audit:** ids **51200-51399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, clear of every earlier audit and of 50000-50071, 52000-52299 and 53000-53799. **Arms (12):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; **`q01`** (the champion of H27's stored `Q01` law search by H27's rule); H32's four 0.02 networks `s02_26011` (H32's champion) to `s02_26014`; the four new `p01_26011` to `p01_26014`. **Every arm is read at 0.01 on the PENALISED priced score**: per id (dark score' + lit score')/2 - 0.01 x (dark kW + lit kW)/2, a lost run scoring 0; the table shows 0.005, 0.01, 0.02 and 0.04, and the pairings at 0.02 are reported.
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `, readings the switchboard's at 2 se paired by id.** **A:** `THE CHAMPION <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02` (at 0.01). **B:** `... THE LAW OF THE PRICE` (`q01`). **C:** `... H32'S CHAMPION` (`s02_26011`, read at 0.01). **D:** `TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `p01_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>` at 0.01, a + b + c = 4). **E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE P01 NETWORKS: <each that lost at least one of 400 audit runs as name n, or NONE>`. **F:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4`. **G:** `THE CHAMPION'S MEAN POWER IS ABOVE H32'S CHAMPION'S: <YES|NO>` (the mean of the dark and lit mean powers over the audit ids). **H:** `AT 0.02 THE CHAMPION <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02` (the pairing at 0.02, the price cross).

### 3. The checks

### 4. The lines

## Round H51, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H50 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read, H49 is registered and not flown): A DIFFERENT SEED COUNT AT EQUAL COST -- SIXTEEN SHORT SEARCHES AGAINST FOUR LONG ONES
### 1. Why
H44 asked what the best of 1 to 8 seeds buys from searches that already existed, and H49 registers eight more seeds of the same 1800-iteration search. **Both add seeds at a cost of 1800 iterations each.** This round asks the other question: **at a fixed number of flights, is it better to spend them on many short searches or on few long ones?** H32's and H34's four-seed searches cost 4 x 1800 = 7200 iterations. This round spends the same 7200 iterations on **sixteen seeds of 450 iterations** (same loop, same 72 train ids, same price 0.02) and picks among them by the 40-id held-out value at 450, exactly as the long groups were picked at 1800. The comparison is the one the series cares about: a pick from many cheap seeds against a pick from few expensive ones.
> (A) Does the held-out pick of the sixteen short networks tie `c02`? (B) Is it better than H32's long champion `s02_26011`? (C) Does the best of the sixteen ON THE AUDIT tie `c02`? (D) How many short and how many long networks are not behind `c02`? (E) Do the sixteen differ from the eight long ones in mean audit value? (F) Does the short pick beat the long pick head to head? (G) Do the short networks lose subs? (H) How well does the 450-iteration held-out rank order the sixteen on the audit?

### 2. The searches, the audit and the arms
**The searches (16):** H32's search, byte for byte (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, held-out ids 26100-26139), with ONE change: **450 iterations** and checkpoints at **225 and 450**. Seeds **27051-27066** (new, no earlier round used them), group **V02**. The pick is the best held-out value at 450 (the first of a tie in seed order). Resumable as in H32; four searches at a time (about 1.4 hours a batch, four batches).
**The audit:** ids **54200-54399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, clear of every earlier audit and of 50000-50071, 52000-52299 and 53000-53799. **Arms (28):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; the eight long networks (H32's `s02_26011` to `s02_26014`, H34's `t02_26031` to `t02_26034`, the stored 1800-iteration weights); the sixteen short `v02_27051` to `v02_27066`. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score, paired by id, at 2 se.
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `.** **A:** `THE SHORT PICK <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02`. **B:** `THE SHORT PICK <..> H32'S CHAMPION` (`s02_26011`). **C:** `THE BEST SHORT NETWORK ON THE AUDIT <..> C02`. **D:** `NOT BEHIND C02: SHORT n OF 16, LONG m OF 8`. **E:** `SHORT MINUS LONG MEAN AUDIT VALUE: <x> (se <s>); WITHIN 2 SE OF ZERO: <YES|NO>` (four decimals; se = sqrt(var_short / 16 + var_long / 8), sample variances over the networks). **F:** `THE SHORT PICK <..> THE LONG PICK` (the long pick = the best held-out value among the eight long networks at 1800, i.e. `s02_26011` by H44's stored values; paired reading). **G:** `LOST SUBS IN THE SHORT NETWORKS: <each that lost at least one of 400 runs as name n, or NONE>`. **H:** `HO RANK VS AUDIT RANK OVER 16 SHORT: rho <r>` (Spearman, ties averaged, two decimals).

### 3. The checks
Fifteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 16 searches, each seed once, each with checkpoints 225 and 450 of 963 finite weights, and the flown short networks are the 450-iteration weights; the eight long networks are the stored 1800-iteration weights; the first parent of every search has `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 ids; the pick is the best held-out at 450; `c02`, `wip`, `q02` as registered; the seeds are new; id sets disjoint and the audit past 47399; Spearman computed on ranks with ties averaged (a gate row); the resume proof (H32's).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH V02` (start, then train and held-out value and accepted count at 225 and 450, lost flights, per seed); `RESUMES`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN`; `PAIRED` (every short network and every long one against `c02`, in order); `HO VS AUDIT` (the sixteen short); `GROUPS` (mean, sd and best of the short sixteen and the long eight); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the best of N short networks by held-out value on the audit for N = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 (mean over all subsets), the same for the long eight at N = 1, 2, 4, 8, and the L1 distances of the short networks from `wip`.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: IS BEHIND 80, TIES 18, BEATS 2. Part B: IS BEHIND 85, TIES 14, BEATS 1. Part C: IS BEHIND 60, TIES 38, BEATS 2. Part D: SHORT modal 0 of 16 (0: 35, 1 to 2: 40, 3 or more: 25 percent), LONG 1 of 8 (as H44, which read 1 of 8 on 200 ids). Part E: the short mean is below the long mean, outside 2 se: 85 percent. Part F: IS BEHIND 85. Part G: at least one short network loses a run 60 percent (shorter searches stay closer to `wip`). Part H: rho above 0.3: 50 percent. The pick's audit value is above the mean of the sixteen: 75 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 25 percent.

### 6. What will be built

Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say short searches are or are not better in general: the cost is held at the number of iterations, not at wall-clock or flights (a candidate costs four flights whatever the length), 450 iterations is one cut-off, and the long networks are the eight that exist (H32's and H34's), not a fresh sixteen. The held-out rank is the 40-id value and is noisy by H42 and H48's design; a rho says nothing about whether 40 ids are enough. One audit of 200 ids has a paired standard error near 0.005 (H46). It says nothing about H36 to H41, H43, H45, H47, H49 or H50, or about other prices.

## Round H52, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H51 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read, H48 and H49 are registered and not flown): A DIFFERENT HELD-OUT SET FOR PICKING THE STOPPING POINT -- DENSE CHECKPOINTS, PICKED BY 40 IDS AND BY 200
### 1. Why
H42 re-picked among the four stored checkpoints (900, 1200, 1500, 1800) of eight searches on a 40-id and a 200-id held-out set; H48 registers twenty 40-id sets on the final networks. **Neither can say what a held-out set is worth for choosing WHEN TO STOP**, because four checkpoints are too few to see a curve and none of the stored searches evaluated a large held-out set while the search ran. This round runs four new searches of H32's recipe with **nine checkpoints (every 200 iterations)** and, at each, evaluates the network on **two held-out sets: HO (the series' 40 ids, 26100-26139) and HX (200 new ids, 55000-55199)**; a held-out evaluation costs flights in proportion to its ids, so HX at nine checkpoints costs about as much as 25 search iterations. All 36 checkpoint networks are then flown on one audit, and the stopping point picked by HO and the one picked by HX are compared on it.
> (A) Does picking the stopping point by HX give a higher audit value than by HO? (B) In how many of the four seeds? (C) Is either pick better than simply stopping at 1800? (D) How well do HO and HX each rank the nine checkpoints of a seed on the audit? (E) Does the best HX-picked network tie `c02`? (F) The best HO-picked? (G) Do the checkpoint networks lose subs? (H) Does the audit value rise with iterations at all, over the nine checkpoints?

### 2. The searches, the audit and the arms
**The searches (4):** H32's search, byte for byte (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, 1800 iterations, state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint), with ONE change: **checkpoints at 200, 400, ..., 1800 (nine)** and at each checkpoint **two held-out values**: the penalised priced value on HO and on HX. Seeds **28001-28004** (new), group **W02**. The held-out evaluations read no random draw: the loop and generator stream are H32's. Resumable as in H32.
**The audit:** ids **56200-56399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, clear of every earlier audit and of 50000-50071, 52000-52299, 53000-53799 and 55000-55199. **Arms (40):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; the 36 checkpoint networks `w02_<seed>_<iter>` (4 seeds x 9 checkpoints). Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score, paired by id, at 2 se.
**Picks:** per seed, the checkpoint with the best HO value (the first on a tie, earliest iteration) and the one with the best HX value (likewise).
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `.** **A:** `HX PICK MINUS HO PICK MEAN AUDIT VALUE: <x>` (the mean over the four seeds of the audit mean of the HX-picked network minus that of the HO-picked, four decimals with sign). **B:** `SEEDS WHERE THE HX PICK IS HIGHER ON THE AUDIT: n OF 4 (EQUAL: e)` (strictly higher; equal when the two picks are the same checkpoint or tie). **C:** `PICK MINUS THE 1800 NETWORK, MEAN OVER SEEDS: HX <x>, HO <y>` (four decimals with sign). **D:** `RANK CORRELATION OVER THE NINE CHECKPOINTS WITH THE AUDIT, MEAN OVER SEEDS: HX <r>, HO <s>` (Spearman, ties averaged, two decimals). **E:** `THE BEST HX-PICKED NETWORK <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02` (the HX-picked network with the best HX value of the four). **F:** `THE BEST HO-PICKED NETWORK <..> C02`. **G:** `LOST SUBS IN THE 36: <each that lost at least one of 400 runs as name n, or NONE>`. **H:** `AUDIT VALUE RISES WITH ITERATIONS: <YES|NO>` (the mean over seeds of the Spearman correlation between the checkpoint iteration and the audit value is above 0.5).

### 3. The checks
Sixteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each seed once, each with nine checkpoints at the registered iterations of 963 finite weights and two finite held-out values, and the flown networks are the checkpoints' weights; **the held-out values are the searches' own** (a gate row: the search's HO value equals the penalised value of the same weights flown on HO, and its HX value the same on HX); the first parent of every search has `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 ids; `c02`, `wip`, `q02` as registered; the seeds are new; HO, HX and the audit are pairwise disjoint, disjoint from the train ids and every earlier id set named above, and the audit is past 47399; Spearman computed on ranks with ties averaged (a gate row); the resume proof (H32's); the loop equals H32's `longSearch` at every common checkpoint when the extra evaluations are switched off (a gate row).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH W02` (start, then per checkpoint train value, HO value, HX value and accepted count, lost flights, per seed); `RESUMES`; `PICKS` (per seed: the HO and HX picks and the 1800 network, with their audit values); `CURVES` (per seed the nine checkpoints' HO, HX and audit values); `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `PAIRED` (the picks and the 1800 networks against `c02`); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the oracle pick (the best checkpoint on the audit) per seed and its audit value, the correlation of HO with HX over the 36, and the standard deviation of HO minus HX.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: positive 60 percent (mean difference within +-0.005: 55). Part B: 2 or more of 4 seeds 55 percent (modal 2). Part C: both picks below the 1800 network on average 40 percent, both above 35, split 25. Part D: HX correlation above HO correlation 70 percent; both below 0.7: 70 percent. Part E: IS BEHIND 65, TIES 33, BEATS 2. Part F: IS BEHIND 70, TIES 29, BEATS 1. Part G: at least one of the 36 loses a run 90 percent. Part H: NO 55 percent (the series' checkpoints zigzag, H32). A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 20 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead52.mjs` (H32's loop written out with the checkpoint list and the extra evaluations handed in, the same generator stream and state with the held-out pairs, the 40 arms, the picks, the readings, the eight-part verdict in every branch, the sixteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead52Cmd.mjs` (`search --workers 4` resumable with state files named for H52 and skipping finished seeds, `fly --parts 4`, `fly --from-json`, a smoke mode writing only to a temporary directory) and `headToHead52-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against loops written out in the gate and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say HX is the right held-out set or 200 ids enough: 200 ids have a standard error near 0.005 on a pairing and the checkpoints of one search are strongly correlated with each other (neighbouring checkpoints differ by a few accepted mutations), so a rank correlation over nine neighbours is a statement about a curve, not nine independent trials. Four seeds are four curves. HX and the audit are disjoint draws of the same distribution of ids, so nothing here says how a held-out set from another distribution of scenarios would pick. It says nothing about H36 to H41, H43, H45, H47 to H51, or about other prices.

## Round H53, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H52 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read, H45 is registered and not flown): A DIFFERENT TRAIN SET SEED -- FOUR TRAIN SETS, ONE SEARCH SEED: HOW MUCH OF THE SPREAD IS THE TRAIN IDS?
### 1. Why
H45 registers one other 72-id train set run with H32's four seeds. That answers whether another train set moves the champion, but a single other set cannot say **how much of the spread between networks is the train ids and how much is the search seed**. Every spread in this series (H34, H44, H49) varies the search seed with the 72 train ids fixed. This round does the opposite: **one search seed (26011) and four different train sets**, so the spread between the four networks is the train-set spread. Set against the spread of the eight stored networks (four seeds each of H32 and H34, one train set) it separates the two sources of luck.
> (A) Does the best of the four (by held-out value) tie `c02`? (B) H32's champion? (C) How many of the four are not behind `c02`, against how many of the eight stored seed networks? (D) Is the spread (sd of audit means) across train sets larger than across seeds? (E) Do the train-set networks differ from the seed networks in mean audit value? (F) Does the same-seed network on the original train set (`s02_26011`) lie inside the range of the four? (G) Do the four lose subs? (H) Is the held-out pick the best of the four on the audit?

### 2. The searches, the audit and the arms
**The train sets (4):** each a pair of ranges of the same shape as H27's (24 ids and 48 ids, 72 in all), new and disjoint from every earlier set: **TA = 70000-70023 and 70100-70147; TB = 71000-71023 and 71100-71147; TC = 72000-72023 and 72100-72147; TD = 73000-73023 and 73100-73147**. **The searches (4):** H32's search, byte for byte (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, 1800 iterations, checkpoints 900, 1200, 1500, 1800, state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, held-out ids 26100-26139), **search seed 26011 for all four** (so the generator stream is the same and only the train ids differ) with the train set TA, TB, TC or TD: group **Z02**, networks `z02_a` to `z02_d`. The first parent's value is `wip`'s on that train set (a check per search). The champion (the pick) is the best held-out value at 1800 of the four (the first of a tie in the order a, b, c, d). Resumable as in H32.
**The audit:** ids **74200-74399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, clear of every earlier audit and of every train set and held-out set named in this round. **Arms (17):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; the eight stored 1800-iteration networks (H32's `s02_26011` to `s02_26014`, H34's `t02_26031` to `t02_26034`); the four `z02_a` to `z02_d`. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score, paired by id, at 2 se.
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `.** **A:** `THE BEST TRAIN-SET NETWORK <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02`. **B:** `THE BEST TRAIN-SET NETWORK <..> H32'S CHAMPION` (`s02_26011`). **C:** `NOT BEHIND C02: TRAIN SETS n OF 4, SEEDS m OF 8`. **D:** `SD OF AUDIT MEANS: TRAIN SETS <x>, SEEDS <y>; TRAIN SETS LARGER: <YES|NO>` (sample sd, four decimals; the seeds are the eight stored networks). **E:** `TRAIN-SET MINUS SEED MEAN AUDIT VALUE: <x> (se <s>); WITHIN 2 SE OF ZERO: <YES|NO>` (se = sqrt(var_train / 4 + var_seed / 8)). **F:** `S02_26011 IS INSIDE THE RANGE OF THE FOUR: <YES|NO>` (inclusive). **G:** `LOST SUBS IN THE TRAIN-SET NETWORKS: <each that lost at least one of 400 runs as name n, or NONE>`. **H:** `THE HELD-OUT PICK IS THE BEST OF THE FOUR ON THE AUDIT: <YES|NO>`.

### 3. The checks
Fifteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; ids aligned; no score outside (0, 1); every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); exactly 4 searches, each train set once, each with four checkpoints of 963 finite weights, and the flown networks are the 1800-iteration weights; **each search ran on its own train set** (every record carries its train-set label and the ids, and the gate shows the loop equal to H32's `longSearch` on each set); the first parent of every search has `wip`'s penalised value on its own train set, computed in this round; the pick is the best held-out at 1800; the eight stored networks are the stored 1800 weights; `c02`, `wip`, `q02` as registered; the train sets are four pairs of disjoint ranges of 24 and 48 ids, disjoint from each other, from H27's 72 ids, from the held-out ids and the audit; the audit is past 47399; the search seed is 26011 in every record; the resume proof (H32's).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH Z02` (train set, start, train and held-out value and accepted count at each checkpoint, lost flights, per search); `RESUMES`; `PICKS`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN`; `PAIRED` (each of the twelve networks against `c02`); `GROUPS` (mean, sd, min and max of the four and of the eight); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the pairings of each `z02` against `s02_26011` (the same seed, the original train set), the L1 distances of each from `wip` and from `s02_26011`, and the audit means in rank order of all twelve.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: IS BEHIND 60, TIES 38, BEATS 2. Part B: IS BEHIND 60, TIES 38, BEATS 2. Part C: TRAIN SETS modal 1 of 4 (0: 25, 1: 35, 2: 25, 3 or 4: 15 percent), SEEDS 1 of 8 (as H44 read). Part D: TRAIN SETS LARGER 40 percent (the series' seed spread is large: H44 saw 0.0129 to 0.0547 behind, and a train set changes the whole objective surface the same way a seed changes its path). Part E: WITHIN 2 SE YES 70 percent. Part F: YES 60 percent. Part G: at least one of the four loses a run 85 percent. Part H: YES 35 percent (one in four by chance, a little better if held-out carries signal). A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 20 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead53.mjs` (H40's loop with the train set and the search seed handed in per search, the same generator stream and state with the train-set label, the champion rule, the 17 arms, the audit, the readings, the eight-part verdict in every branch, the fifteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead53Cmd.mjs` (`search --workers 4` resumable with state files named for H53 and skipping finished sets, `fly --parts 4`, `fly --from-json`, a smoke mode writing only to a temporary directory) and `headToHead53-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against loops written out in the gate, a row that the loop equals H32's `longSearch` on each train set, and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
Four train sets are four draws, and four networks give an sd with a wide interval: part D compares two small-sample sds and says nothing about their ratio's interval. The seed spread uses eight networks from two earlier rounds (H32, H34), whose train set is the same one and which were audited on other ids in their own rounds; here all are flown on one audit. One search seed means the generator stream is shared: the four paths still diverge from the first accepted mutation because the objectives differ, but they are not independent draws of the search. It does not say that a train set's size or construction matters (H41 registers size) and says nothing about H36 to H41, H43, H45 or H47 to H52.

## Round H54, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built or read; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H53 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read; H48 is registered and NOT FLOWN, and this round cannot be built until it is): A DIFFERENT HELD-OUT SET BY DIFFICULTY -- HARD IDS, EASY IDS
### 1. Why
H42, H48 and H52 vary the held-out set by size, by draw and by timing; **every one of those sets is a random draw of ids**. A held-out set is a sample of the task, and a sample can be easy or hard: a pick made on 40 easy ids may rank networks by how they behave when nothing goes wrong. H48 will fly the eight stored 1800-iteration networks (H32's S02, H34's T02) and the autopilot on 800 fresh ids (53000-53799), with per-id scores. **This round reads those stored runs and builds held-out sets by difficulty**, where an id's difficulty is the autopilot's own penalised score on it (a quantity that does not depend on the networks): four quartiles of 200 ids, each cut into five sets of 40. It runs **no search and no flight**; it reads `brain/rig-results/head-to-head48.json` once it exists.
> (A) Does the best network of a group depend on the difficulty quartile? (B) How often does a 40-id set from a quartile pick what all 800 ids pick, by quartile, against H48's twenty random sets? (C) Is the agreement of hard sets lower than that of easy sets? (D) Does a quartile's own 200 ids pick differently from the 800? (E) Does the network ranking on a quartile resemble the overall ranking? (F) Is the margin between the best and second best larger on hard ids?

### 2. What is read and how the sets are built
**Data:** H48's stored dark and lit runs of the autopilot and the eight networks on ids 53000-53799 (800 ids, 160 s), scored per id by H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 (a lost run scores 0); H48's own verdict must read (its checks all PASS) or this round reads NOT WEIGHED. **Difficulty:** `d_i` = the autopilot's penalised score on id i. **Quartiles:** the 800 ids sorted ascending by `d_i` (ties by smaller id first) cut into `Q1` (the 200 lowest `d_i`: hardest) to `Q4` (the 200 highest: easiest). **Sets:** inside a quartile the ids in increasing id order, cut into five sets of 40 (`Q1.0` to `Q4.4`: twenty sets). **Reference picks:** a group's pick on a set of ids is the best mean penalised score of its four networks (the first on a tie in seed order); the 800-id pick is the pick on all 800 (H48's own). **Baseline:** H48's twenty random consecutive 40-id sets (`HC_0` to `HC_19`), read from the same runs.
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `.** **A:** `QUARTILE PICKS (200 IDS): S02 Q1 <seed>, Q2 <seed>, Q3 <seed>, Q4 <seed>; T02 Q1 <seed>, Q2 <seed>, Q3 <seed>, Q4 <seed>; 800-ID PICKS: S02 <seed>, T02 <seed>`. **B:** `40-ID SETS THAT AGREE WITH 800, BY QUARTILE (OF 5): S02 <a1> <a2> <a3> <a4>, T02 <b1> <b2> <b3> <b4>`. **C:** `RANDOM SETS AGREE: S02 <n> OF 20, T02 <m> OF 20; DIFFICULTY SETS AGREE: S02 <n'> OF 20, T02 <m'> OF 20`. **D:** `QUARTILES WHOSE 200-ID PICK DIFFERS FROM THE 800-ID PICK: S02 <k> OF 4, T02 <l> OF 4`. **E:** `RANK CORRELATION OF A QUARTILE'S FOUR MEANS WITH THE 800-ID MEANS, MEAN OVER QUARTILES: S02 <r>, T02 <s>` (Spearman, ties averaged, two decimals). **F:** `MEAN MARGIN OF THE BEST OVER THE SECOND (40-ID SETS): Q1 <x1>, Q2 <x2>, Q3 <x3>, Q4 <x4>` (mean over both groups and the five sets of a quartile, four decimals). **G:** `AUTOPILOT MEAN PENALISED SCORE BY QUARTILE: Q1 <x>, Q2 <x>, Q3 <x>, Q4 <x>` (four decimals, ascending by construction). **H:** `HARD SETS (Q1) AGREE LESS THAN EASY SETS (Q4): <YES|NO>` (the Q1 count of both groups, summed, is strictly smaller than the Q4 count).

### 3. The checks
Nine rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): H48's stored result is the registered one (ids 53000-53799, nine arms, 800 ids in both conditions, aligned, no score outside (0, 1), power finite and at least 200 W, all its checks PASS); the quartiles partition the 800 ids (four of 200, each id once, `d` non-decreasing across them, ties by smaller id); every quartile set is 40 ids of one quartile and the five of a quartile partition it; H48's twenty random sets are the registered consecutive ones; the per-id penalised scores equal the objective recomputed from the stored runs (to 1e-12); the pick rule (first on a tie in seed order) and the agreement counts against loops written out in the gate; Spearman with ties averaged (a gate row); the round runs no flight and no search (the command has neither mode); the 800-id picks equal H48's own verdict's.

### 4. The lines
`QUARTILES` (the cut values of `d`, the ids' count per quartile, the autopilot's mean per quartile); `MEANS BY QUARTILE` (each of the eight networks' mean per quartile and on 800); `PICKS` (per quartile and group, the 200-id pick and the five 40-id picks); `AGREEMENT`; `MARGINS`; `RANDOM VS DIFFICULTY`; `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the same by difficulty terciles of 266, 267 and 267 ids (cut into sets of 40 with the remainder dropped), and the correlation between a network's score and the autopilot's over the 800 ids.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: the S02 pick is the same in at least three quartiles 60 percent; the T02 pick the same in at least three 40. Part B: Q1 agreement at most 2 of 5 for both groups 55 percent; Q4 at least 3 of 5 for S02 50. Part C: difficulty sets agree in fewer cases than random sets (summed over both groups) 55 percent. Part D: at least one quartile pick differs from the 800 pick in each group 70 percent. Part E: both correlations above 0.5 60 percent. Part F: Q1 margin the largest 50 percent. Part G: the autopilot's Q1 mean is below 0.6 and Q4 above 0.8: 40 percent. Part H: YES 55 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead54.mjs` (the quartiles, the sets, the picks, the agreement counts, the correlations, the margins, the eight-part verdict in every branch, the nine checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead54Cmd.mjs` (`read` of H48's stored json; no flight and no search mode; `--from-json` is its only input) and `headToHead54-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against loops written out in the gate and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. **Nothing is built before this registration is committed and H48's result exists.**

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say difficulty by the autopilot is the right notion of a hard scenario (an id the autopilot finds hard may be easy for a network, and the score is the penalised priced one), or that the quartile sets are independent of the 800 ids they are cut from (each quartile set is a fifth of a quartile, so its agreement with the 800-id pick is flattered by overlap, as H48's random sets are: the comparison in part C is between two overlapping designs). Eight networks in two groups of four are two small examples. It says nothing about a held-out set drawn from another distribution of scenarios than H48's ids.

## Round H55, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H54 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read): A DIFFERENT PRICE -- THE SEARCH AT 0.08
### 1. Why
The prices of this series' single-price network searches are 0.02 (H27, H28, H29, H32, H34, H35 and their kin), 0.04 (H30, H31), and, registered and not yet read, 0.005 (H40) and 0.01 (H50); H47 registers the pair 0.01/0.02 as one objective. **Nothing has been registered above 0.04.** At 0.08 a kilowatt costs four times what it does at 0.02, so a pilot that draws what `c02` (the law of H21's reduced family, tuned at 0.02) draws pays heavily, and a network can only beat it by spending less power for a given score. This round runs **H32's search at 0.08**: the same loop, the same 72 train ids, 1800 iterations, the same seeds, with only the price changed (H40's library with the price handed in), and reads everything at 0.08, with H32's four 0.02 networks as twins read at the new price. The nearest stored law is `q04`, the law of the price 0.04 (H27); there is no stored law at 0.08, so Part B reads against a law of another price.
> (A) Does the champion tie `c02` at 0.08? (B) The nearest stored law `q04`? (C) Is it better than H32's champion (trained at 0.02), both read at 0.08? (D) Seed by seed, do these networks beat their 0.02 twins at 0.08? (E) Do they lose subs? (F) How many are not behind `c02`? (G) Does the champion draw LESS power than H32's? (H) Read at 0.02, does the champion tie `c02` (the price cross)?

THE LAW OF PRICE 0.04` (`q04`). **C:** `... H32'S CHAMPION` (`s02_26011`, read at 0.08). **D:** `TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `p08_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>` at 0.08, a + b + c = 4). **E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE P08 NETWORKS: <each that lost at least one of 400 audit runs as name n, or NONE>`. **F:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4`. **G:** `THE CHAMPION'S MEAN POWER IS BELOW H32'S CHAMPION'S: <YES|NO>` (the mean of the dark and lit mean powers over the audit ids). **H:** `AT 0.02 THE CHAMPION <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02` (the pairing at 0.02).

Part D: ahead count modal 3 (0: 5, 1: 10, 2: 25, 3: 35, 4: 25 percent). Part E: at least one network loses a run 40 percent (power is dear, so less envelope is spent). Part F: modal 3 (0: 10, 1: 15, 2: 25, 3: 30, 4: 20 percent). Part G: YES 85 percent. Part H: IS BEHIND 65, TIES 30, BEATS 5. `tools/ship/headToHead55.mjs` (H50's library with the price 0.08, group P08, `q04` for `q01`, the twelve arms, the audit read at the price, the eight-part verdict in every branch with G reading BELOW, the sixteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead55Cmd.mjs` (`search --workers 4` resumable with state files named for H55 and skipping finished seeds, `fly --parts 4`, `fly --from-json`, a smoke mode that writes only to a temporary directory) and `headToHead55-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against loops written out in the gate and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. ### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say 0.08 is the price that matters, that a 0.08 network is better than a 0.02 one at any other price (the table and the 0.02 pairings say what they say on these ids only), or that a network that beats `q04` or `c02` has a larger effect of the price: `q04` is a four-weight law of half this price and `c02` was tuned at 0.02. There is no stored network or law at 0.08, so Parts B and C read against comparators of other prices. Four seeds are four draws and a twin pairing across the same seed is not independent.
