## Round H56, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H55 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read; H55 is built and not flown): A DIFFERENT TRAIN SET SIZE -- 12 IDS AND 36 IDS AGAINST THE SERIES' 72
### 1. Why
H41 (registered, not flown) asks about 24 and 144 ids; H53 (registered, not flown) swaps which 72-sized sets are seen. **Nothing yet goes below 24.** The series' searches have all trained on 72 ids and generalised with a gap; the question left is how small the train set can be before the search stops finding anything that transfers, and whether 36 ids, half the series' set and cheaper by half, already do as well. The sets here are **nested inside the series' 72**, so the sizes differ only in how many of the same ids are seen. This round changes only the size of the train set, at H32's price 0.02, and reads the champion of each size on 200 fresh ids.
> (A) Does the 12-id champion tie `c02`? (B) Does the 36-id champion? (C) Does the 36-id champion beat the 12-id one? (D) Does either tie H32's 72-id champion? (E) Is the generalisation gap ordered 12 > 36 > 72? (F) Do they lose subs? (G) How many networks of each size are not behind `c02`? (H) Does the cost scale with the set: flights per search?

### 2. The searches and the arms
**The searches (8).** H32's search, byte for byte, except **the train set**: the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, the penalised priced objective at **0.02**, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, **1800 iterations**, checkpoints 900, 1200, 1500, 1800, resumable (state every 50 and at every checkpoint), held-out ids 26100-26139, seeds **26011-26014** (H32's) in each group. **Group D12:** train ids **26000-26011 (12)**. **Group D36:** train ids **26000-26023 and 30000-30011 (36)**. Both are the first ids of the series' 72 (26000-26023 and 30000-30047). Champion of a group at a checkpoint: the best held-out value of its four seeds (the first on a tie). The first parent's value is `wip`'s penalised value at 0.02 on each group's train set (a check, computed in this round).
**The audit:** ids **77200-77399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (to 76399). **Arms (16):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02` (H27's law of price 0.02); H32's four 72-id networks `s02_26011` (H32's champion) to `s02_26014`; `d12_<seed>` and `d36_<seed>` for the four seeds each, the 1800-iteration networks. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the penalised priced score, a lost run scoring 0; the table shows 0.005, 0.02 and 0.04.
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; ` (readings are the switchboard's at 2 se, BETTER = BEATS, WORSE = IS BEHIND, else TIES, paired by id).** **A:** `THE 12-ID CHAMPION <..> C02`. **B:** `THE 36-ID CHAMPION <..> C02`. **C:** `THE 36-ID CHAMPION <..> THE 12-ID CHAMPION`. **D:** `THE 36-ID CHAMPION <..> H32'S CHAMPION` and `THE 12-ID CHAMPION <..> H32'S CHAMPION` as two sub-readings joined by ` / `. **E:** `GAP ORDER 12 > 36 > 72: <YES or NO>` (the mean over seeds of train minus held-out value at 1800; H32's from its stored search; YES only when strictly ordered). **F:** `LOST SUBS IN THE SIZE NETWORKS: <arms that lost at least one of 400 runs, each as name n, or NONE>`. **G:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4 (12), m OF 4 (36)`. **H:** `FLIGHTS PER SEARCH 12 : 36 : 72 = <the counts the searches report>` (a reading of the cost, not a test).

### 3. The checks
Sixteen rows (a failure makes the verdict NOT WEIGHED): (1) sizes, (2) ids aligned, (3) scores inside (0, 1), (4) powers finite and at least 200 W, (5) the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ, (6) `law0` = autopilot to 1e-6, (7) the penalised objective is H27's; (8) exactly 8 searches, each seed of each group once, each with four checkpoints of 963 finite weights, flown networks = the 1800 weights; (9) **the train sets are as registered** (D12 is exactly 26000-26011, D36 is 26000-26023 plus 30000-30011, each a prefix of the series' 72 in order, and the loop's train argument is checked in the gate); (10) the first parent equals `wip`'s value on each set; (11) the champion is the best held-out; (12) the s02 arms are H32's stored 1800 networks; (13) `c02`, `wip`, `q02` as registered; (14) seeds are H32's; (15) **ID SETS disjoint** (the audit from every train set, the held-out ids and past 76399); (16) the resume proof (H32's).

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH D12`, `SEARCH D36` (start value, train and held-out value, accepted count per checkpoint); `RESUMES`; `CHAMPIONS PER CHECKPOINT`; `GENERALISATION GAP` (12, 36, 72); `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `EXTRA POWER`; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN`; `PAIRED` (the registered pairings); `VERDICT`; `REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT` (distances from wip, audit means in rank order).

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: IS BEHIND 60, TIES 30, BEATS 10. Part B: IS BEHIND 45, TIES 45, BEATS 10. Part C: BEATS 40, TIES 45, IS BEHIND 15. Part D (36 vs H32's): TIES 45, IS BEHIND 45, BEATS 10; (12 vs H32's): IS BEHIND 70, TIES 25, BEATS 5. Part E: YES 70 percent. Part F: at least one lost sub in the 12-id networks 60 percent, in the 36-id 40 percent. Part G: 12-id modal 1 (0:30, 1:35, 2:20, 3:10, 4:5 percent), 36-id modal 2 (0:15, 1:30, 2:30, 3:20, 4:5 percent). Part H: the ratio 12 : 36 : 72 is within 5 percent of 1 : 3 : 6 (the held-out evaluations add a little, a larger share at 12 ids): 60 percent. The 36-id champion's held-out value at 1800 is above the 12-id champion's: 65 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 15 percent.

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead56.mjs` (H41's design with the train set handed in: groups D12 and D36 with their sets frozen, state files carrying the group and refusing another group's train set; champion rule, sixteen arms, audit, readings, verdict, checks, lines; reads nothing from disk; H41 is registered but not built, so this is written from H32's loop and H53's train-set handling, not copied from a flown round), `headToHead56Cmd.mjs` (search with resume, fly, smoke mode that writes only to a temporary directory, every path named for H56) and `headToHead56-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows that the loop equals H32's when the 72-id set is handed in, a reference loop at 12 and 36 ids, and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed. The searches cost about (12 + 36) / 72 of H32's, around 3.6 hours on 4 workers.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say the best train-set size is 12, 36 or 72 (three points, each from four seeds of a noisy search), that nested sets are comparable to unrelated ones (H53 asks that, and neither is read), or that the first ids of the series' 72 are as hard as the rest (one draw of ids; the 12 are all from one range). The searches take the same number of iterations, so a smaller set is also a smaller budget in flights: the round holds iterations, not flights, fixed. It says nothing about H36 to H55, or about sets above 72 (H41's).

### Round H56, CORRECTION to the registration above (2026-11-11, written when the library was built)
Section 6 of the registration says H41 is "registered but not built". **That is wrong: H41's library, command and gate exist and H41 is queued to fly.** The H56 library was built from H41's, with H41's train-set handling and row structure and the sizes, ids and wording of H56 (D12 = 26000-26011, D36 = 26000-26023 and 30000-30011, the audit 77200-77399, `GAP ORDER 12 > 36 > 72`, `FLIGHTS PER SEARCH 12 : 36 : 72`). Nothing else in the registration changes: the sixteen checks are the library's sixteen, and the expectations stand as recorded. A smoke search of the command first wrote four records to the real search file (the H41 command lacks the temporary default that H47 to H55 carry); the file was deleted before any real search and the smoke default now points to a temporary directory.

## Round H57, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H56 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read; H49 and H51 are registered and not flown, so this round does not use them): A DIFFERENT SEED COUNT AT EQUAL COST -- THIRTY-TWO VERY SHORT SEARCHES
### 1. Why
H51 (registered, not flown) spends H32's 7200 iterations on sixteen searches of 450; **nothing registered spends them on more and shorter ones.** This round spends the same 7200 iterations on **thirty-two seeds of 225 iterations** (H32's loop, the same 72 train ids, price 0.02) and picks among them by the 40-id held-out value at 225, as the long groups were picked at 1800. It asks whether doubling the seed count again and halving the length again moves the pick, against the eight stored 1800-iteration networks. H51 is not read, so the two rounds are not compared here.
> (A) Does the held-out pick of the thirty-two tie `c02`? (B) Is it better than H32's champion `s02_26011`? (C) Does the best of the thirty-two ON THE AUDIT tie `c02`? (D) How many short and how many long networks are not behind `c02`? (E) Do the thirty-two differ from the eight long ones in mean audit value? (F) Does the short pick beat the long pick head to head? (G) Do the short networks lose subs? (H) How well does the 225-iteration held-out rank order the thirty-two on the audit?

### 2. The searches, the audit and the arms
**The searches (32):** H32's search, byte for byte (the 963 weights of `network-Wip`, the (1+1)-ES, `PS.mutate` 1 to 4 weights, sigma 0.3, kept when at least the parent's, H27's penalised priced objective at 0.02 on the 72 train ids, H15's flight with H28's `flyPen`, 160 s, state every 50 iterations and at every checkpoint, held-out ids 26100-26139), with ONE change: **225 iterations** and checkpoints at **75, 150 and 225**. Seeds **29001-29032** (new, no earlier round used them), group **X02**. The pick is the best held-out value at 225 (the first of a tie in seed order). Resumable as in H32; four searches at a time (about 0.7 hours a batch, eight batches).
**The audit:** ids **78200-78399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (to 77399). **Arms (44):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; `q02`; the eight long networks (H32's `s02_26011` to `s02_26014`, H34's `t02_26031` to `t02_26034`, the stored 1800-iteration weights); the thirty-two short `x02_29001` to `x02_29032`. Every arm is read at 0.02 on the PENALISED priced score, paired by id, at 2 se.
**The verdict, eight parts joined by `; `.** **A:** `THE SHORT PICK <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02`. **B:** `THE SHORT PICK <..> H32'S CHAMPION` (`s02_26011`). **C:** `THE BEST SHORT NETWORK ON THE AUDIT <..> C02`. **D:** `NOT BEHIND C02: SHORT n OF 32, LONG m OF 8`. **E:** `SHORT MINUS LONG MEAN AUDIT VALUE: <x> (se <s>); WITHIN 2 SE OF ZERO: <YES|NO>` (four decimals; se = sqrt(var_short / 32 + var_long / 8), sample variances over the networks). **F:** `THE SHORT PICK <..> THE LONG PICK` (the long pick = the best held-out value among the eight long networks at 1800, `s02_26011` by H44's stored values; paired reading). **G:** `LOST SUBS IN THE SHORT NETWORKS: <each that lost at least one of 400 runs as name n, or NONE>`. **H:** `HO RANK VS AUDIT RANK OVER 32 SHORT: rho <r>` (Spearman, ties averaged, two decimals).

### 3. The checks
Fifteen rows (a failure and the verdict are NOT WEIGHED): (1) every arm flew 200 ids in both conditions; (2) ids aligned; (3) no score outside (0, 1); (4) every power and thrust finite, power at least 200 W; (5) the autopilot's dark and lit scores differ; (6) `law0` equals the autopilot to 1e-6; (7) the penalised objective is H27's (a fixture); (8) exactly 32 searches, each seed once, each with checkpoints 75, 150 and 225 of 963 finite weights, and the flown short networks are the 225-iteration weights; (9) the eight long networks are the stored 1800-iteration weights; (10) the first parent of every search has `wip`'s penalised value on the 72 ids; (11) the pick is the best held-out at 225; (12) `c02`, `wip`, `q02` as registered; (13) the seeds are new; (14) id sets disjoint and the audit past 77399; (15) the resume proof (H32's). Spearman computed on ranks with ties averaged is a gate row, not a check row.

### 4. The lines
`SEARCH X02` (start, then train and held-out value and accepted count at 75, 150 and 225, lost flights, per seed); `RESUMES`; `AUDIT`; `LOST` with ids; `PENALISED PRICED GAIN`; `PAIRED` (every short network and every long one against `c02`, in order); `HO VS AUDIT` (the thirty-two short); `GROUPS` (mean, sd and best of the short thirty-two and the long eight); `VERDICT`. **REPORTED, NOT IN THE VERDICT:** the best of N short networks by held-out value on the audit for N = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 (mean over subsets, a fixed sample of 200 subsets for N between 2 and 31), the same for the long eight at N = 1, 2, 4, 8, and the L1 distances of the short networks from `wip`.

### 5. Expectations, recorded BEFORE anything is run
Part A: IS BEHIND 90, TIES 9, BEATS 1. Part B: IS BEHIND 90, TIES 9, BEATS 1. Part C: IS BEHIND 75, TIES 24, BEATS 1. Part D: SHORT modal 0 of 32 (0: 40, 1 to 2: 40, 3 or more: 20 percent), LONG 1 of 8. Part E: the short mean is below the long mean, outside 2 se: 90 percent. Part F: IS BEHIND 90. Part G: at least one short network loses a run 50 percent. Part H: rho above 0.3: 40 percent (very short searches differ little, so the held-out ranks mostly noise). The pick's audit value is above the mean of the thirty-two: 70 percent. A container restart interrupts the searches at least once: 25 percent (one already has, in the queue before this round).

### 6. What will be built
`tools/ship/headToHead57.mjs` (H51's library, which is built, with the group X02, 32 seeds, 225 iterations, checkpoints 75 / 150 / 225, the 44 arms, the eight-part verdict in every branch, the fifteen checks and the lines; reads nothing from disk), `headToHead57Cmd.mjs` (`search --workers 4` resumable with state files named for H57 and skipping finished seeds, `fly --parts 4`, `fly --from-json`, a smoke mode writing only to a temporary directory) and `headToHead57-selfcheck.mjs`, with rows against loops written out in the gate and a serial mutation pass with a SABOTAGE LOG. Nothing is built before this registration is committed.

### 7. What this does NOT say
It does not say very short searches are or are not better in general: the cost is held at the number of iterations, not at wall-clock or flights, 225 iterations is one cut-off, and the long networks are the eight that exist (H32's and H34's), not a fresh thirty-two. The held-out rank is the 40-id value and is noisy by H42 and H48's design. One audit of 200 ids has a paired standard error near 0.005 (H46). It says nothing about H36 to H41, H43, H45, H47 to H56, or about other prices.

## Round H58, REGISTERED (2026-11-11, before anything is built, searched or flown; H36 to H41, H43, H45 and H47 to H57 were not finished and nothing of any of them has been read; H35's, H42's, H44's and H46's results are read; H55 is registered and built, not flown): A DIFFERENT PRICE -- THE SEARCH AT 0, POWER FREE
### 1. Why
The prices of this series' single-price network searches are 0.02 (H27 to H35 and their kin), 0.04 (H30, H31), and, registered and not yet read, 0.005 (H40), 0.01 (H50) and 0.08 (H55); H47 registers the pair 0.01 and 0.02. **Nothing has been registered at price 0**, where a kilowatt costs nothing and the penalised priced objective is the mean of the dark and lit scores alone, a lost run scoring 0. It is the pure-score end of the series: if a network is ever to beat `c02` and the stored laws on score, power being free is where it has the most room, and it is also where nothing stops a pilot spending power without limit. This round runs **H32's search at 0**: the same loop, the same 72 train ids, 1800 iterations, the same seeds, with only the price changed (H55's library with the price handed in as 0), and reads everything at price 0, with H32's four 0.02 networks as twins read at the new price. The nearest stored law is `q005` (H27's law of price 0.005); there is no stored law at 0, so Part B reads against a law of another price.
> (A) Does the champion tie `c02` at price 0? (B) The nearest stored law `q005`? (C) Is it better than H32's champion (trained at 0.02), both read at 0? (D) Seed by seed, do these networks beat their 0.02 twins at 0? (E) Do they lose subs? (F) How many are not behind `c02`? (G) Does the champion draw MORE power than H32's? (H) Read at 0.02, does the champion tie `c02` (the price cross)?

**The audit:** ids **79200-79399 (200), read once**, dark and lit, 160 s, past every earlier audit (to 78399) and disjoint from the train and held-out ids of this series. **Arms (12):** `autopilot`; `law0`; `c02`; **`q005`** (the champion of H27's stored `Q005` law search by H27's rule); H32's four 0.02 networks `s02_26011` (H32's champion) to `s02_26014`; the four new `p00_26011` to `p00_26014`. **Every arm is read at price 0 on the PENALISED score**: per id (dark score' + lit score')/2, a lost run scoring 0; the table shows 0, 0.005, 0.02 and 0.04, and the pairings at 0.02 are reported.

THE LAW OF PRICE 0.005` (`q005`). **C:** `... H32'S CHAMPION` (`s02_26011`, read at 0). **D:** `TWINS: a AHEAD, b BEHIND, c TIED` (each `p00_<seed>` against `s02_<seed>` at 0, a + b + c = 4). **E:** `LOST SUBS IN THE P00 NETWORKS: <each that lost at least one of 400 audit runs as name n, or NONE>`. **F:** `NETWORKS NOT BEHIND C02: n OF 4`. **G:** `THE CHAMPION'S MEAN POWER IS ABOVE H32'S CHAMPION'S: <YES|NO>` (the mean of the dark and lit mean powers over the audit ids). **H:** `AT 0.02 THE CHAMPION <BEATS|IS BEHIND|TIES> C02` (the pairing at 0.02).
